# ===== LM_P059 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P059 (cote 140-3, scans 1–10): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each research note of the first pass (R1–R33, from `answers/unzipped/LM_P059/LM_P059_notes.md`, section 5) is checked against `LM_P059_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with a reason. Quotations follow the corrected transcription. R3, R4, R7, R8, R11, R15, R19, R20, R24, R26 and R33 remain the first-pass model's own ideas. Notes added in this pass are labelled "second pass (own)". The first pass's table of doubts and list of abbreviations are superseded by `LM_P059_changes.md`.

Scans 3–5 are Grothendieck's contents of the second part ("2ème partie — Variations sur l'équation des lacets", §§38–49, ms pp. 291–650). Scans 6–10 are the opening of §38 (ms pp. 291 bis, 292 bis, 293). In the corrected text the cartographic group is written with the same script S as $\mathfrak S_3$: $\mathfrak S_{0,3}$, $\mathfrak S^{+}_{0,3}$ (changes, rows 3 and 58).

## First-pass notes

**R1. Scan 3, §46 heading. Grothendieck's question about notation. Kept.**
"Notations définitives (?)": the question mark qualifies the finality of the notation. *Reason:* the reading is confirmed by the ink and the TOC.

**R2. Scan 3, diagonal margin by §42 VI–VIII. Implication not pursued. Corrected (quotation).**
"NB les calculs de ces sections VI, VII, VIII en partie remplacés par ceux du §46, avec des notations plus souples…" ("souples", with "simples" as the alternative). A comparison should separate changes of notation from changes of assertion. *Reason:* the last word was misread; "sections", "remplacés" and "§46" are now secure.

**R3. Scan 3, §§42–46. Own idea (first pass). Corrected.**
A concordance of parameters should track the two cocycle families named in §42 VII and VIII, "cocycles sous ρ" and "cocycles sous σ_∞" (both in $\mathrm{Sl}(2,\widehat{\mathbb Z})'$). It should also track §44's list "(les ρ?, g?, h, f, α, β…)" and the §46 parameters (α_0, β_0, f, ζ, η). *Reason:* the first pass read "transp" and "non σ̄_0" and could not name the families.

**R4. Scan 4, §48 I. Own idea (first pass). Kept, with a check.**
If ρ acts by conjugation, ρ(α) = ραρ^{-1}, then αρ(α)^{-1} = αρα^{-1}ρ^{-1} = [α,ρ]. The heading's equality therefore fixes the convention [a,b] = aba^{-1}b^{-1}. Then [α,ρ] = [β,ρ] exactly when β^{-1}α centralises ρ, so the fibres of α ↦ [α,ρ] are the left cosets of the centraliser of ρ. In general the map is not a homomorphism. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged; the check is added.

**R5. Scan 4, §48 IV. Claim without proof (contents announcement). Kept.**
"Un épinglage canonique associé à ρ,σ" (now at p. 446) announces a canonical construction whose definition is not on these pages. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged except for the page number.

**R6. Scan 4, right, §48 XI 6°. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The ink reads "Σ ≃ Z/2Z × Z/2Z × Z/2Z", with two-stroke crosses; a free product * remains the alternative. The two readings are different statements. For ×, Σ is elementary abelian of order 8, and one must show three commuting independent involutions. For *, Σ is the infinite free product, and one needs a normal-form or ping-pong argument excluding all relations. *Reason:* the first pass took * as read.

**R7. Scan 5, §49 III. Own idea (first pass). Corrected.**
Both groups carry a hat: "et de Gl(2,Z)^∧/±1 à Gl(2,Z)^∧". The passage is a lift through the central ±1 of the completed group, parallel to "de M̃_{0,3} à Ñ_{1,1}" in the same heading. Whether completion and the quotient by ±1 commute should still be checked against §49 III itself. *Reason:* the first sign was doubtful (hat or tilde); it is a hat.

**R8. Scan 5, §49 IV. Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
"Où on se bat avec "les signes" ε_2, ε_3 dans la relation des lacets — calculs — brouillons…" The heading flags provisional sign computations. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R9. Scan 5, §49 V 8°. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The extension is "de Z^!_{ρ,σ} = M^![0] par L_ρ × L_σ" (×, or * as the alternative), in the "contexte profini". With × the kernel is a direct product; with * it is a free (profinite) product, and the proof obligations differ accordingly. *Reason:* the operator is not securely *.

**R10. Scan 5, §49 V 9°. Claim without proof. Kept.**
"L'hom can. M ⥲ M^⇄_{ρ,σ}/H_ρ ↪ M_{ρ,σ}/H_ρ" asserts an isomorphism followed by an injection. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R11. Scan 5, §49 VI and margin. Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
"raffinement de la construction précédente" and the marginal "[10°) Lien avec §48]" (in square brackets in the ink). *Reason:* the reading is unchanged except for the brackets.

**R12. Scan 6, (1). Claim without proof (recalled). Kept.**
The isomorphism Π_1((X,G),x) ⥲ Π_1((Y,d),y) is recalled from "la prop. du § précédent (p. 262)". *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R13. Scan 6, (2)–(3). Claim without proof. Kept.**
The "hom extérieur canonique" T_b ⊗ Z/d_bZ → Π' is "déduit par passage au quotient" of the peripheral map (3). The overwritten T_b|T_y and k_y|k_b remain unresolved. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R14. Scan 6, (4). Claim without proof. Kept, with a check.**
"G_a est un groupe cyclique d'ordre d_a = d_b fini". G_a is finite because the discrete group acts properly. It embeds in SO(2) because it acts faithfully near a and preserves orientation, and finite subgroups of SO(2) are cyclic. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged; the struck word before "un groupe" is still only doubtfully "donc".

**R15. Scan 6, (2)–(4). Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
An explicit check of orientation conventions on a disk with a rotation. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R16. Scan 7, after (6). Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The subgroup is G_a, now read securely: "ces classes de Π_1(X,x)-conj. de scindages (si X ≠ S²) correspondent biunivoquement aux pts fixes de G_a opérant sur X". Second pass (own) check: the exception is necessary. For X = S² and G_a = Z/n acting by rotations, Π_1(X,x) = 1, so there is one splitting class, but G_a has two fixed points. *Reason:* the first pass left the subscript as 0|a.

**R17. Scan 7, middle paragraph. Claim without proof. Kept.**
Conjugating by all of Π_1(X,G) gives "une corr. 1-1 entre l'ens. des hom ext. (6) qui relèvent l'inclusion G_a ↪ G … et l'ens des orbites de G dans X^!, i.e. les pts de Y^!" (a struck "entre" stands after "i.e."). *Reason:* the reading is corrected only in the deletion.

**R18. Scan 7, Proposition; scan 8, continuation. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The struck qualifier before "Γ ≠ 1" is not securely "finis". It begins with "(" and fits "(cycliques" best, which would be redundant with "les dits Γ sont nécessairement cycliques". No cancelled finiteness hypothesis can be read off it. "maximaux" is still undefined on these pages. *Reason:* the first pass built its finiteness discussion on "finis".

**R19. Scan 7, Proposition. Own idea (first pass). Corrected and settled.**
Take X = R², G = Z acting by translations. Every hypothesis on scan 6 holds: connected surface, discrete group acting properly, trivial stabilisers (orientation-preserving and faithful). Then Y^! = ∅, but Π_1(X,G) ≅ G, and Γ = G ≠ 1 has a lift that is maximal under any reading of "maximaux". So the Proposition as written is false unless Γ (or G) is finite; restoring "cycliques" would not help, since Z is cyclic. With finite Γ (for example G = 𝔖_3, as applied on scan 9) the statement is the expected one. *Reason:* the example is checked; it no longer depends on the status of the struck word.

**R20. Scan 7, Proposition. Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
A disk with a finite cyclic rotation group shows the role of "Γ ≠ 1". *Reason:* unchanged.

**R21. Scan 8, (7). Claim without proof. Corrected.**
"le composé … n'est autre (on s'en serait douté !) que l'hom "lacets tronqué" (2)". The source is written T_b ⊗ Z/nZ. Since it is identified with G_a through (4), n must be d_a = d_b there; it is a local notation or a slip. *Reason:* the denominator now reads n.

**R22. Scan 8, (8). Claim without proof. Kept.**
"X = X̂∖S donc Y = Ŷ∖T … avec Ŷ = X̂/G". The extension of the action to X̂ and the definition of T are not given. The retraced cluster after T remains `\unsure{\ne\varnothing}`. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R23. Scan 8, (9)–(11). Claim without proof. Kept.**
k_a is compared with k_b "(pour b = f̂(a)), moyennant l'isom. can. (11) T_a ≃ T_b", and "on aura un diagramme commutatif de compatibilité". *Reason:* f̂ is now settled. It is the extended quotient map, the f of (13).

**R24. Scan 8, (9)–(11). Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
Use the local model z ↦ z^{d_a} as a test. *Reason:* unchanged; see S3 below for the result.

**R25. Scan 9, (12) and "Nous allons appliquer ceci". Claim without proof. Kept.**
The diagram has can (≀), then d_a·id on the left column, and the isomorphism (1) on the right. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R26. Scan 9, (13). Own idea (first pass). Kept (checked again).**
4(z²−z+1)³ − 27z²(z−1)² = (z+1)²(z−2)²(2z−1)² (values 4 = 4 at z = 0 and 400 = 400 at z = 3), so f(z) − 1 = −(z+1)²(z−2)²(2z−1)²/(4(z²−z+1)³). The formula is §37's (58), "f(z) = 3³/2² · z²(z−1)²/(z²−z+1)³" (CSG draft), as the reference "(p. 286' (58))" says. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged; the source of the formula is now confirmed.

**R27. Scan 9, (14)–(16). Claim without proof. Kept.**
"On voit donc" identifies l'_0, l'_1, l'_∞ with the classes of the ε_i, σ_i and ρ "mod conjugaison". *Reason:* the reading is unchanged; the group on the left is $\mathfrak S^{+}_{0,3}=\Pi_1(U^{\mathrm{an}}_{0,3},\mathfrak S_3)$.

**R28. Scan 9, margin "ou ρ^{-1} ?". Grothendieck's question. Kept.**
See S2 below. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R29. Scan 10, first (17). Claim without proof. Kept.**
"Ceci montre que l'on peut trouver dans Π = 𝔖^+_{0,3} des conjugués ε'_0 de ε_0, σ'_0 de σ_0 … ρσ'_0ε'_0 = 1, en plus de ρ³ = σ'_0² = 1 … une présentation de Π". *Reason:* the insertion now reads "dans" securely.

**R30. Scan 10, "on devrait trouver". Grothendieck's conjecture or expectation. Corrected.**
"on devrait trouver dans le premier mb [= membre] trois générateurs τ'_0, τ'_1, τ'_∞ (correspondants aux gén. analogues σ_0, σ_1, σ_∞ des groupes cartographiques correspondant(s))". The involutions are sought in the left member $\mathfrak S_{0,3}$ of (16), together with the claim that the second (17) presents it. *Reason:* "mb" and the plural "des groupes cartographiques" are new readings.

**R31. Scan 10, (18). Grothendieck's conjecture or expectation. Kept.**
"Il faudrait que ~~τ'_∞τ'_0 soit conjugué de σ_0~~ l'on ait τ'_∞τ'_1 = ε'_0; τ'_0τ'_∞ = σ'_0, τ'_1τ'_0 = ρ". *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R32. Scan 10, (18) against the first (17). Implication not pursued. Kept (checked).**
Using the involution relations, (τ'_1τ'_0)(τ'_0τ'_∞)(τ'_∞τ'_1) = 1. Also (τ'_0τ'_∞)² = σ'_0² and (τ'_1τ'_0)³ = ρ³. So (18) carries the second (17) onto the first, provided the overwritten subscript in (τ'_{0|∞}τ'_∞)² is 0; with ∞ the relation would be trivial. *Reason:* the check holds.

**R33. Scan 10, second (17) and (18). Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
The parity character (every τ' ↦ −1) is well defined because every relator has even length. By Reidemeister–Schreier, its kernel is generated by a = τ'_1τ'_0 and b = τ'_0τ'_∞ with a³ = b² = 1, which is the first (17)'s Z/3 * Z/2 ≅ Sl(2,Z)'. *Reason:* unchanged, with the kernel computed.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S1. Scan 10, (16) and the second (17). Claim checked: holds only up to ±1.**
Three involutions with (τ'_0τ'_∞)² = (τ'_1τ'_0)³ = 1 and no other relations give the Coxeter group of the (2,3,∞) triangle, the extended modular group PGL(2,Z) = Gl(2,Z)/±1. This group has trivial centre. Π_1(U^{an}_{0,3}, 𝔖_3 × Z/2) is the same group: Γ(2)/±1, free of rank 2, has quotient of order 12 ≅ 𝔖_3 × Z/2 in PGL(2,Z). So the annotation "≃ Gl(2,Z)" under 𝒯'_{1,1} is true only as Gl(2,Z)' = Gl(2,Z)/±1, matching "≃ Sl(2,Z)'" in (15). Taken literally with Gl(2,Z), whose centre is {±1}, it is false.

**S2. Scan 9 (14)–(16) with scan 10's first (17). Grothendieck's question "ou ρ^{-1} ?" — a check.**
The assignment ε'_0 ↦ l'_0, σ'_0 ↦ l'_1, ρ ↦ l'_∞ carries ρσ'_0ε'_0 = 1 to l'_∞l'_1l'_0 = 1, and ρ³ = σ'_0² = 1 to l'_∞³ = l'_1² = 1. So (17) is exactly the presentation (14) of Π'_{0,3} ≅ Z/3 * Z/2. Replacing ρ by ρ^{-1} alone breaks the product relation. The marginal question is therefore about the orientation of all three loops at once (invert all three and reverse the order of the product), not about ρ alone.

**S3. Scan 9, (12). Claim checked.**
In the local model z ↦ z^{d_a} near a ∈ S, a small positive loop around the puncture a maps to d_a times the positive loop around b = f̂(a). So the composite T_a → Π_1(X,G) ⥲ Π_1(Y,d) equals k_b ∘ (d_a·id) ∘ can, and diagram (12) commutes as drawn. This settles R24's test for orientation-preserving charts.

**S4. Scan 9, (13). Implication not pursued.**
f(z) = (27/4)·z²(z−1)²/(z²−z+1)³ = 1728/j(z), where j(λ) = 256(λ²−λ+1)³/(λ²(λ−1)²) is the j-invariant of the Legendre curve. So f maps the cusps {0,1,∞} to 0 (j = ∞), {2,−1,1/2} to 1 (j = 1728) and {−j̄,−j} to ∞ (j = 0), with ramification orders 2, 2 and 3. Over 1 and ∞ these are the orders in (14), l'_1² = l'_∞³ = 1. Over 0 the points are punctures, and the order 2 is the factor d_a = 2 of (12), recorded in (16) as "ε_i² = l_i".

**S5. Scan 7, Proposition. Implication not pursued: the Proposition inherits "si X ≠ S²".**
For X = S² and G = Z/n acting by rotations (n ≥ 2), Y^! has two points. But Π_1(X,G) = G is abelian with a single maximal lift (Γ = G), so the bijection fails. The exception stated two sentences earlier for the splitting classes is also needed in the Proposition, together with the finiteness of Γ from R19.

**S6. Scan 4, §46 IV–V. Reading consequence (conjecture about the structure).**
In IV, "0,1,∞" is struck and replaced by "−j̄, −j", next to 2, −1, 1/2. These are the points of U_{0,3} with nontrivial stabiliser in 𝔖_3: order 3 at {−j, −j̄}, order 2 at {2, −1, 1/2}. The cusps {0,1,∞} are thus moved out of IV. This favours the TOC's "q ∈ {0,1,∞}" for the subgroups listed under V ("Récapitulation en termes de M[0]"), although the ink of the sign there looks crossed (∉). The point stays open in the transcription.


# ===== LM_P060 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P060 (cote 140-3, scans 11–20): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each research note of the first pass (R1–R36, from `answers/unzipped/LM_P060/LM_P060_notes.md`, section 5) is checked against `LM_P060_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with a reason. Quotations follow the corrected transcription. R1, R10, R13, R18, R20, R25, R26, R31, R33 and R36 are the first-pass model's own ideas. Notes added in this pass are labelled "second pass (own)". The first pass's table of doubts and list of abbreviations are superseded by `LM_P060_changes.md` (several of their entries, such as ν_a, "type" and the ornamented group letter, had already been revised in the fixed first pass).

Scans 11–20 are sheets 10–19 of the file, in §38 ("Détermination de Π'_{0,3} ⥲ 𝔖^+_{0,3}", ms pp. 293–298). Scans 11–13 state the quotient theorem for a discrete group 𝒢 with an invariant subgroup G and a signature d_X. Scans 14–18 apply it to X = U^{an}_{0,3}, 𝒢 = 𝒯_{0,3} = 𝔖_3 × ℤ/2, G = 𝔖_3, ℋ = ℤ/2ℤ, and compute the involutive generators τ'_0, τ'_1, τ'_∞. Scans 19–20 begin the comparison of Π̃'_{0,3} ≃ 𝔖_{0,3} with GP(1,ℤ) = Gl(2,ℤ)/{±1} through the half-plane and elliptic curves. No witness covers these pages.

## First-pass notes

**R1. Scan 11, stabiliser hypothesis. Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
"les stabilisateurs G_x (x∈X) opèrent fidèlement au voisinage de x, en conservant l'orientation locale en x". Testing the quotient construction on a disk with a finite rotation group is a sensible local check; under the hypothesis each nontrivial G_x is finite cyclic, which is what makes ν_x = [G_x] a ramification index. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R2. Scan 11, d_Y(y) = ν_x d_X(x). Implication not pursued. Corrected.**
The subscript reads ν_x ("si y = f(x), ν_x = [G_x]"). Independence of the lift x is immediate: the points over y form one G-orbit, G_{gx} = gG_xg^{-1} has the same order, and d_X is 𝒢-invariant. *Reason:* the first pass left the subscript and the check open; there is no gap here.

**R3. Scan 12, (19) and c). Claim without proof. Kept.**
"Ce foncteur est une équivalence de catégories" is asserted without proof. The same statement for 𝒢 with invariant subgroup G is footnote 148 of §37 in the reading edition (CSG ll. 10522–10528), also unproved; the case 𝒢 = G, d_X = 1 is §37's Proposition b). *Reason:* reading unchanged; cross-reference added.

**R4. Scan 12, d). Claim without proof. Kept.**
"Description d'un foncteur quasi-inverse par image inverse "normalisée"" announces the inverse; §37's N.B. (CSG ll. 10533–10537) names it, the normalised (X, G)-covering of X ×_Y Y', but neither place checks unit and counit. *Reason:* reading unchanged; cross-reference added.

**R5. Scan 12, the two "Remarques". Claim without proof. Corrected.**
Both begin "OPS" (on peut supposer): one may assume d_X > 0, otherwise replace X by X∖S and Y by Y∖T for S = {x ∈ X^! | d_X(x) = 0}, T = S/G; and one may assume d_X(x) = 1 ⇒ [G_x] ≠ 1, otherwise remove those points from X^!. The second reduction is harmless, since such points impose no condition. The first reads d_X = 0 as "puncture"; that convention for Rev(X, d_X, 𝒢) should be stated before the reduction is used. *Reason:* "Ops|Obs" was not read as the reduction formula.

**R6. Scan 12, (20). Implication not pursued. Kept.**
When d_X = 1 on X^! = {x | G_x ≠ {1}}, Rev(X, d_X, 𝒢) = Rev(X, 𝒢), and (19) identifies Rev(Y, d_Y, ℋ) with the unramified equivariant category "(sans donnée de ramification)": d_Y is then recovered from the stabiliser orders. The connective before "le cas où" now reads "donc|dans". *Reason:* content unchanged.

**R7. Scan 13, Corollaire (21). Claim without proof. Kept.**
An equivalence of categories gives a based isomorphism only once the fibre functors at x and y = f(x) are shown to correspond. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R8. Scan 13, elements as pairs (γ, ℓ). Claim without proof. Corrected.**
With "provient" restored (struck, then dotted for stet), the text asserts that every α ∈ Π_1((X, d_X, 𝒢), x) comes from a pair (γ, ℓ), γ ∈ 𝒢, ℓ a path class in X∖X^! from x to γ^{-1}x, two pairs giving the same element iff γ' = γ and ℓ'^{-1}ℓ lies in the invariant subgroup generated by the λ_a^{d_X(a)}, "pour tt a ∈ X^!". The product of pairs is not written. *Reason:* "provient" and "tt" were misread.

**R9. Scan 13, the kernel. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
"le ss-groupe invariant engendré par les λ_a^{d_X(a)}" is the usual description of the orbifold group of (X, d_X) as a quotient of Π_1(X∖X^!). The glyph before λ_a is a struck k or ℓ, not a factor. *Reason:* reading of the glyph.

**R10. Scan 13, the map f_U. Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
Checking the based comparison on a punctured disk quotient tests the exponent and inverse conventions of scan 14. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R11. Scan 14, "On voit tout de suite". Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The ink writes the induced map as Π_1(f_U, a); the mechanism, λ_a ↦ λ_b^{ν(a)} and so λ_a^{d_X(a)} ↦ λ_b^{d_X(a)ν(a)} = λ_b^{d_Y(b)}, is the local model z ↦ z^{ν(a)}. The choice of base paths for the λ_a, which are defined only up to conjugacy, still has to be fixed. *Reason:* base letter read as a.

**R12. Scan 14, d_Y(b) = d_X(a)ν(a). Implication not pursued. Kept.**
This identity is exactly what lets the defining relations upstairs map into those downstairs; the converse direction (existence of the inverse) needs the normalised pull-back of R4. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R13. Scan 14, f(z). Own idea (first pass). Corrected: carried out.**
f(z) = (27/4)·z²(z−1)²/(z²−z+1)³ has double zeros at 0, 1, ∞, triple poles at the roots −j̄, −j of z²−z+1, and f(2) = f(−1) = f(½) = 1 with f − 1 = −(z−2)²(z+1)²(2z−1)²/(4(z²−z+1)³). So Y^! = {1, ∞} with d_Y = 2{1} + 3{∞} on Y = ℙ^{1an}∖{0}, as written. f has real coefficients, so ℋ = ℤ/2ℤ acts on Y by complex conjugation. *Reason:* the check is done.

**R14. Scan 15, the six points over y = −j̄. Claim without proof. Corrected: checked.**
−j̄ is not a branch value, so f^{-1}(−j̄) has six points; since f is real they split 3 + 3 between the hemispheres. The order-3 element z ↦ 1/(1−z) of 𝔖_3 has determinant 1 and fixed points −j̄, −j, and sends 0 → 1 → ∞ → 0 and ½ → 2 → −1 → ½. So it permutes the triangles (−j̄,0,½) → (−j̄,1,2) → (−j̄,∞,−1) cyclically, hence their three points transitively (the other order-3 element gives the reverse cycle). *Reason:* verified.

**R15. Scan 15, "j'ai envie de prendre (0,½)". Implication not pursued. Kept.**
The other two choices change x by ρ^{±1}, hence the generators by conjugation; the following formulas are literal only for the chosen triangle (−j̄, 0, ½). *Reason:* unchanged.

**R16. Scan 15, the generators τ'_0, τ'_1, τ'_∞. Claim without proof. Kept.**
"s'obtiennent en prenant des chemins ℓ'_0, ℓ'_1, ℓ'_∞ de y = −j̄ vers τy = −j, qui sont les|des arcs de grand cercle coupant l'équateur resp. en 2, −1, ½". That these give involutions is shown only on scans 17–18. *Reason:* unchanged apart from the article.

**R17. Scan 16, the lifts λ_0, λ_1, λ_∞ and the margin. Claim without proof. Kept.**
The lifts are said to cross "chacun" the barycentric subdivision in exactly one point, and the margin characterises z_0, z_1, z_∞ by the triangles (−j̄,½,1), (0,−j̄,−1), (½,0,−j). A lifting argument is not given. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R18. Scan 16, the g_i against the pairs. Own idea (first pass). Corrected: the mismatch is in the ink.**
At 3× the display reads τ'_0 = (σ̇_0τ̇, λ_0), τ'_1 = (σ̇_1τ̇, λ_1), τ'_∞ = (σ̇_∞τ̇, λ_∞), and (21) repeats these subscripts. The insertion gives g_0 = σ̇_∞τ̇, g_1 = σ̇_0τ̇, g_∞ = τ̇, and scans 17–18 compute with exactly these ("(22) τ'_0 = σ̃_∞ (sur σ̇_∞τ̇), τ'_1 = σ̃_0 (sur σ̇_0τ̇), τ'_∞ = τ_∞ (sur τ̇)"). The two displays are uncorrected earlier states; the inserted g_i are the operative values. *Reason:* verified on the image.

**R19. Scan 16, change of base point. Claim without proof. Kept.**
"Nous allons utiliser l'arc de grand cercle ℓ de −j̄ = P … à x" leads to λ'_i = (ġ_i(ℓ^{-1}))(λ_i)ℓ on scan 17. The composition convention (left to right) has to be fixed for that formula. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R20. Scan 16–17, the cancelled sentence. Own idea (first pass). Kept, with the corrected wording.**
The struck text says the paths, "à l'exception du dernier, ne recoupent pas le graphe barycentrique … sauf en P = −j̄, le dernier le recoupant seul^t en un autre point, situé sur le segment sphérique (0,½)". Its replacement by "chemins de P vers ġ_iP" and the homotopy statements of scan 17 suggests that only homotopy classes matter. *Reason:* "coupent/coupant" corrected to "recoupent/recoupant".

**R21. Scan 17, the three homotopies. Claim without proof. Kept.**
λ'_0 and λ'_1 are "homotope[s] au lacet trivial dans U_{0,3}", λ'_∞ to the arc of great circle (−j̄, ½, −j) from P to τ̇P. The drawings suggest the homotopies but do not prove them. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R22. Scan 18, (23). Claim without proof. Corrected: the four first relations checked.**
"les quatre premières sont claires": by (22) the first three say σ̃_∞² = σ̃_0² = τ_∞² = 1 in the notation of [C], and with the NB τ_∞ = σ_∞σ̃_∞ = σ̃_∞σ_∞ one gets τ'_0τ'_∞ = σ̃_∞σ̃_∞σ_∞ = σ_∞, so (τ'_0τ'_∞)² = σ_∞² = 1. The fifth reduces to σ̃_0σ̃_∞ = ρ^{-1}, "[C], formule 13". *Reason:* the reduction is now explicit.

**R23. Scan 18, (24)–(25). Claim without proof. Corrected.**
(25) follows from (24): ℓ'_∞ℓ'_1ℓ'_0 = ρ^{-1}σ_∞σ_∞ρ = 1, ℓ'^2_1 = σ_∞² = 1, ℓ'^3_∞ = ρ^{-3} = 1. That (25) is a complete presentation is the content of R25. The index of the middle relation is retraced (1|∞) in the ink; the algebra requires 1. *Reason:* reading doubt added; derivation given.

**R24. Scan 18, the diagonal margin. Implication not pursued. Corrected: checked.**
From ρ = τ'_0τ'_1 and (23) every marginal identity holds: τ_1 = ρ^{-1}τ_∞ρ = τ'_1τ'_0τ'_∞τ'_0τ'_1 = τ'_1τ'_∞τ'_1 (as τ'_0τ'_∞τ'_0 = τ'_∞), σ_0 = τ'_0τ'_1τ'_0τ'_∞τ'_1τ'_0, ℓ_1 = τ'_0τ'_1τ'_∞τ'_1τ'_0τ'_∞, ε_∞ = ρ^{-1}ε_0ρ = τ'_1τ'_∞τ'_1τ'_0. So the doubtful final factors of the transcription are the algebraically right ones. The "(= τ'_∞τ'_0)" belongs to σ_∞ = τ'_0τ'_∞ (an involution), not to τ_1. *Reason:* the check is done; the readings stay marked as doubtful.

**R25. Scan 18, even subgroup. Own idea (first pass). Corrected (made precise).**
The words of even length form a subgroup of index 2 in ⟨τ'_0, τ'_1, τ'_∞ | τ'^2_i, (τ'_0τ'_∞)², (τ'_1τ'_0)³⟩, and Reidemeister–Schreier gives it the generators ℓ'_1 = τ'_0τ'_∞, ℓ'_∞ = τ'_1τ'_0 with the relations ℓ'^2_1 = ℓ'^3_∞ = 1 only. So (25) is the presentation ℤ/2 * ℤ/3 of Π'_{0,3} ≅ Sl(2,ℤ)', the "caractéristiques" relations (P059, R33, made the same computation for scan 10). *Reason:* the vague comparison is replaced by the result.

**R26. Scans 17–18, verification workflow. Own idea (first pass). Dropped.**
The proposed algebraic check of the marginal identities is carried out in R24 and S3, without altering the diplomatic text. *Reason:* superseded.

**R27. Scan 19, opening. Claim without proof (programme). Corrected.**
"Je voudrais encore expliciter la relation de Π̃'_{0,3} (gr. cartographique non orienté) ≃ 𝔖_{0,3} = Aut_{loc|ext}(Π_{0,3}) avec GP(1,ℤ) ≃ Gl(2,ℤ)' =^{déf} Gl(2,ℤ)/{±1}". These two pages begin the comparison through the half-plane but do not finish it. *Reason:* the group letter (𝔖) and the 2 of Gl(2,ℤ)' are now read.

**R28. Scan 19, (26)–(28). Claim without proof. Corrected: checked.**
For det u < 0, Im f_u(z) = −det u · Im z / |c z̄ + d|² > 0, so (28) preserves D. Because the entries are real, complex conjugation commutes with every Möbius map, which gives f_{uv} = f_u ∘ f_v in all four sign cases. The kernel on Gl(2,ℝ) is ℝ^*, so GP(1,ℝ) acts faithfully, as the group of conformal and anticonformal automorphisms of D. Grothendieck's "conformes" includes the orientation-reversing maps, as in his earlier "automorphismes conformes … Sl(2,R)˜ = {det u = ±1} … θ_u τ^{det u}" (CSG ll. 2996–3002). *Reason:* checked; cross-reference added.

**R29. Scan 19, diagonal NB. Claim without proof. Kept.**
"GP(1,ℝ) a deux comp. connexes": det(λu) = λ² det u, so the sign of the determinant is defined on Gl(2,ℝ)/ℝ^*; the neutral component, GP(1,ℝ)^+, is the image of Sl(2,ℝ) (and of Gl(2,ℝ)^+) and is Sl(2,ℝ)/±1. *Reason:* unchanged; the parenthesis may read "(ou de Gl(2,ℝ)^+)".

**R30. Scan 19, elliptic curves and lattices. Claim without proof. Kept.**
"la donnée d'une courbe elliptique E sur ℂ équivaut à celle d'un espace V de dim 1 sur ℂ, et d'un "réseau" π ⊂ V", with the marginal "équivalence de catégories …": uniformisation, and morphisms must be specified. *Reason:* unchanged.

**R31. Scans 19–20, the conjugate branch. Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
The det < 0 branch should correspond to replacing (E, η) by its complex conjugate, which reverses the orientation of π; this would extend the Sl(2,ℤ)' action on D to Gl(2,ℤ)' as in (28). *Reason:* unchanged.

**R32. Scan 20, normalisation e_1 = 1. Implication not pursued. Kept.**
The passage from a rigidification "de Jacobi unimodulaire de π" (a basis with e_1 ∧ e_2 = η) to τ ∈ D parametrises isomorphism classes of rigidified curves; whether rigidified objects have nontrivial automorphisms (−1 acts on the basis) is not addressed. *Reason:* unchanged apart from the reading π.

**R33. Scan 20, "de rg 1". Own idea (first pass). Corrected.**
The digit is a 1 in the ink (the flagged form of "dim 1" and "devenant 1"), but it is a slip: scan 19 says "sous-groupe discret de rg 2", and τ ∉ ℝ is exactly the condition for 1 and τ to span a lattice of rank 2. *Reason:* the slip is confirmed from the page itself.

**R34. Scan 20, τ ↦ (aτ+b)/(cτ+d). Claim without proof. Kept.**
The formula holds when the matrix acts on the column (τ, 1)^T, i.e. on the basis (e_2, e_1); acting on (e_1, e_2) gives the transposed or inverse formula. *Reason:* unchanged; the convention is now stated.

**R35. Scan 20, last lines. Claim without proof. Kept.**
"Ce groupe opère proprement, Il a exactement deux trajectoires critiques": in Sl(2,ℤ)' = Sl(2,ℤ)/±1 the orbits of i (stabiliser of order 2) and of e^{2πi/3} (order 3) are the only ones with nontrivial stabiliser, from the standard fundamental domain. The page ends at "corres-". *Reason:* unchanged; "l'opération naturelle" now read.

**R36. Scan 20, Sl(2,ℤ)' ≃ GP(1,ℤ)^+. Own idea (first pass). Kept.**
The central ±1 acts trivially on D, so the effective group is Sl(2,ℤ)', and stacky (automorphism-retaining) and coarse quotients differ by exactly this ±1. *Reason:* unchanged.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S1. Scan 18 against scans 9–10 (P059). Grothendieck's question "ou ρ^{-1} ?" answered.**
Scan 18 settles the marginal question of scan 9 in favour of ρ^{-1}: with ρ = τ'_0τ'_1 (margin), (23) and (24) give ℓ'_∞ = τ'_1τ'_0 = ρ^{-1}, ℓ'_1 = σ_∞, ℓ'_0 = σ_∞ρ. This revises (18) of scan 10 (τ'_1τ'_0 = ρ, with ε'_0 = σ'_0ρ^{-1}) by inverting ρ throughout, and (25) holds because σ_∞² = ρ³ = 1.

**S2. Scan 11–13 against §37. Implication not pursued.**
The theorem of scans 11–13 (𝒢 with invariant subgroup G, arbitrary signature d_X) generalises §37's Proposition (𝒢 = G, d_X = 1); footnote 148 of §37 (CSG ll. 10522–10528) announces exactly this "variante" with the application "𝔖_{0,3} ≃ 𝒯'_{1,1} (GL(2,ℤ)^+/±1) (où 𝔖_{0,3} est une extension de 𝒯_{0,3} = 𝔖_3 × ℤ/2ℤ par π_{0,3})", which scans 14–20 carry out.

**S3. Scan 18, margin. Claim checked: ε_i² = ℓ_i.**
From the marginal formulas, ε_0² = (τ'_∞τ'_1)² = ℓ_0, ε_1² = τ'_0τ'_1τ'_∞τ'_1τ'_∞τ'_0 = ℓ_1 (using τ'_0τ'_∞ = τ'_∞τ'_0) and ε_∞² = (τ'_1τ'_∞τ'_1τ'_0)² = ℓ_∞. These are the relations "ε_i² = ℓ_i" announced in (16) on scan 9; they also support the doubtful final factors of the margin.

**S4. Scan 17. Implication not pursued.**
Since λ'_0 and λ'_1 are null-homotopic in U_{0,3}, the generators τ'_0 and τ'_1 are, after the change of base point, the pure elements σ̇_∞τ̇ and σ̇_0τ̇ of 𝒢, the reflections in the great circles (∞, −j̄, ½, −j) (Re z = ½) and (0, −j̄, 2, −j) (|z − 1| = 1) named under the formulas; both fix P = −j̄. Only τ'_∞ carries a path, the arc through ½ from P to τ̇P = −j. This is the geometric content of (22): τ'_0 = σ̃_∞, τ'_1 = σ̃_0 lie over reflections, τ'_∞ = τ_∞ over τ̇.

**S5. Scans 11–20. Own idea (second pass): slips of the pen kept in the transcription.**
Several readings are faithful to the ink but not to the sense: "au dessus de Y = X'/G" (scan 11, for X/G), "γ̇ l'image de 𝒢 dans ℋ" (scan 13, for γ), the base point a|x of λ_a (scan 13), "Π_1(f_U, a)" (scan 14), the pairs (σ̇_iτ̇, λ_i) against the inserted g_i (scan 16), "de rg 1" (scan 20) and "τ de ℂ|E" (scan 20). A reading edition would need an apparatus for them; the diplomatic text keeps them as written.


# ===== LM_P061 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P061 (cote 140-3, scans 21–30): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Text checked: `second_pass/LM_P061_checked.tex`. Scan 21 ends §38 (the modular curve as a quotient of the half-plane); scans 22–30 are §39, "Les conditions de congruence pour l'opération extérieure de Γℚ sur Π̂₀,₃" (ms. pp. 299–303). Each first-pass note keeps its number and is marked **keep**, **correct** or **drop**, with the reason. Notes A–I are the second pass's own, labelled as such. Mathematical identities stated in the notes were checked by hand; anything not proved here is said to be so.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 21, (29) — unproved claim — correct.** The canonical analytic isomorphism D/GP(1,ℤ)⁺ ≃ ℙ^{1an}∖{0} = Y is asserted without proof. The ink settles the normalisation the note left open: the ramification-3 orbit goes to ∞, the ramification-2 orbit to 1, and the removed point 0 is the cusp. Own note A gives the explicit map.

2. **Scan 21, (30) and last paragraph — conjecture / stated intention — correct.** "que je voudrais expliciter" announces an identification of τ′₀, τ′₁, τ′∞ with elements of Gl^{±}(2,ℤ)′ (possibly Gl^{±′}); it is not carried out on these pages. The fundamental group of (30) is now read π₁^{±}(Y, d̲; y) in preference to π₁^{*}, which fits 𝒢 = GP(1,ℤ) containing the orientation-reversing maps.

3. **Scan 21 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Choosing matrix representatives and checking their orders against the normalisation of (29) is the right test; own note B carries it out for one consistent choice.

4. **Scan 22, (1) — unproved claim — correct.** The vertical map N₁,₁ → Gl(2,Ẑ) is not constructed on the page (presumably the action on the Tate module of the universal elliptic curve, with det = χ by the Weil pairing; this interpretation is not in the ink). Once that map exists, "passe au quotient" follows: the kernel of Sl(2,ℤ)^ → Sl(2,Ẑ) is the kernel of 𝒯̂⁺₁,₁ → Gl(2,Ẑ), hence normal in N₁,₁.

5. **Scan 23, centraliser step — unproved claim — correct (an omitted computation, not a gap).** "commute à Sl(2,Ẑ) … c'est un scalaire" is a two-line matrix computation valid over any commutative ring (own note C). With it, the injectivity of (5) is fully proved by the argument on the page.

6. **Scan 23 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Repeating the argument over ℤ/nℤ works verbatim; own note C does it, and it proves the NB of scan 24.

7. **Scan 24, (6) — unproved claim — correct.** The notation is now read (Γ_{ℚ ab})₂, i.e. Γℚ^{ab}/(Γℚ^{ab})². "Par la théorie du corps de classes pour ℚ" is the Kronecker–Weber theorem: the kernel of (6) fixes the compositum of all quadratic fields (own note D). "Cette dernière est fidèle" is the injectivity of (5).

8. **Scan 24, marginal NB — unproved claim — keep, verified.** Own note C over R = ℤ/nℤ shows that the kernel of (ℤ/nℤ)* → Autext Sl(2,ℤ/nℤ) is the group of squares, so ((ℤ/nℤ)*)₂ acts faithfully. The modulus letter (n or m) and the parentheses stay doubtful in the transcription.

9. **Scan 24, around (7) — unpursued implication — keep.** The two "conditions assez fortes" (stability of the congruence kernel; an outer action on Sl(2,Ẑ) coming from Ẑ*/Ẑ*²) are independent tests on an outer automorphism. The text does not claim that they characterise the image of Γℚ.

10. **Scan 25, (8)–(9) — unproved claim ("sauf erreur") — correct.** The group is T = T∞(Q̄₀), a plain T (the first pass's T̄ was a misreading). Its image is the closure of the cyclic group generated by a unipotent element, conjugate in Sl(2,Ẑ) to {(1 0; n 1) : n ∈ Ẑ} as stated; the struck inner hat on ℤ in (8) stays doubtful.

11. **Scan 25, (10) — unproved claim — correct.** "sera" and "caractère" are now read without doubt, and the group is T, not T̄. The claim that Γ → Aut(T) ≃ Ẑ* is the cyclotomic character amounts to T ≃ Ẑ(1) as a Galois module; the sign of the exponent depends on the generator convention, which the page does not fix.

12. **Scans 25–26, "on vérifie qu'" and margin "faire la vérification" — unproved claim — correct.** The two marks flag the existence and uniqueness of χ(u) as unchecked. The verification fails for uniqueness in the Sl(2,Ẑ) form (12): there χ(u) is unique only modulo Ẑ*² (own note E).

13. **Scans 25–26, D(a)U(n)D(a)⁻¹ = U(a⁻²n) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The identity is right (checked by multiplication), and it settles the question the note left open: inside Sl(2,Ẑ) it shows that χ(u) is not unique in Ẑ*, only in Ẑ*/Ẑ*² (own note E). The note's T̄ should read T.

14. **Scan 26, "Ceci dit" — unpursued implication — keep.** Separating the outer automorphisms with the lacet property from those that also preserve the kernel of (7) and act on Sl(2,Ẑ) through the composite is the structure the text sets up but does not analyse.

15. **Scans 26–27, centre {±1} — unproved claim — correct.** Because of the centre, the outer action does not determine N₁,₁; after dividing by the centre, N′₁,₁ is obtained as the pull-back of (13), which is read with Aut_lac (ext struck) and Autext_lac. This uses that the centre of Sl(2,ℤ)^ is exactly {±1} and that Sl(2,ℤ)^/{±1}, the completion of ℤ/2 ∗ ℤ/3, has trivial centre; both are true but not argued on these pages.

16. **Scan 27, "Cette extension … a été aussi interprétée" — unproved claim — correct.** The identifications N′₁,₁ ≃ M₀,₃ ≃ N′₀,₄, 𝔖̂₀,₃ ≃ 𝒯̂′₀,₄ and the extension of 𝔖₃ ≃ Sl(2,ℤ/2ℤ) by Π̂₀,₃ are recalled, not proved here. The letter is now read 𝔖, the cartographic group of §38.

17. **Scan 27, "Nous savons déjà" — unproved claim (recall) — correct.** The faithfulness refers back to §35, titled in the author's table "L'injectivité de ΓQ → Autext_lac(π̂0,3)" (IUT witness l. 94). The commutation with the outer action of 𝔖₃ is reinterpreted on scan 28 as a case of the congruence conditions.

18. **Scans 26–27, central lifting data — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Keeping the quotient extension and the central data separate is sensible; no classification is done on these pages. Confidence medium, as given.

19. **Scan 28, triviality at modulus 2 — unproved claim — correct.** The factorisation through ((ℤ/nℤ)*)₂ is the finite-level form of (5) (own note C); triviality for n = 2 is (ℤ/2ℤ)* = 1, and non-triviality for n ≥ 3 is note 22. The ink reads "n ≠ 1,2 i.e. n ≥ 3", with a struck "2," before.

20. **Scan 28, "On peut essayer de les exprimer" — proposed investigation — keep.** Whether the reformulation through Π̂₀,₃ keeps all of the congruence conditions or only consequences is exactly the question of scan 30 (note 27).

21. **Scan 28, Π(2m) open, normal, invariant — unproved claim — correct.** The symbol is 𝔖₃. Openness and normality are immediate (kernels of maps to finite groups); invariance under 𝔖₃ holds because Π(2m) is the intersection of Π̂₀,₃ with a normal subgroup of the group mapping to Sl(2,2m). That group is 𝒯̂₁,₁ with superscript +′, but the ink adds a stroke and a hump (+′ or +/n), so the precise group is now doubtful.

22. **Scan 28, squares for n ≥ 3 — own idea (first pass) — keep, verified.** (ℤ/nℤ)* is a finite abelian group of order φ(n), even for n ≥ 3, and −1 ≠ 1 lies in the kernel of squaring, so squaring is not surjective and ((ℤ/nℤ)*)₂ ≠ 1. This checks only the unit-group part, as the note says.

23. **Scan 29, (15)–(16) — unproved claim — keep.** The maps φ₂ₘ, φ∞ are "pas bien difficile d'expliciter" by reduction mod 2m; no construction is given. (The ink writes "qu'il en doit", grammatically "ne".)

24. **Scan 29, (17)–(18) — unproved claim — correct.** Exactness at Sl(2,Ẑ)′ in (17) needs the image of Π̂₀,₃ to be the whole kernel of reduction mod 2: Π₀,₃ ≃ Γ(2)/{±1}, and Γ(2) is dense in the principal congruence subgroup of level 2 of Sl(2,Ẑ) because Sl(2,ℤ) → Sl(2,ℤ/N) is onto for every N. The page asserts this without argument.

25. **Scan 29, "de façon naturelle de définir une opération ext." — unproved claim — keep.** Independence of choices up to inner automorphisms and compatibility between the levels 2m and ∞ are not discussed.

26. **Scan 29, Π(∞) = ∩ₘ Π(2m) — own idea (first pass) — keep, verified.** Every n divides some 2m, and an element of Sl(2,Ẑ) congruent to ±1 modulo every 2m is ±1, so the kernel of Sl(2,Ẑ) → Sl(2,Ẑ)′ is the intersection of the kernels of the maps to Sl(2,ℤ/2mℤ)′. Hence Π(∞) = ∩ₘ Π(2m).

27. **Scan 30, "On devrait regarder si cette formulation est vraiment équivalente" — explicit question — keep.** Grothendieck asks whether the conditions on Π̂₀,₃ are equivalent to the congruence conditions in 𝒯̂⁺′₁,₁ "tout entier, et non seulement une conséquence", and leaves it as an exercise.

28. **Scan 30, restriction to the congruence kernel — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Comparing the outer action on the extension of 𝔖₃ by Π̂₀,₃ with its restriction to Π̂₀,₃ is the natural test; agreement on a subgroup need not determine the outer action on the whole group. Confidence medium, as given.

## Second-pass notes (own)

A. **Scan 21, (29) — own verification of an unproved claim.** The isomorphism is t = 1728/j(τ): j(ρ) = 0 at ρ = exp(2iπ/3) (the τ with Z[ρ], ramification 3) goes to t = ∞, j(i) = 1728 (ramification 2) goes to t = 1, and the cusp (j = ∞) goes to the removed point t = 0. This is exactly the normalisation written, so Y = ℙ¹∖{0}. Confidence high (standard values of j).

B. **Scan 21, (30) — own idea.** In Sl(2,ℤ) put S = (0 −1; 1 0), U = (0 −1; 1 1), T₀ = (1 1; 0 1); then S² = −1, U³ = −1 and SU = −T₀, so in Sl(2,ℤ)′ = GP(1,ℤ)⁺ the classes satisfy T₀⁻¹·S·U = 1, with orders ∞, 2, 3 and fixed points ∞ (cusp), i, ρ. Thus τ′₀ ↦ ±T₀⁻¹, τ′₁ ↦ ±S, τ′∞ ↦ ±U is one candidate for the identification Grothendieck wants, up to orientation and base-point conventions the page does not fix; the elements of determinant −1 (e.g. ±diag(1,−1)) are what π₁^{±} and Gl^{±} add. Confidence high for the identities, medium for the match with his τ′.

C. **Scans 23–24 — own proof of the centraliser step and of the NB.** For a commutative ring R and A = (a b; c d), AE = EA with E = (1 1; 0 1) forces c = 0 and a = d, and AF = FA with F = (1 0; 1 1) forces b = 0; so the centraliser of Sl(2,R) in Gl(2,R) is R*. Hence int(u) is inner on Sl(2,R) iff u ∈ Sl(2,R)·R*, and the kernel of R* → Autext Sl(2,R) is R*². R = Ẑ gives the injectivity of (5); R = ℤ/nℤ proves the NB of scan 24 with ((ℤ/nℤ)*)₂ = (ℤ/nℤ)*/squares. Confidence high.

D. **Scan 24, (6) — own verification.** Ẑ*/Ẑ*² = ∏ₚ ℤₚ*/ℤₚ*² ≅ (ℤ/2)² × ∏_{p odd} ℤ/2, and by Kronecker–Weber the kernel of (6) is the subgroup fixing ℚ(√−1, √2, √p : p odd prime), the compositum of all quadratic fields. So "(Γ_{ℚ ab})₂ — le quotient multiquadratique" is the Galois group of that field, identified with Ẑ*/Ẑ*² by (6). Confidence high.

E. **Scans 25–26, uniqueness of χ(u) — own correction of an unproved claim.** For every a ∈ Ẑ*, D(a) = diag(a, a⁻¹) lies in Sl(2,Ẑ) and D(a)U(n)D(a)⁻¹ = U(a⁻²n); so if χ(u) makes (12) commute up to conjugation, so does a²χ(u). In the forms "ou seulement de Sl(2,Ẑ)" and "seulement dans (12)" χ(u) is therefore unique only in Ẑ*/Ẑ*², not in Ẑ* as written. This is the precision the next composite uses (Γ → Ẑ* → Ẑ*/Ẑ*² ↪ Autext_{lac!}(Sl(2,Ẑ))), so the argument survives; uniqueness in (11) would need the normaliser of the cusp subgroup in Sl(2,ℤ)^ to be ± that subgroup, which these pages do not prove. Confidence high for the counterexample, medium for the remark on (11).

F. **Scans 27 and 29 — own note on notation (implication not pursued).** On scan 27 Sl(2,ℤ)^/{±1} is defined as "Sl(2,Ẑ)′" (hat over ℤ in the ink) and that group, the inner automorphism group of Sl(2,ℤ)^, is the kernel in (13); two lines later it is written Sl(2,ℤ)^′ with the inner hat struck. On scan 29 Sl(2,Ẑ)′ in (16)–(17) is Sl(2,Ẑ)/{±1}, a different group (they differ by the congruence kernel). The transcription keeps the ink; a reader must not identify the two uses. Confidence medium.

G. **Scan 30, (20) — own note on an inconsistency in the manuscript.** (20) is introduced as the composite of (19) with Ẑ*/Ẑ*² → (ℤ/2mℤ)*₂, but its target reads (ℤ/mℤ)*₂ with no 2 visible; likewise (15) writes Sl(2,ℤ/2m)′, (18) Sl(2,ℤ/2mℤ)′ and scan 28 Sl(2,2m). These look like slips or shorthand of the author and are kept as written. Confidence medium.

H. **Scans 28–30 — own note, implication not pursued.** By the condition of scan 30, Γℚ acts on the finite quotient Π̂₀,₃/Π(2m) through (ℤ/2mℤ)*₂, so the action is trivial on the subgroup fixing the largest multiquadratic subfield of ℚ(μ₂ₘ). For m = 1 this is the triviality on 𝔖₃ ≃ Sl(2,ℤ/2ℤ) used on scan 28; the text does not state the field-of-definition form. Confidence medium.

I. **Scans 23–24 — own note on two numerals.** In "u ∈ Gl(1,Ẑ)" (scan 23) and "sur Sl(1,Ẑ)" (scan 24) the numeral looks like a 1, which cannot be meant (Gl(1) is Ẑ* and Sl(1) is trivial; the argument needs 2). They are either slips of the pen or 2's written without their foot, and stay `\unsure{1|2}`. Confidence medium.


# ===== LM_P062 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P062 (cote 140-3, scans 31–40): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Text checked: `second_pass/LM_P062_checked.tex`. Scans 31–39 continue §39, "Les conditions de congruence pour l'opération extérieure de Γℚ sur Π̂₀,₃" (ms. pp. 303–307). It asks how much of N₁,₁ and of the homomorphism (21) N₁,₁ → Gl(2,Ẑ) can be rebuilt from a group Γ of outer automorphisms of π̂₀,₃. Scans 39–40 begin §40, "Paraphrase topologico-isotopique (en termes des groupes fondamentaux) de la Théorie de Legendre" (ms. p. 307 verso–308). Each first-pass note keeps its number and is marked **keep**, **correct** or **drop**, with the reason. Notes A–I are the second pass's own, labelled as such. Mathematical identities stated here were checked by hand; anything not proved is said to be so.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 31, ll. 1–10 — conjecture (question) — correct.** The "conditions" are not outside the manuscript. They are the congruence conditions defined on scans 29–30 (P061): the outer action of Γℚ on Π̂₀,₃/Π(∞) given by χ and Ẑ*/Ẑ*² is compatible with the action on Π̂₀,₃. The doubt is whether they restrict outer automorphisms of Π̂₀,₃ that commute with 𝔖₃ at all, since all known ones come from Γℚ. The inserted parenthesis widens this to "des élémts non triviaux de 𝒯̂̂^{!*}₀,₃" (one word illegible).

2. **Scan 31, ll. 11–13 — conjecture (expectation) — keep.** "Il faudrait quand même refaire à l'occasion un effort en ce sens" states an intention to look for non-arithmetic constructions. It claims nothing.

3. **Scan 31 l. 14 to scan 32 — conjecture (question) — correct.** The question is whether one can rebuild a) the finer extension N₁,₁ (an extension of N′₁,₁ by {±1}) and b) the homomorphism (21), or at least (22) N′₁,₁ ≃ M₀,₃ → Gl(2,Ẑ)/{±1}. The rebuilding is to use only the profinite groups with loops, the outer automorphisms of π̂₀,₃, or 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃ ≃ 𝒯̂⁺′₁,₁. That group is the cartographic group 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃, which the first pass wrote 𝒢̂⁺₀,₃. The author treats b) first, as announced.

4. **Scan 32, (23)–(24) — unproved claim — correct.** "qui passe au quotient" needs the congruence kernel C = Ker(Sl(2,ℤ)^ → Sl(2,Ẑ)) to be stable under conjugation by N₁,₁. If (21) exists this is automatic, since C is the kernel of (21) restricted to 𝒯̂⁺₁,₁ (P061 note 4). From the bare extension it is a condition, the first of the "conditions assez fortes" of scan 24 (P061 note 9). The page does not say which of the two it relies on.

5. **Scan 32, unnumbered display and (25) — unproved claim — correct (verified).** The kernel of Gl(2,R) → Aut(Sl(2,R)) is the group of scalars R* for every commutative ring R. A matrix that commutes with [[1,1],[0,1]] and [[1,0],[1,1]] is scalar. With R = Ẑ this gives the injection of (25) at all primes at once.

6. **Scan 32, (26) — unproved claim — keep.** "il se trouve que (24) se factorise" records a fact, presumably known from the geometric representation (21), not something deduced from the extension. This fact is what lets φ (27) be rebuilt from N₁,₁.

7. **Scan 33, (28)–(29) — unproved claim — correct (verified).** The kernel of (28) is {λ·1 : λ² = 1}. The 2-torsion of Ẑ* = ∏ ℤ_p* is ∏_p {±1}, also at p = 2 because ℤ₂* = {±1} × (1+4ℤ₂). So ₂Ẑ* ≃ (ℤ/2ℤ)^P, as stated.

8. **Scan 33, (28) and the arrow under GP(1,Ẑ) — unpursued implication — correct (verified).** The image of (28) is exactly the fibre product GP(1,Ẑ) ×_{Ẑ*/Ẑ*²} Ẑ*. If det u = λμ², then μ⁻¹u lifts ū and has determinant λ. The drawn arrow GP(1,Ẑ) → Ẑ*/Ẑ*² is det modulo squares, which is well defined on GP.

9. **Scan 33, (30)–(31) and margin — unproved claim (a condition) — correct.** "se factorise par l'image de (28)" means det φ(u) ≡ χ(u) mod Ẑ*² for u ∈ N₁,₁ (note 8). The margin calls this "encore une condition sur l'ext. N₁,₁ …, i.e. [one illegible word] de l'op. ext. de Γℚ sur 𝒯̂⁺₁,₁". For the representation (21) it holds because det = χ (the Weil pairing; this is not in the ink). For a bare extension it is a hypothesis.

10. **Scan 33 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Writing the compatibility as a condition on determinant square classes is right. Notes 8 and B carry it out.

11. **Scan 33, (29) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The right truncations are the finite sets S of primes. ν = (ℤ/2)^P/diag is the inverse limit of (ℤ/2)^S/diag, and the indeterminacy Hom(Γ, ν) of (48) is the limit of the Hom(Γ, (ℤ/2)^S/diag). Finite levels alone cannot select a canonical lift (note E).

12. **Scan 34, ll. 4–8 — conjecture (expectation) — correct.** "peut sûrement se construire directement à partir de l'extension N′₁,₁ …, tout comme tantôt ψ". The model is ψ (31), whose construction used the injectivity of (25); here the analogue is the injectivity of (33). The first pass left ψ or φ open; the ink has ψ. The inserted "≃ Sl(2,Ẑ)′" in the same sentence is false as an isomorphism (note C).

13. **Scan 34, (33) — unproved claim — correct (verified).** "est encore injectif" holds (note A).

14. **Scan 34, ll. 11–14, (34) — conjecture (question) — correct.** The ink reads "La question ici (en termes de th. des gr. profinis…) comment expliciter le relèvement de ψ′ de (31) en l'homom. canonique (34)", with no "est". "(31)" is the display that defines ψ′, which carries the number (31) in the ink (note D). "canonique" qualifies the homomorphism sought, so the question asks for a distinguished lift, not just for one.

15. **Scan 34, (34)–(35) — unproved claim — correct.** Pulling (35) back along a lift N′₁,₁ → Gl(2,Ẑ)′ gives an extension of N′₁,₁ by {±1} with a map to Gl(2,Ẑ). It is N₁,₁ with (21) only if the lift is the one that (21) induces. So "on en déduit l'ext. N₁,₁" holds for the right lift, and characterising that lift is the question of (34).

16. **Scan 34, (34)–(35) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The author does exactly this on scan 37: (46) is the pulled-back central extension, and the lifts are its splittings.

17. **Scan 35, opening — conjecture (question) — correct.** The ink reads "la question essentielle semble ici la détermination du relèvement (34) de (32)", not "semble être". "(32)" is the ψ′ display, which is numbered (31) on scan 34 (note D).

18. **Scan 35, (36)–(37) — unproved claim — correct.** (36) is the conjugation action of M₀,₃ on its normal subgroup 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃. The stated restriction to the inner action is therefore tautological. The injectivity of (37) is that of (33) (note A).

19. **Scan 35 to scan 36 — conjecture (question) — keep.** "si ce n'est déjà une conséquence de l'hyp. de commutation à 𝔖₃" is left open on the page.

20. **Scans 35–36, (38) — own idea (first pass, low) — keep.** Comparing the two conditions on finite quotients is a reasonable first test of their independence.

21. **Scan 36, (39) — unproved claim — correct.** The outer action of Ẑ*/Ẑ*² on Sl(2,Ẑ)′ is conjugation by diag(λ,1). It is faithful in Out(Sl(2,Ẑ)′) (note B). The condition is the profinite form of the compatibility stated on scans 29–30.

22. **Scan 36, between (39) and (40) — unproved claim — correct (verified, up to stability).** Under the condition, each u ∈ M₀,₃ acts on Sl(2,Ẑ)′ through an automorphism whose outer class lies in the image of Ẑ*/Ẑ*². The automorphisms with this property are exactly the image of GP(1,Ẑ). By the injectivity of (37), φ′ is then defined and unique ("bien déterminé"). That (36) descends to Sl(2,Ẑ)′ at all needs the kernel of (38) to be stable, which belongs to the condition.

23. **Scan 36, (41)–(42) — unproved claim — correct (verified).** The outer class of φ′(u) is det φ′(u) mod squares (note B). The condition says this is the class of χ(u), so (φ′(u), χ(u)) lies in the image of (28), which (43) identifies with Gl(2,Ẑ)/₂Ẑ*. Hence ψ′ (42), and "plus précisément" is justified.

24. **Scan 36, (39)–(42) — unpursued implication — keep.** Separating the projective information (φ′) from the determinant information (χ) is exactly what the page does.

25. **Scan 36, (41)–(42) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The calculation it proposes is done in notes 23 and B.

26. **Scan 37, (44)–(46) — unproved claim — correct (verified).** The kernel of Gl(2,Ẑ)/±1 → Gl(2,Ẑ)/₂Ẑ* is ₂Ẑ*/{±1} = (ℤ/2)^P/diag. It consists of scalars, so it is central in Gl(2,Ẑ)′ and the pull-back (46) is a central extension. The name of the middle group of (46) remains doubtful (M̃ or M̄).

27. **Scans 37–38 — conjecture (explicit question) — keep.** "est-il possible d'expliciter un tel relèvement canonique … en termes de la seule donnée de Γ ⊂ Autext_lac(Π̂₀,₃)". This is the main open question of the section.

28. **Scan 38, upper paragraph — unproved claim — correct.** The subgroup is written 𝒯̂⁺₀,₃ (the T-glyph), whereas scans 34–36 write the kernel of M₀,₃ → Γ as 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃. Both are ≃ Sl(2,ℤ)′^, and for (48) the subgroup must be that kernel. The known lift is the congruence map Sl(2,ℤ)′^ → Sl(2,Ẑ)′ followed by the injection into Gl(2,Ẑ)′.

29. **Scan 38, (48) — unproved claim — correct (verified).** The splittings of a central extension by ν form a torsor under Hom(M₀,₃, ν). Fixing them on 𝒯̂⁺₀,₃ leaves the kernel of restriction, which is Hom(M₀,₃/𝒯̂⁺₀,₃, ν) = Hom(Γ, ν) (continuous homomorphisms; Hom(−,ν) is left exact). This needs 𝒯̂⁺₀,₃ to be the kernel of M₀,₃ → Γ. Whether one splitting exists is a separate question.

30. **Scan 38, last lines — unpursued implication — correct.** Under the hypothesis on χ, Hom(Γ, ν) = Hom((Ẑ*)₂, ν), with (Ẑ*)₂ = Ẑ*/Ẑ*² ≅ (ℤ/2)² × ∏_{p odd} ℤ/2. Hence "c'est gros !" (note E). The subscript 2 is clear in the ink. The written hypothesis "Γ/Γ_ab →χ Ẑ*" stays doubtful (note I).

31. **Scan 39, first paragraph — conjecture (expectation) — keep.** "on devine" ties the missing ingredient to reading M₀,₃ as N′₁,₁, i.e. to bringing the profinite theory of elliptic curves into that of rational curves. The ink confirms "astucieux". Note F suggests one concrete form of this ingredient.

32. **Scan 39, §40 and (49) — unproved claim — correct.** The heading is "Paraphrase topologico-isotopique (en termes des groupes fondamentaux) de la Théorie de Legendre", not "Reformulation", and the group in (49) is 𝔖₀,₃. A connected groupoid is equivalent to the torsors under the automorphism group of one of its objects, so once the representative Π₀,₃ is fixed the equivalence is formal. The substance is (49), Aut_lac(Π₀,₃) ≃ GP(1,ℤ) (note G). The exponent after Gl(2,ℤ) in (49) stays doubtful; a hat would be wrong for discrete groups.

33. **Scans 39–40, (50)–(51) — unproved claim — correct.** That Out(F₂) → Gl(2,ℤ), through the abelianisation, is an isomorphism is Nielsen's theorem. Every automorphism of F₂ = ⟨x, y⟩ sends [x,y] to a conjugate of [x,y]^{±1}, so the loop condition is automatic and Autext_lac(Π₁,₁) = Out(F₂) ≅ Gl(2,ℤ). The page cites neither fact.

34. **Scan 40, (52)–(54) — unproved claim (construction) — correct.** The vertical arrows of (53) are equivalences, so the square determines L up to unique isomorphism once quasi-inverses are chosen ("à isom can. près"). Note H gives an explicit form. The numeral in "Gl(1|2,ℤ)/±1" of (54) stays doubtful in the ink; mathematically it must be 2.

35. **Scans 37–38, (46)–(48) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** A relative lifting problem with prescribed restriction to 𝒯̂⁺₀,₃ is the right frame (note 29). Finite levels can test it, but they cannot produce a canonical lift (note 11).

36. **Scans 39–40 — own idea (first pass, low) — keep.** Note H gives a concrete form of L to test it on.

## Second-pass notes (mine)

A. **Scans 32, 34, 35, (25), (33), (37) — unproved claims, checked (second pass).** The map GP(1,Ẑ) → Aut(Sl(2,Ẑ)′) is injective. If ū acts trivially, then u g u⁻¹ = ε(g) g with ε a continuous homomorphism Sl(2,Ẑ) → {±1}. Comparing traces gives ε = 1 on elementary matrices (trace 2, and 4 ≠ 0 in Ẑ); these topologically generate Sl(2,Ẑ) = ∏ Sl(2,ℤ_p), so u centralises Sl(2,Ẑ) and is scalar. This proves "encore injectif" (33) and (37).

B. **Scans 33–37 — unproved claims, checked (second pass).** The image of GP(1,Ẑ) in Out(Sl(2,Ẑ)′) is GP(1,Ẑ)/(image of Sl(2,Ẑ)) ≅ Ẑ*/Ẑ*² through det, because det(Ẑ*·Sl(2,Ẑ)) = Ẑ*². So the congruence condition (38)–(39) is equivalent to det φ′ ≡ χ mod Ẑ*². That is the factorisation (42) through (43); the same argument derives (31) from (30).

C. **Scans 34–35 — claim in the ink, false as written (second pass).** The insertion on scan 34 identifies 𝒯̂⁺′₁,₁ ≃ 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃ "≃ Sl(2,Ẑ)′" (s34_D). The profinite completion of Sl(2,ℤ)′ is not Sl(2,Ẑ)′: Sl(2,ℤ) has non-congruence subgroups of finite index, so the congruence kernel is non-trivial. On scan 35 the author strikes the same isomorphism ("Utilisant l'isom 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃ ≃ Sl(2,Ẑ)′") and uses the homomorphism (38) 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃ ≀ Sl(2,ℤ)′^ → Sl(2,Ẑ)′ instead.

D. **Scans 34–35 — numbering (second pass).** The display that defines ψ′ is numbered (31) in the ink, duplicating (31) on scan 33. "ψ′ de (31)" on scan 34 refers to it, and scan 35 cites it as "(32)". Renumbering it silently, as the first pass did, would hide which object "(31)" denotes in the question of scan 34.

E. **Scan 38, (48) — unpursued implication (second pass).** Twisting a given lift by a family (η_ℓ) of continuous quadratic characters of Γ, a different one on each ℓ-component of Ẑ, gives another lift. Families that differ by one common character give the same lift in Gl(2,Ẑ)′, so all such families modulo a common twist lie in the indeterminacy Hom(Γ, ν). For the Galois-theoretic N₁,₁ these lifts are Tate-module-like representations with a different quadratic twist at each ℓ. Only the untwisted one is geometric.

F. **Scans 38–39 — own idea (second pass), confidence medium.** Among the lifts of note E, the geometric lift (from the Tate module of the universal or the Legendre curve) is singled out by compatibility across ℓ, for instance Frobenius traces independent of ℓ. A purely group-theoretic substitute for that compatibility would be one form of the "élément un peu astucieux" of scan 39. The description of the twists is exact; whether such a substitute exists is open.

G. **Scan 39, (49) — unproved claim, partly checked (second pass), confidence medium.** Π₀,₃ can be realised as Γ̄(2) = Ker(GP(1,ℤ) → Gl(2,𝔽₂) × {±1}), the map being reduction mod 2 and det. This is a free group of rank 2 with three cusp classes (the loops), normal of index 12 in GP(1,ℤ). Conjugation gives an injection GP(1,ℤ) → Aut(Π₀,₃) that respects the loops; 12 = |𝔖₃ × ℤ/2| matches the outer part. That the image is all of Aut_lac(Π₀,₃) is the claim (49), not checked here beyond these counts.

H. **Scan 40, (52)–(53) — own idea (second pass), confidence medium.** An explicit form of L: L(Π) = Ker(Autext_lac(Π)/±1 → Gl(Π_ab ⊗ 𝔽₂) × {±1}), the map being reduction mod 2 and the determinant of the action on Π_ab. For Π = Π₁,₁ this is the Γ̄(2) ≅ Π₀,₃ of note G. It is functorial for isomorphisms of exterior groups and is the twist of Π₀,₃ by the pushed-out torsor, which should make (53) commute. This is the discrete form of Legendre's λ (level-2 structures). Still to check: the loop structure, i.e. that the three cusps correspond to the three non-zero classes of Π_ab/2.

I. **Scan 38 — unproved claim, checked (second pass).** Because ν has exponent 2, Hom(Γ, ν) depends only on Γ^{ab}/2. So the hypothesis needed after (48) is that χ induce Γ^{ab}/2 ≅ Ẑ*/Ẑ*², weaker than the isomorphism written "Γ/Γ_ab →χ Ẑ*" (doubtful notation in the ink). For Γ = Γℚ both hold by Kronecker–Weber.


# ===== LM_P063 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P063 (cote 140-3, scans 41–50): research notes, checked

The first pass's notes R01–R30 are checked against the corrected transcription `LM_P063_checked.tex`. Each keeps its number; the verdict and its reason come first, then the note (scan, kind, one to four sentences). Notes S01–S05 are new, written in the second pass (Claude) and labelled as such. "Without proof" means on these pages; the point may be settled elsewhere in the manuscript. No witness transcribes these pages (see `LM_P063_changes.md`), so the notes rest on the ink alone. All ten scans belong to §40, "Paraphrase topologico-isotopique de la théorie de Legendre" (table of contents, p. 307).

## First-pass notes

**R01. Scan 41. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: corrected. Reason: the horizontal connectors of (56) carry no heads in the ink (changes, row 6), and the groupoid is first written C^{cat′}_{1,1} (row 1).
The "équivalence de catégories" (55) C^{ext′}_{1,1} → C₀,₃ is obtained from the descriptions of both groupoids as torsor groupoids, through the isomorphism (57) Gl(2,ℤ)′ ≃ GP(1,ℤ); the square (56) records the compatibility. A complete account has to show that the description of C^{ext}_{1,1} by Gl(2,ℤ)-torsors (scan 40, (53)) passes to the quotient by the universal automorphism −id.

**R02. Scan 42. Kind: programme (authorial intention).** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged.
The author announces that he will make the Legendre equivalence (55) explicit "directement en termes des groupes fondamentaux", where it had been obtained from the Teichmüller groups of both sides and an "isomorphisme ad-hoc" between them. Scans 43–48 carry this out and end in (70).

**R03. Scan 42. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with a check added. Reason: the claim is sound once Autext_lac(π′) ≃ Gl(2,ℤ) is granted.
(−1)_{π′} is called the unique nontrivial element of the centre of Autext_lac(π′) for π′ of type (1,1). With Autext_lac(π′) ≃ Gl(2,ℤ) (scan 40), the centre of Gl(2,ℤ) is {±1}, and det(−1) = +1 agrees with the margin ("conserve l'orientation i.e. est de multiplicateur +1"). The symbol at the head of the margin note is still doubtful.

**R04. Scan 43. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with the standard argument added. Reason: the claim follows from the triviality of the centre.
π′ is free of rank 2, so its centre is trivial; an outer action G → Out(π′) then determines an extension of G by π′ up to unique isomorphism (pull back Aut(π′) → Out(π′) along G). This is what makes (58) "canonique".

**R05. Scan 43. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged; S02 gives a proof sketch in the discrete case.
"Exactement trois classes de π′-conjugaison de scindages" of (58) is justified only by the topological model, through the three fixed points of x ↦ −x on E∖{0}.

**R06. Scan 43. Kind: conjecture (authorial qualification).** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged ("relatif" is now settled, "écrit" without agreement as written).
The theory of "forages de trous simultanés" relative to a finite group of exterior operators on a group with loops exists, even in the discrete case, only as "énoncés conjecturaux"; the construction of scan 44 rests on it. The margin says that in the present case one can manage with drillings of one hole at a time, done successively.

**R07. Scan 43, margin. Kind: unpursued implication.** Verdict: corrected. Reason: the margin reading is weaker than the first pass's note assumed.
Legible are "mais ici, on est dans un cas", "peut s'en tirer", "des forages", "d'un trou à", "la fois,", "successivement", and a word like "expliqués" or "explicités"; "avec" and "répétés" are not visible. The remark replaces the conjectural simultaneous drilling by successive single-hole drillings; whether the result is independent of the order of the drillings is not discussed.

**R08. Scan 43. Kind: model's own idea (first pass).** Verdict: kept, sharpened by S02. Reason: a cocycle calculation is unnecessary in the discrete case, where the count follows from the structure of the extension.
After choosing one splitting of (58), compute the other conjugacy classes of splittings by a nonabelian cocycle calculation, recording where the choice enters.

**R09. Scan 44. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged.
"Construire canoniquement" the exterior group π̃′ of type (1,4) with its G-action, the part J = I∖{i₀} and the isomorphism (59) π̃′(J) ≃ π′ compatible with G is asserted on the strength of the conjectural theory of R06. A proof would have to say at which level (effective group, exterior group, groupoid) the construction is canonical.

**R10. Scan 44, margin. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with a check added. Reason: the topological model confirms it.
"En fait, G opère trivialement sur I = I(π̃′)": in the model, x ↦ −x fixes each of the four points of order 2 of E, which are the punctures of E∖₂E, so G fixes every element of I. This is what makes (62) I(ℰ̃) ≃ I(π̃′)/G ≃ I(π̃′) possible.

**R11. Scan 44. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: corrected (reading). Reason: π̃′ is called "le groupe fondamental cat." of E∖₂E (changes, row 14), where the first pass left "cat./ext." open.
The extension ℰ̃ of G by π̃′ given by the outer action is identified with the "groupe fondamental mixte" of (E∖₂E, G). Since −1 fixes exactly the points of order 2, G acts freely on E∖₂E, as stated, and the mixed fundamental group is the fundamental group of the quotient (61). What needs proof is that the topological extension and the one defined by the outer action agree, and in which sense this is canonical.

**R12. Scan 45. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged.
The loop structure of ℰ̃ (type (0,4)) is called "facile à décrire" in terms of that of π̃′, and (62) is called canonical. The loop subgroups of ℰ̃ have to be constructed from those of π̃′ through the quotient; (62) rests on R10.

**R13. Scan 45. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: corrected. Reason: the effective/exterior contrast is stronger than the first pass recorded (changes, row 18).
Filling the hole at i₀ gives "un groupe effectif à lacets de type (0,3)", π = L(π′) (63), with "effectif" inserted. A proof must say which conjugacy class is killed, how the remaining loop structure is recovered, and why the result is an effective group, whereas π′ was given as an exterior group.

**R14. Scans 45–46. Kind: unpursued implication.** Verdict: corrected. Reason: the first pass read "effectif" as struck in the reverse construction; it is underlined.
The inverse construction goes from "un groupe à lacets effectif de type (0,3)" (underlined) to "un groupe à lacets extérieur π̃ de type (0,4)" (underlined). The underlinings mark that the two sides of Legendre's correspondence live in different groupoids, effective groups of type (0,3) and exterior groups; at which categorical level the two constructions are inverse remains open (see S03).

**R15. Scan 46. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged.
The drilling functor adds a distinguished puncture i₀ and gives (64) I(π̃) ≃ {i₀} ⨿ I(π), with I(π) of cardinal 3. Its independence of choices and its action on morphisms are not discussed.

**R16. Scan 46. Kind: model's own idea (first pass); now an inaccuracy in the text.** Verdict: corrected and sharpened. Reason: the check can be done here.
A group with loops of type (0,4) is free of rank 3: the product of its four loops is 1, so π̃_ab is T^{I(π̃)} modulo the diagonal T, not T^{I(π̃)}, and (π̃_ab)₂ is (ℤ/2ℤ)^{I(π̃)} modulo the diagonal; (65) and the first arrow of (67) are inaccurate as written. The coefficient-sum map of (67) still descends to the quotient, since the diagonal has coefficient sum 4 ≡ 0 mod 2, so (66) is well defined and the conclusion is unaffected. The transcription keeps (65) as written.

**R17. Scan 46. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with a check added. Reason: the type (1,4) follows from Riemann–Hurwitz.
The homomorphism (66) is "ramifié" at every i ∈ I(π̃): every loop goes to 1 ∈ ℤ/2ℤ, which respects the relation since 4·1 = 0. The corresponding double cover of the four-holed sphere becomes, after filling, a double cover of P¹ branched at four points, of genus 1 (2g − 2 = 2·(−2) + 4), with one point over each puncture; so the kernel π̃′ is of type (1,4), as stated.

**R18. Scans 46–47. Kind: unpursued implication.** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged.
The kernel π̃′ "n'est pas tout à fait un groupe extérieur": it is defined not modulo inner automorphisms only, but also modulo the exterior symmetry of ℤ/2ℤ. The exact sequence (68) and (69) are where this extra ambiguity can be followed on representatives and on morphisms.

**R19. Scans 47–48. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with a reading caveat. Reason: the ink after "modulo symétrie" shows no clear prime (changes, row 20), though the sense requires C^{ext′}_{1,1}.
The result of filling the holes is an object of C^{ext′}_{1,1} ("modulo symétrie"); to get an object of C^{ext}_{1,1} one must choose a kernel π̃′ as an exterior group, i.e. an object in a (ℤ/2ℤ)-gerbe, a connected groupoid with fundamental group ℤ/2ℤ. This gerbe of choices is not constructed on these pages.

**R20. Scan 48. Kind: conjecture (authorial expectation).** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged except for the convention 𝔖.
"Il me semble que ces réflexions conceptuelles prouvent bel et bien et sans bavure(s)" the canonical isomorphism (70) 𝒯₁,₁/{±1} ≃ 𝔖₀,₃, "au moins mod autom intérieurs", pending "une rédaction plus soignée". The margin adds that the ambiguity can be specified as inner automorphisms coming from 𝔖^{?+}₀,₃ ≃ Π₀,₃, the superscript still doubtful.

**R21. Scan 48. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with a reading caveat. Reason: in "identifier purement et simplement 𝒯₁,₁ à Gl(·,ℤ)" the numeral looks like 1, where the sense requires 2 (changes, row 21).
The author holds (70) to be independent of the fact that the natural homomorphism 𝒯₁,₁ → Gl(2,ℤ) is an isomorphism. Checking this means auditing the construction of scans 43–48 for hidden uses of that identification.

**R22. Scans 48–49. Kind: unpursued implication.** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged.
Identifying 𝔖₀,₃ with 𝒯₁,₁/±1 or with Gl(2,ℤ)′ is "plus sujet à caution", except as exterior groups, because of the inner-automorphism ambiguity in (70). The information lost when a homomorphism is replaced by its class modulo inner automorphisms matters as soon as such identifications are composed.

**R23. Scan 49, margin. Kind: conjecture (authorial proposal).** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged except for the convention 𝔖.
"Avec un peu plus de soin, on pourrait inclure dans les réflexions aussi la conjugaison complexe", so as not to be restricted in (73) to the subgroups 𝒯⁺ and 𝔖⁺. This would extend the analytic construction and the group actions by the complex involution.

**R24. Scan 49. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept. Reason: the readings of (71) and (72) are unchanged and remain partly doubtful.
A canonical morphism (71) of analytic modular multiplicities induces the isomorphism (72) 𝕄^{an′}₁,₁ ≃ M^{an}₀,₃. The first target of (71) is a doubled 𝕌 written over 𝕄, its index 3 retraced, under the brace "courbe analytique complexe de type (0,3) universelle"; the target of (72) has a plain M; neither is to be reconciled silently with the other.

**R25. Scan 49. Kind: question (author).** Verdict: kept. Reason: the reading is unchanged; the abbreviation before (73) is now read "cat.", with "ext." as alternative (changes, row 23).
"Est-il possible de le préciser de façon utile, en un hom effectif, non seulement extérieur?" asks for an actual homomorphism in place of the exterior one (73). The answer begun on scan 50 uses the canonical universal covering by the upper half-plane.

**R26. Scan 50. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with a check added. Reason: the statement is classical.
(74) π₁(M^{an}₁,₁) ≃ Sl(2,ℤ), "pas seulement extérieur", relative to the universal covering by the half-plane D: the orbifold fundamental group of the analytic moduli stack of elliptic curves, uniformised by D, is Sl(2,ℤ) (−1 acts trivially on D but is in every stabiliser). This agrees with the restriction to the index-2 subgroup 𝒯⁺₁,₁ = Sl(2,ℤ) of 𝒯₁,₁ = Gl(2,ℤ); the comparison with (50) lies outside this package.

**R27. Scan 50. Kind: claim without proof.** Verdict: kept, with a check added. Reason: the statements are classical.
Γ(2)/±1 is free of rank 2 and acts freely on D with quotient P¹∖{0,1,∞} (the Legendre modulus λ), so D is a universal covering of U₀,₃; Sl(2,ℤ)′ = Sl(2,ℤ)/±1 acts compatibly through Sl(2,ℤ/2ℤ) ≃ 𝔖₃, which permutes λ by the anharmonic group, and is the mixed π₁ of (U₀,₃, 𝔖₃). Which isomorphism Sl(2,ℤ/2ℤ) ≃ 𝔖₃ "convient" (the reference is left blank) depends on how the three nonzero points of (ℤ/2ℤ)² are matched with 0, 1, ∞.

**R28. Scan 50, brace under (76). Kind: deferred reference.** Verdict: kept, with a reading caveat. Reason: the first words are "cet iso." or "l'iso." (changes, row 25).
The isomorphism Sl(2,ℤ/2ℤ) ≃ 𝔖₃ that "convient ici est explicité dans § , p." with both numbers left blank, as is the § of the margin beside (75). The references are to be found in the rest of the manuscript, not guessed.

**R29. Scan 50. Kind: unpursued implication.** Verdict: kept, with a check added. Reason: the base point is a special point.
"Pour pouvoir l'identifier à π₁((U₀,₃, 𝔖₃); P = −j̄)", the sentence breaks off at "il". The points −j and −j̄ (that is, e^{∓iπ/3}) are the two points of P¹∖{0,1,∞} fixed by the 3-cycles of the anharmonic group (the roots of λ² − λ + 1), so the chosen base point has a stabiliser of order 3 in 𝔖₃; the next leaf should say how this enters the identification.

**R30. Scans 47–50. Kind: model's own idea (first pass).** Verdict: kept. Reason: it is still a fair test.
Formulate the groupoid of kernel choices (the gerbe of scans 47–48) and the groupoid of choices giving an effective version of (73), and test whether the construction gives a functor between them or leaves an obstruction.

## New notes (second pass, Claude)

**S01. Scans 41, 44, 49. Kind: question (notation).** (Claude.) The author writes "cat" where the first pass read or allowed "ext": the superscript of the groupoid C^{cat′}_{1,1} on scan 41 (called C^{ext′}_{1,1} in (55), (56) and on scan 47), "le groupe fondamental cat." of E∖₂E on scan 44, and probably "l'hom cat." before (73) on scan 49. These pages do not say what "cat." abbreviates; before identifying it with "ext." one should look for its definition elsewhere in the manuscript, since the author also writes "(ext.)" on scan 46.

**S02. Scan 43. Kind: own idea (proof sketch).** (Claude.) In the topological model the extension (58) is the orbifold fundamental group of (E∖{0})/±1, a sphere with one puncture and three cone points of order 2, i.e. the free product ℤ/2 ∗ ℤ/2 ∗ ℤ/2. Splittings are involutions mapping onto the generator of ℤ/2ℤ; by the Kurosh subgroup theorem every finite subgroup of a free product is conjugate into a factor, and the three factors are pairwise non-conjugate (they differ in the abelianisation), so there are exactly three classes. ℰ-conjugacy and π′-conjugacy of a splitting agree, since conjugating by the splitting involution fixes it. The profinite version would need the analogue for free profinite products (torsion is conjugate into a factor, Herfort–Ribes); to check.

**S03. Scans 41, 45–46. Kind: claim check.** (Claude.) The corrected "effectif" fits (55). An object of C^{ext′}_{1,1} has automorphism group Gl(2,ℤ)/±1 = PGL(2,ℤ), an extension of a group of order 12 by the free group Γ(2)/±1 of rank 2. An effective group with loops of type (0,3) has as automorphism group an extension of Out_lac (𝔖₃ × ℤ/2, order 12) by its inner automorphisms, again free of rank 2, while an exterior one has a finite automorphism group of order 12. So the target C₀,₃ of (55) is naturally the groupoid of effective groups of type (0,3), as π = L(π′) on scan 45 is "effectif"; this is a consistency check, not a proof, to compare with the definition of C₀,₃ on scan 40.

**S04. Scans 44–45. Kind: claim check.** (Claude.) The topological statements of scans 44–45 check out: x ↦ −x fixes exactly the four points of order 2 of E, so G acts freely on E∖₂E (Euler characteristic −4), and the quotient V of (61) has Euler characteristic −2 with four ends, a sphere with four holes, i.e. of type (0,4) as stated.

**S05. Scan 46. Kind: unpursued implication.** (Claude.) The kernel π̃′ of (66) is the fundamental group of the double cover of V branched at all four punctures, which is E∖₂E again: Legendre's correspondence is here the classical one between an elliptic curve with its four points of order 2 and P¹ with four marked points, through E → E/±1. The text uses this only through group theory; making the identification of π̃′ with the π̃′ of scan 44 explicit would show that the two constructions (forward on scans 43–45, reverse on scans 45–47) are inverse up to the gerbe of R19.


# ===== LM_P064 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P064 (scans 51–60): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note is given with its verdict (keep, correct or drop) and the reason, then its text as it now stands. Notes added in this pass are labelled "Second pass". Readings follow `LM_P064_checked.tex`: the parameter of §41 is γ (the first pass's σ), (9) reads ℓ₁^p·int(h₀)(ℓ₀^p)·int(ℓ₁^γρ(h₀)^{-1})(ℓ_∞^p) = 1, (11) has u(ℓ_∞) = int(g₀ℓ₀^{-γ}ρ^{-1}(h₀))(ℓ_∞^p), (14) has h = (ρ^{-1}σ_∞)(g₀)ℓ₀^γg₀^{-1}, (10 bis) has ℓ₀.

## First-pass notes

### Scans 51–53

**1. Scan 51 — unproved claim — keep.** The kernel of Sl(2,Z)′ → Sl(2,Z/2Z) ≃ 𝔖₃, named Π(2) on scan 52, is said to be « lui-m̄ isomorphe à Π₀,₃ ». This is the freeness of rank 2 of the level-2 congruence subgroup, matching U₀,₃^an ≃ D/Π(2) on scan 52; the page gives no argument. The first digit of the first Sl stays doubtful in the ink.

**2. Scans 51–52 — unproved claim — correct (now checked).** With the lower-left entries read as 1 and 1 (changes rows 7–8), the three matrices send j to j+1, j/(j+1) and −1/j, each equal to −j̄, and none is ≡ 1 mod 2. The list is exhaustive: the elements of PSL(2,Z) taking j to −j̄ form one coset of the stabilizer of j, which has order 3. So j and −j̄ are not congruent mod Π(2), as asserted.

**3. Scan 52 — unproved claim — correct (quotation).** Choosing the point above P to be a root of unity is said to "normaliser" the homomorphism (73) into an effective one, and with it (70). The ink calls (73) "l'hom cat." (the first pass read "ext."; the abbreviation is not explained here). The page does not say which ambiguity by inner automorphisms remains.

**4. Scan 52 — conjecture / research intention — keep.** « On aurait envie de reprendre les constructions précédentes dans le contexte des groupes profinis » is held back by the missing theory of "trouage" or "forage de trous" for profinite groups à lacets, "même de façon conjecturale". "forage de trous" is now read without doubt; "trouage" stays doubtful.

**5. Scan 53 — conjecture / expectation — keep.** A "gruppentheoretisch" understanding of (40), M̂₀,₃ → Gl(2,Ẑ)/±1 ≃ GP(1,Ẑ), is named the main motivation of this § and the preceding ones, and an incitement to rethink "trouage" profinitely.

**6.
