Scans 53–54 — conjecture / question — keep.** Whether the constructions extend to the profinite context without arithmetic data (a subgroup Γ ⊂ Autext_loc(Π̂₀,₃) with congruence relations) is made subordinate to whether those congruence relations are arithmetic, specific to automorphisms coming from Γ_Q, or formal consequences of a purely "algebraic" formalism.

**7. Scans 51–52 — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The proposed table of the three actions and their reductions mod 2 is carried out in note 2; the check is complete, exhaustiveness included.

**8. Scans 52–53 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Formulating the missing profinite construction first on compatible finite quotients, then asking for stability under the arithmetic subgroup, separates two questions; confidence low, as stated.

### Scans 54–56

**9. Scan 54 — conjecture (open alternative) — keep.** The congruence relations are either "de nature arithmétique", specific to outer automorphisms coming from Γ_Q, or ± automatic consequences of a formalism "purement « algébrique »" of profinite groups modelled on the discrete case; the page leaves this open.

**10. Scan 54 — conjecture (programme) — keep.** « à titre expérimental essayer d'en dégager un »: a candidate formalism is to be extracted without prejudging its plausibility.

**11. Scan 54 — question — correct.** §41 opens by asking for further necessary conditions, beyond the congruence conditions of §38|39, "pour qu'un autom. cat. de Π̂₀,₃ provienne de Γ_Q"; the ink has "cat.", not the first pass's "ext.", so which class of automorphisms is meant depends on that abbreviation (see second-pass note C). It is a question about necessary conditions, not a characterization.

**12. Scan 54 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Testing a candidate formalism on finite quotients can refute an implication but not prove it; confidence medium, as stated.

**13. Scan 55 — unproved claim — keep.** Passage to completions is said to give the exterior hom (3) with an epimorphism onto 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃; (3) also records 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃ ⊂ 𝒯̂̂₀,₃ = Aut[…]_loc(Π̂₀,₃), with a struck word between Aut and loc. None of this is argued on the page.

**14. Scans 55–56 — unproved claim — keep.** A subgroup Γ commuting with 𝔖₃ is said to act "automatiquement" on the exterior group 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃ because it fits into the extension (4) containing (5).

**15. Scan 55 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Tracking the closed normal subgroup generated by ℓ₁², ℓ_∞³ under the automorphisms distinguishes preserving the relations up to conjugacy from the quotient compatibility used in (6); confidence medium.

**16. Scan 56 — conjecture — keep.** Commutation of (3) with the action of Γ_Q = Γ "me semble a priori une condition non triviale", elementwise on Γ.

**17. Scan 56 — implication — correct.** The first pass filed the request to make the condition explicit "en termes des calculs ± explicites de [C]" as not pursued. It is pursued at once: scans 58–60 recall the parametrization (7)–(11) of the automorphisms u and compute their action (12)–(18) on 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃.

**18. Scans 56–57 — unproved claim — keep.** "Cette condition équivaut" to (3) extending to the homomorphism of extensions (6); neither direction is proved on the page.

**19. Scan 56 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Computing the defect of lifting the kernel map to a map of extensions, and whether it can be removed by changing representatives; confidence medium.

### Scans 57–58

**20. Scan 57 — unproved claim — keep, sharpened.** M₀,₃, read as the fundamental group "à signature" of (P¹_ℚ − {0}, 2{1} + 3{∞}), is said to be the quotient of Π̂₀,₃ (so the ink) by the closed invariant subgroup generated by ℓ₁², ℓ_∞³. Since M₀,₃ is an extension of Γ by 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃, the quotient meant is that of ℰ₀,₃ (the middle term of (6)); "Π̂₀,₃" reads as a slip, and the margin identifies ℰ₀,₃ with M^!₀,₃ of §46.

**21. Scan 57 — conjecture — keep.** « Je commence à soupçonner qu'il y aura toute une kyrielle de conditions de même nature sur Γ », to be extracted "de façon plus générale".

**22. Scan 57 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Replacing ℓ₁², ℓ_∞³ by ℓ₁^m, ℓ_∞^n and asking which outer actions preserve the kernel gives a family of descent conditions on which to test the expected "kyrielle"; confidence medium.

**23. Scan 58 — unproved claim — correct.** The outer automorphisms of Π̂₀,₃ commuting with 𝔖₃ are recalled from [C] to lift uniquely to automorphisms u commuting with σ_∞ itself (the punctuation is "σ_∞;", the first pass's alternative σ′_∞ is gone) and exteriorly with ρ. Existence and uniqueness of this normalization are not proved here.

**24. Scan 58 — unproved claim — keep.** "biunivoquement" asserts a bijection with the pairs (g₀ mod L₀, p ∈ Ẑ*) subject to (9), (10); scan 59 adds the generation condition (11 bis), so (9)–(10) alone are not claimed sufficient.

**25. Scan 58 — unproved claim — correct.** The parameter, now read γ (not σ), is said to be determined uniquely by u, or by the outer automorphism; the margin then claims γ = (p−1)/2 (note 27).

**26. Scan 58, margin — unproved claim — keep, with a check.** "Admettant que Π̂₀,₃^{σ_∞} = {1}", g₀ is known once h₀ is. The implication is immediate: σ_∞(g)^{-1}g = σ_∞(g′)^{-1}g′ iff g′g^{-1} is fixed by σ_∞. The content is the fixed-point statement, which the note assumes.

**27. Scan 58, margin — unproved claim — correct.** The reading is now firmer: "en fait, on verra que (9) est superflu(e), et γ = (p−1)/2". Both are announced, not proved; see second-pass note B for what (9) amounts to.

**28. Scan 58 — own idea (first pass) — correct (settled).** The division by 2 is harmless: p ∈ Ẑ* has a unit 2-adic component, so p − 1 ∈ 2Ẑ, and 2 is not a zero-divisor in Ẑ; γ = (p−1)/2 is a well-defined unique element of Ẑ.

**29. Scan 58 — unproved claim — correct (checked).** The subscripts of (10 bis) read ℓ₀. With a = ℓ₀^γh₀^{-1}, ρ(ℓ₀) = ℓ₁, ρ(ℓ₁) = ℓ_∞, ρ³ = 1, one gets aρ(a)ρ²(a) = ℓ₀^γh₀^{-1}·(ℓ₁^γρ(h₀)^{-1}ℓ_∞^γ)·ρ^{-1}(h₀)^{-1}, and (10) turns the bracket into h₀ℓ₀^{-γ}ρ^{-1}(h₀), so the product is 1. So "(10) s'écrit aussi (10 bis)" holds for that action of ρ, the one also used in the margin of (14).

**30. Scan 58 — own idea (first pass) — correct.** (10 bis) is exactly the norm condition aρ(a)ρ²(a) = 1 that makes ρ^k ↦ aρ(a)⋯ρ^{k−1}(a) a 1-cocycle of the cyclic group ⟨ρ⟩ of order 3. The author takes this up himself: his table of contents lists "Calculs dans SL(2,Ẑ): cocycles sous ρ / sous σ∞" (§42 VII–VIII) and "Digression sur les 1-cocycles des groupes cycliques" (§43) (Circle ll. 147–154).

### Scans 59–60

**31. Scan 59 (11 bis) — implication not pursued — keep.** "en toute rigueur" the two elements u(ℓ₀), u(ℓ₁) given by (11) must also generate Π̂₀,₃; whether this follows from (9), (10) is not discussed.

**33. Scan 59 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Testing generation by u(ℓ₀), u(ℓ₁) in finite quotients can detect failure, not prove generation; confidence medium.

**34. Scan 60 (16) — unproved claim — correct (checked).** (16) does follow from (15) and (10), given ρ³ = 1 and ρ: ℓ₀ → ℓ₁ → ℓ_∞ → ℓ₀. With g = σ_∞(g₀)ℓ₁^γρ(g₀)^{-1} and h₀^{-1} = g₀^{-1}σ_∞(g₀), gρ(g)ρ²(g) = σ_∞(g₀)·(ℓ₁^γρ(h₀)^{-1}ℓ_∞^γ)·ρ²(h₀)^{-1}ℓ₀^γg₀^{-1}; (10) replaces the bracket by h₀ℓ₀^{-γ}ρ^{-1}(h₀), leaving σ_∞(g₀)h₀g₀^{-1} = 1. The check needs the exponent in (15) to be the γ of (10).

**35. Scan 60 (17) — conjecture — keep.** The replacement "devrait avoir" and the marginal "?" mark (17), u(ℓ′₀) = int(g₀)(ℓ′₀^p), as expected rather than established; its square is the first relation of (11).

**36. Scan 60 — unproved claim — keep.** (17) follows "si on admet que le centralisateur de L₀ = ℓ₀^Ẑ dans Π̂₀,₃ est réduit à L₀"; neither the centralizer statement nor the deduction is given.

**37. Scan 60 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Compare the two square roots and place their ratio in the centralizer; equality of squares alone does not suffice; confidence medium.

**38. Scan 60 (18) — conjecture / open question — keep.** "On devrait avoir aussi" u(ρ) conjugate to ρ^p (the congruence "mod Π̂₀,₃" is struck); it is "pas clair" whether this follows from the conditions on u so far, and the author promises to return to it.

**39. Scan 60 — implication not pursued — keep.** (16) guarantees u(ρ)³ = 1; (18) asks more, the conjugacy class of u(ρ). See second-pass note D for the exact gap.

**40. Scan 60 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Separating the classes of u(ρ), ρ and ρ² in finite quotients can exhibit an obstruction; confidence medium.

## Second-pass notes

**A. Scan 58 (9) — second pass, own check.** With (10) and (11) as corrected, (9) as now read is exactly the image under u of the relation ℓ₁ℓ₀ℓ_∞ = 1, conjugated by σ_∞(g₀)^{-1}: u(ℓ₁) becomes ℓ₁^p, u(ℓ₀) becomes int(h₀)(ℓ₀^p), and u(ℓ_∞) becomes int(h₀ℓ₀^{-γ}ρ^{-1}(h₀))(ℓ_∞^p) = int(ℓ₁^γρ(h₀)^{-1})(ℓ_∞^p) by (10). So (9) says that (11) respects the defining relation. The first-pass reading fails already in the abelianization; the cyclic order of the relation (ℓ₁ℓ₀ℓ_∞ = 1 rather than ℓ₀ℓ₁ℓ_∞ = 1) should be checked against [C].

**B. Scan 58, margin — second pass, own idea; confidence medium.** Abelianizing (10) gives (1 + ρ + ρ²)[h₀] = γ([ℓ₀] + [ℓ₁] + [ℓ_∞]) = 0, an identity, since 1 + ρ + ρ² vanishes on H₁ = Ẑ[ℓ₀] ⊕ Ẑ[ℓ₁]; the abelianization of (9) is empty too. So the announced γ = (p−1)/2 and "(9) superflu" can only come from higher commutators: the next test is to write (9) and (10) in Π̂₀,₃ modulo its third lower central term, where commutators of the ℓ's carry γ and p.

**C. Scans 52, 54 — second pass, question.** The ink has "cat." where the first pass read "ext." twice: "l'hom cat. (73)" and "un autom. cat. de Π̂₀,₃". The preceding pages (P063) use "cat" as a superscript on C₁,₁ beside "ext". Which class of homomorphisms or automorphisms "cat." denotes is not defined on these pages; it decides whether the opening question of §41 concerns outer automorphisms of Π̂₀,₃ or a narrower class.

**D. Scan 60 (18) — second pass, own idea; confidence medium.** By (15), u(ρ) = gρ, and (16) is the cocycle condition for g under ⟨ρ⟩ (note 30). For p ≡ 1 mod 3, u(ρ) is conjugate to ρ^p = ρ exactly when g = cρ(c)^{-1} for some c, i.e. when the class of g in the nonabelian H¹(⟨ρ⟩, 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃) is trivial; for p ≡ 2 mod 3 the same holds after twisting by ρ. So (18) is a vanishing statement in H¹, which explains why it does not follow from (16) alone and connects it to the cocycle calculations of §§42–43.

**E. Scan 55 (3) — second pass, question.** The inclusion 𝔖̂⁺₀,₃ ⊂ 𝒯̂̂₀,₃ makes sense for automorphisms rather than outer automorphisms: 𝔖⁺₀,₃ ≅ PSL(2,Z) acts by conjugation on its normal subgroup Π(2) ≅ Π₀,₃, with Π(2) acting by inner automorphisms. That fits "Aut_loc" with "ext" struck, but it conflicts with 𝒯̂̂ standing elsewhere for the outer group Autext_lac (IUT l. 7276: "Autextlac(π) = T̂̂(π)"). The struck word, and so which group 𝒯̂̂₀,₃ denotes here, is unresolved.

**F. Scan 57, margin — second pass, implication.** The margin "ℰ₀,₃ est le groupe noté M^!₀,₃ dans le §46" is a later cross-reference: §46 is "Notations définitives (?)" in the author's table of contents, and "!" is his notation for marked-point variants (M^!₀,₄, CSG ll. 10198–10208). It supports the reading of note 20, that the group divided by ⟨ℓ₁², ℓ_∞³⟩ to give M₀,₃ is ℰ₀,₃ = M^!₀,₃.


# ===== LM_P065 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P065 (scans 61–70, §41): research notes checked against the corrected text

Text checked: `LM_P065_checked.tex`. Notation as corrected there: the hatted group is 𝔖̂+0,3 (the first pass's "C|G"), the
quotient 𝔖3, the extension groups ℰ, ℰ!. Formulas (7)–(17) are on scans 58–60 (LM_P064_checked.tex). Verdicts: keep, correct,
drop. Part B holds the second pass's own notes, labelled as such.

## A. The first pass's notes

1. **Scan 61, (21)→(22); unproved claim. Keep.** The "donc" step from int(g0^{-1}λ^{-1}φ̂(g0))(ℓ'0^p) = ℓ'0^p to
   g0^{-1}λ^{-1}φ̂(g0) = ℓ'0^β uses that the centralizer of ε0^p (p ∈ Ẑ*) in 𝔖̂+0,3 is the procyclic group of ε0 = ℓ'0. It is
   admitted here, as the centralizer of L0 in Π̂0,3 is admitted in the bracket of scan 60.
2. **Scan 61, (20)–(22); unpursued implication. Correct.** The dependence of β on the representative g0 (defined mod L0) is not
   left unpursued: the left-margin NB of scan 62 takes it up (β' = β − 3λ). See 4 and B1.
3. **Scan 61, insertion after (22); claim resting on (24 bis). Keep, made precise.** g0h0^{-1} = σ∞(g0) follows from
   h0 = σ∞(g0)^{-1}g0 (scan 58). The equality λ = φ̂(g0h0^{-1}) follows from (24 bis) only when the exponent of σ∞ there is 0;
   with φ̂(h0) = σ∞^ε g0 ε0^β one gets φ̂(g0h0^{-1}) = λσ∞^ε.
4. **Scan 62, left-margin NB; unproved claim. Correct.** The note claims β' = β − 3λ for g'0 = g0ℓ0^λ and concludes a unique
   normalization β ∈ {0,1,2}; Grothendieck marks it himself ("Correct?" with an arrow, "?" after "Ceci montre"). Substituting
   g'0 in the definition (22) of β, with ℓ0 = ε0² (25) and φ̂(ℓ0) = ε0 (18), gives β' = β − λ (B1); the 3λ, the normalization
   to {0,1,2} and the mod-3 question do not follow from the formulas as written.
5. **Scan 62, end of the NB ("Il faudrait élucider si β prend effectivement toutes les trois valeurs mod 3"); question. Keep as
   his question, correct its scope.** With β' = β − λ every u admits a representative g0 with β = 0, so the question as posed
   is empty; the invariant that remains is the subject of B2.
6. **Scan 62, right margin, (24 bis) φ̂(h0) = σ∞^ε g0 ε0^β; unproved claim. Keep, made precise.** By the overbrace,
   (24) says (g0ε0^β)~ = (φ̂(h0))~, and μ̃ = ν̃ holds iff νμ^{-1} commutes with σ∞; (24 bis) is then the statement that the
   centralizer of σ∞ in 𝔖̂+0,3 is {1, σ∞}, which is not proved here. The exponent ε (read as ε, tiny) is implicitly 0 or 1.
7. **Scan 62, (26)–(27); first pass's idea (μ ↦ μ̃ as a non-abelian coboundary). Keep.** μ̃ = σ∞(μ)^{-1}μ = [σ∞, μ] is the
   coboundary for the action of Z/2 by int(σ∞), and (27) is a coboundary equation. The author pursued exactly this: his table
   of contents lists "VIII. Calculs dans SL(2, Ẑ) : cocycles sous σ∞" (§42) and §43 "Digression sur les 1-cocycles des
   groupes cycliques".
8. **Scan 63, Proposition, γ "uniquement déterminé"; unproved claim. Correct.** The ink has γ ∈ Ẑ* here, but (7) on scan 58
   has γ ∈ Ẑ and the margin there says γ = (p−1)/2, which is not a unit in general; the star is probably a slip. The data
   (g0 mod L0, p, γ) are those of (7)–(11) on scans 58–59.
9. **Scan 63, Proposition and last paragraph; unproved claim. Keep.** "il f. et s. que l'on puisse trouver un β" via (24) or
   (27) ("mieux : 24 bis"), and, for fixed β, the equivalence with (20) for x = ℓ∞, i.e. (28)–(29); both directions with one
   conjugator λ need the argument.
10. **Scan 63, (29); first pass's idea. Keep (medium).** g∞ = g0ℓ0^{-γ}ρ^{-1}(h0) is the conjugator of the third line of (11)
    (scan 59); checking (28) under g0 ↦ g0ℓ0^λ tests the normalization of scan 62 directly (cf. B1).
11. **Scan 64, "il semble bien que la condition en question soit hautement non tautologique !"; expectation. Keep.** He
    expects (24) ⟺ (30) to be a real restriction on u, beyond the congruence conditions of §39 that he wants made explicit next.
12. **Scan 64, last paragraph; programme. Correct.** The adjective is "congruentielles" (first pass "augmentelles"): he wants
    the congruence conditions of §39 ("Propriétés de congruence de l'opération de IΓQ sur π̂0,3, liées à l'homomorphisme
    N1,1 → GL(2, Ẑ)") written in terms of (g0, p), to compare them with (24)/(30).
13. **Scan 64, (24) against (30) and p mod 3; first pass's idea. Keep.** (30) sees p only through ρ^p = ρ^{±1}, while (24)
    sees p only through h0; tracking how h0, β (or α = β + γ) and γ = (p−1)/2 move with p is the right test. Unchanged.
14. **Scans 65, (32); unpursued implication. Keep.** The reduction of the inner ambiguity to int(g) with g ∈ Π̂0,3 uses that
    the centre of 𝔖3 is trivial ("son image dans 𝔖3 soit central, i.e. = 1").
15. **Scan 66, "Un tel autom u0 est-il connu par sa restriction à Π̂0,3 ?"; question, answered on the page. Keep.** The
    argument restricts to the inverse image of {1, σ̇∞} and ends "OK"; the inserted Π̂ there has the subscript "0,∞" in the ink.
16. **Scan 66, "g ∈ Centre(Π̂0,3) i.e. g = 1"; unproved claim. Keep.** Π̂0,3 is free profinite of rank 2, whose centre is
    trivial (standard); the page does not prove it.
17. **Scan 66, last paragraph; question. Correct.** The corrected reading is "Mais à quelle condition un autom. de Π̂0,3 se
    prolonge-t-il en un autom. de 𝔖̂+0,3 qui induise l'identité sur 𝔖3 ?". Necessity (commuting with the outer action of 𝔖3,
    "faut" underlined) is shown here; sufficiency is asserted at the top of scan 67 ("C'est suffisant, car …") through (33)–(34).
18. **Scans 65–66, a quotient with non-trivial centre; first pass's idea. Keep.** Repeating (32) for Q with Z(Q) ≠ 1 shows
    where the preimage of Z(Q) enters; a check, not a claim.
19. **Scan 67, (33) "car le centre de Π̂0,3 est trivial"; unproved claim. Keep** (same fact as 16).
20. **Scan 67, Prop "isom. can."; unproved claim. Keep.** Independence of choices and the inverse construction are not written.
21. **Scan 67, margin "Mais tout autom. de 𝔖3 est intérieur !"; implication. Correct.** The glyph is settled as 𝔖3. Since
    Out(𝔖3) = 1, "qui induisent dans 𝔖3 l'autom. ext. trivial" is automatic for automorphisms preserving Π̂0,3; the
    corollaries of scan 68 then pass to the stricter "identité sur le quotient 𝔖3".
22. **Scan 68, "Cette extension ℰ! … est scindée (non canoniquement)"; unproved claim. Keep.** The splitting subgroup is the
    automorphisms fixing σ∞ (and ρ mod Π̂0,3); the margin attributes it to a fact about Π̂0,3-conjugacy of the elements of
    order 2 above σ̇∞ (see B6).
23. **Scan 68, cancelled calculation "(gσ∞)² = 1"; first pass's idea. Keep (medium).** The same condition is kept elsewhere:
    g·σ∞(g) = 1 on scan 66, and for ρ the condition (38) on scan 69; the cocycle reading of 7 applies.
24. **Scan 68, (36); first pass's idea. Correct.** The condition on g is stated on the next page: (38) gρ(g)ρ²(g) = 1, plus the
    marginal requirement that σ∞ and gρ generate with only the relations σ∞² = (gρ)³ = 1. An exploratory check should impose
    both.
25. **Scan 69, (37)–(38) and margin; unproved claim. Keep.** The margin separates the relation (38) from generation (an
    automorphism, not only an endomorphism). The group generated is written Π̂0,3 in the ink, where 𝔖̂+0,3 (or Π̂'0,3 ≅ 𝔖̂+0,3,
    scans 55–56) is what (37) presents; a slip or the primed group.
26. **Scans 69–70, "ce p est alors unique"; unproved claim. Keep.**
27. **Scan 69, "or on sait que σ0(ℓ0) = int(a)ℓ0"; earlier result cited. Keep.**
28. **Scan 70, (39)–(41); unpursued implication. Keep.** The ink names the conjugator a in "int(a)" and "(a ∈ Π̂0,3)" and g
    elsewhere; g∞ = gρ(g)ρ²(g0), and a further turn closes with (38).
29. **Scan 70, "le seul élément de 𝔖̂+0,3 de carré égal à ℓ0 est ε0", margin "vérifier"; unproved claim. Keep.**
30. **Scan 69, finite quotients for (38) without generation; first pass's idea. Keep (medium).**
31. **Scan 70, square roots through the centralizer of ℓ0; first pass's idea. Keep (medium).** In PSL(2, Z) = Z/2 * Z/3 the
    centralizer of ε0² is ⟨ε0⟩, which gives the discrete statement; the profinite one needs the centralizer in the completion.

## B. Second-pass notes (Claude)

B1. **Scan 62, left-margin NB; claim checked (consequential). Confidence high, relative to the formulas as written.** By (22),
ℓ'0^β = g0^{-1}λ^{-1}φ̂(g0), where the conjugator λ of (19)–(20) depends only on u, and u does not change under
g0 ↦ g0ℓ0^λ (by (11)). With ℓ0 = ε0² (25), φ̂(ℓ0) = ε0 (18) and ℓ'0 = ε0, the new value is ε0^{-2λ}ε0^βε0^λ = ε0^{β−λ}, so
β' = β − λ, not β − 3λ. Every u therefore has a representative g0 with β = 0; Grothendieck's own "Correct?" stands.

B2. **Scan 62, (24 bis); model's idea. Confidence medium.** With φ̂(h0) = σ∞^ε g0 ε0^β, the change g0 ↦ g0ℓ0^λ gives
φ̂(h'0) = σ∞^{-λ}φ̂(h0)ε0^λ, so (β, ε) ↦ (β − λ, ε − λ mod 2) (ε and β are unique for given g0, since ε0 has infinite order).
The invariant of u is β − ε mod 2, i.e. ε after normalizing β = 0. The meaningful form of his mod-3 question is whether this
Z/2-invariant takes both values, for instance on automorphisms coming from ΓQ.

B3. **Scans 60–64; implication for the references. Confidence high.** Two formulas carry the number (18): scan 60
"(18) u(ρ) conjugué de ρ^p", which scan 64 calls "la formule présomptive (18)", and scan 61 "(18)" defining φ̂, which scan 63
cites as "défini par (18)". (28) for x = ℓ∞ is what makes the presumptive (18) of scan 60 precise.

B4. **Scan 63, inserted "(On prolonge u en autom de 𝔖̂+0,3 par int(u), cf (15))"; implication. Confidence high.** The
reference is (15) of scan 59: u(σ∞) = σ∞, u(ρ) = g.ρ with g = σ∞(g0)ℓ1^γρ(g0)^{-1}. The same shape returns as (36) on scan 68,
and (38) on scan 69 is (16) of scan 60; the Proposition and the Corollary of scan 69 therefore speak about one family of u.

B5. **Scan 66, "(≃ Sl(2, Z)'^)"; claim to check. Confidence medium.** The presentation (37) σ∞² = ρ³ = 1 is that of
Z/2 * Z/3 ≅ PSL(2, Z), so the completion is PSL(2, Z)^ rather than SL(2, Z)^. Whether the small mark after the parenthesis
is meant to indicate the quotient by ±1 is not settled by the ink.

B6. **Scan 68, splitting of ℰ!; claim without proof. Confidence medium.** Composing u ∈ ℰ! with int(h), h ∈ Π̂0,3, so as to
fix σ∞ needs every element of order 2 above σ̇∞ to be Π̂0,3-conjugate to σ∞; in the discrete Z/2 * Z/3 this is the Kurosh
theorem, and the profinite case needs the corresponding result on finite subgroups of free profinite products (results of
Herfort and Ribes; to check). Uniqueness of h needs Π̂0,3^{σ∞} = 1, admitted on scan 58; the dependence on choosing σ∞ among
σ0, σ1, σ∞ is the "non canoniquement".

B7. **Scan 66, inserted "Π̂0,∞ · {1, σ∞}"; observation. Confidence medium.** The inverse image of {1, σ̇∞} ⊂ 𝔖3 in 𝔖̂+0,3 is
Π̂0,3 · {1, σ∞}; the ink's subscript "0,∞" is either a slip or a name not defined in these pages, and stays marked.


# ===== LM_P066 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P066 (scans 71–80, manuscript pp. 324–328, §41): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Basis: `second_pass\LM_P066_checked.tex`. Notation as corrected there: 𝔖3 and Ŝ+0,3 (the first pass's 𝒢/𝔖 doubt is
settled), ξ rather than ζ, and ρ̇ for the image of ρ in 𝔖3. The checks below use only relations stated in the text:
ℓ0ℓ∞ℓ1 = 1, σ∞(ℓ0) = ℓ1, ρ(ℓ0) = ℓ1, ρ(ℓ1) = ℓ∞ (scans 72, 73, 78), and Ŝ+0,3 = ⟨σ∞, ρ | σ∞² = ρ³ = 1⟩ (scan 69).
Literature named below is cited from memory and marked "to check". Scan numbers are the archive scan numbers.

## The first pass's notes

**N1. Scans 71–72. Claim without proof. Keep.** Formula (41) and the proof on scan 72 use N_{Π̂0,3}(L1) = L1 as known
(the symbol is overwritten on scan 71 and a clean 𝒩 on scan 72). It is the self-normalising property of the loop
subgroups that the manuscript records earlier as N_E(L_i) = E_i; it is not proved on these pages.

**N2. Scans 71–72, 75. Claim without proof. Keep, now checked.** "γ bien déterminé", "g ne dépend que de g0 mod L0" and
the invariance of (46) under g0 ↦ g0ℓ0^λ follow by substitution: σ∞(ℓ0^λ) = ρ(ℓ0^λ) = ℓ1^λ leaves
g = σ∞(g0)ℓ1^γρ(g0)^{-1} unchanged, and h0 ↦ ℓ1^{-λ}h0ℓ0^λ sends ξ0 = ℓ0^γh0^{-1} to ℓ0^{-λ}ξ0ρ(ℓ0^λ), which conjugates
ξ0ρ by ℓ0^{-λ} and so preserves (ξ0ρ)³ = 1. Reason: the first pass asked for exactly this substitution.

**N3. Scans 73–74. Model's own idea (first pass). Keep.** The corrected text confirms the inclusion ⊂ in (43); the surviving
computation and its NB give int(g0^{-1})u(ε0) = ε0^{2γ+1}, hence (44) with p = 2γ+1, and the NB of scan 74 proves the
converse, that every p ∈ Ẑ* has (p−1)/2 ∈ Ẑ. The remark that the power map fills the procyclic subgroup only for a unit
exponent matches the separate unit condition of scans 79–80.

**N4. Scan 74. Claim without proof. Correct.** The condition declared "superflue, i.e., automatiquement vérifiée" is the
boxed (9) of scan 58 (LM_P064), whose margin announces "on verra que (9) est superflu(e), et γ = (p−1)/2"; the digit here has
an open top like a 7, so the transcription keeps `\unsure{(9)|(7)}`. No proof is given ("on voit"): one must derive (9)
from (10) = (46) and the definitions of h0 and γ. Reason: the cross-reference identifies the condition the first pass left
open.

**N5. Scan 75, Théorème. Claim without proof. Keep, corrected.** Two lifts commuting with σ∞ differ by int(x) with
x ∈ Π̂0,3 and x^{-1}σ∞(x) central, so uniqueness follows from the triviality of the centre of Π̂0,3 and of Π̂0,3^{σ∞}, the
fact the margin of scan 79 invokes; existence rests on scans 71–74 and is not argued again here. The qualifier after "autom.
ext." is "à lacets" or its abbreviation "à lcts": the remaining doubt is of spelling, not of meaning.

**N6. Scan 75, condition b). Implication not pursued. Keep, completed.** By the NB, a) is equivalent to (gρ)³ = 1, so
σ∞ ↦ σ∞, ρ ↦ gρ defines an endomorphism of Ŝ+0,3; b) makes it surjective, and a surjective endomorphism of a topologically
finitely generated profinite group is bijective (such groups are Hopfian). So a) and b) together give an automorphism; the
text does not state this last step.

**N7. Scan 76, cancelled Corollaire. Claim without proof, cancelled. Correct.** The labels are [(24)] (not (21)) and
[(30)] or [(38)] (overwritten), and the qualifier is "extérieurement", now read without doubt; the two formulas are (24) of
scan 62 and (30) of scan 64, with α = β+γ as in the margin there, so the corollary recapitulates LM_P065. The margin "β resp.
α sont uniquement déterminés" is an unproved uniqueness claim, and the whole block is crossed out: it is not an accepted
result.

**N8. Scan 77, NB "λ ↦ ^λξ est injectif". Claim without proof. Keep, with a route.** Injectivity says that no λ ≠ 1 of
Π̂0,3 centralises the order-3 element ξρ. In Ŝ+0,3 = Ẑ/2 ⨿ Ẑ/3 the centraliser of a torsion element is the finite group it
generates (centraliser theorem for free profinite products, Herfort–Ribes 1985, to check), and that meets the torsion-free
Π̂0,3 trivially.

**N9. Scan 77, (51)–(54). Reclassified: Grothendieck's own formulation (the first pass called it a model idea). Keep.** The
text itself identifies the solutions of (51) with the splittings of the sub-extension of Ŝ+0,3 of Z/3Z by Π̂0,3 generated
by ρ̇, and with the order-3 elements above ρ̇, on which Π̂0,3 acts by (54); in modern terms this is the non-abelian
H¹(Z/3Z, Π̂0,3). The first pass's caution about conventions (cocycles versus lifts) still applies.

**N10. Scan 78. Grothendieck's explicit assumption ("Admettons (quitte à le vérifier par la suite)"). Keep, with a proof
route.** If every finite subgroup of the free profinite product Ŝ+0,3 is conjugate into a factor (Herfort–Ribes 1985,
to check), an order-3 element above ρ̇ is aρ^{±1}a^{-1}; since the centraliser of ρ̇ in 𝔖3 is ⟨ρ̇⟩ and ρ̇^{-1} is conjugate
to ρ̇ only by transpositions, this leaves exactly the Π̂0,3-classes of ρ and of σ∞ρ^{-1}σ∞. They are distinct because ρ and
ρ^{-1} have different images in Ŝ+0,3^{ab} = Z/6Z. (The word "deux" is now `\unsure` in the transcription, on ink grounds.)

**N11. Scan 78, bracketed remark after (56). Implication not pursued. Correct.** With ρ(ℓ0)^{-1} now settled, the line
ℓ1^{-1} = ℓ0(ℓ0^{-1})ρ(ℓ0)^{-1} is (56) with λ = ℓ0 (as ρ(ℓ0) = ℓ1): it checks that the representative ξ0 = ℓ1^{-1} of the
second class lies in family (56); the cancelled "i.e. ℓ0 = ρ(λ)ℓ1λ^{-1} … λ = ℓ∞ℓ0" is an abandoned attempt at the same
check. The families (55) and (56) are disjoint because they are the two classes of N10.

**N12. Scan 79, "au plus un seul g0". Claim without proof. Keep.** Uniqueness of g0 for fixed h0 follows from
Π̂0,3^{σ∞} = {1}, which the corrected margin invokes ("en utilisant que …"). That fixed-point statement is the centraliser
fact of N8 for the involution σ∞ and is not proved here.

**N13. Scan 79, "un tel g0 existe ssi (59)". Claim without proof. Keep, sharpened.** (59) σ∞(h0)h0 = 1 is equivalent to
(h0^{-1}σ∞)² = 1, so the "ssi" says that every involution of the coset Π̂0,3σ∞ is Π̂0,3-conjugate to σ∞, i.e.
H¹(⟨σ∞⟩, Π̂0,3) = 1. This follows from the conjugacy of finite subgroups into factors (as in N10), because the centraliser
of σ̇∞ in 𝔖3 is ⟨σ̇∞⟩; the page gives no argument.

**N14. Scan 79, "il existe au plus un γ". Claim without proof. Correct: it has a two-line proof.** Abelianising (60) in
Π̂0,3^{ab} = Ẑℓ0 ⊕ Ẑℓ1 gives ξ̄ + σ∞(ξ̄) = γ(ℓ0 + ℓ1), so γ is unique; S2 below computes it.

**N15. Scan 79, last parenthesis. Model's own idea (first pass). Correct.** The ink settles the star: "∃γ′ ∈ Ẑ*". The
identity (2γ+1)(2γ′+1) − 1 = 2(2γγ′ + γ + γ′) in the torsion-free Ẑ shows that the right range is Ẑ (γ = 0 forces γ′ = 0,
which is not a unit), so the star is the author's slip, not a transcription doubt.

**N16. Scan 80, opening NB. Implication not pursued. Keep, checked.** The substitutions are consistent: λ′ = ℓ0^αλ gives
ξ′ = ℓ0^αξρ(ℓ0)^{-α} and h′0 = ξ′^{-1}ℓ0^γ = ρ(ℓ0)^αh0ℓ0^{-α}, and since ρ(ℓ0) = σ∞(ℓ0) = ℓ1 this is exactly the h0 of
g′0 = g0ℓ0^{-α}. So the left coset L0λ of the Corollaire of scan 79 corresponds to the right coset g0L0 of the Théorème.

**N17. Scan 80, steps 1°–2°, (A)–(C). Proposed procedure. Keep.** Grothendieck calls it "la démarche la plus claire … me
semble celle-ci": choose ξ solving (A) in a form (B), then γ satisfying (C) with 2γ+1 ∈ Ẑ*. The page does not decide for
which λ such a γ exists; the overwritten step reference (1°|2°) is unresolved but does not change the content.

**N18. Scan 80. Model's own idea (first pass). Keep, as a computational suggestion.** Testing (A) and (C) in finite
quotients on which σ∞ and ρ act can refute a candidate λ but cannot prove existence. With S2, the abelian quotient already
determines γ and p mod 6.

## Second-pass notes (mine)

**S1. Scans 74–80. Own idea (an identification, to check in the literature).** The two conditions obtained here —
h0σ∞(h0) = 1 ((59)) and ξρ(ξ)ρ²(ξ) = 1 with ξ = ℓ0^γh0^{-1}, γ = (p−1)/2 ((46), (48), (51)) — have the shape of the
two-cycle and three-cycle relations f(x,y)f(y,x) = 1 and f(z,x)z^m f(y,z)y^m f(x,y)x^m = 1, m = (λ−1)/2, of Drinfeld's
ĜT (1990), without the pentagon, with p in the role of λ and h0 in that of f up to normalisation. If the match survives a
careful comparison of conventions, these pages describe in 1981 the outer automorphisms of Π̂0,3 that commute with 𝔖3 by
such pairs (p, h0); whether the literature (Ihara; Lochak–Schneps; Schneps's surveys) records this must be checked before
any claim.

**S2. Scans 78–80. Own idea (a computation).** In Π̂0,3^{ab} = Ẑℓ0 ⊕ Ẑℓ1 (ℓ∞ = −ℓ0 − ℓ1; ρ: ℓ0 ↦ ℓ1 ↦ ℓ∞; σ∞
exchanges ℓ0 and ℓ1) write λ̄ = aℓ0 + bℓ1. For ξ = λρ(λ)^{-1} (55) one gets ξ̄ = (a+b)ℓ0 + (2b−a)ℓ1, and the abelianised
(60) gives γ = 3b, p = 1 + 6b; for ξ = λℓ0^{-1}ρ(λ)^{-1} (56), γ = 3b − 1, p = 6b − 1. So γ is computable from λ, b is well
defined on L0\Π̂0,3, case A means p ≡ 1 mod 3 and case B p ≡ −1 mod 3 (matching the role of p mod 3 noted on scan 64),
λ = 1 gives p = 1 (identity) and p = −1, and the supplementary condition becomes 1 + 6b ∈ Ẑ* (resp. 6b − 1 ∈ Ẑ*), a real
restriction since no rational integer other than ±1 is a unit of Ẑ.

**S3. Scans 77–79. Own idea (a literature route).** Four statements used or assumed on these pages — Π̂0,3^{σ∞} = {1}
(margin of scan 79), the injectivity NB (scan 77), the two-class assumption (scan 78) and the existence half of (59) — all
follow from the theorems on torsion in free profinite products (finite subgroups conjugate into a factor; centralisers of
torsion elements finite), applied to Ŝ+0,3 = Ẑ/2 ⨿ Ẑ/3 with its quotient 𝔖3. Those theorems postdate 1981 (Herfort–Ribes
1985; the profinite Bass–Serre theory of Zalesskii–Melnikov, to check), so on these pages they are working assumptions; a
write-up should cite the exact statements.


# ===== LM_P067 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P067 (scans 81–90) — research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Part A takes the first pass's research notes (`answers/unzipped/LM_P067/LM_P067_notes.md`, §5) one by one: the
decision (keep, correct, drop) with its reason, then the note as it now stands, read against `LM_P067_checked.tex`.
Part B gives the second pass's own notes, labelled as such. "Unproved here" means that these pages give no proof.
Notation follows the corrected text: the group is 𝔖_{0,3} (not 𝒢), the cocycle is ξ, the exponent γ, the hatted map φ̂.

## A. First-pass notes

### Scans 81–83

1. **Scan 81, diagonal NB — question.** Correct: the reading changed. The NB reads "Il faudrait s'assurer par contre
   que la connaissance de g₀ ne détermine pas γ i.e. p, i.e. que le γ tel que ξ = ℓ₀^γ h₀⁻¹ satisfasse (A) ne soit
   pas unique. À éclaircir!" Grothendieck asks to verify that g₀ does not determine γ (equivalently p), i.e. that
   several γ make ℓ₀^γh₀⁻¹ a solution of (A), and leaves the point open.

2. **Scan 81, "Ici, la seule équation délicate" — claim without proof / stated difficulty.** Correct: the equations
   are not missing, they are on scan 80 (package P066): (A) ξρ(ξ)ρ²(ξ) = 1; (B) ξ = λρ(λ)⁻¹ or ξ = λℓ₀⁻¹ρ(λ)⁻¹;
   C σ∞(ξ) = ℓ₁^γ ξ⁻¹ ℓ₀^γ. Only C is delicate: the existence (not the uniqueness, "en bon cas") of γ ∈ Ẑ, plus
   2γ+1 ∈ Ẑ*, which makes it a condition on λ ∈ Π̂_{0,3}, of two types according to the branch of (B). Unproved here.

3. **Scans 81–82, "Je prévois" — conjecture.** Keep. The branch of (B) should be fixed by p ≡ 1 or −1 mod 3
   (γ ≡ 0 or −1 mod 3; γ ≡ 1 is excluded because 3 would divide p), "c'est en tous cas ce qui sera le cas si
   u ∈ Γ_{Q₀}". Unproved here.

4. **Scans 82–83, the test by reduction — deferred work.** Correct: notation, and the relation used. 𝔖̂⁺_{0,3} is
   reduced modulo the single relation ℓ₀³ = 1, i.e. (σ∞ρ)⁶ = 1 (a display with three cubic relations is struck),
   which gives an extension of 𝔖₃ by ⟨ℓ₀, ℓ₁, ℓ∞ | ℓᵢ³ = ℓ∞ℓ₁ℓ₀ = 1⟩; "ou mieux", 𝔖̂⁺_{0,3} ≃ Sl(2,Z)′^ is reduced
   to Sl(2,Z/6Z)′ ≃ Sl(2,Z/2Z) × Sl(2,Z/3Z)′. The matrices of σ∞ and ρ in Sl(2,Ẑ) are postponed ("explicitation
   que nous ferons tantôt").

5. **Scan 83, (61)–(63) — claim without proof (conjectured parametrization).** Correct: the map and the unknowns.
   "On pense donc que u correspond à (ξ, g₀, γ, β)" subject to (62), where g₀ = φ̂(ξ)ε₀^{γ−β} comes from (24 bis)
   and φ̂ is the homomorphism Π̂_{0,3} → 𝔖̂⁺_{0,3}. Eliminating g₀ gives (63) in the unknowns (ξ, r, s), r = γ−β,
   s = γ+β (r and s are written over γ and β), with r+s+1 = p ∈ Ẑ*. The correspondence itself is conjectured.

6. **Scan 83, last paragraph — implication not pursued.** Keep (with ξ). λ is determined up to λ ↦ ℓ₀^α λ, which
   turns ξ into ℓ₀^α ξ ℓ₁^{−α}; scan 80 adds g₀ ↦ g₀ℓ₀^{−α}. Which data of (63) are invariant is not examined.

7. **Scan 82 — model's own idea (finite check modulo 6).** Keep. Once σ∞ and ρ have explicit matrices, compute in
   Sl(2,Z/6Z)′ and test the two classes of p mod 3 separately. A positive finite check would not prove the
   profinite statement.

8. **Scan 83 — model's own idea (formal elimination).** Correct: carried out. The forward elimination (62) ⇒ (63)
   holds (note B3). The converse, rebuilding g₀ and u from a solution of (63), still needs the facts of the
   earlier pages (Π̂_{0,3}^{σ∞} = {1}, the lifting of u).

### Scans 84–86

9. **Scan 84, heading — claims without proof.** Correct: the group's name. Under "Récapitulation sur le groupe",
   𝔖_{0,3} is identified with Π₁(U^{an}_{0,3}, 𝔖₃; −j̄), with Π̃′_{0,3} ≃ Π₁((ℙ^{1an}, 0{0}+2{1}+3{∞}), {1,τ}; −j̄),
   with Gl(2,Z)′ = Gl(2,Z)/{±1} ≃ GP(1,Z) and with 𝒯′_{1,1} = 𝒯_{1,1}/{±1}. None is proved on this page.

10. **Scan 84, (1) — claim without proof.** Keep (with 𝔖). 𝔖_{0,3} is an extension of 𝒯_{0,3} ≃ 𝔖₃ × Z/2Z by
    Π_{0,3} = Π₁(U^{an}_{0,3}; −j̄); §37 states the same ("S0,3 est une extension de T0,3 = S3 × Z/2Z par π0,3",
    CSG/IUT), so the construction is earlier in the manuscript.

11. **Scan 84, (2)–(4) — formulas to be checked.** Keep, with a precision. (3) holds for composition read from
    right to left (σ̇₀σ̇₁(z) = σ̇₀(1/z) = 1/(1−z) = ρ̇(z)). The insertion over σ̇₀ is a separate doubt (B5).

12. **Scan 85, margin NB — claim without proof.** Keep: the empty fixed sets of ρ̇τ and ρ̇⁻¹τ are verified in B6.

13. **Scan 85, "L'image la plus suggestive" — geometric picture, not a conjecture.** Keep. τ, τ̇′₀, τ̇′₁, τ̇′∞ are
    the reflections in the equator and in three meridians through the poles −j̄, −j, which cut the equator at
    0, ½, 1, 2, ∞, −1; the figure also names the reflections ṙ₀, ṙ₁, ṙ∞ (τ̇′₀ = ṙ₁, τ̇′∞ = ṙ₀).

14. **Scan 85, (7)–(9) — model's own idea (ledger of fixed loci).** Keep; carried out in B6 for (8), (9) and the NB.

15. **Scan 86, the 12 triangles — claim without proof.** Keep. The four great circles cut the sphere into 12
    spherical triangles, fundamental domains permuted simply transitively by 𝒯_{0,3} ≃ 𝔖₃ × Z/2 of order 12; the
    insertion identifies them with the barycentric subdivision of the two hemispheres of ℙ¹_ℝ with vertices 0, 1, ∞.

16. **Scan 86, (11)–(12) — model's own idea (reflection-group presentation).** Keep, with B7. The reflections
    τ, τ̇′∞, τ̇′₀ in the sides of the triangle (−j̄, 0, ½) give a (2,2,3) Coxeter presentation of 𝒯_{0,3}; B7 shows
    that (12) agrees with (3) only if its middle generator is τ̇′₀τ̇′∞ = ρ̇⁻¹.

17. **Scan 86, (13) — claim without proof.** Keep. Π_{0,3} is generated by ℓ₀, ℓ₁, ℓ∞ with the single relation
    ℓ∞ℓ₁ℓ₀ = 1; the order of the product depends on the basepoint −j̄ and on loop conventions not drawn here.

18. **Scan 86, the stabilizer of −j̄ — claim without proof.** Keep (with 𝔖′₃ and sg). The index-2 subgroup
    𝔖′₃ = (𝒯_{0,3})_{−j̄} = {(g, τ^ε) : sg(g) = (−1)^ε} = {1, τ̇₀, τ̇₁, τ̇∞, ρ̇, ρ̇⁻¹} acts on Π_{0,3}, its actions
    being named 1, τ′₀, τ′₁, τ′∞, ρ, ρ⁻¹; B8 checks one element against the basepoint.

19. **Scan 86, the two families of involutions — model's own idea.** Keep, sharpened. On the sphere the τ̇ᵢ = τσ̇ᵢ of
    (6) and the τ̇′ᵢ of (7) are the same maps (τσ̇₀(z) = z̄/(z̄−1)); the two names only separate for the lifts to
    𝔖_{0,3}, where τᵢ = σᵢτ′ᵢ (scan 87).

### Scans 87–88

20. **Scan 87, (17)–(18) — claims without proof.** Correct: the doubt is now located. (17) and the first two chains
    of (18) agree with (14) and (16); the third chain ends with ℓ₀ρ⁻¹ in the ink, whereas (14) gives ρℓ∞ = ℓ₀ρ.
    The transcription keeps the ink (B10).

21. **Scan 87, the characterization of τᵢ — claim without proof.** Keep. τᵢ = σᵢτ′ᵢ = τ′ᵢσᵢ "est caractérisé" by lying
    over τ, having square 1 (insertion) and commuting with σᵢ (or with τ′ᵢ); the margin "rappeler pourquoi on a cette
    caractérisation" defers the proof of uniqueness.

22. **Scan 87, last lines — deferred work.** Correct: the reference. It is "[C]", with square brackets, and the same
    [C] occurs on scan 86 ("τ′₀, τ′₁, τ′∞ étaient notés précédemment (dans [C]) σ̄₀, σ̄₁, σ̄∞"): [C] is an earlier
    text of the author in which these operators were described under other names. The adjective of "la
    description … (faite dans [C])" is still uncertain.

23. **Scan 88, εᵢ² = ℓᵢ — claim without proof.** Keep. εᵢ (ε₀ = σ∞ρ = ρσ₁, ε₁ = σ₀ρ = ρσ∞, ε∞ = σ₁ρ = ρσ₀) lies in
    𝔖⁺_{0,3} over σ̇ᵢ and is "caractérisé" by εᵢ² = ℓᵢ; uniqueness of this square root over σ̇ᵢ is not proved here.

24. **Scan 88, the exception for ρ̇τ, ρ̇⁻¹τ — claim without proof.** Keep. The two elements of order 6 cannot be
    lifted with the same order, "en effet ℙ¹^{ρ̇τ} = ℙ¹^{ρ̇⁻¹τ} = ∅"; the empty fixed sets are checked in B6, the step
    from fixed points to lifts is the general theory invoked in the next sentence.

25. **Scan 88, "On sait que" — claim without proof.** Keep. Lifts of the same order, up to Π_{0,3}-conjugation,
    correspond to the connected components of the fixed points in U = U^{an}_{0,3} of the corresponding
    topological automorphisms; this underlies all the counts that follow.

26. **Scan 88, the counts — claim without proof.** Keep. One class each over σ̇ᵢ and τ̇′ᵢ; three over τ (τ₀, τ₁, τ∞);
    two over ρ̇, ρ̇⁻¹ (poles −j̄, −j; −j̄ is chosen); the lifts at −j are obtained by conjugating with a τᵢ.

27. **Scans 87–88 — model's own idea (audit on free-group words).** Keep; partly done in B8–B10, which find two
    entries that disagree with the rest of the tables ((14) last entry, (18) last chain).

28. **Scan 88 — model's own idea (cocycle formulation).** Keep. Lifts of order n of an element of order n of
    𝔖_{0,3}/Π_{0,3}, up to Π_{0,3}-conjugation, are nonabelian 1-cocycles of Z/n in Π_{0,3}; comparing this with the
    fixed-component description is a route for checking the counts, not a result.

### Scans 89–90

29. **Scan 89, (23) — claim without proof.** Keep; the inserted identities are verified in B11. That ρ₀, ρ₁, ρ∞ are
    not Π_{0,3}-conjugate to ρ is asserted, not proved; it is what the fixed-point correspondence predicts (two
    poles, two classes).

30. **Scan 89, (24) — claim without proof.** Keep. Π_{0,3}^{σᵢ} = Π_{0,3}^{ρ} = Π_{0,3}^{τᵢ} = Π_{0,3}^{τ′ᵢ} = {1} is
    drawn from "la théorie générale" and from the 1-connectedness of the components of the fixed-point sets.

31. **Scan 89, the stabilizers — claim without proof.** Keep. The two exceptional orbits (2, −1, ½) and (−j̄, −j)
    have stabilizers of order 4 (at 2: D₂ ≃ Z/2Z × Z/2Z, generated by σ̇₀, τ̇₀) and 6 (at −j̄: 𝔖′₃ ≃ D₃), both lifted
    to 𝔖_{0,3}.

32. **Scans 89–90, the classes of these lifts — claim without proof.** Keep. D₂^{(0)} has one fixed point (2),
    𝔖′₃ = D₃ two (−j̄, −j); the class for −j is obtained by conjugating with any τᵢ, consistent with (23), where
    the three conjugates are Π_{0,3}-conjugate to each other.

33. **Scan 90, (25)–(27) — implication not pursued.** Keep; verified in B12. The unoriented triangulated
    cartographic group Π̃′_{0,3} has involutive generators σ₀, σ₁, σ₂ (retraced marks read as primes) with
    relations (25); its index-2 subgroup Π′_{0,3} is the quotient of Π_{0,3} by ℓ₁², ℓ∞³, via (27).

34. **Scan 90, last line — deferred continuation.** Keep. "Cet homomorphisme se prolonge en" continues on scan 91,
    outside this package.

35. **Scan 89, (23)–(24) — model's own idea (universal cover).** Keep. Realize the lifts as lifts of the conformal
    maps to the universal cover of U based over −j̄ and compare the classes of ρ and ρ₀ through their fixed points.

36. **Scan 90 — model's own idea (presentation of the index-2 subgroup).** Correct: done. The even-length subgroup
    of (25) is generated by ρ_f, ρ_s, σ with the relations (26) (B12).

## B. Second-pass notes (mine)

B1. **Scan 81, NB — question, read precisely (second pass).** Since h₀ = σ∞(g₀)⁻¹g₀ is fixed with g₀, the NB asks
    whether {γ ∈ Ẑ : ℓ₀^γ h₀⁻¹ satisfies (A)} has more than one element. This is not the uniqueness stated on scan 80
    (there ξ is fixed and C has at most one solution γ); here h₀ is fixed and ξ moves with γ. The first word of
    "ne détermine pas" could also be "n'en".

B2. **Scan 82 — implication not pursued (second pass).** The prime in Sl(2,Z/6Z)′ and Sl(2,Z/3Z)′ is presumably the
    quotient by {±1}, as Gl(2,Z)′ = Gl(2,Z)/{±1} on scan 84; then the displayed isomorphism is the Chinese-remainder
    splitting, −1 being 1 modulo 2. The relation ℓ₀³ = 1 ⇔ (σ∞ρ)⁶ = 1 matches ε₀ = σ∞ρ, ε₀² = ℓ₀ of (22).

B3. **Scan 83 — check of the elimination (62) ⇒ (63) (second pass).** With σ∞(x) = σ∞xσ∞⁻¹ (scan 87: g(x) = gxg⁻¹),
    σ∞² = 1 (by (16) σ∞² fixes ℓ₀, ℓ₁) and (22), σ∞(ε₀) = ρσ∞ = ε₁. Putting g₀ = φ̂(ξ)ε₀^r into
    ξσ∞(g₀)⁻¹g₀ = ℓ₀^γ = ε₀^{2γ} gives σ∞(φ̂(ξ))⁻¹φ̂(ξ) = ε₁^r ξ⁻¹ ε₀^{2γ−r} = ε₁^r ξ⁻¹ ε₀^s, the second line of (63),
    and 2γ+1 = r+s+1: (63) follows from (62) as written.

B4. **Scan 84, margin — claim without proof (second pass).** Grothendieck counts five descriptions ("visions") of
    𝔖_{0,3} in the recapitulation and says there are in fact about ten, "cf. (V) ci-dessous"; the author's table of
    contents has §42 V "Les avatars de S0,3" (ms p. 336). The five are presumably Aut_lac(Π_{0,3}), the
    equivariant π₁ with 𝔖₃, the orbifold group Π̃′_{0,3}, Gl(2,Z)/{±1} ≃ GP(1,Z) and 𝒯′_{1,1}.

B5. **Scan 84, (2) — check (second pass).** z/(z−1) = 1 + 1/(z−1). The insertion over σ̇₀ is correct only with the
    denominator z−1, while the dark retraced strokes look like 1−z; the transcription keeps both readings.

B6. **Scan 85, (8), (9) and the NB — check (second pass).** σ̇₀, σ̇₁, σ̇∞, ρ̇ fix {0,2}, {1,−1}, {∞,½}, {−j̄,−j}
    (for ρ̇: z² − z + 1 = 0), as displayed. Fix(τ̇′₀) = {z + z̄ = zz̄} = {|z−1| = 1} = 1 + 𝕌, so the stacked insertion,
    if it reads "= ½ − 𝕌", is wrong. ρ̇τ(z) = 1/(1−z̄) = z forces z − |z|² = 1, so z real with z² − z + 1 = 0,
    impossible, and ∞ ↦ 0; ρ̇⁻¹τ likewise: the NB is right.

B7. **Scans 84 and 86, (3) against (12) — inconsistency in the ink (second pass).** From (3) (σ̇∞σ̇₀ = ρ̇, involutions)
    σ̇₀ρ̇σ̇∞ = (σ̇₀σ̇∞)² = ρ̇⁻² = ρ̇ ≠ 1. What holds is σ̇₀·(τ̇′₀τ̇′∞)·σ̇∞ = (ττ̇′∞)² = 1 with τ̇′₀τ̇′∞ = ρ̇⁻¹ by (10); so
    (12) is right only if its ρ̇ stands for the generator τ̇′₀τ̇′∞, which supports the exponent −1 in the generator
    list above (12). The unsubscripted σ̇² in (12) is a further slip or abbreviation.

B8. **Scans 86–87 — checks of the stabilizer, (16) and (14) (second pass).** τ̇′₀(−j̄) = −j/(−j−1) = −j̄, so τ̇′₀
    fixes the basepoint, as stated. Applying σᵢ to ℓ∞ℓ₁ℓ₀ = 1 gives each third entry of (16) from the first two
    (e.g. σ∞(ℓ∞) = (ℓ₀ℓ₁)⁻¹), which supports the overwritten ℓ₁, ℓ₀ of the σ∞ row. In (14), τ′∞ = τ′₁ρ (from (15))
    gives τ′∞(ℓ∞) = ℓ∞⁻¹ and τ′∞(ℓ₀) = ℓ₁⁻¹ as written, and τ′∞(ℓ₁) = ℓ₀⁻¹, not the ℓ₁⁻¹ of the last written entry.

B9. **Scan 87 — implication (second pass).** The identification Aut_lac(Π_{0,3}) ≃ 𝔖_{0,3} makes each σ both an
    element and the automorphism x ↦ σxσ⁻¹; this convention is the one behind σ∞(g₀) in (62)–(63) and has to be
    kept when (17)–(22) are checked.

B10. **Scan 87, (18) — inconsistency in the ink (second pass).** σ∞σ₀ = ρℓ∞ = ℓ₀ρ follows from ρℓ∞ρ⁻¹ = ℓ₀ in
     (14); the ink writes ℓ₀ρ⁻¹ (the exponent is clearly visible and is kept). The other chains, ρℓ₀ = ℓ₁ρ and
     ρℓ₁ = ℓ∞ρ, follow the same rule.

B11. **Scan 89, (23) — check (second pass).** From (17) ρτ₀ = τ₁ρ, (20) τ₀² = 1 and (21) τ₁τ₀ = ℓ∞:
     ρ₀ = τ₀ρτ₀ = τ₀τ₁ρ = ℓ∞⁻¹ρ, and ℓ∞⁻¹ρ = ρℓ₁⁻¹ because ρ⁻¹ℓ∞ρ = ℓ₁ by (14). Likewise ρ₁ = ℓ₀⁻¹ρ = ρℓ∞⁻¹ and
     ρ∞ = ℓ₁⁻¹ρ = ρℓ₀⁻¹: these are exactly the inserted formulas, so each ρᵢ differs from ρ by a peripheral loop.

B12. **Scan 90, (25)–(27) — check, and a link with scan 85 (second pass).** With ρ_f = σ′₁σ′₀, ρ_s = σ′₂σ′₁,
     σ = σ′₀σ′₂, (25) gives ρ_fσρ_s = σ′₁σ′₀σ′₀σ′₂σ′₂σ′₁ = 1, σ² = 1 and ρ_f³ = 1, i.e. (26), and (27) sends
     ℓ∞ℓ₁ℓ₀ = 1 to ρ_fσρ_s = 1, so Π′_{0,3} = Π_{0,3}/⟨⟨ℓ₁², ℓ∞³⟩⟩; this supports σ′₀σ′₂ as the third generator. The
     margin's wish to name the generators "carrément r₀, r₁, r∞" matches the labels ṙ₀, ṙ₁, ṙ∞ of the reflections
     in the sides of the fundamental triangle in the sphere figure of scan 85.

B13. **Scan 90 — plan of §42 (second pass).** Heading III announces both the cartographic group Π̃′_{0,3} and "et
     avec Sl(2,Z)"; the author's later table of contents splits this into III (p. 334) and IV "Relations avec
     SL(2, Z)" (p. 335), and writes π0,3 for Π̃′_{0,3}. The target of "Cet homomorphisme se prolonge en" is on scan 91.


# ===== LM_P068-069 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P068-069 (scans 91–110, ms pp. 335–344) — research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note R01–R70 is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_P068-069_checked.tex`; S1–S9 are the second
pass's own notes. Group letter 𝔖 (𝔖_{0,3}, 𝔖̂⁺_{0,3}, 𝔖₃) and cocycle letter ξ are now settled; the first pass's
ζ in its research notes means this ξ.

## First-pass notes

- **R01** — scan 91 — unproved claim — keep. "On trouve donc que 𝔖_{0,3} a des relations génératrices" (31), and (32)–(33) for 𝔖⁺_{0,3}, are stated as presentations without proof of completeness.
- **R02** — scan 91 — own idea — drop. A generic suggestion (subgroup rewriting) with nothing specific to the page; S2 gives a concrete check of the same presentations.
- **R03** — scan 92 — unproved claim — keep. The hatched strip 0 ≤ Re z ≤ ½ above |z| = 1 is asserted to be a fundamental domain for Gl(2,Z)' acting by (37); no argument on the page.
- **R04** — scan 92 — unpursued implication — correct. The ink pairs i ↦ ½, −j̄ ↦ −j̄ (no subscript) and ∞ ↦ 0 under the covering map to the triangle (−j̄, 0, ½); the reflections r₀(z) = 1/z̄, r₁(z) = 1 − z̄, r∞(z) = −z̄ fix the three sides (unit circle, Re z = ½, Re z = 0), as the figure labels τ̇'∞ = ṙ₀, τ̇'₀ = ṙ₁, ṙ∞ = τ̇∞ say.
- **R05** — scan 93 — unproved claim — keep. The isomorphism (40) 𝔖_{0,3} ≅ Gl(2,Z)' ≅ GP(1,Z) and the presentation (41) need surjectivity and injectivity; the image of r∞ is now read diag(1, −1).
- **R06** — scan 93 — own idea — correct. The margin reads "il vaut mieux changer des (de) signes cf. Formulaire", braced against the rows of r₁ and r∞; S2 shows the sign that the chosen representatives get wrong.
- **R07** — scan 94 — unproved claim — correct. The ink has Π_{0,3} (not Π_{0,2}) as the kernel of Sl(2,Z)' → Sl(2,Z/2Z) ≅ 𝔖₃, with (48) 1 → Π(2)' → Sl(2,Z)' → Sl(2,Z/2Z) → 1 and Π(2)' ≅ Π_{0,3}; generation of the kernel by the images of ℓ₀, ℓ₁, ℓ∞ is not proved here.
- **R08** — scan 94 — unpursued implication — keep. ℓ∞ℓ₁ℓ₀ = −I in Sl(2,Z) (46) while ℓ'∞ℓ'₁ℓ'₀ = 1 in the quotient: identities move between Sl(2,Z) and Sl(2,Z)' only up to ±1.
- **R09** — scan 95 — unproved claim — correct. The ink says "cette extension s'interprète comme 𝒯_{1,1}": it is the central extension Gl(2,Z) of 𝔖_{0,3}, not 𝔖_{0,3}, that is identified with 𝒯_{1,1}, deferred with "nous y reviendrons" (see S3).
- **R10** — scan 95 — unproved claim — keep. The list of avatars 1)–10) asserts isomorphisms without proof here.
- **R11** — scan 95 — unpursued implication — keep. NB "𝒰^{conf}_{0,3} ≃ (U_{0,3}, 𝒯_{0,3}) ce qui permet d'identifier 7) à 4)".
- **R12** — scan 95 — own idea — drop. A generic bookkeeping suggestion; no mathematical content beyond R10.
- **R13** — scan 96 — unproved claim — keep. Item 9): Π₁(M^{conf}_{1,1}, pt)' ≃ Π₁( )/centre{±1} ≃ Π₁(M'^{conf}_{1,1}, pt), and the analytic analogue.
- **R14** — scan 96 — unproved claim — keep. "Les multiplicités modulaires sont (D, Gl(2,Z)), (D, Sl(2,Z)) … et (D, Gl(2,Z)' = GP(1,Z)) resp. (D, Sl(2,Z)' = GP(1,Z)⁺)".
- **R15** — scan 96 — own idea — keep. Compare the two centre quotients as maps of moduli objects with automorphism groups (stabilizers of j = 0, 1728), not only of points.
- **R16** — scan 97 — unproved claim — keep. All four arrows of (49) are marked isomorphisms; the superscript of Autext^{…}(Π̂_{0,3})' is still unread.
- **R17** — scan 97 — unproved claim — keep. "On admet ici que le centre de 𝔖̂⁺_{0,3} est trivial."
- **R18** — scan 97 — unproved claim — keep. The 1–1 correspondence with the g satisfying (51) and the "condition liminaire" (53) (σ∞, gρ present 𝔖̂⁺_{0,3}).
- **R19** — scan 97 — own idea — keep. Look for cocycle solutions of (51) for which σ∞, gρ do not generate, to see that (53) is a real extra condition.
- **R20** — scan 98 — unproved claim — keep. Equivalence of (54)(i)–(v).
- **R21** — scan 98 — unproved claim — keep. p = χ(u) "bien déterminé par u", g₀ "déterminé a priori mod L₀".
- **R22** — scan 98 — unproved claim — correct (quotation). The ink reads "ne dépendent que de l'autom. ext. défini par u, dans Π̂_{0,3} ou dans 𝔖̂⁺_{0,3}".
- **R23** — scan 98 — own idea — keep. Check that χ is multiplicative on the u respecting the lacet structure.
- **R24** — scan 99 — unproved claim — keep. "dans une large mesure il détermine g (formule (62))".
- **R25** — scan 99 — unproved claim — keep. Exactly two conjugacy classes of order-3 lifts of ρ̇, for the fixed points −j̄, −j of ρ̇ on U^{an}_{0,3} (subscript 0,3 now firm).
- **R26** — scan 99 — unproved claim — keep. Margin "Ces représentations paramétriques sont univoques" (reading still doubtful).
- **R27** — scan 99 — unproved claim — keep. Classes ↔ p ≡ ±1 (3) promised "plus bas"; see S4 for the corresponding γ.
- **R28** — scan 99 — own idea — keep. (58) as a classification of nonabelian 1-cocycles of Z/3Z.
- **R29** — scan 100 — unproved claim — keep. (62) g = σ∞(g₀)ℓ₁^γρ(g₀)⁻¹, said equivalent to (54)(iv)/(v).
- **R30** — scan 100 — unproved claim — keep. Invariance of (62) under g₀ ↦ g₀ℓ₀^α (margin NB).
- **R31** — scan 100 — unproved claim — keep. (51) ⇔ (64) via ξ = ℓ₀^γh₀.
- **R32** — scan 100 — unpursued implication — keep. Margin: h₀ (for σ∞, order 2) plays a role symmetric to ξ (for ρ, order 3), "ceci n'apparaît pas (non plus?) dans les notations".
- **R33** — scan 100 — own idea — keep. Track where the condition liminaire (53) enters the parametrization by (g₀, γ).
- **R34** — scan 101 — unproved claim — keep. Π̂^{σ∞}_{0,3} = {1} "qu'on admet provisoirement".
- **R35** — scan 101 — unproved claim — keep. Existence of g₀ with g₀⁻¹σ∞(g₀) = h₀ ⇔ (67) σ∞(h₀)h₀ = 1, "on l'admettra aussi ici".
- **R36** — scan 101 — unpursued implication — keep. Margin: better to view (65bis) as the "équation en h₀", symmetric to the "équation en ξ" (68).
- **R37** — scan 102 — unpursued implication — keep. d) condition liminaire not conveniently expressed in ξ, γ.
- **R38** — scan 102 — unproved claim — keep. (69) criterion γ = γ', ξ' = ℓ₀^{−α}ξℓ₁^α.
- **R39** — scan 102 — unproved claim — keep. (70): (64) replaced by ξ = λρ(λ)⁻¹ or ξ = λℓ₀⁻¹ρ(λ)⁻¹.
- **R40** — scan 103 — unproved claim — keep. (72) λ' = ℓ₀^{−α}λ, γ = γ'.
- **R41** — scan 103 — unpursued implication — keep. Simultaneous cocycle equations (73) for Z/3Z (via ρ) and Z/2Z (via σ∞), coupled through ℓ₀^γ.
- **R42** — scan 103 — own idea — keep. Intersect the two parametrized cocycle loci, with the identifications (69), (72).
- **R43** — scan 104 — unproved claim — keep. (75) λ₀ = g₀ℓ₀^{−γ}λ.
- **R44** — scan 104 — unpursued implication — keep. "8 fonctions (continues) sur Γ_Q", starting with the cyclotomic character; the functions live on the ss-groupe Autext_lac(Π̂_{0,3})' of outer automorphisms commuting with 𝔖₃.
- **R45** — scan 104 — unproved claim — keep. Margin: normalization of g₀ (hence h₀, ξ, λ, λ₀) promised.
- **R46** — scan 104 — own idea — keep. Test composition laws of the eight parameters on finite quotients.
- **R47** — scan 105 — unproved claim — keep. (75bis)(i)–(vi) equivalent, all meaning χ(u) = 1.
- **R48** — scan 105 — conjecture/expectation — keep. "le seul cas que je sache écrire explicitement (sauf u = 1)": u = τ∞ ∈ 𝔖^{*}_{0,3} (superscript unclear), with (75ter).
- **R49** — scan 105 — question — keep. Are the many necessary conditions on elements coming from Γ_Q automatically satisfied?
- **R50** — scan 105 — own idea — keep. Use (75ter) as a test case for finite-quotient searches.
- **R51** — scan 106 — unproved claim — keep. Sl(2,Ẑ)' is a "(tout petit !)" quotient of Sl(2,Z)'^.
- **R52** — scan 106 — unproved claim — keep. (77)–(78) are correct as written (checked: ρgρ⁻¹, ρ⁻¹gρ, det(g)ρ^{±1}(g⁻¹) and δA, δB, δC, δD all agree with ρ = (0 1; −1 1)); the NB C + D − B = 1 also holds.
- **R53** — scan 106 — own idea — keep. Distinguish matrix equality from equality in Gl(2,Ẑ)'.
- **R54** — scan 107 — unproved claim — keep. Margin "Quitte à remplacer g par −g" reduces (79) to (82) with sign +.
- **R55** — scan 107 — unproved claim — keep. The product Sl(2,Ẑ)' ≃ ∏_ℓ Sl(2,Z_ℓ)' (Sl now firm on both sides).
- **R56** — scan 107 — unproved claim — keep. δ³ = 1 "évidente a priori" (determinant of (82)).
- **R57** — scan 107 — own idea — keep. c + d − b = 1 versus c + d − b = 1/δ.
- **R58** — scan 108 — unproved claim — keep. Under (83), (82) ⇔ δ³ = 1.
- **R59** — scan 108 — unproved claim — keep. Margin: the conditions are sufficient regardless of a + b, necessary outside case 2°.
- **R60** — scan 108 — unproved claim — keep. (86)–(87) for a + b = 0 (checked: (87) is b⁻¹ times the direct computation, using b³ = 1).
- **R61** — scan 108 — own idea — keep. The factor (bδ)² + bδ + 1 in residue characteristic 3.
- **R62** — scan 109 — unproved claim — keep. Case a): 3c + 3/δ = 0 gives c = −1/δ in the torsion-free Z_ℓ; the division by 3 of the struck (89) was abandoned.
- **R63** — scan 109 — unproved claim — keep. (90) g = (−δ' δ'; −δ' 0), δ³ = 1 the only condition (checked).
- **R64** — scan 109 — unproved claim — correct. The reduction is not doubtful mathematically: with b = uδ², (α) becomes δ²(u² + u + 1) = 0, automatically true (S6).
- **R65** — scan 109 — own idea — keep. The cancelled argument (δ + u + 1 = 0, b = δ) belongs to an abandoned reduction.
- **R66** — scan 110 — unproved claim — correct. Besides the proof of the Proposition, its case 2°) as written is inconsistent: see S7; exclusivity of the two cases holds (S8).
- **R67** — scan 110 — unproved claim — keep. Corollary, a + b ≠ 0: d = b − c + 1/δ, c = (a(b + δ') − δ)/(a + b) (checked from ad − bc = δ).
- **R68** — scan 110 — unproved claim — keep. Corollary, a + b = 0: g = (−uδ' uδ'; c −δ'u' − c), u² + u + 1 = 0 (checked: it satisfies c + d − b = δ').
- **R69** — scan 110 — own idea — keep. The Proposition's 1°) c + d − b = 1 against the Corollary's 1/δ; equal for δ = 1, the only case used (margin, scan 107).
- **R70** — scan 110 — own idea — keep. Existence of primitive cube roots of unity in Z_ℓ (ℓ ≡ 1 mod 3) decides whether the second family exists.

## Second-pass notes (own)

- **S1** — scan 92, (34) — claim with a slip (own check). (ρ⁻¹σ∞ρ⁻¹)² = ρ⁻¹σ∞ρ⁻²σ∞ρ⁻¹ = ρ⁻¹σ∞ρσ∞ρ⁻¹, but the ink writes the middle factor ρ⁻¹; that product is −(3 −1; 1 0) in Sl(2,Z), of trace ±3, not ℓ∞. The matrix ℓ∞ = ε∞² = (3 −2; 2 −1) of (44) agrees with the correct square.
- **S2** — scan 93, (40)–(42) — implication not pursued (own). With r₀ ↦ (0 1; 1 0), r₁ ↦ (−1 1; 0 1), r∞ ↦ (1 0; 0 −1) one gets r₁r₀ ↦ (1 −1; 1 0) = ρ⁻¹ and r₀r∞ ↦ (0 −1; 1 0) = σ∞ exactly, but r∞r₁ ↦ (−1 1; 0 −1) = −ε₀. So (40) and (42) agree only modulo ±1, which is presumably what "il vaut mieux changer des signes cf. Formulaire" is about. No choice of signs gives all three products exactly: (r₁r₀)(r₀r∞)(r∞r₁) = I whenever the rᵢ square to I, while ρ⁻¹σ∞ε₀ = ρ⁻¹σ∞²ρ = −I.
- **S3** — scan 95 — unproved claim (author, deferred). "Cette extension s'interprète comme 𝒯_{1,1}": Gl(2,Z), the central extension of 𝔖_{0,3} ≅ Gl(2,Z)' by ±1, is 𝒯_{1,1} ≅ Autext_lac(Π_{1,1}); with item 8) 𝒯'_{1,1} = 𝒯_{1,1}/{±1} this gives 𝔖_{0,3} ≅ 𝒯'_{1,1}. "Nous y reviendrons" defers the proof.
- **S4** — scan 103, (74) — claim check (own). p = 2γ + 1 (63), so p ≡ 1 (3) ⇔ γ ≡ 0 (3) and p ≡ −1 (3) ⇔ γ ≡ −1 (3); "γ ≡ 1 (3)", as the retraced digit reads, would give p ≡ 0 (3), impossible for p ∈ Ẑ^*. The example (75ter) has γ = −1, p = −1.
- **S5** — scan 107, (81) — own check. The identities Q = δ − a(c + d − b), R = (c + d)(c + d − b) − δ and S = δ − c(c + d − b) hold for every g, with δ = det g; the author struck the condition "si det g = +1" over the Q identity (writing δ over 1) but left "si det g = 1" under those for R and S.
- **S6** — scan 109, case b) — implication (own check). With b = uδ² (u³ = 1, u ≠ 1) and δ³ = 1, (α) δ² + b²δ + b = δ²(1 + u² + u) vanishes identically, so case b) imposes nothing beyond b = uδ²: this is the Corollary's second family, and it explains why the paragraph deriving δ + u + 1 = 0 and b = δ is struck.
- **S7** — scan 110, Proposition 2°) — claim with a slip (own check). The ink writes g = −δ'(−1 1; 1 0) = −δ'ρ⁻¹, but (−1 1; 1 0) is not ρ⁻¹ = (1 −1; 1 0) (45), and −δ'(−1 1; 1 0) has determinant −δ'² = −δ ≠ δ. The case derived in (90) is g = (−δ' δ'; −δ' 0) = −δ'(1 −1; 1 0) = −δ'ρ⁻¹; the slip swaps the signs of the first row.
- **S8** — scan 110 — own check. The two cases do exclude each other: the matrix of 2°) has c + d − b = −2δ', and −2δ' = 1 (or = δ' = 1/δ) is impossible in Z_ℓ for a unit δ' with δ'³ = 1 (it would give −1/8 = 1, resp. 3δ' = 0).
- **S9** — scan 91, (29) — implication (own). The notes under (29) say τ'∞ and τ'₀ lie over σ̇∞τ and σ̇₀τ in 𝒯_{0,3}, and τ∞ over τ; this matches τᵢ = σᵢτ'ᵢ of LM_P067 (scan 87), since then τ̇'ᵢ = σ̇ᵢτ.


# ===== LM_P070-071 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P070-071 (scans 111–130): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

The first pass's notes R01–R39 are checked one by one against `LM_P070-071_checked.tex` (keep / correct / drop, with the reason). Notes C01–C08 are the second-pass checker's own, labelled as such. Kinds: conjecture or question; claim without proof; implication not pursued; own idea. Equation numbers (44)–(91) of §42 refer to package LM_P068-069 (scans 94–110).

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 111 — claim without proof — keep, corrected.** The counts 2, 1, 0 of parameters and the variation of the cases from one ℓ to another rest on the case analysis of scans 107–110 (Proposition of scan 110, with (86) the case a+b = 0 on scan 108), which is in the preceding package, not outside the manuscript. The ring in "une telle solution dans Gl(2, Ẑ)" is written with a hat and no ℓ.

**R02 — scan 111 — question — keep, corrected.** The second factor is now read −l₁⁻¹: the question is whether every ξ ∈ Sl(2, Ẑ)′ with ξ = (A B; C D) satisfying the norm equation has the form λ₀ρ(λ₀)⁻¹ or λ′₀(−l₁⁻¹)ρ(λ′₀)⁻¹, and Grothendieck says he does not know, even under g ≡ id (2). The insertion rewrites the second form as λ₀(…)ρ(λ₀)⁻¹ with λ₀ = λ′₀τ∞ (reading doubtful), the device that the margin of scan 116 uses to merge the two forms.

**R03 — scan 112 — conjecture (cancelled) — keep, corrected.** The cancelled guess (case B, c+d−b = −1, two parameters) is confirmed by the marginal "OK, cf. (99) ci-dessous": on scan 116, (99) gives for ξ = λ′l₀⁻¹ρ(λ′)⁻¹ the NB "C+D−B = −1" and then (100) pD = −1. The lower-case c+d−b of the cancelled sentence is retained.

**R04 — scan 112 — implication not pursued — keep.** The family ξ = (−u u; C −u′−C) has determinant u·u′ = 1 for every C, so it is a one-parameter family, in agreement with "2, 1 et 0 paramètres" of scan 111; whether it can occur under the full equation in ξ is the reservation "Sans pouvoir néanmoins exclure".

**R05 — scan 113 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Studying the equation (**) as symmetry of h₀ is what (*) and (92)–(93) do; see C01 for a slip in the matrix of (90)–(91), which does not touch (*).

**R06 — scan 114 — implication not pursued — keep, corrected.** The subgroup is now read Π′(2) (the Π(2)′ = Ker(Sl(2, Ẑ)′ → Sl(2, ℤ/2ℤ)) of scan 117). The exclusion of h₀ = −ᵗh₀ uses only that the image of Π̂₀,₃ is ≡ 1 (2), so D ≡ 1 (2) ≠ 0; the margin of scan 116 repeats the argument for case B.

**R07 — scan 115 — implication not pursued — keep.** p is recovered as 1/D after the sign of λ is fixed; the independence from other choices of λ (λ ↦ λβ with β in the centralizer) is not discussed on these pages.

**R08 — scan 115 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The identity c²+cd+d² = (c−dω)(c−dω²) for ω²+ω+1 = 0 is correct; it is the norm form of ℤ[ω], which explains the residues 0, 1 mod 3 found on the page.

**R09 — scan 116 — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The margin, now read, answers it: "Inutile maintenant de traiter ces cas séparément — puisqu'on a ξ = λρ(λ)⁻¹, λ = λ′τ∞, det λ = −1", with (98) D ≡ p ≡ δ (3). Grothendieck himself folds the second form into the first with det λ = −1, using τ∞ρ(τ∞)⁻¹ = l₁⁻¹ ((34) of scan 130). What remains unchecked on the page is that det τ∞ = −1 in Gl(2, Ẑ).

**R10 — scan 117 — claim without proof — keep.** In the second case the corrected value is D = −(c²+d²+cd) (replacing −(3c²+3cd+d²)): this is the merged parametrization of R09, with δ = −1.

**R11 — scan 118 — claim without proof — keep.** The reconstruction ξ ↦ p, γ, h₀, g₀, g, u uses (65), (66) [unique g₀ needs Π̂₀,₃^{σ∞} = 1, admitted on scan 101], (62) and (50); Remarque 2/ adds the sign proviso.

**R12 — scan 118 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** (104) shows that D is invariant under ξ ↦ l₀^{−α}ξl₁^α; that D is a complete invariant is not claimed.

**R13 — scan 119 — claim without proof — keep.** The surjectivity of χ: Π_ℚ → Ẑ* is quoted, not proved; the direct verification is reduced to the Corollaire.

**R14 — scan 119 — claim without proof — keep.** The local statement is correct as far as checked: for ℓ ≠ 3 every unit of ℤ_ℓ is a norm from ℤ_ℓ[ω]; for ℓ = 3 both forms take on units only the class ≡ 1 (3), resp. ≡ −1 (3). The margin "on s'en fiche de −(3(c²+cd)+d²) !" drops the second form, consistent with R09.

**R15 — scan 119 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The norm of d+cω with ω²−ω+1 = 0 is d²+cd+c²; this is the same norm form as in R08.

**R16 — scan 120 — question / acknowledged gap — keep.** "pas même d'en construire explicitement une seule, en dehors de celles qui correspondent au cas u = 1, et u = τ∞" is legible as transcribed.

**R17 — scan 120 — question — keep, corrected.** The alternative is now read ξ = λ(−l₀⁻¹)ρ(λ₀)⁻¹ (minus sign in the ink); the lifting question itself is unchanged, with λ or ξ for the lifted element still doubtful.

**R18 — scan 120 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Testing the lifting question in finite quotients is a reasonable first step; nothing on the page decides it.

**R19 — scan 121 — claim without proof — keep.** In Π̃ = Π·ℤ/nℤ one has (gρ)ⁿ = g ρ(g) ⋯ ρⁿ⁻¹(g) ρⁿ and ρⁿ = 1, which gives (6) ⟺ (8) at once.

**R20 — scan 121 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**

**R21 — scan 122 — implication not pursued — keep.** Scan 123 answers it with (13)–(15).

**R22 — scan 123 — question answered — keep.** "classe à droite modulo Π(ρ, g)" for the set αΠ(ρ, g) is the author's wording.

**R23 — scan 123 — claim without proof — keep.** Proof: if ρ′_{g′} = αρ′_gα⁻¹ with α ∈ Π, then Π^{ρ′_{g′}} = αΠ^{ρ′_g}α⁻¹ because Π is normal in Π̃.

**R24 — scan 124 — claim without proof — keep.** Conjugation by Π̃ preserves splittings because the quotient ℤ/nℤ is abelian.

**R25 — scan 124 — implication not pursued — keep.** "réduites aux classes de Π-conjugaison" (reading kept) follows from (17).

**R26 — scan 125 — claim without proof — keep.** With Pᵢ = gρ(g)⋯ρⁱ⁻¹(g) one has Pᵢ₊₁ = gρ(Pᵢ), hence ρⁱ(g) = Pᵢ⁻¹gρ(Pᵢ), which is (19) for i ∈ ℕ.

**R27 — scan 126 — claim without proof — keep.**

**R28 — scan 127 — claim without proof — keep, corrected.** The wording is "qu'on ne puisse la mettre sous la forme standard αρ(α)⁻¹ avec α ∈ Π". The criterion is immediate: g = αρ(α)⁻¹ with α ∈ ℰ_ρ means ρ′_g = αρα⁻¹, i.e. α̇[1]_ρ = [g]_ρ.

**R29 — scan 127 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**

**R30 — scan 128 — question answered — keep.** The kernel of ℰ^ρ → G^ρ is ℰ^ρ ∩ Π = Π^ρ, hence the injectivity under (29).

**R31 — scan 128 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**

**R32 — scan 129 — claim without proof — keep.** See C07 for the centralizer G^ρ and the action on the two poles.

**R33 — scan 129 — claim without proof — keep.** The cancelled justification "(conséquence immédiate de la relation Π^ρ = (1))" is indeed not enough, since τ lies in G^ρ: ℰ′^ρ = {1} also needs that no element α of the coset Π·τ∞ centralizes ρ. That follows from "τ les permute": such an α would give α̇[1]_ρ = [αρ(α)⁻¹]_ρ = [1]_ρ, whereas τ moves [1]_ρ to the other class.

**R34 — scan 129 — implication not pursued — keep.** See C06.

**R35 — scan 130 — claim without proof — keep.** The presentation by three involutions τ₀, τ₁, τ∞ "sans plus" is stated; l₀ = τ∞τ₁, l₁ = τ₀τ∞, l∞ = τ₁τ₀ as read.

**R36 — scan 130 — conjecture — keep.** (33) is explicitly "chose à vérifier ultérieurement"; the ink writes Π̃^∧₀,₃ = Π̃₀,₃·{1, τ∞} (right side with tilde, no hat).

**R37 — scan 130 — conjecture — keep.** The added hypothesis concerns 𝔖̂₀,₃ and 𝔖₀,₃ (letter now fixed as 𝔖).

**R38 — scan 130 — claim without proof — keep, corrected.** The text says "se met sous la forme standard h = βσ∞(β)⁻¹ … et ceci de façon unique"; see C08 for (37).

**R39 — scan 130 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**

## Checker's notes (second pass)

**C01 — scan 113 — claim without proof (slip in the ink) — checker's note.** By the line above it, [σ∞, g⁻¹] = (σ∞g⁻¹σ∞⁻¹)g = ᵗg·g = (a²+c², ab+cd; ab+cd, b²+d²), but the matrix written in (90) is (a²+b², ac+bd; ac+bd, c²+d²) = g·ᵗg, and (91) is its inverse. What follows, (*) h₀σ∞(h₀) = h₀·ᵗ(h₀)⁻¹ = 1 ⟺ h₀ = ᵗh₀ and (92)–(93), does not use these entries, so the slip has no consequence on these pages.

**C02 — scans 114–117 — implication not pursued — checker's note.** (93) C−B = 2γD together with C−B = 1−D (case 1, from (78), the "1−D" under C−B) and C−B = −1−D (case 2, from (99)) gives (96) pD = 1 and (100) pD = −1. With the merged parametrization of the margin of scan 116 both read pD = 1, D = δ(c²+d²+cd), δ = det λ, and (98) p ≡ δ (3) proves the announcement (59) of scan 99: the two classes of (58) correspond to p ≡ 1 and p ≡ −1 (3).

**C03 — scan 116 — claim checked — checker's note.** With C = −(3c²+3cd+d²)+3ac+ad+2bc+bd as now read, (99) gives C+D−B = −(ad−bc) = −det λ′, i.e. the marginal "C+D−B = −1" for λ′ ∈ Sl(2, Ẑ), and B−C = −1+D as written below; this is the computation that the cancelled guess of scan 112 anticipated.

**C04 — scan 126 — claim without proof (slip in the ink) — checker's note.** "(donc ρ′ ∈ ℰ̃)": an element of ℰ over ρ̇ lies in the inverse image of ℤ/nℤ, which the page has just called Π̃; the tilde over the letter of ℰ is presumably meant for Π̃. The equivalence "int(α)(ρ′) reste au-dessus de ρ̇ ⟺ α̇ centralise ρ̇" is immediate from the image α̇ρ̇α̇⁻¹ in G.

**C05 — scan 127 — claim without proof (slip in the ink) — checker's note.** The first description of Centr_G(ρ) is written {g ∈ G | ρgg⁻¹ = g}; the intended condition is ρgρ⁻¹ = g, as in the second description {g ∈ G | [ρ, g] = 1}.

**C06 — scans 129–130 — implication not pursued — checker's note.** With ℰ′ = Π·{1, τ∞} and ℰ′^ρ = {1} (31), α ↦ αρ(α)⁻¹ is a bijection from ℰ′ onto the solutions of (32) in Π₀,₃; the coset Π·τ∞ gives the second class because τ∞ρ(τ∞)⁻¹ = l₁⁻¹ (34). This is the structural reason for the two parametric forms of (58) and of scans 111–116 (λ₀ρ(λ₀)⁻¹ and λ′₀l₁⁻¹ρ(λ′₀)⁻¹), and it explains the margin of scan 99 ("Ces représentations paramétriques sont univoques") under Π^ρ = 1.

**C07 — scan 129 — claim checked — checker's note.** In G = 𝒯₀,₃ ≃ 𝔖₃ × ℤ/2ℤ the centralizer of the 3-cycle ρ̇ is A₃ × ℤ/2ℤ ≃ ℤ/3ℤ × ℤ/2ℤ, as written; ρ̇ fixes the two poles −j̄, −j and τ (complex conjugation) exchanges them, so the order-2 factor acts simply transitively on the two classes, as stated.

**C08 — scan 130 — claim checked — checker's note.** If σ̇∞ is the involution of the projective line fixing ∞ and exchanging 0 and 1, it is z ↦ 1−z, with fixed points ½ and ∞, of which only ½ lies in U₀,₃; this agrees with the reading {½} of (37). The uniqueness in (36) needs Π^{σ∞} = 1, which scan 101 admits provisionally for the profinite group.


# ===== LM_C3-131-149 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-131-149 (scans 131–149): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (R01–R46 and the three combined proposals) is checked against `LM_C3-131-149_checked.tex` and
kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Notes added in the second pass are labelled S01–S08 ("second pass,
own"). Kinds: conjecture/question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, own idea. The cocycle letter is
now read ξ throughout (it was ζ|ξ in the first pass). Conventions used in the checks: ρ(x) = ρxρ⁻¹,
σ∞(x) = σ∞xσ∞⁻¹, ε₀ = σ∞ρ, ε₁ = ρσ∞, ℓ₀ = ε₀², ℓ₁ = σ∞(ℓ₀) = ρ(ℓ₀) (scan 143).

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 131 — claim without proof (assumption) — KEEP, sharpened.** "Nous admettrons encore que ces faits
s'étendent aux éléments de Π̂₀,₃": the profinite statement is assumed, not proved. Display (38) contains only
"Π̂₀,₃." — the intended Π̂₀,₃^{σ∞} = {1}, the analogue of the display above, is not written out. See S01 for where
this assumption is used later.

**R02 — scan 131 — claim without proof — KEEP, corrected.** With (40) now read 𝒢₀,₃^{σ∞} ≃ Z/2Z × Z/2Z (the same
glyph as in (39)), "le groupe (40) d'ordre (4)" is a Klein group generated by σ∞, τ∞, and the indeterminacy
β = β₀σ∞^ε τ∞^{ε′} (41) is consistent with it. The fixed-group computation itself is not given here.

**R03 — scan 132 — claim without proof — KEEP.** "Pour qu'on ait β ∈ 𝒢⁺₀,₃, il f. et s. que ε′ = 0": σ∞ lies in
𝒢⁺₀,₃ and τ∞ does not, which is the one-line check behind it; the page does not write it.

**R04 — scan 133 — claim without proof (deferred) — KEEP.** The "condition liminaire" that (3) are generating
relations of 𝒢̂⁺₀,₃ is left open by the author.

**R05 — scan 133 — claim without proof — KEEP.** If 𝒢̂⁺₀,₃ is the profinite group with generating relations
σ∞² = ρ³ = 1 (as stated), any pair hσ∞, gρ of orders dividing 2 and 3 defines an endomorphism; that it is an
automorphism, and that it induces the identity on 𝒯⁺₀,₃, are separate points not proved here.

**R06 — scan 133 — own idea — KEEP.** Testing pairs (h, g) in finite quotients for both the norm equations (2) and
generation of the image remains a sensible check.

**R07 — scan 134 — claim without proof — KEEP.** "c'est une chose remarquable que la connaissance des éléments g, h
… implique déjà la connaissance de u": it follows from u(σ∞) = hσ∞, u(ρ) = gρ and the generation of 𝒢⁺₀,₃ by σ∞, ρ
(the parenthesis says so), but the kernel of u ↦ (int g, int h) is not discussed.

**R08 — scan 134 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** u(ℓᵢ) = int(gᵢ)ℓᵢ^p for i ∈ {0, 1, ∞} with one multiplier; the
three conjugating elements are tied together by (8) on scan 136 (f₁ = ρ(f₀)g⁻¹, f∞ = ρ²(f₀)ρ(g)⁻¹g⁻¹), so they are
not independent parameters.

**R09 — scan 135 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** The change f₀ ↦ f₀⁻¹ is made "pour obtenir … des formules où la
symétrie des rôles joués par ρ et par σ∞ apparaisse plus nettement", matching h₀ = g₀σ∞(g₀)⁻¹ with ξ = λρ(λ)⁻¹
(the first pass had "g|ζ" for ξ here).

**R10 — scan 135 — claim without proof — KEEP, completed.** (6) follows from (5) by squaring, since ℓ₀ = ε₀². The
reference "(§ 43, formule (65,)" points at the definition of h₀ in terms of g₀; see S05.

**R11 — scan 136 — claim without proof — CORRECTED: verified.** With ε₁ = ρε₀ρ⁻¹ one gets
u(ε₁) = gρ·f₀⁻¹ε₀^μ f₀·ρ⁻¹g⁻¹ = int(gρ(f₀)⁻¹)(ε₁^μ), so f₁ = ρ(f₀)g⁻¹, and likewise f∞ = ρ(f₁)g⁻¹; (7)–(8) are right
as written.

**R12 — scan 136 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** μ − 1 = 2ν has a unique solution ν ∈ Ẑ because μ ∈ Ẑ* is odd
in the 2-adic factor and Ẑ is torsion-free; scan 141 records the converse condition 2ν + 1 ∈ Ẑ*. The parameter ν is
the γ of § 43 (corrected reading "désigné par γ"; see S06).

**R13 — scan 136 — own idea — CORRECTED.** The ink settles the last displays: fhσ∞(gρ(f)⁻¹) = ℓ₀^ν, with the inverse
outside ρ(f). The step (9)–(11) has been checked (S02), so the proposed finite-quotient check of the rearrangement is
no longer needed; the right-hand bracket of (9) still lacks its outer parenthesis in the ink.

**R14 — scan 137 — claim without proof — KEEP.** "f déterminé mod L₀ seulement f ↦ ℓ₀^n f": the margin itself states
the left-coset ambiguity; determination of the class, not of the representative.

**R15 — scan 137 — claim without proof — KEEP, completed.** "se résolvent paramétriquement de façon unique": the
uniqueness of β in h = βσ∞(β)⁻¹ is exactly the assumption Π̂₀,₃^{σ∞} = {1} of scan 131; for α ∈ Π̃̂₀,₃ the
analogue needs Π̃̂₀,₃^ρ = {1}, not stated here. Existence (vanishing of the 1-cohomology of the cyclic groups) refers
to § 43. See S01.

**R16 — scan 138 — claim without proof — KEEP.** "ν est déterminé de façon unique par α, β": not argued on the page;
the invariance of (15) under f ↦ ℓ₀^n f only handles the representative of f.

**R17 — scans 138–139 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** The dissymmetry (β always in Π̂₀,₃, α only when
ν ≡ 0 (3), i.e. μ ≡ 1 (3)) decides whether u is Π̂₀,₃-conjugate to an automorphism commuting with σ∞ or with ρ.

**R18 — scan 139 — claim without proof (announced) — CORRECTED.** The margin now reads "On verra que μ est déterminé
déjà par f, du moins modulo signe, cf plus bas" (f, not h). Remarque 1 of scans 142–143 is the "plus bas": changing
f by τ∞ changes μ into −μ, which accounts for "modulo signe".

**R19 — scan 140 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** For μ = 1 the compatibility (14) says φ = ξ, so one element must
satisfy both φσ∞(φ) = 1 and ξρ(ξ)ρ²(ξ) = 1; the pages do not classify such elements.

**R20 — scan 140 — claim without proof (announced) — KEEP, reading updated.** The margin reads "On trouve[ra] que
tant ξ que φ déterminent déjà μ, donc ν" ("tant" is legible). No argument on these pages.

**R21 — scan 140 — own idea — CORRECTED.** The proof obligation it names (uniqueness of f from φ = fσ∞(f)⁻¹ needs
the σ∞-fixed subgroup to be trivial) is met by the assumption Π̂₀,₃^{σ∞} = {1} made on scan 131; the note should
point there rather than leave it open. See S01.

**R22 — scans 140–141 — own idea — KEEP.** The comparison of the two normalisations (ξ = ℓ₀^νφ for h = 1,
η = ℓ₀^{−ν}ψ for g = 1), with the opposite signs of the exponent, stays a reasonable reformulation to try.

**R23 — scan 141 — textual observation — KEEP, sharpened.** (18) reads "u(ρ) = 1" followed by a small struck mark;
with g = 1, (1) gives u(ρ) = gρ = ρ, so the written 1 is presumably a slip (the struck mark may be a ρ). The text is
kept; the first pass's stray "18)" inside the formula was a LaTeX error and is fixed.

**R24 — scan 142 — claim without proof — KEEP.** The "famille principale" condition (f ∈ Π̂₀,₃, not only in
Π̃̂₀,₃) is extra to the two equations (24); the correspondence needs it.

**R25 — scan 142 — implication not pursued — CORRECTED.** The last display reads int(f₀⁻¹)(τ∞(ℓ₀^μ)) = int(f₀⁻¹)(ℓ₀^{−μ}):
τ∞ acts on ℓ₀^μ and inverts ℓ₀, which gives the multiplier −μ of scan 143. With f = τ∞f₀ the computation is
consistent; the non-principal component carries the opposite multiplier (see S07).

**R26 — scan 143 — claim without proof — KEEP.** The invariance of (16), (24), (14), (23) under f ↦ ℓ₀^n f checks
directly from (26), since ℓ₁ = σ∞(ℓ₀) = ρ(ℓ₀) and ℓ₀ commutes with ℓ₀^ν. The exponent "ℓ₀^{−n}|ℓ₀^n" in the prose is
still unresolved in the ink; with the twisted conjugation x ↦ a·x·ρ(a)⁻¹, (26) is the transform by ℓ₀^n.

**R27 — scan 143 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** (27)–(28) express σ∞(h)g as a ρ-twisted conjugate of ξ and as a
σ∞-twisted conjugate of η.

**R28 — scan 144 — own idea — KEEP.** The author's own explanation of the extra η⁻¹ in (32) (ℓ₀ is the square of
σ∞ρ = ε₀, not of ρσ∞ = ε₁) suggests the check proposed.

**R29 — scan 145 — claim without proof — KEEP.** α, β, α₀, β₀, f are matrices "modulo signe" while g, h, ξ, η, φ, ψ are
"sans ambiguïté de signe": each is a twisted quotient x·τ(x)⁻¹ in which the central sign of x cancels; this is
