immediate but not written.

**R30 — scan 145 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** "Dans le choix des représentants" (continued on scan 146) reopens
the lift of ε₀ and ℓ₀.

**R31 — scan 146 — conjecture/question — KEEP.** "sont elles vraies sans signe correcteur ??" is open; scans 147–148
reduce it to gρ(g)ρ²(g) = 1 in Sl(2, Ẑ) and leave it "en suspens".

**R32 — scan 146 — implication not pursued — KEEP, reading updated.** The margin prefers ε₀ = +(1 −1; 0 1): "on aime
voir un élément unip. … engendrant un ss-groupe à 1 paramètre". The word is abbreviated "unip." and followed by a
struck word.

**R33 — scan 146 — textual observation — KEEP, completed.** (42), the first line of (43) and (44) write α₀ρ(α₀)
without the inverse, while (36) and the definition in (45) (scan 148) have α₀ρ(α₀)⁻¹: the absence on scans 146–147
is a slip of the author; the transcription keeps it.

**R34 — scan 147 — conjecture/question — KEEP.** "la question qui se pose est si on a tjrs ε = +1".

**R35 — scan 147 — claim without proof — CORRECTED: verified.** If g′ = αgρ(α)⁻¹, then
g′ρ(g′)ρ²(g′) = α·gρ(g)ρ²(g)·ρ³(α)⁻¹ = α(ε₀·1)α⁻¹ = ε₀ because ρ³ = −1 is central; so the scalar is transferred and
ε₀ = ε follows as written (S03).

**R36 — scan 147 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** NB: ρ³ = −1 in Sl(2, Ẑ) while the inner automorphism "défini
par ρ" has order 3 (corrected reading: a struck "qu'il" before "défini|définit").

**R37 — scan 147 — own idea — KEEP.** Testing the sign ε in finite congruence quotients of Sl(2, Ẑ) under the exact
hypotheses remains a sensible experiment, not a proof.

**R38 — scan 148 — conjecture/question — KEEP.** The equivalence with gρ(g)ρ²(g) = 1 is stated and the question "nous
laissons en suspens pour le moment".

**R39 — scan 148 — claim without proof — CORRECTED.** (45) reads "d'où ξ = εℓ₀^νφ, ε ∈ {±1}" (after a struck ℓ₀^ν);
the first pass had doubled εℓ₀^ν. The summary records the sign in that relation, in gρ(g)ρ²(g) = ε·1 and in
g = ε·αρ(α)⁻¹ with α = (ℓ₀^νf)⁻¹α₀.

**R40 — scan 148 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** The margin proposes to define g = αρ(α)⁻¹ (so that
gρ(g)ρ²(g) = 1) and to put the sign ε = ε₂ into the loop formula for g; its last clause stays partly illegible.

**R41 — scan 148 — own idea — KEEP.** (46), the symmetric case g = 1, ends at "=" in the ink; the comparison proposed
stays to be done.

**R42 — scan 149 — claim without proof — KEEP.** In the "arithmétique" case α₀, f ≡ 1 (2): a, d, r, u ≡ 1,
b, c, s, t ≡ 0 (2) — this needs the images of Π̂₀,₃ and Π̃̂₀,₃ in Gl(2, Ẑ) to lie in the congruence subgroup of level 2,
not shown here.

**R43 — scan 149 — claim without proof — KEEP.** "changement de signes dans α₀, f est inessentiel": ξ and φ do not
change (R29); the sign ε in (56) is then well defined.

**R44 — scan 149 — implication not pursued — KEEP, reading updated.** The margin names (56) the "relation des lacets"
(plural); the eight-variable coordinate form with the parity and determinant conditions is the next step.

**R45 — scan 149 — claim without proof — CORRECTED.** From (52), δ(C + D − B) = ad − bc; so "C + D ∓ B = 1" (54) holds
with "−" exactly when ad − bc = δ. The ink of the sign stays unresolved; the algebra is recorded here, not used to
choose the reading (S04).

**R46 — scan 149 — own idea — KEEP, merged with S04.** (48) reads clearly "ab − cd = δ", whereas the determinant of
α₀ = (a b; c d) is ad − bc and (54) needs ad − bc = δ; the written (48) is a slip of the author.

**Combined proposal 1 — scans 137–144 — own idea — KEEP.** Defining the two twisted-conjugation equivalences and
asking whether the compatibility relation describes common orbits remains open.

**Combined proposal 2 — scans 145–147 — own idea — KEEP.** Which central subgroup "/±1" means, and whether independent
signs modulo coprime levels occur among the lifts, is worth checking before trusting one global sign ε.

**Combined proposal 3 — scans 148–149 — own idea — CORRECTED.** The doubtful sign of (54) and the written (48) are now
related by S04; the separate symbolic check of the formulas as written still makes sense.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S01 — scans 131, 137, 140 — implication not pursued (second pass, own).** The admitted fact Π̂₀,₃^{σ∞} = {1}
(scan 131, (38)) is what makes β unique in h = βσ∞(β)⁻¹ (137, (13)–(14)) and f unique in φ = fσ∞(f)⁻¹ (140, "déterminé
de façon unique en termes de φ par (12)"): two solutions differ by a σ∞-fixed element. The ρ-side statement, uniqueness
of α in g = αρ(α)⁻¹, needs Π̃̂₀,₃^ρ = {1}, which is not stated in these scans.

**S02 — scan 136 — claim checked (second pass, own).** int(f)u(ε₀) = fhσ∞gρf⁻¹ = fhσ∞(g)·ε₀f⁻¹ε₀⁻¹·ε₀ =
[fhσ∞(gρ(f)⁻¹)]ε₀, so (8bis) gives fhσ∞(gρ(f)⁻¹) = ε₀^{μ−1} = ℓ₀^ν, and applying σ∞ gives (11):
σ∞(h)g = σ∞(f)⁻¹ℓ₁^νρ(f). The page's computation is correct.

**S03 — scan 147 — claim checked (second pass, own).** The transfer g′ = αgρ(α)⁻¹ ⇒ g′ρ(g′)ρ²(g′) = ε₀ holds
because ρ³ = −1 is central in Gl(2, Ẑ) (R35); hence ε₀ = ε. The open question is therefore exactly whether
gρ(g)ρ²(g) = +1 for g = σ∞(f)⁻¹ℓ₁^νρ(f) computed in Sl(2, Ẑ).

**S04 — scan 149 — implication not pursued (second pass, own).** δ(C + D − B) = ad − bc and δ(A + B) = a² + b² + ab
follow from (52). With det α₀ = ad − bc = δ, (54) is C + D − B = 1; the written (48) "ab − cd" cannot give (54) and is
a slip. A check of (52) itself from ξ = α₀ρ(α₀)⁻¹ needs the matrix of ρ fixed in § 42 (formula (78)), not on these
scans.

**S05 — scan 135 — claim without proof (reference) (second pass, own).** "(§ 43, formule (65,)" is cited for
h₀ = g₀⁻¹σ∞(g₀); in the checked § 43 (scan 113) that formula is (66), while (65) is h₀ = l₀^{−γ}ξ. The reference may be
off by one; the text keeps it as written.

**S06 — scans 136, 139, 140 — implication not pursued (second pass, own).** "on note donc ν ce qui était désigné par
γ précédemment": § 43's h₀ = l₀^{−γ}ξ becomes φ = ℓ₀^{−ν}ξ here (14), and the margin of scan 139 calls φ "essentiellement
l'élément noté h₀ précédemment". The renaming γ → ν and h₀ → φ is consistent across the two sections.

**S07 — scans 139, 142–143 — implication not pursued (second pass, own).** The two cosets of Π̂₀,₃ in
Π̃̂₀,₃ = Π̂₀,₃·{1, τ∞} correspond to the multipliers μ and −μ (Remarque 1); this explains the "du moins modulo signe"
of the margin of scan 139 and suggests treating the principal family as the choice of sign of μ.

**S08 — scan 141 — conjecture/question (second pass, own).** The written u(ρ) = 1 in (18), against u(ρ) = gρ = ρ for
g = 1, should be compared with the scans after 149 (§ 46 recapitulates these formulas) before it is called a slip.


# ===== LM_C3-150-168 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-150-168 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

Source: Grothendieck, *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* (1981), Montpellier archive cote 140-3, scans 150–168 (§44, ms. pp. 365–382). References are to `LM_C3-150-168_checked.tex`. Part A takes the first pass's notes in order (F1–F50) with a verdict; Part B adds notes written in the second pass (S1–S8).

## A. First-pass notes, checked

### Scans 150–154

- **F1. Scan 151 — unproved claim / expectation.** Keep. "On doit pouvoir vérifier que le schéma (sur Spec ℤ) des solutions de ces équations est de dim. relative 3 …", corrected in the margin to "non, il est de dim. rel totale 4"; a proof needs the exact equation set, the base and a fibre analysis. The corrected reading "de ces équations" does not change the note; S2 gives a likely account of the marginal 4.
- **F2. Scan 151 — unproved claim.** Keep, now checked (S1). The NB "cette dernière est conséquence des autres, compte tenu de C+D−B=1 et AD−BC=1" holds over any coefficient ring once μU=ε (equivalently μD=1 with D=εU) and RU−T²=1 are used; invertibility enters through U=ε/μ.
- **F3. Scan 152 — expository plan (first pass: conjecture / expectation).** Correct. The margin reads "en d'autres termes, on a μ≡ε (4) car U=t²+u²≡1 (4) … Ce serait le moment d'inclure [aussi|ici] la relation μ≡δ (3), cf. avec la démonstration idoine…": a restatement of the mod-4 result and a plan to insert the mod-3 relation with its proof, which the main text then gives ("On avait vu de façon toute analogue … que μ≡δ (3)"). Not a conjecture.
- **F4. Scan 152, (63) — own idea (first pass).** Keep. "Égal à 1 sss μ_p ∈ (ℤ_p*)²" is right at p=3 (μ≡1 mod 3) but not at p=2: the 2-adic unit squares are 1+8ℤ₂, whereas ε₂ is the mod-4 character with kernel 1+4ℤ₂ (μ=5 separates them). The transcription keeps the written criterion.
- **F5. Scan 152 — unpursued implication.** Keep. The modulus in "μ mod 3" is a 3 written over a 2 (doubt mark kept); with ε₃ ↔ μ mod 3, ε₂ ↔ μ mod 4, the margin's (ℤ/12ℤ)* ≃ (ℤ/4ℤ)*×(ℤ/3ℤ)* only re-expresses the pair of signs and imposes no further condition on μ.
- **F6. Scan 153, (65) — unpursued implication.** Keep, with an addition: the invariance is made explicit on scan 159 by (79), ℓ₀ⁿ leaving the lower row (t,u) unchanged, so g in (65) depends only on (t,u) and μ.
- **F7. Scan 153, left margin — unproved claim.** Keep, with the corrected reading: the note ends "alors que f etc sont [?] choix d'ambiguïtés f↦l₀ⁿf etc…", i.e. f is defined only up to f↦ℓ₀ⁿf, while α is claimed determined up to sign by the automorphism. The claim is not proved at this place.
- **F8. Scan 154, (68 bis)–(69) — unpursued implication.** Keep. The remaining freedom is u=αu₀ with u₀ in the centraliser of ρ, then cut down by (66), (67).
- **F9. Scan 154 — unproved claim recalled from earlier.** Keep. "Nous avons déjà remarqué que (67), (68) déterminent u seulement modulo … un scalaire λ∈ℤ̂*" refers to earlier pages outside this package.
- **F10. Scan 154 — expectation.** Keep. "On espère néanmoins tomber sur un choix canonique" is answered on scan 159 ("on va définir canoniquement, modulo signe, … la matrice u par la formule (75)") and questioned again at the end of scan 168.
- **F11. Scan 154, (70) — own idea (first pass).** Keep, verified: ρ=(0 1; −1 1) is cyclic (e₁, ρe₁ form a basis), so its centraliser in M₂(A) over any commutative ring A is A[ρ] = {p+qρ} = {(p q; −q p+q)}, and det(p+qρ)=p²+pq+q² as in (71).

### Scans 155–159

- **F12. Scan 155, after (73) — Grothendieck's conjecture.** Keep, with the reading corrected to "sss p≡1 (4)": he suspected that ε′=+1 exactly when the multiplier is ≡1 mod 4, after a first universal answer proved incompatible with class field theory. The Théorème on scan 158 confirms it in the form α₂≡0 (2) sss μ≡1 (4); see S5 on the letter p.
- **F13. Scan 155, margin "(⟺ sans doute, ε₂=1)" — conjecture.** Keep; settled on scans 156–158, where the commuting case is exactly ε₂=1.
- **F14. Scan 155, (71)–(73) — own idea (first pass).** Keep. (73) gives u₀=μ(cρ+d) directly, and (71) with p=d, q=c gives det(cρ+d)=c²+cd+d², the norm form of ℤ̂[ρ].
- **F15. Scan 156, margin "On pourrait aussi appliquer les formules §43 (78)" — unpursued implication.** Keep; §43 lies outside this package.
- **F16. Scan 156, final paragraph — conjecture.** Keep. The guess "au pif" for ε₂=−1 is tested and repaired on scan 157; S6 records the change of τ∞ made on the way.
- **F17. Scan 157 — unpursued implication.** Keep. The trial with the reflection on the left gives "2μut=0 i.e. ut=0 — décidément, ça déconne !", and placing it on the right works; both stages should be kept.
- **F18. Scan 158, (77) — own idea (first pass).** Correct. The minus sign itself is doubtful in the ink (a minute mark, now `\unsure{-|\cdot}`). From the "i.e." clause u(σ∞)=ε₂σ∞ one gets [u,σ∞]=uσ∞u⁻¹σ∞⁻¹=ε₂·id, and for ε₂=−1 the right factor τ=diag(1,−1) does give τσ∞τ⁻¹=−σ∞ (σ∞=(0 1; −1 0)); so (77) should read +ε₂·id.
- **F19. Scan 158, Corollaire — unproved claim.** Keep, with the group read as Ŝ⁺₀,₃ (script S, quotient 𝔖₃ ≃ Ŝ⁺₀,₃/Π̂₀,₃) rather than Ĝ/Γ̂. Descent to Sl(2,ℤ̂)′ and the identification with conjugation by a matrix of determinant μ are asserted, not proved, here; the margin on scan 159 says it has been done "pour le moment" only for u commuting with σ∞.
- **F20. Scan 159, NB in the margin — unproved claim.** Keep. The reduction to u commuting with σ∞ "quitte à conjuguer avec un autom. intérieur" is stated without the conjugating element.
- **F21. Scan 159, second margin — unpursued implication.** Keep: "On a fait ceci, pour le moment, pour u commutant à σ∞" limits the scope of the computations of scans 155–158.
- **F22. Scan 159, "modulo signe" and (79) — unpursued implication.** Keep. The canonical definition of u rests on (t,u) being determined mod sign; the remaining ambiguity is the simultaneous sign.
- **F23. Scan 159 — own idea (first pass).** Keep. The comparison of tσ∞+u with cρ+d is what scans 160–161 carry out, ending with (84) f(tσ∞+u)τ∞^{α₂}=δℓ₀^{−ν}α₀(cρ+d).

### Scans 160–164

- **F24. Scan 160, (80) — question.** Keep, with an addition: the inserted "sauf erreur" before "on doit avoir une relation" and the "?" over the equality mark the same provisional status; the margin points to the correcting factor δ=ε₃ of (82).
- **F25. Scan 161, (83)–(84) — unpursued implication.** Keep, with the reading corrected: "et sous cette forme la formule était en fait connue, comme le contenu essentiel de la relation des lacets sous forme matricielle (56) ou (57): ε₂U=D, ε₂T=C !" (*contenu* now read without doubt).
- **F26. Scans 161–162, Remarque — expectation.** Correct the quoted wording: "sous une fausse apparence d'arithmétique, on a fait par la bande des gammes d'algèbre linéaire, valables sur des anneaux quelconques et formulables sans doute sous des conditions beaucoup plus générales". The substance of the note (state the base ring, determinant and invertibility hypotheses before generalising) stands.
- **F27. Scan 162, final paragraph — expectation.** Keep, with the reading "Modulo des ajustements agréables, toutes les formules du présent § devraient pouvoir se développer dans ce contexte plus général (ainsi que des compléments substantiels)", followed by the hesitation "mais je ne sais si je vais expliciter ici…".
- **F28. Scan 162, (*) — own idea (first pass).** Keep; S8 checks that (*) is the matrix form of (56).
- **F29. Scan 163 — unproved claim.** Keep. "Schéma relatif lisse de dim relative 4" for the solutions of (*) is stated without proof; it agrees with the marginal correction "dim. rel totale 4" on scan 151 (S2).
- **F30. Scan 163 — own idea (first pass).** Keep: the Jacobians of t²+u² and c²+cd+d² degenerate in characteristics 2 and 3 respectively, so smoothness must be checked there.
- **F31. Scan 163, Proposition and (85) — proof obligation.** Keep. Scans 164–166 supply the construction (choice of r,s by (87), then a,b by (88)), and F42 below shows the solution set is a ℤ̂-torsor.
- **F32. Scan 163 — own idea (first pass).** Keep. The ink indeed has "(−ε₂,−ε₃,−ν)" against the inserted triple (ε₂,ε₃,2ν+1); μ↦−μ corresponds to ν↦−ν−1.
- **F33. Scan 163, (86) — own idea (first pass).** Keep, ink confirmed: the last term of (86) is u² (no ascender, same shape as in t²+u²), a slip for d²; (85), (96) and scan 165 have d².
- **F34. Scan 164 — unproved claim.** Keep, ink confirmed: the parenthesis reads "(car r²+s² ∈ ℤ̂* — donc ∀p, r_p et s_p ne sont pas tous deux dans pℤ_p)", whereas the existence of r,s with ur−ts=1 needs t²+u² ∈ ℤ̂*, i.e. (t,u) unimodular.
- **F35. Scan 164, (87) — own idea (first pass).** Keep, verified: if ur₀−ts₀=1 and ur−ts=1, then u(r−r₀)=t(s−s₀), and choosing α,β with αt+βu=1 gives (r−r₀, s−s₀)=λ(t,u) with λ=α(r−r₀)+β(s−s₀). So (r,s)=(r₀+λt, s₀+λu) gives all solutions; equal signs in the overwritten (87) only reparametrise λ.
- **F36. Scan 164, cancelled block — unpursued implication.** Keep. The cancelled route (D, C, B, then A=(1+BC)/D and the upper-left relation of (57)) is replaced on scan 165 by solving directly for a,b.

### Scan 165

- **F37. After (88) — unproved claim.** Keep, verified: the coefficient matrix of (88) in (a,b) is (d −c; c+d d), with determinant d²+c(c+d)=c²+cd+d²=1/(με₃), a unit, so the solution is unique.
- **F38. Middle — unpursued implication.** Keep. The ink is "μ≡ε₂ (2)"; modulo 2 cannot determine ε₂, and (87) on scan 163 has the correct "(4)".
- **F39. Lower paragraph — conjecture.** Keep: "les systèmes (t,u) comme (c,d) 'dépendent de 1 paramètre', chacun en un sens très concret qu'on pourrait sans doute préciser si on y tenait".

### Scan 166

- **F40. (89) — unproved claim.** Keep; F42 gives the proof.
- **F41. Remarque — unpursued implication.** Keep: the congruence conditions mod 2 are only begun here and on scan 167.
- **F42. (89) — own idea (first pass).** Keep, carried out: the homogeneous system forces (a,b)=κ(c,d), (r,s)=λ(t,u), and the third equation gives (ε₃/μ)(κ−λ)=0 since c²+cd+d²=ε₃/μ, t²+u²=ε₂/μ. So the kernel is ℤ̂·(c,d,t,u), and the solutions of (89) form a ℤ̂-torsor; the action of scan 168, (α₀,f)↦(ℓ₀ⁿα₀,ℓ₀ⁿf), is this action with λ=−2n.

### Scan 167

- **F43. (90) — unpursued implication.** Keep.
- **F44. Torsor paragraph — unproved claim.** Keep, now supported by F42: since d is odd, moving λ by 1 changes the parity of b, so the conditions (92) fix λ mod 2 and the lifts form a torsor under 2ℤ̂ ≃ ℤ̂, as stated ("Aucun mystère !").
- **F45. Own idea (first pass).** Keep.

### Scan 168

- **F46. Action formula — unproved claim.** Keep; verified by F42 (the action is λ=−2n on the ℤ̂-torsor of solutions of (89)). The reference now reads "(cf (87) et proposition qui va avec)".
- **F47. (95)–(97) — unpursued implication.** Keep.
- **F48. (96) — own idea (first pass).** Keep, ink confirmed: "ε₂(t²+u²)=ε₂(c²+cd+d²)=1/μ"; by (86) the second coefficient should be ε₃.
- **F49. Last paragraph — deferred question.** Correct: the extract ends "mais il me semble peu intéressant" (inserted, underlined *peu*), not "peu rationnel". Grothendieck recalls a natural normalisation of f in Π̂₀,₃ (§41, (24bis)), promises to return to it, and the sentence continues beyond scan 168.
- **F50. Own idea (first pass).** Keep.

## B. Notes added in the second pass (mine)

- **S1. Scan 151, NB beside (61) — claim checked (mine).** With C=εT, D=εU, B=C+D−1, AD−BC=1, RU−T²=1 (det f·ᵗf=1, S=T) and μU=ε: A=(1+BC)/D=ε(1+T²+TU−εT)/U=ε(R+T−εT/U)=ε(R−(μ−1)T). The fourth relation of (61) is therefore a consequence of the other three.
- **S2. Scan 151, margin "non, il est de dim. rel totale 4" — own idea (mine), confidence medium.** By S1 the system (48), (49), (61) has at most five independent equations in the nine variables a,b,c,d,r,s,t,u,μ, which leaves relative dimension 4 if they cut out a complete intersection. This would explain why Grothendieck corrected "3" to "4", and it agrees with "schéma relatif lisse de dim relative 4" on scan 163.
- **S3. Scan 151, (62) — claim checked (mine).** With ρ=(0 1; −1 1) and det α₀=δ, [α₀,ρ]=δ(a²+ab+b²−(ac+bc+bd), ac+bc+bd; ac+bd+ad−(c²+cd+d²), c²+cd+d²). This gives the four lines of (62), with +ab in the last line, and C+D−B=δ(ad−bc)=1.
- **S4. Scans 156–158, τ∞ — own idea (mine).** τ∞ is defined on scan 156 as diag(−1,1), but scans 157–158 compute with diag(1,−1) ((u t; t −u) in u·ᵗu, and diag(1,ε₂) in (75)). Since diag(−1,1)=−diag(1,−1), the switch changes u only by −1, inside the "modulo signe" of (75), and leaves (76)–(78) unchanged.
- **S5. Scan 155, "sss p≡1 (4)" — implication not pursued (mine).** The ink has p, but the condition that the later pages establish is on the multiplier (Théorème of scan 158: α₂≡0 (2) sss μ≡1 (4)); p is probably a hasty μ. The transcription keeps p.
- **S6. Scans 160–161, "si ε₂=+1" labels — claim checked (mine).** The two equalities so labelled (scan 160 bottom, scan 161 top) hold for both signs: −1+ε₂μu²=−ε₂μt² and 1−ε₂μt²=ε₂μu² are each equivalent to μ(t²+u²)=ε₂. This justifies Grothendieck's deletion of "si ε₂=1" after (82) and in (83); the surviving labels are leftovers.
- **S7. Scan 166 — implication not pursued (mine).** "Ce qui détermine ensuite r,s de façon unique" repeats r,s, which were just chosen with the parameter λ; by (88) it is a,b that are then determined uniquely. Probably a slip for a,b (the ink has r,s).
- **S8. Scan 162, (*) — claim checked (mine).** With ℰ₀(λ)=ε₀^λ=(1 −λ; 0 1), ℰ₀(μ−1)=(1 −2ν; 0 1)=ℓ₀^ν by (79). So (*) [α₀,ρ]=ε·ℰ₀(μ−1)[f,σ] with σ=σ∞ is exactly (56) ξ=εℓ₀^νφ, with ξ=α₀ρ(α₀)⁻¹ and φ=fσ∞(f)⁻¹.


# ===== LM_C3-169-187 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-169-187 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 169–187)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-169-187/LM_C3-169-187_notes.md, §5), each checked
against LM_C3-169-187_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second pass)**
are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, programme), unproved claim,
unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 169–173

- **R169.1 — scan 169 — unpursued implication — keep.** The reading "calculs l-adiques" is confirmed. Grothendieck
  sets aside the normalisation (of the f, mod ℓ₀ⁿ, previous page) to keep visible the role of c, d, t, u ∈ Ẑ; what
  the normalisation would suppress, and whether the four quantities survive it, is not examined.
- **R170.1 — scan 170 — conjecture (open problem) — keep.** "je n'ai pas de caractérisation bien plausible à proposer
  de Γ_Q comme sous-groupe de Γ̃": the recognition problem for Γ_Q inside Γ̃ is stated, with no candidate.
- **R170.2 — scans 170–171 — unproved claim — keep.** The canonical splitting of (1) by Γ̃_σ (the u commuting with
  σ∞) and the "produit ½ direct" (3) M̃₀,₃ ≃ Ĝ⁺₀,₃·Γ̃_σ are asserted without the intersection and generation
  arguments.
- **R171.1 — scan 171 — unproved claim — correct.** φ′ is introduced as an "application (continue)" before the
  homomorphism theorem; continuity depends on the earlier definitions of t, u, μ, α. The exponent of τ∞ in (5) and on
  scan 172 is ε in the ink (confirmed); it stands for diag(1, ε) = τ∞^α (see C1), as scan 179 writes.
- **R172.1 — scan 172 — unproved claim (prior proof cited) — keep.** (9) is "un fait déjà établi" by the construction
  of φ′σ (refs (67), (68), corollary to th. (77)), outside this package; locally conditional on those results.
- **R172.2 — scans 172–173 — unpursued implication — keep.** The cocycle relations (10)–(12) put the factor u′(·)
  first for g, h, α, β and second for f; a uniform convention is suggested by the author himself (R173.1).
- **R173.1 — scan 173 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** From g(u′u) = A_{u′}(g(u))·g(u′), k = g⁻¹ satisfies
  k(u′u) = k(u′)·A_{u′}(k(u)), the order of the f-relation; this is what "remplacer g et h par g⁻¹, h⁻¹" achieves.
  Verified (inverse of a product, A an automorphism). Confidence high.
- **R173.2 — scan 173 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The matrix identities are explicit and can be audited
  symbolically or modulo integers; done for scans 174–175 in C2, which found the author's slips listed there.

### Scans 174–178

- **174–178 #1 — scan 174, (13)–(14) — unproved claim — correct.** (14) follows from (13) once ε₂ is a character of
  μ: the previous pages make ε₂ a function of μ (scan 168, "ε₂ = ε₂(μ)"; scan 152, "μ ≡ ε (4)"), i.e. the character
  Ẑ* → {±1}, μ ↦ μ mod 4. The derivation is not written here.
- **174–178 #2 — scan 175 — conjecture (expectation) — keep.** "ces calculs devraient s'insérer dans un tapis
  d'algèbre linéaire que j'ai la flemme de dérouler in extenso": a conceptual reformulation is expected, not given.
- **174–178 #3 — scans 175–176 — unpursued implication — correct.** He would describe M̃₀,₃ by (g, h, f) — "ou plutôt"
  (α, β, f) — through their reductions in Gl(2,Ẑ)^{±1}′, then replace Ẑ by an arbitrary ring (the target "Gl(2, A)"
  is overwritten, A|Ẑ). Which scalars must be units over a general ring (μ, and the divisions by μ′) is not settled.
- **174–178 #4 — scan 176 — conjecture (deferred intention) — keep.** "Peut-être y reviendrai-je finalement...."
- **174–178 #5 — scan 176, (19) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Follow the class of the central extension Ñ₁,₁ of
  M̃₀,₃ by ±1 (pull-back of Gl → Gl′) and its restrictions to Ĝ⁺₀,₃ and Γ̃_σ; no claim that it splits.
- **174–178 #6 — scan 177 — unproved claim — keep.** That φ′ agrees on M₀,₃ with the "modulaire" homomorphism of §39
  (1) is "admis" ("je vais l'admettre ici").
- **174–178 #7 — scan 177 — unproved claim — correct.** "il suffit de le vérifier sur Γ_{Q,σ}": immediate once both
  maps are homomorphisms that agree on Ĝ⁺₀,₃, since the splitting (3) restricts to M₀,₃ = Ĝ⁺₀,₃·Γ_{Q,σ}; the agreement
  on Ĝ⁺₀,₃ (the canonical Sl(2,Z)^ → Sl(2,Ẑ)′) is part of what is admitted.
- **174–178 #8 — scan 177, (20) — unproved claim — keep.** Granting the comparison, the inverse image of M₀,₃ in Ñ₁,₁
  is N₁,₁ "à isom. can. près", and that of Ĝ⁺₀,₃ is T̂⁺₁,₁ ≃ Sl(2,Z)^ (the caret after the parenthesis in the ink).
- **174–178 #9 — scan 178 — conjecture (programme) — keep.** "il resterait encore à caractériser Γ_Q comme sous-groupe
  de Γ̃" (the "à" is now read).
- **174–178 #10 — scan 178 — conjecture (question) — keep.** "Que devient dans (20) le scindage (3) de M̃₀,₃ ?
  Y-a-t-il une façon privilégiée …" of normalising the lift u̲₀ of φ′(u); continued on scan 179.
- **174–178 #11 — scans 174–175 — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The proposed symbolic check was carried out; see C2.
- **174–178 #12 — scans 176–178 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Test candidate sign normalisations of the lifts on
  finite quotients Gl(2, ℤ/n); success there would not give a compatible profinite choice.

### Scans 179–183

- **179 #1 — scan 179 — conjecture (question) — keep.** Normalise the lift u̲₀ of the element u̲ = φ′σ(u) (now read
  "l'élément") by Sl(2,Z)^ so that u(σ∞) = ±σ∞.
- **179 #2 — scan 179 — conjecture (question) — keep.** "y-a-t-il un relèvement canonique Γ̃_σ → Ñ₁,₁", i.e. a way
  "de lever cette ambiguïté du signe".
- **179 #3 — scans 179–180 — unproved claim — keep.** On Γ_{Q,σ} "les yoga(s) Standard suggèrent" that the lifts
  correspond to elliptic curves over Q with level-2 Jacobi rigidification at the point 1/2 of U₀,₃.
- **179 #4 — scan 179 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Compare lifting through the central ±1 with lifting to each
  finite level; finite existence does not give a compatible global lift.
- **180 #1 — scan 180 — unproved claim — keep.** The correspondence lifts ↔ rigidified elliptic curves at λ = 1/2 via
  "M′₁,₁ Q ≃ U₀,₃ Q" is asserted without the construction.
- **180 #2 — scan 180 — unproved claim — keep.** The indeterminacy of the curve "est justement dans H¹(Q, {±1})",
  identified with Hom(Γ_Q, {±1}), and is the same as that of the lifts; C3 explains why ±1 survives at level 2.
- **180 #3 — scan 180 — unproved claim — keep.** "il n'y a pas (même sur Γ_{Q,σ}) de choix vraiment privilégié": the
  sense of "privilégié" (natural, Galois-equivariant) is left informal.
- **180 #4 — scans 180–181 — unpursued implication — keep.** Existence of lifts on Γ_{Q,σ} comes from the existence of
  such curves; changing the curve by a quadratic twist should change the lift by the corresponding character.
- **181 #1 — scan 181 — conjecture (task left open) — keep.** "voilà le choix fait ! Il resterait à essayer de
  l'expliciter dans un formulaire calculatoire, si ce n'est pas sans espoir."
- **181 #2 — scan 181 — conjecture (deferred) — keep.** "Il faudrait que j'examine cette question de plus près, à
  l'occasion."
- **181 #3 — scan 181, (22) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** P(1−x) = −P(x) for P = x(x−1)(x−1/2) is right; it
  reflects that λ = 1/2 is the fixed point of λ ↦ 1−λ and that the curve has j = 1728 (C3). The map x ↦ 1−x swaps the
  2-torsion points 0 and 1, so it does not respect the level-2 rigidification.
- **182 #1 — scan 182, (1) — unproved claim — keep.** "restr." is marked as an isomorphism between automorphisms à
  lacets of Ĝ⁺₀,₃ preserving Π̂₀,₃ and automorphisms à lacets of Π̂₀,₃ commuting outerly with the action of 𝔖₃;
  extension and uniqueness are not argued.
- **182 #2 — scan 182, margin NB — unproved claim — correct.** With the double hats now read: M̃₀,₃ is the normaliser
  of Ĝ⁺₀,₃ in the double-hat group of all automorphisms à lacets of Π̂₀,₃, and Ñ₀,₃ the normaliser of 𝒯̂₀,₃ = 𝒯₀,₃ (a
  finite group, equal to its completion) in 𝒯̂̂₀,₃. Neither normaliser statement is proved here.
- **182 #3 — scan 182, (2)–(3) — unproved claim — keep.** Exactness of 1 → Π̂₀,₃ → M̃₀,₃ → Ñ₀,₃ → 1 and Ñ₀,₃ ≃ outer
  automorphisms à lacets commuting with 𝔖₃ need the triviality of the centraliser of Π̂₀,₃ in Ĝ⁺₀,₃.
- **182 #4 — scan 182, (4) — unproved claim — correct.** 𝔖₃ → Aut(𝔖₃), x ↦ int(x), is an isomorphism because 𝔖₃ is
  complete (trivial centre, all automorphisms inner); standard, no gap.
- **182 #5 — scan 182, (5)–(6) — unproved claim — correct.** (5) equals the composite (6) through the double-hat group
  of all automorphisms à lacets (read now in (6)) followed by the action on the three classes of loops; the matching of
  𝔖₃ = Ĝ⁺₀,₃/Π̂₀,₃ with the permutations of the classes is not written.
- **183 #1 — scan 183, (7) — unproved claim — correct.** Trivial on Π̂₀,₃ is immediate: int(π), π ∈ Π̂₀,₃, acts on
  Ĝ⁺₀,₃/Π̂₀,₃ by conjugation by the image of π, which is 1.
- **183 #2 — scan 183, (8)–(9bis) — unproved claim — keep.** (9) as the quotient of (8) by Π̂₀,₃ and the added
  extension (9bis); the column of the diagram writes Π₀,₃ without a hat.
- **183 #3 — scan 183, (10)–(11) — unproved claim — correct.** The commutation needed for the direct product follows
  from the definition of Ñ!₀,₃; see C4. The claim is justified.
- **183 #4 — scan 183, (12) — unproved claim — keep.** 𝒯₀,₃ ≃ 𝔖₃ × ℤ/2ℤ embeds in Ñ₀,₃, 𝔖₃ = 𝒯⁺₀,₃ by (10), the
  generator τ of ℤ/2ℤ = 𝒯!₀,₃ to the element τ of Ñ!₀,₃; injectivity and the factorwise description are asserted.
- **183 #5 — scan 183 — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The proposed computation of the action of the image of (10)
  on the kernel of (7) is done in C4.

### Scans 184–187

- **184 #1 — scan 184, (14) — unproved claim — keep.** χ: M̃₀,₃ → Ẑ* is "surjectif" and factors through Ñ₀,₃ and
  Ñ₀,₃/𝔖₃ ≃ Ñ!₀,₃; a route to surjectivity is in C5.
- **184 #2 — scans 184–185, (18) — unproved claim — keep.** The "plongement canonique" Γ_Q = Gal(Q̄₀/Q₀) ↪ Ñ!₀,₃,
  complex conjugation going to τ of (12) (now read "(τ)"), is stated, not constructed here.
- **185 #1 — scan 185 — conjecture (programme) — keep.** Find "un maximum de conditions nécessaires" on elements of
  Ñ!₀,₃ to come from Γ_Q, "jusqu'à trouver, si possible, une caractérisation (au moins plausible, sinon prouvée !)".
- **185 #2 — scan 185 — unproved claim (work announced) — keep.** "Nous allons 'déterminer' par le calcul le groupe
  Ñ!₀,₃": §46.II (heading now read Ñ!₀,₃, as in the author's table) begins this.
- **185 #3 — scan 185 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Compare the strength of candidate necessary conditions on finite
  quotients; this cannot by itself prove equality with the Galois image.
- **186 #1 — scan 186, margin of (22) — unproved claim — keep.** "f déterminé mod f ↦ ℓ₀ⁿf" (ℓ₀ now read): that this is
  the whole ambiguity needs the centraliser of ε₀ in Ĝ⁺₀,₃ to be the procyclic group it generates, whose intersection
  with Π̂₀,₃ is ⟨ℓ₀⟩ (ℓ₀ = ε₀²).
- **186 #2 — scan 186, (24)–(26) — unpursued implication — correct.** Two kinds of conditions: the relations (24)–(25)
  for an endomorphism, and (26) for respecting the "structure à lacets" (not "à bouts", as the first pass read).
  Which conditions survive the passage to outer automorphisms is not examined.
- **186 #3 — scans 186–187, (26)–(27) — unproved claim — keep.** (26) on ε₀ is called "équivalente" to (27) on
  ℓ₀ = ε₀²; squaring gives (26) ⇒ (27); the converse needs uniqueness of square roots up to the relevant conjugation
  in Ĝ⁺₀,₃.
- **187 #1 — scan 187, final NB — unproved claim (explicit extra hypothesis) — keep.** (24) must also be a
  "présentation-génération" of Ĝ⁺₀,₃ for (h, g, f) to define an automorphism; this "condition liminaire" is
  "sous-entendue" from then on.
- **187 #2 — scan 187, margin NB — unproved claim — correct.** "ν est uniquement déterminé par g, h", with 2ν+1 = μ "le
  multiplicateur": μ is the exponent in u(ε₀) ~ ε₀^μ, fixed by u alone and independent of the choice of f, so ν =
  (μ−1)/2 is determined once the multiplier is well defined (ε₀^μ conjugate to ε₀^{μ′} ⇒ μ = μ′).
- **187 #3 — scan 187 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Pass (29) to an abelian quotient where ℓ₁ survives to read off
  ν independently of f. Confidence medium.
- **187 #4 — scan 187, (28) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** In finite experiments keep 2ν+1 ∈ Ẑ*, not only ν ∈ Ẑ;
  ν = 1 gives 3, not a unit of Ẑ. Confidence high.

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scans 171–172, 179 — unpursued implication (notation) — Claude (second pass).** The ink writes
  μ(tσ∞+u)τ∞^ε = μ(u εt; −t εu) in (5) and on scan 172, but μ(tσ∞+u)τ∞^α on scan 179. Since tσ∞+u = (u t; −t u), the
  displayed matrix needs the factor diag(1, ε) = τ∞^α (ε = (−1)^α); read literally, τ∞^{±1} would both equal the
  involution τ∞. So τ∞^ε is the author's shorthand for τ∞^α. Confidence high.
- **C2 — scans 174–175 — unproved claim checked — Claude (second pass).** With u̲′ = μ′(u′ ε′t′; −t′ ε′u′) and
  t′²+u′² = ε′/μ′, det u̲′ = μ′ (margin NB) and u̲′⁻¹ = (ε′u′ −ε′t′; t′ u′). The four entries of u̲′fu̲′⁻¹ are as written
  except the (2,2) term +ε′rt′², written −ε′rt′² in the ink; the bottom row of f″ = f′u̲′fu̲′⁻¹ is then
  (ε′tu′+ut′, −ε′tt′+uu′) with no 1/μ′ (the ink strikes the 1/μ′ and the μ′ after μμ′, but (18) keeps 1/μ′ and its u″,
  like the (1,1) entry of the last check, lacks ε′). With these values u̲″ = u̲′u̲ holds entry by entry and
  t″²+u″² = (t²+u²)(t′²+u′²) = εε′/(μμ′), so (8), and with (9) the theorem, is confirmed. Confidence high.
- **C3 — scans 180–181 — own idea — Claude (second pass).** The point 1/2 of U₀,₃ (Legendre λ = 1/2) is the fixed point
  of λ ↦ 1−λ and has j = 256(λ²−λ+1)³/(λ²(1−λ)²) = 1728; x = X+1/2 turns (22) into y² = X³ − X/4, the quadratic twist by
  2 of y² = x³ − x (CM by ℤ[i]). At level 2 the Jacobi rigidification does not kill the automorphism −1 (an earlier part
  of the manuscript requires échelon n ≥ 3; CSG draft, l. 10838), which is why the choice of curve, and of lift, is
  ambiguous exactly by H¹(Q, ±1) = quadratic twists. Confidence high for the computations, medium for their bearing on
  a canonical lift.
- **C4 — scan 183, (11) — unproved claim proved — Claude (second pass).** For u ∈ M̃!₀,₃ and x ∈ Ĝ⁺₀,₃,
  u∘int(x)∘u⁻¹ = int(u(x)) with u(x) ∈ xΠ̂₀,₃, so u commutes with int(x) modulo int(Π̂₀,₃), i.e. in Ñ₀,₃. Hence the image
  of (10) centralises Ñ!₀,₃; it meets Ñ!₀,₃ trivially (its composite with (7) is the identity) and both generate Ñ₀,₃,
  which gives the direct product (11). Confidence high.
- **C5 — scan 184, (14) — unproved claim — Claude (second pass).** On Γ_Q, embedded by (18), the multiplier is the
  cyclotomic character (the earlier text calls the multiplier "caractère cyclotomique"; CSG draft, l. 6790), which maps
  Gal(Q̄/Q) onto Ẑ*. So χ is already surjective on the inverse image M₀,₃ of Γ_Q, granted that (18) is compatible with χ.
  Confidence medium.
- **C6 — scan 187, (27)–(29) — unproved claim checked — Claude (second pass).** With ε₀ = σ∞ρ and ε₀(x) = ε₀xε₀⁻¹,
  ε₀f⁻¹ = ε₀(f⁻¹)ε₀ = σ∞(ρ(f)⁻¹)ε₀, so fhσ∞(gρ(f)⁻¹) = ε₀^{μ−1} = ℓ₀^ν (ε₀² = ℓ₀, ν = (μ−1)/2 ∈ Ẑ as μ is odd); applying
  the involution σ∞ and σ∞(ℓ₀) = ℓ₁ gives σ∞(f)σ∞(h)gρ(f)⁻¹ = ℓ₁^ν, i.e. the boxed (29). Every step checks.
  Confidence high.
- **C7 — scan 182, (1) — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** As read, (1) asks u(ε₀) = int(f⁻¹)(ε_i^μ) with
  i ∈ {0, 1, ∞} free, so u may move the class of ε₀ to that of ε_i, which is what the map to 𝔖₃ in (5)–(6) records.
  Then f is determined modulo L_i, and the OBS "L₀\Π̂₀,₃" fits only i = 0, the !-case of (21). The left argument is
  overwritten (ε₀|ε_i), so this is also an open reading question. Confidence medium.


# ===== LM_C3-188-206 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-188-206 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 188–206)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-188-206/LM_C3-188-206_notes.md, §5, notes 1–46), each
checked against LM_C3-188-206_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second
pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, programme), unproved claim,
unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Matrix conventions used in the checks below (C1): ρ acts on Gl(2,Ẑ) as conjugation by R = (0 1; −1 1), σ∞ by
J = (0 1; −1 0), τ∞ = diag(1, −1); then ℓ₀ = (1 −2; 0 1), ℓ₁ = (1 0; 2 1), ε₀ = JR = −(1 −1; 0 1), ε₀² = ℓ₀,
σ∞(ℓ₀) = ρ(ℓ₀) = ℓ₁. These are not stated on these pages; they are the ones for which (62), (66), (68), (76) and (iv)
hold, and all of those identities were verified with them.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 188–191

- **N1 — scan 188, (31)–(32) — unproved claim — correct.** The "correspondance biunivoque" between pairs (g, h)
  satisfying (25) and pairs (α₀, β₀) needs two facts not proved here: every ρ- and σ∞-cocycle in the indicated groups
  is a coboundary (existence), and x ↦ xρ(x)⁻¹, x ↦ xσ∞(x)⁻¹ are injective, i.e. ρ and σ∞ have no non-trivial fixed
  points in Π̃^₀,₃ resp. Π̂₀,₃ (uniqueness). The Circle table places "Digression sur les 1-cocycles des groupes
  cycliques" at §43, p. 350, the likely source. Revised: the fixed-point condition was missing.
- **N2 — scan 188, after (34) — unproved claim — keep.** "ν ∈ Ẑ est uniquement déterminé en termes de α₀, β₀"
  requires that ℓ₁ generates a copy of Ẑ (so ℓ₁^ν determines ν) and that the admissible f do not change ν; the same
  claim is made for (29) in the margin of scan 187.
- **N3 — scan 189, left margin — unproved claim — correct.** "c'est un inconvénient mineur, qui va être levé plus
  bas" (now read "du couple" securely; "va être levé" still doubtful). It is taken up on scans 202–204: the
  representatives are normalised by α ≡ β ≡ 1 (2) (corollary, (89)), the residual freedom is a Ẑ-torsor T_Θ, and f is

### Scans 202–206

- **N33 — scan 202, (84)–(87) — unpursued implication — keep.** Verified: ℓ₀ⁿ = (1 −2n; 0 1) changes only the upper

- **N34 — scan 202, Proposition — unproved claim — correct.** Proved in this pass (sketch): under (88), D = εU and
  μD = 1 hold automatically; the (a, b) with ad − bc = δ form the orbit (a, b) + k(c, d), likewise (r, s) + m(t, u),
  and B = εS becomes k = m + (εS − B)/D, so the solutions are a torsor "sous Ẑ" (read so, not "sur"), acted on by
  the unipotent lift of ε₀ⁿ. The cited proposition is on p. 384.
- **N35 — scan 202, margin — unproved claim — correct.** "Pour μ fixé, on peut tjrs trouver c, d, t, u satisfaisant
  (88) — heureusement !" is true: at p ≠ 3 (resp. p ≠ 2) the forms x² + xy + y² (resp. x² + y²) are norms from étale
  algebras and take every unit value; at 3 (resp. 2) their unit values are exactly the units ≡ 1 mod 3 (resp. 4), which
  is what ε₃/μ ≡ 1 (3) and ε₂/μ ≡ 1 (4) give. Choose solutions prime by prime. Confidence high.
- **N36 — scan 202 — own idea (first pass) — drop.** Superseded by the direct proofs in N34–N35.
- **N37 — scan 203, corollary — unproved claim — correct.** Checked: α ≡ β ≡ 1 (2) forces (89); conversely, mod 2 one
  has B ≡ b, S ≡ s, D ≡ 1, so the shift k − m = (εS − B)/D is ≡ b − s and both b and s can be made even at once. Only
  even shifts preserve the congruences, hence the torsor under ℓ₀ⁿ = (ε₀²)ⁿ.
- **N38 — scan 203, Scholie — unpursued implication — keep.** The "invariants adéliques" Θ = (μ, c, d, t, u), up to

  the outer automorphism is not examined.

  on (α, β, f). Confidence medium.
- **N40 — scan 204 — unpursued implication — keep.** The datum f is "un peu trop faible pour reconstituer" u̳ = φ(u̲)
  = u_{α,β,f}; the precise loss is the twisting by the torsor described next.
- **N41 — scan 204, display and margin — unproved claim — keep.** The quotient of T_Θ × Sl(2,Ẑ) by the diagonal
  Ẑ is the twist of Sl(2,Ẑ) by Ť_Θ, "un Sl(2,Ẑ)-torseur à droite"; left and right actions are not written out.

- **N43 — scans 204–205 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Formulate the diagonal quotient as a moduli problem with
  the sign ±1 kept, and ask whether u̳ is defined as a matrix or as a class; u̳ changes sign with f ↦ −f (scan 204).

- **N44 — scan 206, after (94) — unproved claim — correct.** By (49), β₀′ = γβ₀, and h′ = 1 iff γβ₀ is fixed by σ∞;

## Notes of the second pass — Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scans 194–200 — own idea — Claude (second pass).** The matrix conventions stated at the top (ρ ~ R, σ∞ ~ J,
  τ∞ = diag(1, −1), ℓ₀, ℓ₁, ε₀) make (62), (66), (68), (iv), (76) and σ∞(ℓ₀) = ℓ₁ (scan 187) true; τ∞^{α₂} = diag(1, ε)
  agrees with package 169–187 (R171.1). They are presumably fixed in §44; recording them makes every later sign
  checkable. Confidence high.
- **C2 — scans 200–201, (81)–(83) — own idea (sign error) — Claude (second pass).** Since τ∞ J τ∞⁻¹ = −J and tJ + u
  commutes with J, the matrix (78) gives u_{α,β} J u_{α,β}⁻¹ = ε·βJβ⁻¹, while u_{α,β}Ru_{α,β}⁻¹ = αRα⁻¹ exactly; hence
  u_{α,β}(ε₀) = ε₀^μ and u_{α,β,f}(ε₀) = int(f⁻¹)(ε₀^μ), the loop relation (26) of scan 186 without sign. Example:
  μ = −1, α = diag(−1, 1), β = 1 satisfy (64) and (60); u_{α,β} = diag(−1, 1) sends J to −J and ε₀ to ε₀^μ, not
  ε·ε₀^μ. So (81) (first line), (82) and (83) hold only modulo ±1; the check on scan 201 is right for
  int(β)(σ∞)·int(α)(ρ). Confidence high for the computation.
- **C3 — scan 198, (73) — unproved claim (slip) — Claude (second pass).** From (66), (68) and (72), c² + cd + d² =
  δε(t² + u²) = δ/μ and ac + bd + bc = δε(rt + su); the ink (checked: no δ in either line) omits δ. The example of C2
  (δ = ε = −1, c² + cd + d² = 1, ε(t² + u²) = −1) violates the first line as written. (74) and (88) are right.
  Confidence high.
- **C4 — scan 189, (40) — unproved claim (slip) — Claude (second pass).** The middle member f⁻¹(βσ∞(β)⁻¹) lacks the
  factor σ∞(f) that the last member has (h = f⁻¹βσ∞(β)⁻¹σ∞(f)); compare (39), where ρ(f) is written. Confidence
  high.
- **C5 — scan 196, (v) — unproved claim — Claude (second pass).** "comparée à ξ = αρ(α)⁻¹ et à (iv), donne
  α̲₀ = ℓ₀^ν α": from ξ̲ = α̲₀ρ(α̲₀)⁻¹ = (ℓ₀^να)ρ(ℓ₀^να)⁻¹ this follows only if x ↦ xρ(x)⁻¹ is injective, the
  fixed-point condition of N1. Confidence high.
- **C6 — scan 198, (71) — unproved claim (slip?) — Claude (second pass).** The second relation reads "T ▪ S = 1 i.e.
  T = S" with a heavy dash for the operator; (68) gives S = T, so the analogue of C + D − B = 1 is T − S = 0. If the
  dash is a minus, "= 1" is a slip; "i.e. T = S" is right. Ink not settled; confidence low.
- **C7 — scans 188–190 — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** The corrected readings put the
  parametrisations (33), (36), (45) on u ∈ M̃!₀,₃ (automorphisms), while (49) shows that α, β, ξ, η depend only on the
  image in Ñ!₀,₃: the four systems (92) mix an outer invariant (α, β or ξ, η) with the inner datum f. Making this a
  fibration M̃!₀,₃ → Ñ!₀,₃ with fibre read through f would organise §46.II. Confidence medium.


# ===== LM_C3-207-225 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-207-225 — research notes, checked against the corrected text (scans 207–225)

Each first-pass note is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_C3-207-225_checked.tex`; notes marked **own (second
pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations), unproved claim (assertions
without proof or with an explicitly deferred verification), unpursued implication, own idea. Matrix conventions used
in the checks: ℓ₁ = (1 0; 2 1) (package 188–206, scan 196), ℓ₀ⁿ = (1 −2n; 0 1) (here (176)), and the ρ for which (147)
is equivalent to (150) C + D − B = 1, namely ρ = (0 −1; 1 −1) (tr ρ = −1).

## Scan 207

- **207, (98)–(100), unproved claim — keep.** The conditions (93) (94) "prennent aussi la forme" (98) and imply that u̲
  normalises the subalgebra of M₂(Ẑ) generated by σ∞, using (99) τ∞(σ∞) = −σ∞. Normalising the algebra Ẑ[σ∞] and
  centralising σ∞ are different conditions; the passage gives no proof of the implication.
- **207, (100) and last line, unpursued implication — keep, with a pointer.** The isomorphism M̃(½) ⥲ Ñ in (100) is
  asserted; its continuation is on scan 208 (the composite k₁/₂ of (101) factors through the normaliser of the torus
  (102)).
- **207, own (second pass), unproved claim.** The reference "conventions du § 44|§ 4 h" (row 2 of the changes) says that,
  up to α ↦ ℓ₀^να = α̲₀, the present relation of loops is the §44 one, with ξ and φ = η under the braces. The identity of
  φ with η is asserted, not checked here; it should be compared with the formulary after ms. p. 364 cited on scan 196.

## Scan 208

- **208, "Je suspecte qu'il n'y a que le scindage k₁/₂ …", conjecture — correct.** The verb is "suspecte" without doubt,
  and the margin begins "Sûrement canulé :" — "canulé" is Grothendieck's own word for a wrong statement (it opens
  footnotes elsewhere in the CSG and Carmona–Kuenzer texts). So the margin retracts the uniqueness claim: the curves
  isogenous to the CM curve give further splittings that factor through the normaliser.
- **208, (103)–(105), unproved claim — correct the detail.** For any u there is γ ∈ Π̃̂₀,₃ with γu commuting with ρ,
  "savoir γ = α₀⁻¹", and γ ∈ Π̂₀,₃ ⟺ ε₃(u) = 1 "i.e. μ(u) = 1 (3)" (written with "=", read mod 3). The proof is not given;
  the α₀ is retraced over β₀ in both places, matching case 1 (h = 1 ⟺ β₀ = 1) with ρ in place of σ∞.
- **208, own (second pass), unproved claim.** The margin attaches the orbit {½, 2, −1} to "la courbe elliptique
  d'invariant modulaire j = 0". For the Legendre parameter, j(λ) = 256(λ²−λ+1)³/(λ²(λ−1)²) gives j = 1728 at λ = −1, 2, ½
  (the curve with CM by Z[i], matching the order-4 element σ∞ and the torus {tσ∞ + u : t² + u² ∈ Ẑ*} of (102)); j = 0
  belongs to λ = −j, −j̄, the case of scan 209. The ink reads "= 0", so this is a slip or a different normalisation of j.

## Scan 209

- **209, "Je suspecte que c'est le seul scindage de M̃!₀,₃ …", conjecture — correct.** The verb is "suspecte" (not
  "suppute") and the extension is M̃!₀,₃ ("ou m̄ de M!₀,₃"), not Ñ. The margin "canulé, pour mêmes raisons que tout à
  l'heure…" retracts it, for the j = 0 curve and its isogenous curves.
- **209, (106)–(109), unproved claim — keep.** The image of M̃(−j̄) is called the index-2 kernel of u ↦ ε₃(μ(u)), (107)
  is an asserted isomorphism, and its inverse gives the splitting k(−j̄) of (108); the composite is said to factor through
  the torus T_ρ. Kernel, injectivity and the factorisation are three separate obligations, none proved here.
- **209, (109), own idea of the first pass — correct.** The ink reads c² + cd + d² (both signs are the same glyph, row 11).
  With ρ = (0 −1; 1 −1), the ρ that turns (147) into (150), det(cρ + d) = c² − cd + d²; c² + cd + d² is the determinant for
  the other lift −ρ (trace +1). So either T_ρ is written with the lift −ρ or the sign is a slip; the transcription keeps +.

## Scan 210

- **210, (113)–(115), unproved claim — keep.** "en exigeant f = 1" is said to fix α, β uniquely; this needs that the
  transformations (α, β, f) ↦ (ℓ₀ⁿα, ℓ₀ⁿβ, ℓ₀ⁿf) act simply transitively on the f with f ≡ 1 (L₀), which is not argued.
- **210, insertion after (114), unpursued implication — keep.** u normalises L₀ = {ℓ₀ⁿ}, "ou encore (agrandissant Π̂₀,₃)"
  u normalises L′₀ = {ε₀ⁿ}, with NB L₀ = L′₀ ∩ Π̂₀,₃. The equivalence of the two normalisation conditions is not shown.
- **210, own (second pass), unproved claim.** The upper-left margin now reads "On peut aussi écrire β au lieu de f,
  f (= β) déterminé mod f ↦ ℓ₀ⁿf, id. pour α, β": in case 2 the third parameter is defined only up to left
  multiplication by L₀, as α, β are. This is the same ambiguity that case 3 removes by f = 1.
- **210, own (second pass), conjecture.** The lower-left margin (fragmentary: "On ne remonte|relève donc … par
  M̃!₀,₃(0), … section de l'ext. … par Π̂₀,₃ — … section M̃!₀,₃(0) plus … p. 412′") records that M̃(0) does not give a
  section of the extension, and points ahead to ms. p. 412 for one; scan 214 says a uniformising parameter at 0 should
  give it.

## Scan 211

- **211, upper-left margin, unproved claim — correct the reading, keep.** "Dans tout ceci, je laisse tomber la
  "condition liminaire" sur les relations génératrices…" (both words now certain). Any converse classification drawn from
  (116) must restore that condition; the theorem of scan 216 does.
- **211, (117)–(118), own idea of the first pass — correct and sharpen.** With the inserted g = αρ(α)⁻¹, h = βσ∞(β)⁻¹ and
  (117) u(ρ) = gρ, u(σ∞) = hσ∞, one gets u(ρ) = αρα⁻¹ = int(α)ρ and u(σ∞) = int(β)σ∞. The ink of (118) has
  u(σ∞) = int(α)σ∞, u(ρ) = int(β)ρ, so α and β are interchanged in (118) as written.
- **211, (119)–(124), unpursued implication — keep, with an addition.** The left action of L₀ is written in three
  parametrisations; (122) as written gives "(f = ℓ₀ⁿℓ₁⁻ⁿ, g = ℓ₀ⁿℓ₁⁻ⁿ)" and "f = g = λσ∞(λ⁻¹) = λρ(λ)⁻¹". The first
  letter must be h, the σ∞-cocycle of int(λ) (f = 1 in case 3 by (115)); the ink shows f both times.

## Scan 212

- **212, (126)–(127) and margin, conjecture — keep.** "on s'attend à … un scind. partiel k_q" for every algebraic point q,
  with the margin "k_q ne sera déterminé qu'à autom. int. près…". The source of k_q in (127) is now marked N|M in the
  transcription (an M-shaped retracing); scan 213 treats N(q) as the domain.
- **212, (125), own idea of the first pass — keep.** The multiplication g″ = u′(g)g′, h″ = u′(h)h′ (and for α, β) is a
  cocycle rule; reading the pairs as nonabelian 1-cocycle data modulo L₀ is an interpretation to test, not a result.
- **212, own (second pass), unproved claim.** The corollary asserts a canonical bijection of Ñ!₀,₃ with pairs (α, β)
  satisfying (97) modulo (α, β) ↦ (ℓ₀ⁿα, ℓ₀ⁿβ); it rests on the isomorphism M̃(0)/L₀ ⥲ Ñ. Its surjectivity (every outer
  class has a representative normalising L₀, because u(ε₀) is conjugate to ε₀^μ for an automorphism "à lacets") is taken
  from the earlier pages and not re-proved here.

## Scan 213

- **213, opening, conjecture — correct the quotation.** "N(q) désigne un sous-groupe hypothétique de Ñ (qu'il faudrait
  arriver à déterminer, si tant est …)": there is no "encore" (the first pass took the end of the margin's "élément" and
  its arrow for a word). The problem stated is to find N(q) over the stabiliser Γ_ℚ(q) and the splitting above it.
- **213, margin, unproved claim — keep.** For q in a cyclotomic field, Γ_ℚ(q) is said to be the kernel of
  Γ_ℚ →χ Ẑ* → G with G the finite quotient attached to ℚ(q); the identification is sketched only.
- **213, transport of splittings, unproved claim — keep, with the reading.** ρ, ρ² carry k₁/₂ to k₂, k₋₁, and a σ_i
  carries k_{−j̄} to a splitting "du type k_{−j|−1}"; the phrase reads "des scindages|du scindage k₁/₂". Equivariance of
  the extension under these transformations is assumed.
- **213, ε₂∘χ, own idea of the first pass — keep, sharpen.** The ink clearly has ε₂. The stabiliser of −j̄ is
  Gal(ℚ̄/ℚ(√−3)), the kernel of the mod-3 character, which is ε₃ (as in (105)–(106), μ(u) ≡ 1 (3)); ε₂ is the mod-4
  character of package 188–206 ((61): μ ≡ ε₂ (4)), i.e. ℚ(i). So ε₂ here is a slip for ε₃.

## Scan 214

- **214, first paragraph, unproved claim — keep.** M̃[q] for q = 0, 1, ∞ meet Π̂₀,₃ in L₀, L₁, L∞, so they are not
  sections; the intersection for q = 0 is (119) on scan 211; q = 1, ∞ follow by ρ, ρ², which is asserted.
- **214, second paragraph, unproved claim — keep, with the reading.** "le choix d'un uniformisant(e) de l'anneau local …
  en 0 … détermine un scindage" of M̃(0) over Ñ! by L₀; the task left is to express "un tel scindage (associé à une
  uniformisante)" as a normalisation of "toute donnée mod L₀ de (α, β)". "J'y reviendrai par la suite, sûrement."
- **214, own idea of the first pass — keep.** Comparing two uniformisers and computing the induced change of
  representatives (α, β) mod L₀ is the natural first test of that programme.

## Scan 215

- **215, (130bis), unproved claim — keep, with a correction.** With ε₁ = ρ(ε₀) and ε∞ = ρ⁻¹(ε₀), (130) gives
  u(ε₁) = int(ξρ)(ε₀^μ) = int(ξ)(ε₁^μ) and u(ε∞) = int(ρ⁻¹(ξ⁻¹))(ε∞^μ). The ink has one more sign after "(ξ⁻¹)" (ℓ₀ or a
  retraced parenthesis, row 30); with these conventions no factor ℓ₀ is needed.
- **215, (132)–(134), own idea of the first pass — keep.** (133) are the cyclic cocycle conditions for orders 2 and 3, and
  (134) ξ = ηℓ₁^ν couples them; the step "d'où σ∞(η) = η⁻¹" uses only (133), and (131) follows from ησ∞(ξ) = ℓ₀^ν by
  applying σ∞ with σ∞(ℓ₀) = ℓ₁.

## Scan 216

- **216, condition 2ν + 1 ∈ Ẑ*, conjecture — correct.** In (134) "ν est un élément convenable de Ẑ, évidemment
  uniquement déterminé" (not "quelconque"), subject to 2ν + 1 ∈ Ẑ*, which is "impliquée par la condition liminaire, et
  peut-être l'implique…". The open question is the converse (does the unit condition imply the generation condition?).
- **216, theorem, unproved claim — keep.** The bijection of M̃(0) with pairs (ξ, η) satisfying (133), (134) and the
  "condition liminaire" is stated; reconstructing an automorphism from (ξ, η) is where the generation condition enters.
- **216, margin "cf plus bas th. p. 409!", unproved claim — correct: checked.** The forward reference is the theorem on
  scan 220 (ms. p. 409, after the upper number 409 of scan 219), whose a) says μ = 2ν + 1 is invertible, from μD = 1 (155).
  It is proved there for the images in Sl(2,Ẑ) with the sign fixed by (147), which is all the unit condition needs.
- **216, own idea of the first pass — keep.** Testing (133), (134) and the generation condition separately in finite
  quotients is a reasonable way into the converse.

## Scan 217

- **217, NB, unproved claim — keep.** "μ est déjà déterminé de façon unique par la seule connaissance de ξ, comme le
  montrent les calculs adéliques": verified on scans 218–220 (μ = 1/D by (155), for the lift fixed by (147)).
- **217, (145)–(146), unpursued implication — keep, with a reading note.** Eliminating η gives (145), eliminating ξ gives
  (146). In (146) the third factor reads ρ²(ηℓ₁^{ν|−ν}); since ξ = ηℓ₁^ν all three factors must be ηℓ₁^ν, so a −ν would
  be a slip.
- **217, own (second pass), unproved claim.** "il f. et s. qu'il existe n ∈ ℤ" (ℤ without hat, as written) such that
  (144) holds: the action of L₀ is by ℓ₀ⁿ with n ∈ Ẑ elsewhere ((119)–(124), (135)–(137)), so the integer ring here is
  presumably a slip for Ẑ; the transcription keeps ℤ.

## Scan 218

- **218, η-sign, unproved claim — keep.** The main text says there is "pas de façon simple" to fix the sign of η, the
  margin "mais si, on peut faire le choix de η en exigeant ξ = +ηℓ₁^ν", incompatible with the convention η = βσ∞(β)⁻¹.
  Scan 219 adopts ε = 1 by that choice.
- **218, final parenthesis, unproved claim — keep.** The ℓ-adic alternative ξ_ℓ = −ρ⁻¹ is excluded by ξ ≡ 1 (2) "pour
  ∀ ℓ"; a congruence mod 2 constrains only the 2-adic component directly, so the exclusion at odd ℓ needs the cited
  proposition of p. 394.
- **218, own idea of the first pass — keep.** Testing the exclusion at ℓ = 2 and at odd ℓ separately is the right check.
- **218, own (second pass), unproved claim.** The written matrix −ρ⁻¹ = (1 −1; −1 0) has determinant −1. With
  ρ = (0 −1; 1 −1) one has −ρ⁻¹ = (1 −1; 1 0); for it ξρ = −1, so ξρ(ξ)ρ²(ξ) = −1, and C + D − B = 2 ≠ 1, as the argument
  needs. The lower-left sign of the written matrix is a slip.

## Scan 219

- **219, η = ±ᵗη, unpursued implication — keep.** The sign − is excluded from U = −U ⇒ U = 0 against η ≡ 1 (2). The
  congruence only constrains the 2-adic component, so the argument settles the sign when it is one global sign; if the
  sign of ησ∞(η) = ±1 could vary with ℓ (as the alternative on scan 218 is taken "dans chaque composante ℓ-adique"), the
  odd components are not covered by it.
- **219, margin "Obs ici ε = 1 …", unproved claim — correct the reading, keep.** "la convention de signe ne coïncide pas
  avec une convention antérieure axée sur les α, β, mais dans l'optique présente elle paraît plus sympa": a deliberate
  change of lift convention.
- **219, (154)–(155bis), unpursued implication — keep.** μD = 1 is where invertibility of μ and D appears.
- **219, own (second pass), unproved claim.** With (153) the entries of the right member are A′ = ε(R + 2νS), B′ = εS,
  C′ = ε(T + 2νU), D′ = εU, and (150) becomes C′ + D′ − B′ = 1. The ink labels the three terms A′, B′, C′ and paraphrases
  "A + B − C = 1"; scan 220 then uses the standard labels (C′ = ε(T + 2νU), A′ = ε(R + 2νS)), so the labels on 219 are a
  slip.

## Scan 220

- **220, three-coefficient argument, own idea of the first pass — keep.** The comparison A = (1 + BC)/D = A′ uses only
  the two determinant identities and that D = D′ is invertible; it is valid over any commutative ring.

## Scan 221

- **221, margin "l'introduction des congruences ici est tout à fait accessoire", unproved claim — keep.** The algebraic
  parametrisation by (B, D) does not use the congruences: ν = (μ − 1)/2 lies in Ẑ for every μ ∈ Ẑ* (2-adic units are
  odd). The congruences only cut out the pairs with ξ ≡ η ≡ 1 (2), which by (157) and (159) is exactly the inserted
  condition B ≡ 0 (2).
- **221, (160)–(162), unpursued implication — keep, sharpen.** (161) u̲·ᵗu̲ = μη checks: with u̲ = (μ μB; 0 1) one gets
  (μ²(1 + B²) μB; μB 1) = μ(R S; S U) by (159). In (162) the second line ξ = ηℓ₁^{−ν} contradicts (134) ξ = ηℓ₁^ν and the
  page's own η = ξℓ₁^{−ν}; both −ν of the margin are slips for ν.
- **221, (157)–(160), own idea of the first pass — keep.** The parameters (μ, μB) of the triangular u̲ compose by matrix
  multiplication; scan 223 proves that ξ ↦ u̲_ξ is a homomorphism, which confirms the idea.

## Scan 222

- **222, P² = 1, own idea of the first pass — keep, made precise.** In Ẑ = ∏ ℤ_ℓ the solutions of P² = 1 are the
  P = (±1)_ℓ with independent signs, not only ±1; so the matrices satisfying (164) and det u̲ = μ are the (μP M; 0 P) for
  all such P, and "la solution la plus évidente P = 1" is a choice, matching the normalisation of (160).

## Scan 223

- **223, (167)–(168), unproved claim — keep, with a pointer.** "il suffit de vérifier" that solutions related by (167)
  satisfy (168); on scan 224 this is reduced to the cocycle identity (171), "ce qui est trivial".
- **223, own (second pass), unproved claim.** "où cette formule se réduit à (161)": for x = σ∞ and x = ρ, (166) reduces to
  (163) u̲_ξ(σ∞) = ησ∞, u̲_ξ(ρ) = ξρ, not to the margin formula (161) u̲·ᵗu̲ = μη; the number may be a slip for (163).

## Scan 224

- **224, (171), unproved claim — keep.** [u̲′u̲, ρ] = int(u̲′)([u̲, ρ])·[u̲′, ρ] is a one-line check, and the scalar factor is
  multiplicative.
- **224, own (second pass), unproved claim.** As corrected, (170) keeps the prefactor (det u̲)⁻¹ of the struck version
  (F = μ⁻¹ u̲·ᵗu̲ ℓ₁^{…}, cf. (162)) in front of [u̲, ρ]. Since det [u̲, ρ] = 1, the right member then has determinant μ⁻²
  and lies in Sl(2,Ẑ) only for μ = ±1; (174) on scan 225 has ξ̲ = [u̲, ρ̲] without the factor. The prefactor seems a
  remnant of the earlier formula.
- **224, corollary (172) and margin, unproved claim — keep, correct the reading.** "ce n'est pas un produit
  semi-direct, puisque M̃(0) ∩ Ŝ⁺₀,₃ = L₀ ≠ {1}, il faut donc montrer que la formule de définition ne dépend pas de la façon
  d'écrire un u sous la forme u_ξ.a — cela revient à voir que pour λ ∈ L₀, ces deux images … comme élément de l'un ou
  l'autre facteur, sont les m̄."

## Scan 225

- **225, "les seuls invariants adéliques …", unpursued implication — correct the quotation, keep.** "(ce qui revient au
  m̄, mais est plus joli) u̲" (not "plus précis"); the claim that (ξ̲, η̲), equivalently u̲, are the only natural adelic
  invariants before using α, β is a heuristic statement.
- **225, margin "prière de faire la vérification oubliée …", unproved claim — keep.** It refers to the margin of scan 224
  (well-definedness of (172) on L₀).
- **225, own (second pass), unproved claim.** That verification is in (175)–(176): for λ = ℓ₀ⁿ, ξ = η = ℓ₀ⁿℓ₁⁻ⁿ =
  (1 + 4n² −2n; −2n 1) (which satisfies (149) and (150)), so B = −2n, D = μ = 1 and u̲ = (1 −2n; 0 1) = ℓ₀ⁿ, the same
  matrix as the image of λ as an element of Π̂₀,₃. Hence the two images of λ agree, and by (177) λ acts on 𝔅 by
  M ↦ M − 2n.
- **225, (177) and the unfinished sentence, own idea of the first pass — keep.** The quotient of the subgroups 𝔅′₁ of
  𝔅 by the action M ↦ M − 2n is the continuation's topic. For u̲ from M̃(0), M ≡ 0 (2) by (173), and translations by 2n
  (n ∈ Ẑ) are transitive on 2Ẑ, so modulo L₀ only μ would remain; the next page should confirm or correct this.


# ===== LM_C3-226-244 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-226-244 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 226–244)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-226-244/LM_C3-226-244_notes.md, §5, R01–R34), each
checked against LM_C3-226-244_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes C0–C3 and C6–C11 are
new and marked **Claude (second pass)** (the points first drafted as C4–C5 went into R11 and R15). Kinds: question (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations), unproved claim,
unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks (not stated on these pages; those of package 188–206): σ(x) = σxσ⁻¹ for σ, ρ, τ∞;
φ̂(ℓ₀) = ε₀ = σ∞ρ, φ̂(ℓ₁) = σ∞, φ̂(ℓ∞) = ρ⁻¹ ((1), scan 229); ε₀² = ℓ₀, ε₁² = ℓ₁, σ∞(ε₀) = ε₁ (scan 237).

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 226–228

- **R01 — scan 226 — unproved claim — keep.** "le seul invariant abélien qu'on sache lui associer dans le contexte
  présent" records the state of knowledge, not a theorem. With the corrected reading "au quotient M^{!~}_{0,3}(0)/L₀ ⥲
  N^{!~}_{0,3}" (round brackets in the ink where the normaliser M[0] is meant) the quotient is the normaliser modulo L₀.
- **R02 — scan 228 — question — keep.** "mettre ces scindages canoniques en relation avec le choix d'une uniformisante
  en 0". It takes up an early remark of the manuscript (CSG draft, notes 11–12: "le choix d'une uniformisante en s_i
  définit un splittage, et même [seulement ?] le choix d'une base de l'espace tangent", "Deux bases différentes
  définissent des scindages différents !"); scan 238 returns to it (R20–R21).
- **R03 — scan 228 — unproved claim — keep.** (183) and "s'applique évidemment sans chgt pour L₁, L∞" are not proved;
  since ρ permutes 0, 1, ∞ cyclically and carries L₀ to L₁ (ρ(ℓ₀) = ℓ₁ in the conventions above), the claim is the
  ρ-equivariance of the construction of M(q). The dot over ρ in (183) is still doubtful.
- **R04 — scan 228 — own idea — correct.** The "tangential data" proposed here is the author's own notion: a uniformiser
  at 0 modulo m₀² is a basis of the cotangent space at 0, i.e. a tangential base point in the later terminology
  (Deligne 1989), and scan 238 narrows the choice to z or −z by an integrality condition. Revised: replaced by the
  author's formulation; see C8.

### Scans 229–231

- **R05 — scan 230 — unproved claim — correct.** The subscript of Centr is now read Π̂_{0,3} without doubt, but the
  margin NB says φ̂(f) need not lie in Π̂_{0,3}, and the author overwrote ℓ₀ʳ by ε₀ʳ before (6) and in (6): the
  centraliser must be taken in Ĝ⁺_{0,3}, which contains ε₀ (ε₀² = ℓ₀). The step needs that the centraliser of ε₀^μ
  (μ a unit) in Ĝ⁺_{0,3} is the closed procyclic subgroup generated by ε₀; not proved here.
- **R06 — scan 230 — unproved claim — correct.** With the exponents now read +1 (written over −1), case 2 reads
  int(φ̂(f₁)λβ₀)σ∞ = σ∞, and the existence of i ∈ Z/2Z uses that the centraliser of σ∞ in Ĝ⁺_{0,3} is {1, σ∞}. In
  PSL(2, Z) ≅ Z/2 ∗ Z/3 this is the free-product centraliser theorem; the profinite analogue (centralisers in free
  profinite products, studied by Herfort and Ribes) is a lead to check, not proved here.
- **R07 — scan 230 — own idea — keep.** A finite-quotient check of the two generator calculations is still useful; C1
  gives the algebra that the +1 exponents restore. Merged with R33.
- **R08 — scan 231 — own idea — keep (verified).** From (9), r' + i' ≡ (r − n) + (i + n) ≡ r + i mod 2, so r + i mod 2
  depends on u only; the normalised choice (10) has r = 0, so its i is this invariant. This holds whichever of i, α
  the overwritten letter is.
- **R09 — scan 232 — question — keep.** "Il faudrait examiner si cette définition coïncide avec celle de la p. 41?',
  413, formulée sans faire intervenir φ̂": p. 413 is scan 227, where (ξ, η) is called normalised when u_{ξ,η} ∈
  M^{!~}(0), i.e. B = 0. The third digit of the first reference is unread; the NB of scan 237 seems to ask the same
  question (C7).
- **R10 — scan 232 — unpursued implication — keep.** Corrected reading: (11) is "la relation des lacets". Both (10) and
  (11) are independent of f, which is why f only enters through int(f⁻¹) in (17)–(19).

### Scans 232–236

- **R11 — scan 233 — unproved claim — correct (the claim is true).** φ̂° sends ℓ₀, ℓ₁, ℓ∞ to σ∞ρ, σ∞, ρ⁻¹, of orders 2,
  2, 3 in 𝔖₃, which generate 𝔖₃. The quotient of the free profinite group Π̂_{0,3} by the closed normal subgroup
  generated by ℓ₀², ℓ₁², ℓ∞³ is the profinite completion of the von Dyck group ⟨a, b | a² = b² = (ab)³ = 1⟩, the
  dihedral group of order 6, so the surjection onto 𝔖₃ is an isomorphism and the kernel is exactly that subgroup (the
  order of the relation among ℓ₀, ℓ₁, ℓ∞ does not matter). Revised: proof sketch replaces "should establish equality".
- **R12 — scan 234 — unproved claim — correct.** The margin now reads securely "il faut de plus la "condition"
  "liminaire"…": the parametrisation by (14)–(15) needs a further condition not stated on these pages. The earlier
  "Question liminaire" of the manuscript (CSG draft, line 8073) is a lead for what is meant, not checked.
- **R13 — scan 234 — unproved claim — correct.** Uniqueness of α with αρ(α)⁻¹ = ξ is equivalent to int(ρ) having no
  fixed point other than 1 in Π̃^_{0,3}; existence is the triviality of the class of the 1-cocycle given by ξ, whose
  cocycle condition is (14). Both belong to §43 ("Digression sur les 1-cocycles des groupes cycliques", Circle table,
  p. 350). The "α ∈ Π̂_{0,3}" on the left of (16) is in the ink; Corollaire 1 and the right side have Π̃^_{0,3}.
- **R14 — scan 234 — own idea — keep.** The margin "en fait, on n'a pas vraiment besoin de α, β" is borne out by (18)
  and (31), which define u_ξ from ξ and ν alone.
- **R15 — scan 235 — unproved claim — correct.** Corrected reading: "Les automorphismes à lacets de Ĝ⁺_{0,3}". The
  proof is on scan 236 ("En effet…"); it uses two facts not proved there: f normalises L₀ only if f = ℓ₀ⁿ (the
  normaliser of L₀ in Π̂_{0,3} is L₀), and ε₀ⁿ = 1 forces n = 0 (ε₀ of infinite order, ⟨ε₀⟩ torsion-free).
- **R16 — scan 235 — own idea — correct (settled).** Strict commutation (20) and normalising L₀ are both stable under
  composition and inverses (φ̂∘uv = uφ̂v = uvφ̂), so by Corollaire 3 the normalised u_ξ form a subgroup: the first claim
  of Corollaire 4 follows at once.
- **R17 — scan 236 — unproved claim — correct.** The subgroup property follows from Corollaire 3 (R16); the triviality
  of the intersection with Π̂_{0,3} is proved on scan 237, modulo the facts in R18 and C6. Corrected reading of the
  description of N^{!≈}: "automorphismes ext. à lacets de Ĝ⁺_{0,3}" (first pass "à la carte").

### Scans 237–241

- **R18 — scan 237 — unproved claim — correct.** "ε₁^{2n} = 0" is in the ink, a slip for = 1. The reduction to
  α = β = ℓ₀ⁿ cites "prop. de la page 404", outside the package. Both parity cases need that the closed subgroups
  generated by ε₀ and ε₁ are torsion-free and meet trivially in Ĝ⁺_{0,3}; C6 gives a shorter route to n = 0.
- **R19 — scan 237 — question — correct.** Now partly read: "NB [?] M^{!~}_{0,3}[0] — une [..] N^{!~}_{0,3} tout
  entier, mais il reste à voir si … l'autre…". The visible words point to a section over N^{!~}_{0,3} tout entier,
  which is what (179)–(180) gave on scans 227–228; see C7 for the comparison the note seems to ask for.
- **R20 — scan 238 — question — keep.** "Il faudrait vérifier que cette section canonique est associée au choix d'une
  uniformisante" (now: "qui est en fait une section du sous-groupe de M^{!}_{0,3} normalisateur de L₀"); not settled.
- **R21 — scan 238 — question — keep.** The integrality condition (a basis over Z of m₀/m₀²) fixes the element "au signe
  près", i.e. z or −z; "Mais est-ce bien le bon ? Il faudrait y revenir !" leaves the choice open.
- **R22 — scan 238 — own idea — keep.** A finite-quotient comparison of the sections for z and −z remains a reasonable
  diagnostic; C8 suggests what to compare.
- **R23 — scan 239 — unpursued implication — keep.** The multiplication rule in the coordinates (u₀, f) of (27) is
  not written out; (32)–(33) give the u₀-part, and the f-part follows from int(f⁻¹)u_ξ int(f'⁻¹)u_{ξ'} =
  int(f⁻¹u_ξ(f'⁻¹))u_ξ u_{ξ'}.
- **R24 — scan 240 — unproved claim — keep.** The "Rescholie (Récapitulation)" restates the bijection with solutions
  of (30) and its α-form without new proof; existence and uniqueness of α are the §43 facts of R13.
- **R25 — scan 240 — own idea — correct (verified).** u_{ξ'}u_ξ(ρ) = u_{ξ'}(ξ)u_{ξ'}(ρ) = u_{ξ'}(ξ)ξ'ρ gives
  ξ'' = u_{ξ'}(ξ)ξ', which is (33) on scan 241 ("ξ'' = u'(ξ)·ξ'"); the struck α''-computation on 240 leads to the same.
- **R26 — scan 241 — unproved claim — correct.** The step is short: N^{!≈} ⊂ N^{≈} = G₃ × N^{!≈} followed by the
  projection to N^{!≈} is the identity, so M^{!≈}(0), which maps isomorphically onto N^{!≈} in (24), also does so in
  (36) and meets its kernel Ĝ⁺_{0,3} trivially; hence (37). The revision before (36) is still unresolved.

### Scans 242–244

- **R27 — scan 242 — unproved claim — correct (verified).** With (u·a)(u'·a') = uu'·(u'⁻¹(a)a'), Φ(u·a) = uφ̂(a) is
  multiplicative iff φ̂(u'⁻¹(a)) = u'⁻¹(φ̂(a)) for all a ∈ Π̂_{0,3}, i.e. iff (20) holds for u'. So "Φ est un
  homomorphisme" is exactly the strict commutation the text invokes ("commutent effectivement (pas seulement
  extérieurement)").
- **R28 — scan 242 — question — keep.** The equality with the Φ of §41 (p. 316') "de façon effective" is announced,
  not proved.
- **R29 — scan 243 — unproved claim — correct.** See C10: (10) gives β = φ̂(ξ)σ∞^i explicitly, so the "fondements qui
  n'ont pas encore été établis" concern the existence of β with βσ∞(β)⁻¹ = η, a σ∞-cocycle question of §43. The
  noun before "délicate" remains unread ("paramètre" not confirmed).
- **R30 — scans 243–244 — unpursued implication — keep.** The x = ℓ∞ test ends at (44) without being compared with (10).
- **R31 — scan 244 — unproved claim — correct.** The prose and (42) read j (now settled), (41) an overwritten μ|j.
  j ≢ 0 (3) because u is an automorphism and ρ has order 3; (42)–(43) are then a definition and (40).
- **R32 — scan 244 — unpursued implication — keep.** See C11 for the expected centraliser of ρ.
- **R33 — scans 242–244 — own idea — keep.** Merged with R07: finite-quotient checks of the ℓ₁- and ℓ∞-tests.
- **R34 — scan 242 — own idea — drop.** Duplicate of R27, now carried out there.

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

- **C0 — scan 229 — own idea (medium).** (1) is the map from π₁ of P¹ − {0, 1, ∞} to the orbifold fundamental group
  of the j-line, Ĝ⁺_{0,3} ≅ PSL(2, Z)^ (an extension of 𝔖₃ by Π̂_{0,3}), obtained by filling 1 and ∞ with cone points of
  orders 2 and 3 (φ̂(ℓ₁) = σ∞, φ̂(ℓ∞) = ρ⁻¹) and keeping 0 as the cusp (φ̂(ℓ₀) = ε₀). On this reading φ̂ is surjective,
  its kernel is the closed normal subgroup generated by ℓ₁², ℓ∞³, and it comes from a morphism defined over Q (C9).
- **C1 — scan 230 — own idea (high for the algebra).** The +1 exponents repair the slip "= int(φ̂(f₁))σ∞" (inverse
  lost) in the line φ̂(u(x)): from int(φ̂(f₁)⁻¹)σ∞ = int(λβ₀)σ∞ one gets φ̂(f₁)λβ₀ = σ∞^i, hence φ̂(f₁⁻¹) = λβ₀σ∞^i,
  the form substituted into φ̂(gρ(f)⁻¹) = φ̂(f)⁻¹ε₀ʳ fβ₀σ∞^α.
- **C2 — scans 230–231 — unproved claim / inconsistency.** The author changed ℓ₀ʳ into ε₀ʳ before (6) and in (6) after
  the margin NB, but the reprise on 231 ("λ = φ̂(f)⁻¹ℓ₀ʳ f", ink checked) and the struck condition on 230 keep ℓ₀ʳ.
  Only ε₀ʳ fits (7) and (9), where β' = ℓ₀ⁿβ = ε₀^{2n}β shifts r by n.
- **C3 — scan 231 — unpursued implication.** By (9) the normalisation r' = 0 of (10) is reached by n = r, and by no
  other n if r is well defined; this is the "de façon unique" of Corollaire 1, which therefore also rests on
  ⟨ε₀⟩ ≅ Ẑ (R05).
- **C6 — scan 237 — own idea (medium; conventions above).** With ν = 0, φ̂(ξ) = φ̂(ℓ₀ⁿℓ₁⁻ⁿ) = ε₀ⁿσ∞⁻ⁿ and
  σ∞(φ̂(ξ))⁻¹ = σ∞ⁿε₁⁻ⁿ, so (15) reads ℓ₀ⁿℓ₁⁻ⁿ = ε₀ⁿε₁⁻ⁿ, i.e. ε₀ⁿ = ε₁ⁿ, with no parity split. The manuscript's
  ε₀ⁿσ∞⁻ⁿε₁ⁿσ∞ⁿ differs by the place of σ∞^{±n} and an inverse; either way n = 0 needs that the closed subgroups
  generated by ε₀ and ε₁ meet trivially in Ĝ⁺_{0,3} (true for the cusp stabilisers at ∞ and 0 in PSL(2, Z)).
- **C7 — scans 232, 237 — question (interpretation).** Two sections are now on the table: M^{!~}(0) of (179), defined by
  the matrix normalisation B = 0 over all of N^{!~}, and M^{!≈}(0) of (23), defined by strict commutation with φ̂ over
  N^{!≈}. The margin of 232 and, it seems, the NB of 237 ask whether the first restricts to the second over N^{!≈}.
- **C8 — scan 238 — own idea (low).** z and −z differ by the half-turn about 0, and Ĝ⁺_{0,3} contains a half-loop at 0,
  ε₀ (ε₀² = ℓ₀). The sections attached to z and −z may differ by int(ε₀), which would tie the sign question to the
  normalisation r = 0 of (10), written with ε₀ʳ; to be tested on a finite quotient (R22).
- **C9 — scan 238 — unproved claim.** "ce sous-groupe contient Γ_Q" (N^{!≈} ⊃ Γ_Q) is asserted without proof: it needs
  that the outer Galois action on Π̂_{0,3} commutes with φ̂ up to inner automorphisms, which holds if φ̂ comes from a
  morphism defined over Q (C0).
- **C10 — scan 243 — unpursued implication.** The x = ℓ₁ test (10) gives β = φ̂(ξ)σ∞^i in closed form, so the
  "délicate" β, whose existence from η = ξℓ₁^{-ν} is a σ∞-cocycle problem, is explicit once ξ satisfies (15); this is
  why the author calls the condition "remarquable".
- **C11 — scan 244 — unpursued implication.** In PGL(2, Z) the centraliser of an element of order 3 is the cyclic group
  it generates; if the same holds for ρ in Ĝ_{0,3} = Ĝ⁺_{0,3}·{1, τ∞}, (44) becomes τ'∞^{α₃}φ̂(f∞)α ∈ ⟨ρ⟩, the
  ℓ∞-analogue of (10).


# ===== LM_C3-245-263 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-245-263 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 245–263)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-245-263/LM_C3-245-263_notes.md, §5, notes 1–50), each
checked against LM_C3-245-263_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude
(second pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, programme), unproved
claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks: the matrices of the pages themselves, ρ = (0 1; −1 1) (scans 258, 261) and
σ = σ∞ = (0 −1; 1 0) (scans 258, 260); for the coboundary map ρ(α) = ραρ⁻¹, so [α, ρ] = αρα⁻¹ρ⁻¹. ρ has order 3 and
σ∞ order 2 as automorphisms (in PSL(2, Z)); in the quotient 𝔖₃ of (51), σ̇ρ̇σ̇⁻¹ = ρ̇⁻¹.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 245–249 (§47, the parameters α, β)

- **N1 — scan 245, opening — unproved claim — correct.** The "principes généraux (pas encore établis … dans le cadre
  profini)" are used to make the commutant of ρ in Ĝ₀,₃ = Ĝ⁺₀,₃·{1, τ∞} (scan 244) equal to {1, ρ, ρ²}, which turns
  the commutation relation (*) of scan 244 into τ′∞^{α₃}φ̂(f∞)α = ρ^j with j ∈ Z/3Z. The profinite centralizer
  statement is the input to be proved. Revised: the group and the relation are named.
- **N2 — scan 245, upper-left margin — conjecture (programme) — keep.** "il faudrait … formules … précise …!!!" asks
  for explicit formulas; the rest of the note is still unread, so nothing more specific can be attributed to it.
- **N3 — scan 246, (45)–(48) — unpursued implication — keep.** α̃ = ατ∞^{α₃} lies in Π̂₀,₃ in all cases, and for
  α₃ = 1 the coboundary relation becomes the twisted one (46), ξ = α̃ℓ₁⁻¹ρ(α̃)⁻¹. Checked: it follows from
  ξ = αρ(α)⁻¹, α = α̃τ∞ and (**) τ∞ρ(τ∞)⁻¹ = ℓ₁⁻¹.
- **N4 — scan 247, opening — unproved claim — correct.** The equivalence of (47) with (10) = (49) for α, β linked by
  ξ = ηℓ₁^ν "mériterait une vérification directe" and is not given. The ink writes η = βσ∞(η)⁻¹: the argument of σ∞
  is a slip for β (η = βσ∞(β)⁻¹ on scan 249 and in (11) of scan 232). Revised: the palaeographic doubt is settled.
- **N5 — scan 247, (51) — unpursued implication — correct.** Reduction mod Π̂₀,₃ keeps exactly the triple
  (i, α₃, j′) of (51); everything else in (47) and (10) lies in the Π̂₀,₃-components α̃, β. What remains open is how
  these components depend on ξ. Revised: the vague "what information is retained" is answered by the page.
- **N6 — scans 247–248 — conjecture — keep.** "Il faudrait voir si i, j′ peuvent, comme α₃, s'expliciter en termes du
  multiplicateur μ, voire … ε_ℓ(μ)". Data on these pages: u = 1 gives (i, α₃, j′) = (0, 0, 0) with μ = 1; u = τ∞
  gives (1, 1, −1) with μ = −1 ((59)–(60)).
- **N7 — scan 247, bottom-left margin — conjecture — keep.** Whether (i, α₃, j′) takes all values ("ttes sortes de
  valeurs", reading still uncertain) in Z/2 × Z/2 × Z/3.
- **N8 — scan 248, (52)–(53) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The proposed check was carried out. (52) follows from
  (47)·(10) and ρ⁻¹(ξ)ξ = ρ(ξ)⁻¹ (apply ρ² to ξρ(ξ)ρ²(ξ) = 1). Solving (52) for β⁻¹α̃ gives
  σ∞^i φ̂(ρ(ξ)) ρ^{j′} σ∞^{α₃}, while the ink of (53) has σ∞^{α₃}ρ^{j′}; reducing (53) as written mod Π̂₀,₃ with
  (52) gives 1 = ρ̇^{2j′} when α₃ = 1, false for j′ ≠ 0 (e.g. u = τ∞). So (53) has the last two factors in the wrong
  order; the recap (54) keeps the order of (52), which is correct.
- **N9 — scans 248–249 — conjecture (expectation) — keep.** Working with ξ, η alone would dispense with the delicate,
  unproved existence results "du type ξ = αρ(α)⁻¹, η = βσ∞(β)⁻¹" (inserted on scan 249); (57)–(58) on scan 250 are
  a first instance, since they involve ξ only.
- **N10 — scan 249, (56) — unproved claim — correct.** "qui implique déjà la première formule (14)" is immediate:
  ξ = yρ(y)⁻¹ with y = φ̂(ρ⁻¹(ξ⁻¹)) gives ξρ(ξ)ρ²(ξ) = yρ³(y)⁻¹ = 1, ρ³ acting trivially. Formula (14) is
  ξρ(ξ)ρ²(ξ) = 1 (scan 234). Revised: the implication is proved by this telescoping.
