- **N11 — scan 249, (55) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** For α₃ = 1 the relation replacing (56) is the twisted
  coboundary (46), ξ = α̃ℓ₁⁻¹ρ(α̃)⁻¹, so (57) has no direct analogue; this is what the margin of scan 250 records
  ("ici α₃ = 1 donc (57) n'est pas valable"). Revised: the obstruction is identified on the pages themselves.

### Scans 250–253 (examples and the commutant of τ)

- **N12 — scan 250, Remarque — unpursued implication — correct.** Now read: "l'unique élément τ ∈ T^!₀,₃ ⊂ N^{!≈}₀,₃,
  qui dans M^{!≈}₀,₃(0) correspond à l'élément τ∞ = u_ξ = u_{ξ,η}" (struck: "aux invariants"). "l'unique" refers to
  the non-trivial element of T^!₀,₃ ≃ Z/2, not to M(0). The remark only records that τ is the one non-trivial element
  the author can write down, even in the larger N^{!~}₀,₃; it does not claim there are no others.
- **N13 — scan 250, lower-left margin — unproved claim (scope) — correct.** Reading: "ici α₃ = 1 donc (57) n'est pas
  valable" (applicable less likely). (57) was derived under (55), α₃ = 0, i.e. μ ≡ 1 (3), and τ∞ has μ = −1; the
  example is to be checked against (54), as the struck lines and the margin "et aux relations (54)" begin to do.
- **N14 — scan 251, Rmq — conjecture (question) — correct.** The question concerns u_{α,β} = u_{ξ,η} ∈ M^{!~}₀,₃[0]
  "pas nécessairement normalisés" (not "inversibles"), i.e. not assumed to commute with φ̂, with f = 1 and β = 1. The
  page answers it: ξ = ℓ₁^ν = (1 0; 2ν 1), the cocycle criterion of p. 344|342 (§VII "cocycles sous ρ") forces
  C + D − B = 2ν + 1 = μ = ±1, so ν ∈ {0, −1} and u ∈ {id, τ∞}.
- **N15 — scan 251, NB — unproved claim — keep.** "On sait déjà que si α = 1 i.e. ξ = 1, alors u = 1" appeals to an
  earlier result (with f = 1); it is distinct from the case β = 1 treated in the remark.
- **N16 — scan 251, diagonal margin — unproved claim — keep.** The multiplier is "(au signe près quand la normalisation
  de ξ n'est pas faite) égal à l'un des 'coefficients …' de ξ, donc égal à ±1"; the qualifier after "coefficients" is
  still unread, so the statement points back to an earlier argument rather than giving one.
- **N17 — scan 252, Proposition — unproved claim — correct.** The commutant of τ in N^{!~}₀,₃ is {1, τ}; the paragraph
  proves more, in "le gros normalisateur de L₀ dans M^{!~}₀,₃", i.e. M^{!~}₀,₃[0] = Norm(L₀) of scan 243 (the first
  pass read "normalisateur N₀|L₀ de M"). The reduction to (62) is explicit; the conclusion uses (64) on scan 253. The
  margin points to §46 VI|II.
- **N18 — scan 252, (61)–(62) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Separate the particular solution (63) from the
  fixed-point group that measures the ambiguity; checked: (62) follows from (61), u_ξ(ε₁) = int(ξ)(ε₁^μ), ℓ₁ = ε₁²
  and μ = 2ν + 1.
- **N19 — scan 253, (64) — conjecture — keep.** Uniqueness of (63) is conditional on admitting that "les résultats
  discrets se prolongent au cas profini", i.e. (Π̂₀,₃)^{τ∞} = {1}.
- **N20 — scan 253, (64) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Checked: two solutions x, y of (62) give
  τ∞(yx⁻¹) = yx⁻¹, so the uniqueness of (63) is exactly (64).
- **N21 — scan 253, last line — own idea (first pass) — correct.** Both readings stand in the ink: "ν = 0 ou −1" on
  251 and "ν = ±1, i.e. u ∈ {1, τ∞}" on 253. Since 2ν + 1 = μ = ±1 and τ∞ has ν = −1, the line on 253 is a slip for
  μ = ±1 (ν = 1 would give μ = 3, not a unit of Ẑ); its conclusion u ∈ {1, τ∞} is that of scan 251.

### Scans 254–263 (§48 I, the morphism α ↦ [α, ρ])

- **N22 — scan 254, after (3) — conjecture (programme) — keep.** "Il s'agit de caractériser l'image" of
  G/T_ρ ↪ G; (4) is a first necessary condition, and scans 255–257 give the characterization for forms of Sl(2),
  Gl(2).
- **N23 — scan 254, G_ab — unproved claim — correct.** For Gl(2) the quotient by the derived group Sl(2) is G_m via
  det, and (4) is det ξ = 1 (condition c) of scan 257). For Sl(2), which is semisimple, the derived group is Sl(2) and
  G_ab is the unit group, although the abstract group Sl(2, Z) has abelianization Z/12; (4) is a condition on the
  sheaf, not on the abstract group.
- **N24 — scan 254, (2)–(3) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The fibres of F_ρ are the cosets αT_ρ by (2); existence
  over the base is the separate question settled in the proposition and corollary.
- **N25 — scan 255 — unproved claim — correct.** The inserted quantifier reads "après tt chgt de base" (tout). The
  passage invokes Steinberg for local conjugacy of sections in reductive groups and the invariant scheme I_G with a
  universal property "(sur A, et aussi après tout changement de base)"; for the forms of Sl(2), Gl(2) used here,
  (7)–(8) make I_G explicit.
- **N26 — scan 256, (7) — unproved claim — keep.** G/int(G) → I_G is an epimorphism (fppf) but not a monomorphism;
  e.g. in Sl(2) over a field, 1 and (1 1; 0 1) have the same trace and are not conjugate.
- **N27 — scan 256 — unproved claim — keep.** Equality of invariants suffices for local conjugacy of two regular
  sections, and "aussi si les deux sections sont ½ simples (… une condition sur chaque fibre)"; see N29 for the
  limits of the second statement.
- **N28 — scan 256 — unproved claim — keep.** For groups locally isomorphic to Sl(2), GP(1) or Gl(2), regular sections
  are those central at no point; checked on the field-valued level in all characteristics (a non-central element of
  Sl(2) has a one-dimensional centralizer).
- **N29 — scan 256 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Over k[ε]/(ε²) the Sl(2)-sections I and I + εE₁₂ have the same
  invariants and the same, semisimple, fibre, but are not conjugate; so the "½ simples … condition sur chaque fibre"
  sufficiency needs a stronger notion of semisimple section over non-reduced bases.
- **N30 — scan 257, (7)–(8) — unproved claim — keep.** I_G ≃ E¹ via the trace for forms of Sl(2), and
  I_G = E^{1*} × E¹ via (det, tr) for forms of Gl(2); the matrix computation must be descended to forms.
- **N31 — scan 257, Proposition — unproved claim — correct.** The reference reads "dans l'image de F_ρ ((1) ou (3))":
  the morphism and the monomorphism G/T_ρ ↪ G, not the invariant map (8). Conditions a)–c) (fibrewise
  non-centrality of ξρ, tr ξρ = tr ρ, det ξ = 1 for Gl(2)) are stated "pour mémoire" without proof.
- **N32 — scan 258, Corollaire — unproved claim — keep.** The solutions of [α, ρ] = ξ form a right T_ρ-torsor, T_ρ
  "lisse commutatif" of relative dimension 1 or 2; see N33.
- **N33 — scan 258 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Checked: the centralizer of (1 1; 0 1) in Sl(2) over F₂ is
  {(a b; 0 a), a² = 1} ≅ μ₂ × G_a, not smooth, although (1 1; 0 1) is non-central; so "lisse" fails for forms of
  Sl(2) in characteristic 2. For the ρ of (12) it holds: at 3 the norm-one equation becomes (a − b)² = 1, étale in
  characteristic 3, and at 2 the fibre is a torus.
- **N34 — scan 258, (9) — unproved claim — correct.** Forms of Sl(2) and of GP(1) are classified by PGL(2)-torsors,
  i.e. Azumaya algebras of rank 4, but Gl(2) has the outer automorphism g ↦ det(g)⁻¹g (it inverts the centre), so
  "une forme de Gl(2) équivaut à … une Algèbre d'Azumaya" holds for inner forms only. Revised: the needed restriction
  is identified.
- **N35 — scan 259, (10) — unproved claim — keep.** Fibrewise regularity makes O_S + O_Sρ = A_ρ a quadratic
  subalgebra, locally a direct factor of M, compatible with base change; not proved here.
- **N36 — scan 259, (11)–(11bis) — unproved claim — keep.** T_ρ is the unit scheme of A_ρ, resp. its norm-one part
  for SG(M); this needs the centralizer computation and the agreement of det with the norm of A_ρ.
- **N37 — scan 259, (12) — unproved claim — correct.** The lower-right entry of (12) is overwritten in the ink;
  ρ⁶ = 1, ρ³ = −1 and the same ρ on scans 258 and 261 give (0 1; −1 1). Its characteristic polynomial x² − x + 1 has
  discriminant −3, so ρ has distinct eigenvalues exactly in characteristic ≠ 3, as stated; the text then reads "donc
  au|en sens strict T_ρ (… sur Spec Z) n'est pas un tore" (first pass: "sur Ainsi").
- **N38 — scan 260 — unproved claim — keep.** Checked: σ has characteristic polynomial x² + 1 (discriminant −4),
  unipotent in characteristic 2, where T_σ ⊗ F₂ = (F₂[x]/(x + 1)²)^* ≅ G_m × G_a; likewise T_ρ ⊗ F₃ for ρ.
- **N39 — scan 260 — unproved claim — keep.** T_ρ is the Weil restriction of G_m from A_ρ, so its torsors are the
  invertible A_ρ-modules and are Zariski-locally trivial; torsors under ST_ρ need not be.
- **N40 — scan 260, last paragraph — conjecture — keep.** "mais peut-être pas dans SG(M)": the determinant-one
  lifting problem, answered on scans 262–263 by the norm obstruction (21).
- **N41 — scan 261, margin — unproved claim — keep.** Trace and determinant conditions (16) give a local solution "du
  moins si on suppose que la condition de régularité n'est violée sur aucune fibre".
- **N42 — scan 261, (17) — conjecture (expectation) — correct.** Checked: A_ρ = Z[ρ] ≅ Z[e^{2iπ/6}], the Eisenstein
  integers, which form a principal ideal domain (class number 1 of Q(√−3)); so Pic(A_ρ) = 1 and the "sauf erreur" is
  confirmed. Reading: "l'obstruction est un A_ρ-Module inversible, or A_ρ est principal".
- **N43 — scan 261, (16) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Separate the scheme AD − BC = 1, C + D − B = 1 from its
  fibrewise regular open part and determine the excluded locus at 3.
- **N44 — scan 262 — conjecture — correct.** "les calculs faits par ailleurs suggèrent que ce signe est aussi égal au
  reste de D mod 3": proved below for D ≢ 0 (3), and D ≡ 0 (3) occurs with both signs (C2).
- **N45 — scan 262, last display — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The ink "f ∈ Γ O̲_S^*" is confirmed. The
  argument needs f ∈ Γ(S, T_ρ), the units of A_ρ (as "f ∈ A_ρ^*" for Z on the same page, and as (20)–(21) use,
  det f being a norm from A_ρ); for a scalar f, det f = f² and (20) would not follow. A slip.
- **N46 — scan 262 — unpursued implication — keep.** The sign of det α does not depend on the solution α (det f =
  f f̄ > 0 for f ∈ A_ρ^*); it is an invariant of ξ.
- **N47 — scan 263, (20)–(21) — unpursued implication — keep.** The determinant-one problem becomes the class of det α
  in A^*/N_{A_ρ/A}A_ρ^*; functoriality of these classes is not developed.
- **N48 — scan 263 — conjecture (expectation) — correct.** Checked by local class field theory: for ℓ ≠ 3,
  A_ρ ⊗ Z_ℓ is étale over Z_ℓ (split or unramified), so every unit is a norm; for ℓ = 3 it is the ring of integers of
  the ramified Q₃(√−3), and Z₃^*/N ≅ Z/2 (N(a + bρ) = a² + ab + b² ≡ (a − b)² mod 3, so −1 is not a norm). The
  "sauf erreur" claim is right.
- **N49 — scan 263, margin norm formula — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The formula N(cρ + d) = c² + cd + d² is
  right (ρ ↦ ω with ω + ω̄ = ωω̄ = 1). The proposed computation was done mod 3 for the sign of scan 262 (C2).
- **N50 — scan 263, last sentence — unproved claim (continuation) — keep.** "des calculs immédiats déjà faits montrent
  que" continues on the next page; nothing is inferred here.

## New notes

- **C1 — scan 251, matrix of ±ρ⁻¹ — own idea, Claude (second pass).** The ink writes ξ̲ = ±ρ̲⁻¹ = ±(−1 1; 1 0)
  (lower-left entry 1, checked on a crop). With ρ = (0 1; −1 1), ρ⁻¹ = (1 −1; 1 0), and the written matrix has
  determinant −1, so it is not ±ρ⁻¹; probably a slip for ∓(1 −1; 1 0). It does not affect the argument, since this
  alternative is dismissed ("ce qui manifestement n'est pas le cas").
- **C2 — scan 262, the sign of det α — own idea, Claude (second pass).** For α = (p q; r s) ∈ Gl(2, Z) and
  ξ = [α, ρ] = (A B; C D): mod 3, ρ ≡ −(1 + n) with n = (2 2; 1 1), n² = 0, and a direct computation gives
  D ≡ det(α)·(r − s)² (mod 3). Hence D ≢ 0 (3) implies det α ≡ D (3), which proves the author's suggestion "1 si
  D ≡ 1 (3), −1 si D ≡ −1 (3)". But D ≡ 0 (3) occurs with both signs: α = (1 0; 1 1) gives ξ = (0 1; −1 3) with
  det α = 1, and α = (1 0; −1 −1) gives ξ = (−2 1; 5 −3) with det α = −1; then tr ξ ≡ 0 (3) for det α = 1 and ≡ 1
  for det α = −1 (checked on all 744 α ∈ Gl(2, Z) with entries in [−6, 6]). This is the explicit form of the ℓ = 3
  obstruction Z₃^*/N ≅ Z/2 of scan 263: the sign depends only on ξ mod 3.
- **C3 — scan 250, (57)–(58) — unproved claim, Claude (second pass).** (58) is (15) with ν ≡ 0 (3), and (57) implies
  (14) by the telescoping of N10; the converse, (14) (15) ⟹ (57), goes through α (via (47 bis) with α₃ = 0), so the
  stated equivalence for μ ≡ 1 (3) rests on the existence results that N9 hopes to avoid. Only the conjunction of
  (57) and (58) can be equivalent to (14) (15), which supports the reading "l'ens des deux relations".


# ===== LM_C3-264-282 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-264-282 — research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Scans 264–282 (ms. pp. 433–442, §48 I end and §48 II). Notes 1–43 are the first pass's notes (section 5 of
`answers\unzipped\LM_C3-264-282\LM_C3-264-282_notes.md`), each with a decision: keep, correct or drop. Notes S1–S9 are
new, written in the second pass. References to scans and equation numbers follow `LM_C3-264-282_checked.tex`.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 264 — claim.** *Correct.* "D est inversible ssi N(cρ+d) l'est" follows at once from (23) when ad−bc is a unit, and N_{A_ρ/A}(cρ+d) = det(cρ+d) = c²+cd+d² because Tr ρ = det ρ = 1. The claim is proved on the page; no further hypotheses are needed.
2. **Scan 264 — question ("est-ce que D peut être égal à 0 mod 3 ?").** *Keep.* G's own computation (ξ = (A 1; −1 0), ξρ = (−1 A+1; 0 −1), regular iff A ≠ −1) shows D ≡ 0 mod 3 does occur with ξρ regular, so he leaves the case open. The single excluded point A = −1 is ξ = −ρ⁻¹, the singular point of S5.
3. **Scan 264 — claim without proof, Pic(A_σ) = 0 "(sauf erreur)".** *Correct.* "(sauf erreur)" is now read without doubt; the value is written 0 over 1 (or 1 over 0). The claim is true: σ² = −1, so A_σ = Z[σ] ≅ Z[i], a principal ideal domain (S4).
4. **Scan 265 — claim.** *Correct.* (27) reads η = βσ(β)⁻¹ = [β,σ] with σ(β) = σβσ⁻¹ (S1). Passing from (28)–(29) and Pic = 0 to a solution β ∈ Gl(2,Z) is the Latimer–MacDuffee correspondence: ησ is regular with characteristic polynomial x²+1, Z² is an invertible Z[σ]-module, free since Pic = 0, so ησ is conjugate to σ; the page gives no proof.
5. **Scans 265–266 — implication, (31) and "le reste de U mod 4".** *Correct.* The ink of (31) is "(rt − su)U = t²+u²", but the computation (S2) gives (ru − st)U = t²+u², with ru − st = det β as in (23): the written factor is a slip. The mod-4 description of the obstruction is right for Z and Z₂ (S4).
6. **Scans 266–267 — claim, the Proposition.** *Keep.* The margin "(base quelc.)" is joined by a line to the "ssi" of scan 266, l. 1: the equivalence "U inv. ssi tσ+u inv." is asserted over any base, the residue statements only for Z, Z_ℓ, Ẑ.
7. **Scan 267 — implication, item 3 "automatique".** *Correct (answered).* For ℓ ≠ 3, 3 is a unit of Z_ℓ and Z_ℓ[ρ] is étale over Z_ℓ, so norms of units are onto and nothing is obstructed; likewise ℓ ≠ 2 for σ. Over Ẑ = ∏ Z_ℓ the obstruction sits at ℓ = 3 (resp. 2) only (S4).
8. **Scans 267–268 — expectation that μ is a section of G_m.** *Correct (proved later).* Scan 270 proves it under (42): (43) P(ξ)μ = c with c invertible forces P(ξ) and μ invertible, μ = c/P(ξ) (44), over any base, nilpotents included.
9. **Scan 268 — implication (reduced system vs. lifting α, β).** *Keep.*
10. **Scan 264 — first-pass own idea (enumerate modulo 3ⁿ).** *Keep*, low priority: S5 already locates the decisive point modulo 3.
11. **Scans 267–268 — first-pass own idea (treat the equations as functors).** *Keep* as a methodological remark; it adds nothing specific.
12. **Scan 269, NB — claim λ₀² = 1.** *Correct.* It is proved by one determinant: det(ε₁σ) = det(−λ₀ρ) gives 1 = λ₀² when det ρ = det σ = 1 (ε₁ unipotent). Over an integral A this gives λ₀ = ±1; in general λ₀ ∈ μ₂(A) (note 13).
13. **Scan 269 — first-pass own idea (μ₂ in characteristic 2).** *Keep.*
14. **Scan 270 — implication (c not a unit).** *Keep.*
15. **Scan 271, NB — claim of equivalence with (40 bis).** *Correct.* The sign is now settled: the ink reads "Q′(η) = c^te = λ′₀ Trσ − Trρ". Taking the constant term as for ξ (Tr(ηε₁(μ−1)ρ) − Trρ = P′(η)μ − Q′(η) at μ = 0) gives Q′(η) = Trρ − Tr ηε₁⁻¹ρ = Trρ + λ′₀Trσ = λ′₀c, once ε₁⁻¹ρ = −λ′₀σ, which (40 bis) gives with λ′₀ = 1/λ₀. So the written definition (sign, subscript 0 for 1) and the written value carry slips, while "c′ = λ′₀c" (scan 272) and "c inv. ⟺ c′ inv." are right.
16. **Scan 272 — first-pass own idea (symmetric treatment).** *Keep.*
17. **Scan 272, (47)–(49) — implication.** *Correct.* "N₁² = 1" is confirmed in the ink (a plain stroke) and is a slip for N₁² = 0: the same line says "de carré nul" and (48)–(50) use N₁² = 0. The "λ" before (1−μ) is overwritten by the parenthesis and has no role, since ε₁(1−μ) = 1 + (1−μ)N₁.
18. **Scan 273, Corollary — claim.** *Correct.* "de dim 1" is confirmed in the ink and is a slip for 2 (a trace level set in SG(𝓜), of relative dimension 3). "définit toujours un sous-schéma lisse" is too strong: the level set is singular where ξρ is scalar (S5); the open condition "P(ξ) inversible" removes those points, so the Corollary itself stands.
19. **Scan 273 — first-pass own idea (critical points have P(ξ) = 0).** *Keep*; confirmed by the example in S5.
20. **Scan 273 — implication (P(ξ) = D, P′(η) = U).** *Keep*; scan 274 carries it out: the solution scheme is that of the (D, B) with D invertible.
21. **Scan 274, (55) — claim.** *Keep.* The matrices (μ M; 0 1) = (1/D B/D; 0 1) normalise ε₀(λ) = (1 −λ; 0 1): they form the group B′₀ of scan 280, so (55) already shows the final answer of the section.
22. **Scan 274 — "T − S = 1" and "avec S inv."** *Correct.* Both are firm in the ink. From η = ξε₁(1−μ): S = B, U = D, T = C + (1−μ)D; with μD = 1 and C + D − B = 1 this gives T − S = 0 (cf. (29)) and U = D invertible. So both are slips (for "T − S = 0" and "U inv."), and the rewritten first letter of the second row of (54) must be C.
23. **Scan 275 — implication (the two characters χ, χ′).** *Keep.*
24. **Scan 275 — exact sequence and marginal warning.** *Correct (answered).* In the split model B̃₀* = {(p ℓ; 0 q) : q² = 1} and the kernel of det is {p = q = ±1} = μ₂·L₀, not L₀; the sequence 1 → L₀ → B̃₀* → G_m → 1 is therefore false, as the margin "C'est faux, cf plus bas" says. G reaches this on scan 279 (μ₂) and scan 280 ((71), q² = 1).
25. **Scan 275 — first-pass own idea (p/q = pq).** *Keep*; G carries it out on scan 280, (71).
26. **Scan 276 — expectation (64).** *Keep*; proved for u ∈ L₀ on scan 276 and reduced to the definition of B̃₁* on scans 277–278.
27. **Scan 276 — sign audit.** *Correct (done).* With ε₀(λ) = 1 + λN₀ (58), ε₀(−λ) = 1 − λN₀, so the annotation "1 + N₀λ" and "1 + (σN₀σ⁻¹)λ" carry a sign slip. The conclusion (65) σε₀(λ)σ⁻¹ = ε₁(λ) is still right, because σN₀σ⁻¹ = N₁, and [u,σ] = ε₀(λ)ε₁(−λ) follows.
28. **Scan 277 — implication.** *Keep.*
29. **Scan 278 — question "est-ce un isomorphisme ?"** *Keep*; answered on scans 279–281: the kernel of (66) is μ₂ (69), B̃₀/μ₂ ↪ X_{ρ,σ} (70), and B′₀ ↪ X_{ρ,σ} (76) is asserted to be an isomorphism.
30. **Scan 278 — first-pass own idea (T_ρ ∩ B̃₀ on scalar sections).** *Correct.* G himself finds T_ρ ∩ B̃₀* = μ₂ on scan 279; what remains of the note is that μ₂ is to be taken as a group scheme (non-reduced in characteristic 2).
31. **Scan 279, (68) — claim.** *Keep*, with a proof route: over a field, if 1, ρ, σ are linearly independent, the subalgebra they generate in M₂ has dimension ≥ 3 and its commutant is the scalars. Over S, the commutant is the kernel of x ↦ ([x,ρ],[x,σ]) on 𝓜; O_S·1 is a direct summand, and the induced map 𝓜/O_S → 𝓜² is injective on every fibre, hence locally split injective (Nakayama), so the commutant is O_S and T_σ ∩ T_ρ = G_m over any base.
32. **Scan 279, (69) — implication.** *Keep.*
33. **Scan 280, (70)–(71) — implication.** *Keep.*
34. **Scan 280 — "par un calcul immédiat".** *Correct (verified).* For u = (p ℓ; 0 q) and N₀ = (0 −1; 0 0), uN₀u⁻¹ = (p/q)N₀, so χ′(u) = p/q and χ(u) = pq, which gives (71).
35. **Scan 280, (72)–(74) — claim ("se décompose intrinsèquement").** *Keep*, with a route: locally, where 𝓜 = End(E), Im N₀ = Ker N₀ is a B₀-stable line L, and p(u), q(u) are the characters of u on L and on E/L; a global version should use the one-sided ideal N₀𝓜 or 𝓜N₀ instead of E.
36. **Scan 281 — claim (lisse even in characteristic 2; exact sequence).** *Correct (verified in the split model).* B′₀ = {(p ℓ; 0 1)} ≅ G_a ⋊ G_m is smooth of relative dimension 2 in every characteristic; det = p, and its kernel is the upper unipotent group L₀, so 1 → L₀ → B′₀ → G_m → 1 is exact (the star on L₀ stays doubtful in the ink). "Canonique" needs the intrinsic q of note 35.
37. **Scan 281 — claim ("en vertu des principes généraux … surjectif sur les pts à val. dans un corps alg clos").** *Keep*; S8 gives the principle presumably meant and the hypotheses it needs.
38. **Scan 281 — implication (the remark that starts from ε₁ and ρ).** *Keep.*
39. **Scan 282 — claim (scalar multiples).** *Correct.* The upper-left margin now reads "Je prends la constante qui intervient dans le cas qui nous intéresse le plus, où −σ⁻¹ = σ, de sorte que (40 bis) devient ε₁ = ρσ": the dropped constant is λ₀ = 1. F_ρ and F_σ are unchanged by scalars, but c = Tr N₁ρ scales with ρ, which is what note 42 uses.
40. **Scan 282 — claim (equivalent independence conditions).** *Keep.* Right multiplication by ρ⁻¹ (resp. σ⁻¹) is an automorphism of the module 𝓜 taking 1, ρ, N₁ρ to ρ⁻¹, 1, N₁; with ρ + σ = N₁ρ = −N₁σ this gives all the stated equivalences.
41. **Scan 282, (78 bis) — implication.** *Keep.* The identities follow from N₀ = ρ⁻¹N₁ρ (57) and σN₀σ⁻¹ = N₁ (scan 276): N₁ρ = ρN₀ and N₁σ = σN₀.
42. **Scan 282, lower-left margin — claim (normalisation).** *Correct.* γ₀ (or δ₀) = N₁ρ and Tr N₁ρ = c (main text), so the normalisation is c = 1. Replacing ρ by tρ with ε₁ fixed forces σ ↦ tσ (σ = −ε₁⁻¹ρ) and c ↦ tc, so when c is a unit exactly one t gives c = 1: this is the "normalise entièrement".
43. **Scan 282 — implication (the final Proposition, unfinished at "— on").** *Keep.*

## Second-pass notes (own)

S1. **Scans 265–266 — own note (notation).** The commutator is written [β,σ] = βσ(β)⁻¹ with σ(β) = σβσ⁻¹, the convention of the author's later table for §48 I ("α → αρ(α⁻¹) = [α,ρ]") and §48 III ("αρ(α⁻¹) = βσ(β)⁻¹ε₁(μ−1)"). With this reading the system of the Proposition is the pair of commutator equations; the first pass's alternative (βσ)⁻¹ would make it trivial. Confidence high.

S2. **Scan 265 — own idea (verification).** For β = (r s; t u) and δ = ru − st one finds η = βσβ⁻¹σ⁻¹ = δ⁻¹(r²+s², rt+su; rt+su, t²+u²). So η is symmetric ((29)) and of determinant 1 automatically, and δU = t²+u² = N(tσ+u): this corrects the factor "(rt − su)" written in (31). Confidence high.

S3. **Scan 266 — own note (claim check).** (16) is written "B + D − C = 1", but item 1) of the same Proposition displays ξρ = (−B A+B; −D C+D), whose trace is C + D − B; with Tr ρ = 1 the trace condition is C + D − B = 1, the form used in (54) on scan 274. (16) comes from an earlier page outside this package, so it is either a slip or another convention. Confidence medium.

S4. **Scans 264–267 — own idea (verification of the arithmetic).** A_ρ = Z[ρ] ≅ Z[ζ₆] (ρ² − ρ + 1 = 0) and A_σ ≅ Z[i] are principal, so Pic = 0. Norms of units satisfy c² + cd + d² ≡ (c − d)² ≡ 1 (mod 3) in the ramified Z₃[ρ] and t² + u² ≡ 1 (mod 4) in the ramified Z₂[σ], while for other ℓ the extension is unramified and the norm on units is onto. Hence Z_ℓ*/N(A*) = ±1 exactly at ℓ = 3 (resp. 2), detected by D mod 3 (resp. U mod 4), as items 2)–3) of the Proposition state. Confidence high.

S5. **Scans 264 and 273 — own idea (test case for "toujours lisse").** Over F₃, ξ = −ρ⁻¹ = (−1 1; −1 0) has det ξ = 1 and Tr(ξρ) = −2 = 1 = Tr ρ, while ρξ = −1 is scalar, so the differential of ξ ↦ Tr(ξρ) on SL₂ vanishes there and the level set is singular at that point. There D = P(ξ) = 0, so the open set "P(ξ) inversible" of the Corollary removes it; it is also exactly the point A = −1 where ξρ fails to be regular in G's mod-3 computation on scan 264. Confidence high.

S6. **Scan 269 — own note (supports an uncertain reading).** By (33), η = ξε₁(1−μ), so Tr ησ = Tr σ becomes Tr((ξε₁(1−μ) − 1)σ) = 0. This is exactly what the first pass reads, with doubt, in the overwritten third condition of (36) (σ moved out of the parenthesis, −1 inserted), and the second condition Tr(ξ − 1)ρ = 0 is likewise Tr ξρ = Tr ρ; the reworked formulas are therefore coherent. Confidence medium.

S7. **Scan 267 — own note (cross-reference).** The author's table titles §48 II (p. 434) "L'équation ξ = ηε₁(μ − 1) et le groupe B′₀ ⊂ B_{ρ,σ}" and starts III at p. 443, just after this package. The title names the outcome of scans 280–281, the "bon" group B′₀ (q = 1), not the first candidate B̃₀. Confidence high.

S8. **Scan 281 — own idea (the "principes généraux").** One route: a monomorphism locally of finite presentation between smooth S-schemes of the same relative dimension (here 2: B′₀ ≅ G_a ⋊ G_m, and X_{ρ,σ} by the Corollary of scan 273) is unramified, hence étale on fibres (an injective tangent map between spaces of equal dimension), hence flat by the fibrewise criterion (EGA IV 11.3.10) and étale; an étale monomorphism is an open immersion (EGA IV 17.9.1), so surjectivity on geometric points makes it an isomorphism. This uses the smoothness of X_{ρ,σ}, i.e. the open condition P(ξ) inversible (S5). Confidence medium.

S9. **Scan 274 — own note (link to the final group).** In the explicit case the identification (55) sends a solution to (1/D B/D; 0 1), and μ = 1/D is its determinant, which is the character det of B′₀; so the isomorphism B′₀ ≅ X_{ρ,σ} asserted on scan 281 can be checked directly here, with (66) u ↦ ([u,ρ], [u,σ], det u) restricted to q = 1. Confidence medium.


# ===== LM_C3-283-301 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-283-301 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 283–301)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-283-301/LM_C3-283-301_notes.md, §5, 43 notes), each
checked against LM_C3-283-301_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second
pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, programme), unproved claim,
unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks (C0): ρ(x) = ρxρ⁻¹, so ζ = [α,ρ] = αρα⁻¹ρ⁻¹ and η = βσβ⁻¹σ⁻¹; matrices act on
columns, as in (31) on scan 300 (N₁e₀ = e₁, N₁e₁ = 0, N₁ = (0 0; 1 0)). In the numerical case of scan 288,
ρ = (0 1; −1 1), σ = (0 −1; 1 0), ε₁ = −ρσ⁻¹ = (1 0; 1 1), ε₀ = ρ⁻¹ε₁ρ = σ⁻¹ε₁σ = (1 −1; 0 1); so L₀ is the upper
unipotent group, B₀ the upper triangular Borel and B'₀ = {(x y; 0 1)}. With these, δD = c²+cd+d² and δ′U = t²+u² on
scan 288 come out as written, and (74), (79), (90) can be checked by hand.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 283–287

- **283-R1 — scan 283, margin NB — unproved claim — correct.** The numerals are 2°), 3°), not 29), 30): fibrewise
  independence of 1, ρ, σ (2°) follows from invertibility of Tr N₁ρ (3°). The margin calculation proves it once there is
  a local basis with N₁e₀ = e₁, N₁e₁ = 0 and ρe₀ ∈ D₁: then ρ = (0 u; a b), Tr N₁ρ = u, and after u = 1,
  σ = −ε₁⁻¹ρ = (0 −1; −a 1−b) and ρ+σ = diag(0,1), so 1, ρ, σ span the same module as 1, diag(0,1), ρ, free of rank 3
  on every fibre because the upper-right entry of ρ is 1. Such a basis exists exactly when D₀ = ρ⁻¹D₁ is a complement
  of D₁, which is the proposition of scans 297–298 ((i) ⟺ (iii), with c = Tr N₁ρ).
- **283-R2 — scan 283, exact sequence and character — unproved claim — correct.** The ink has B₀ without prime in all
  three places (checked enlarged), but both statements hold only for B'₀: for the Borel B₀ the kernel of det is the
  Borel subgroup of SG, of relative dimension 2, and u = diag(x, z) acts on N₀ by x/z, not by det u = xz. For
  B'₀ = {(x y; 0 1)} (C0) one has uN₀u⁻¹ = xN₀ = det(u)N₀ and ker det = L₀, as (74) on scan 281 states; so this is a
  slip of the pen for B'₀, not a gap.
- **283-R3 — scan 283, final morphism — own idea — keep.** The computation it proposes can be done in the numerical case
  of scan 288, where B'₀ = {(x y; 0 1)} is explicit (C0).
- **284-R1 — scan 284, (79) and Corollaire — unproved claim — keep.** The proof strategy is on scan 282 ("il suffit …
  de montrer que c'est surjectif sur les pts à val. dans un corps alg clos … Je laisse tomber le détail des
  vérifications"), so the verifications are left out by the author. Both projections have source 𝒳_ρ in the ink
  (checked), where 𝒳_{ρ,σ} is meant.
- **284-R2 — scan 284, (80) and Scholie — unpursued implication — keep.** On 𝒳_{ρ,σ} the third coordinate is
  μ = 1/Tr ζ(ρ+σ) = 1/Tr η(ρ+σ), with ρ+σ = −N₁σ = N₁ρ = ρN₀ = −σN₀ (the insertion under the trace, now transcribed with
  its brace); the transported group law is made explicit on scan 285.
- **285-R1 — scan 285, (81) — unpursued implication — correct.** (81) is the cocycle identity and is immediate:
  [u′u, ρ] = u′(uρu⁻¹ρ⁻¹)u′⁻¹ · u′ρu′⁻¹ρ⁻¹ = u′(ζ)ζ′, and μ″ = det(u′u) = μμ′. The note's question, a law written in
  (ζ, η, μ) alone without u′, remains open and needs the inverse of (79).
- **285-R2 — scan 285, (83) épimorphisme — unproved claim — correct.** Given (79) and (87), (83) is the projection
  B'₀ × T_ρ × T_σ → B'₀ ≅ 𝒳_{ρ,σ}, because [ua⁻¹, ρ] = [u, ρ] for a commuting with ρ; so it is a split epimorphism,
  split by (84). What remains unproved here is (87) itself (and (79)).
- **286-R1 — scan 286, (84)–(85) — unproved claim — correct.** That (84) lands in 𝒢_{ρ,σ} is immediate from (79):
  ([u,ρ],[u,σ],det u) ∈ 𝒳_{ρ,σ} says [u,ρ] = [u,σ]ε₁(det u − 1), which is (82) for (u, u, det u). The description of all
  diagonal solutions, which the author postpones ("qu'on tâchera de déterminer aussi"), follows from (87)–(90): see O2.
- **286-R2 — scan 286, tuple and (86) — unpursued implication — keep.** The tuple has (ua, ub) and (86), (89) have
  (ua⁻¹, ub⁻¹) (both checked on the ink); T_ρ and T_σ are groups, so the two parametrisations differ by a ↦ a⁻¹, and
  (89) fixes the form used in the theorem.
- **287-R1 — scan 287, (87)–(90) — unproved claim — correct.** (90) gives the uniqueness of a, b in (88): if αa and αa′
  lie in B'₀ then a⁻¹a′ ∈ T_ρ ∩ B'₀ = {1}; the ink's "l'unicité de α, β" is a slip for a, b, as is "[β,ρ]" for [β,σ]
  in the line before (89). In the numerical case (90) holds as schemes: cρ + d = (d c; −c c+d) lies in B'₀ iff c = 0 and
  d = 1, and tσ + u = (u −t; t u) iff t = 0 and u = 1.
- **287-R2 — scan 287, product parametrisation — own idea — keep.** (87) is an isomorphism of schemes; which group law
  the product B'₀ × T_ρ × T_σ carries is not stated on these pages.

### Scans 288–292

- **B1 — scan 288, (94)–(95) — unproved claim — correct.** The denominators are units wherever α, β occur in a
  solution: αa ∈ B'₀ with a = c₀ρ + d₀ forces (c₀, d₀) = t(c, d) with t(c²+cd+d²) = 1, and likewise for β. Checking the
  formulas themselves shows (94) right and (95) wrong with the σ written on the page: see O1.
- **B2 — scan 288, margin "Il faudra revenir sur ces formules dans le cas général" — conjecture (programme) — keep.**
  (94)–(95) are tied to the numerical ρ, σ; in general only the shape a = c₀ρ + d₀, b = t₀σ + u₀ ((91)–(92)) is stated.
- **B3 — scans 288–289, the factor τ∞^α — conjecture (expectation) — correct.** The author resolves the apparent
  incompatibility on scan 289: loc. cit. wrote the equation of the loops ("des lacets", now read) with a sign,
  ζ = εηℓ₁^ν with ℓ₁^ν = ε₁(μ−1) in the present notation, and on p. 398' the factor τ∞^{α₂} carries that sign
  ((−1)^{α₂} = ε), whereas the present theory assumes ε = +1. What remains is a stated generalisation (B4).
- **B4 — scan 289, (95|96) — conjecture (programme) — keep.** The author will redo the theory for ζ = ε.ηε₁(μ−1) with ε a
  section of 𝒪_S, ε² = 1 by determinants, "sans doute" without assuming ε ∈ {±1}, after finishing the case ε = +1. The
  Circle table's §48.V (p. 454, "L'équation αρ(α⁻¹) = (βσβ⁻¹)ε₁(µ−1); le groupe G*_{ρ,σ}", symbols partly lost in
  extraction) is a lead for where this is done; not checked.
- **B5 — scan 289, ε ∈ {±1} — own idea — keep.** A = F₂[t]/(t²), ε = 1 + t: ε² = 1 and ε ≠ ±1. The author's own caveat
  on scan 295, "en car. 2 λ₀ n'est déterminé que mod μ₂ !!!", is the same phenomenon, so μ₂ (non-reduced in
  characteristic 2) is the natural home both for ε and for the ambiguity of λ₀.
- **B6 — scan 290, forms and P¹-bundles — unproved claim — keep.**
- **B7 — scan 290, bijections induced by SG → G → PG — unproved claim — keep.**
- **B8 — scans 290–291, quotient by homotheties and the conic — own idea — keep.** See also O4 on the definition (1) of
  the conic.
- **B9 — scan 291, a ∈ Γ(S, 𝒪_S*) — unproved claim — correct.** Immediate: N and N′ = aN are non-zero on every fibre, so
  a(s) ≠ 0 at every point and a is a unit.
- **B10 — scan 291, the trace identities — own idea — correct.** The ink "2 Tr λ" (checked enlarged) makes the trace
  identities false as written: for a scalar λ, Tr(λ + N) = 2λ + Tr N, so either "Tr λ" is the trace of the scalar
  operator (= 2λ) and the 2 is redundant, or "Tr" is. The determinant identities are right, and their use (λ = 1:
  (3) ⟺ (1)) is unaffected.
- **B11 — scan 292, λ ∈ ΓG_m — unproved claim — correct.** Immediate as in B9: e′ = 1 + λN is ≠ 1 on every fibre, so
  λ(s)N(s) ≠ 0 and λ is a unit; the ink says "on aura néc.ᵗ λ ∈ ΓG_m".
- **B12 — scan 292, normalisers and Borels — unproved claim — keep.**
- **B13 — scan 292, the affine line E = 𝒫 ∖ {x∞} — unpursued implication — correct.** The package pursues it: the margin
  of scan 294 identifies L with the translation bundle of 𝒫 ∖ {x}; scan 296 takes F to be the extension of 𝒪_S by 𝒩 that
  gives this affine line and (20)–(21) identify 𝒩 = D°⊗D; (27) on scan 299 identifies U₁ = 𝒫 ∖ {x₁} with L₁ once x₀ is
  chosen.

### Scans 293–297

- **C1 — scan 293, vector-bundle structure on L — unproved claim — keep.**
- **C2 — scan 294 margin, L ≅ translations of 𝒫 ∖ {x} — unproved claim — correct.** It follows from (20)–(21): the lines
  D′ complementary to D are the images of the sections of F → F/D, a torsor under Hom(F/D, D) = D°⊗D = 𝒩, and 1 + u
  (u ∈ 𝒩) acts on them by translation by u.
- **C3 — scan 294, (10) — unproved claim — correct.** The first identification is false as written (C4); the other four
  are the standard ones (automorphisms of SG and of PG, Skolem–Noether for M, automorphisms of a P¹-bundle).
- **C4 — scan 294, (10) — own idea — correct (settled).** g ↦ det(g)⁻¹g is an automorphism of Gl(2)_S (a homomorphism
  because scalars are central, and its own inverse) that is not inner: it sends λ·1 to λ⁻¹·1, while inner automorphisms
  fix the centre. So PG → Aut_{S-gr}(G) is not an isomorphism for G = Gl(2)_S or its forms. What the argument uses, that
  ρ, σ act on G through their images in PG, is unaffected.
- **C5 — scan 295, (14)–(16) — unproved claim — keep.** L₁ → PG is a closed immersion (1 + λN₁ scalar forces λ = 0,
  since N₁ is regular and so has locally a unit off-diagonal entry), so ε̃₁ has a unique lift ε₁ in L₁; how (16) then determines λ₀ is C6.
- **C6 — scan 295, NB — unproved claim — correct.** The caveat is now read: "(Mais en car. 2 λ₀ n'est déterminé que mod
  μ₂ !!!)". Proof of the whole NB: ε₁ = −λ₀ρσ⁻¹ unipotent means det = 1 and Tr = 2, i.e. λ₀² det(ρσ⁻¹) = 1 and
  −λ₀Tr(ρσ⁻¹) = 2; two solutions differ by θ with θ² = 1 and 2(θ − 1) = 0, so θ = 1 when 2 is regular, and in
  characteristic 2 only θ ∈ μ₂ is left.
- **C7 — scan 295, rescaling by 1 + δ in characteristic 2 — own idea — correct.** This is the author's "mod μ₂": over
  k[δ]/(δ²), char 2, θ = 1 + δ gives θε₁ with det 1 and trace 2, but θε₁ = 1 + δ + (1+δ)N₁ ∉ L₁. So in characteristic 2
  the normalisation (16) does not fix ε₁; its definition (15) as the lift in L₁ of ε̃₁ does.
- **C8 — scan 296, F as an extension — unproved claim — keep.**
- **C9 — scan 296, (20)–(21) — own idea — correct.** Reading 𝒫 = P(F) as the bundle of lines D ⊂ F (as the text does),
  the tangent line at x_D is Hom(D, F/D), and 𝒩 = D°⊗D = Hom(F/D, D) is its dual, the cotangent line at x_D; by C2 it
  is also the translation bundle of 𝒫 ∖ {x_D}. The V/V̌ convention in (9) was not checked against the author's.
- **C10 — scan 297, reduction to an algebraically closed field — unproved claim — correct.** (22) needs no reduction:
  ρ̃σ̃⁻¹ ∈ ΓL̃₁ normalises L̃₁, so ρ̃⁻¹(L₁) = σ̃⁻¹(L₁) as subgroup schemes. The reduction serves (i) ⟺ (iii), whose
  conditions are fibrewise (c a unit; ε₁ ≠ 1 and ρ̃⁻¹(x₁) ≠ x₁ on every fibre), so passing to geometric points is
  legitimate; scan 282 appeals to the same "principes généraux".
- **C11 — scan 297, (iii) as a principal open — unpursued implication — keep.** See O3: (iii) does not depend on the
  choice of λ₀.

### Scans 298–301

- **D1 — scan 298, matrix calculation — own idea — correct (settled).** With the conventions of (31) (C0),
  σ = (a b; c d) gives σe₁ = be₀ + de₁; the ink's "σe₁ = ae₀ + be₁" (checked; b and the last index are retraced) is a
  slip. The conclusion is right: Tr N₁σ = b and D + σD = D + 𝒪_S·be₀, which is F iff b is a unit.
- **D2 — scan 298, (23)–(25), "tore (maximal)" — unproved claim — keep.**
- **D3 — scan 299, opposite Borels ↔ maximal tori of B₁ — unproved claim — correct.** Over a base both sides are
  L₁-torsors: L₁ acts simply transitively by conjugation on the maximal tori of B₁ = T.L₁, and by translation on the
  sections x₀ of U₁ = 𝒫 ∖ {x₁} ((27)); B₀ ↦ B₀ ∩ B₁ is L₁-equivariant, hence bijective.
- **D4 — scan 299, (27) — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **D5 — scan 300, (28), "épinglage complet" — unproved claim — correct.** In the normal form (31)–(32) the three
  sections are x₁ = [e₁], x₀ = [e₀] and ε₁x₀ = [e₀ + e₁], the points ∞, 0, 1 of P¹_S up to the labelling; the claim is
  that (ρ, σ) with the regularity conditions give this pinning canonically.
- **D6 — scan 300, (29)–(31) — own idea — keep.** After D₁ ≃ 𝒪_S, e₁ is fixed up to a unit and e₀ is then fixed by
  N₁e₀ = e₁ (30); the model is canonical only up to that unit.
- **D7 — scan 301, (33) — unproved claim — keep.** The residual freedom is a common scalar, (ρ, σ) ↦ (λρ, λσ), which
  preserves ρσ⁻¹ = −ε₁; (34) fixes it by the upper-right entry, a unit since det ρ = −uv is.
- **D8 — scan 301, (*) and (34) — own idea — correct.** Done: (33) gives σ = −ε₁⁻¹ρ = (0 −1; −v 1−w) and
  ρ + σ = diag(0, 1), the margin normal form of scan 283 with (a, b) = (v, w) and (37) at the top of scan 302; v = −1,
  w = 1 is the numerical case of scan 288. Nothing changes in characteristic 2.

Verdicts: 43 notes; keep 20, correct 23, drop 0.

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

- **O1 — scan 288, (95) — own idea — Claude (second pass).** With the σ on the page, σ = (0 −1; 1 0), formula (95) fails:
  β(tσ + u) has lower-left entry 2tu, and β = σ (t = 1, u = 0) gives b = σ, βb = σ² = −1 ∉ B'₀. The formula that works
  is b = (u − tσ)/(t² + u²), i.e. (tσ∞ + u)/(t² + u²) with σ∞ = σ⁻¹ = (0 1; −1 0), the matrix of p. 398'; (94) is right,
  since α(cρ + d) = (δ, ac+bc+bd; 0, c²+cd+d²). Confidence high (explicit 2×2 computation with C0); it bears out the
  author's "sauf erreur".
- **O2 — scan 286, (85) — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** The diagonal solutions follow from (87)–(90):
  α = β in (89) forces a = b ∈ T_ρ ∩ T_σ, the common centraliser of ρ and σ, which is G_m when 1, ρ, σ are independent
  on every fibre (2°). So the solutions of (85) are the pairs (u, μ) with u ∈ Γ(B₀), B₀ = B'₀·G_m, and μ the character
  by which u acts on N₀ (u(N₀) = μN₀). Confidence medium: in the numerical case T_ρ ∩ T_σ = G_m holds as schemes
  (cρ + d commutes with σ iff c = 0); the general case needs the same check.
- **O3 — scans 297–298, condition (iii) — own idea — Claude (second pass).** (iii) does not depend on the choice of λ₀
  left open in characteristic 2: two admissible values differ by θ with θ² = 1 and 2(θ − 1) = 0, so
  (θ − 1)² = −2(θ − 1) = 0, and c changes by the square-zero element (θ − 1)λ₀Tr ρ. So "c = λ₀Tr ρ + Tr σ inversible"
  is well posed over every base. Confidence high.
- **O4 — scan 290, (1) — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** The ink replaces a struck "N² = 0" by (1), "det N
  = 0, Tr N = 0, d'où N² = 0". For sections non-zero on every fibre the two agree: Cayley–Hamilton gives
  Tr(N)·N = det(N)·1, and locally N has a unit off-diagonal entry, which forces Tr N = 0 and then det N = 0. Without
  regularity they differ (N = δ·1, δ² = 0, 2 ≠ 0: N² = 0 but Tr N = 2δ), so (1) is the sound definition of the conic.


# ===== LM_C3-302-320 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-302-320 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 302–320)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-302-320/LM_C3-302-320_notes.md, §4, notes 1–39), each
checked against LM_C3-302-320_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second
pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, programme), unproved claim, unpursued
implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks (those of the pages): after the normalisation u = 1, ρ = (0 1; v w), σ = (0 −1; −v 1−w)
(on scans 306–307 ρ = (0 v₁; v w), σ = (0 v′₁; v′ w′)), N = ρ + σ = (0 0; 0 1), N₀ = (0 1/v; 0 0),
N₁ = ρN₀ρ⁻¹ = σN₀σ⁻¹ = (0 0; 1 0), ε₀ = 1 + N₀, ε₁ = 1 + N₁, ε₁(t) = 1 + tN₁, [x,y] = xyx⁻¹y⁻¹ and ρ(x) = ρxρ⁻¹. With
these, σ⁻¹ρ = −ε₀ (39 bis), ρσ⁻¹ = −ε₁, (42), (46), (47), (47 bis), ζ = ηε₁(μ−1), (54), (56)–(58) and (36)–(37) of
scan 320 were all recomputed and hold, except for the slips listed below.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 302–305

- **N1 — scan 302, lower-left NB — unproved claim — correct.** The claim holds over any base: 1, ρ, N = ρ+σ and
  ρN = (0 1; 0 w) have coordinate determinant v, a unit because det ρ = −v, so they form a basis of M₂(O_S). Hence 1, ρ, σ
  are independent on every fibre, the commutant of {ρ, σ} is O_S, and T_ρ ∩ T_σ = G_m, T̃_ρ ∩ T̃_σ = {1} hold
  scheme-theoretically. (Revised: the first pass only asked for a proof.)
- **N2 — scan 302, last paragraph — unproved claim — correct.** "u = αv" is immediate: the chain gives N = ρN₀ =
  (0 0; 0 vα) and the display gives N = (0 0; 0 u). The inserted "N₀ (= ρN₁ρ⁻¹)" is a different matter, see C1.
- **N3 — scan 302, (37 bis) — own idea — correct.** An idempotent always splits the module, so the proviso "when image
  and kernel are locally direct summands" is automatic; with Tr N = c = 1 both are invertible modules and N gives a
  canonical splitting O_S² = Im N ⊕ Ker N that the normalisation u = 1 turns into coordinates. The question of a
  basis-free reading of the normalisations stands.
- **N4 — scan 303, after the γ display — unpursued implication — keep.** The page gives no reason for preferring
  μ = γ + 1; scan 319 ((31)–(32)) supplies one meaning, μ = det u = δ(ũ), the factor by which ũ acts on N₀ (see N34).
- **N5 — scan 303, (40) — own idea — keep.** P(ζ) = D makes the locus "D invertible" the natural domain, as (42),
  (58) and scan 320 confirm.
- **N6 — scan 304, (41)–(43) — unpursued implication — keep.** Nothing on these pages treats D = 0. The last member of
  (41) reads "Tr σ|α" in the ink (unresolved); only Tr σ fits (II) and the use on scan 309.
- **N7 — scan 304, before (45) — unproved claim — correct.** The announced calculation is complete on scan 305: every
  entry of [u,ρ], [u,σ] and (46)–(48) was recomputed and holds (apart from the slip of C3), and (48) is the inverse on
  the open set where D is invertible.
- **N8 — scan 305, (46)–(48) — own idea — drop.** The manuscript does it itself on scan 306: (47 bis)–(48 bis) recover
  μ, λ from η (S = B, U = D, u = (1/U S/U; 0 1)).

### Scans 306–309

- **N9 — scan 306, NB beside (54) — unproved claim — correct.** Every term of (−δ_α vv₁)B and (−δ_α vv₁)D contains v₁,
  and v₁ is a unit because det ρ = −vv₁ is; so the division is legitimate over any base, and (56) carries it out by
  striking v₁.
- **N10 — scan 306, left margin — own idea — correct.** The margin now reads "On ne pose pas v₁ = 1, pour n'avoir pas à
  refaire le m̄ calcul pour [β,σ] que pour [α,ρ] !": σ has v′₁ = −v₁, which cannot also be 1, so one general computation
  serves both commutators, and (55) is (54) with (a,b,c,d,v,v₁,w) replaced by (r,s,t,u,v′,v′₁,w′).
- **N11 — scan 307, (56)–(58) — unproved claim — correct.** Verified: for v₁ = 1 the numerator −vd² + wcd + c² equals
  det(dρ + c) and the denominator −vδ_α equals det ρ · det α, so (58) holds whenever det α is a unit.
- **N12 — scan 307, (57)–(58) — own idea — keep.** The principal open set det(dρ + c) invertible is exactly the domain
  of u(α) = (1/D B/D; 0 1); see also N39.
- **N13 — scan 308, before (1) — conjecture — keep.** The projective version (1) is posed as a programme; scans 309–319
  carry it out (G̃*_{ρ,σ}, (9)–(13), (30)).
- **N14 — scan 308, last paragraph — unproved claim — correct.** Both points are immediate: [λα,ρ] = [α,ρ] for a unit λ,
  so (2) involves only α̃, β̃, and ε = [α,ρ] ε₁(μ−1)⁻¹ [β,σ]⁻¹ is determined.
- **N15 — scan 309, "ε Tr σ = Tr σ" — unpursued implication — keep.** The condition says (ε − 1)Tr σ = 0 for the given
  ε; the lemma of scan 310 asks for it for all sections of μ₂.
- **N16 — scan 309, compared with scan 310 — own idea — correct.** Resolved: "Tr η" on scan 309 abbreviates Tr(ησ).
  Since η₀σ = βσβ⁻¹, Tr(η₀σ) = Tr σ, so Tr(ησ) = ε Tr σ, which is the underbrace "ε Tr η₀ = ε Tr σ" read with the
  factor σ; (41) and the lemma state the condition in full.

### Scans 310–313

- **N17 — scan 310, "E^{μ₂} = (0)" — unproved claim — keep.** As stated; scan 314 gives the argument over S
  ("faisant varier ε") and with ε = −1 when 2 is invertible.
- **N18 — scan 310, insertion "Enfin …" — unproved claim — correct.** Add the label history: c) "σ² ∈ ΓG_m" was struck,
  the normal-form condition (labelled d) over c), unresolved) became the third condition, and "d) On a σ² ∈ ΓG_m" was
  added in the margin. The sentence "L'équivalence de b) et d) est immédiate, reste à prouver b) ⟺ c)" keeps the labels
  of the first version (c) = σ² scalar, d) = normal form); Hamilton–Cayley gives b) ⇒ σ² scalar and, for σ nowhere
  scalar, the converse.
- **N19 — scan 310, fixed points — own idea — keep.** Testing with non-reduced points of μ₂ in characteristic 2 is the
  right check of "a) ⟺ b)"; scan 314 ((e), (f)) separates the two cases in the same way.
- **N20 — scan 311, first paragraph — own idea — correct.** Enlarged, the ink has "Tr σ = 0 ⟹ σ = det σ.1" (no exponent,
  no sign) and "(Tr σ)σ = σ² − det σ.1"; Hamilton–Cayley gives σ² = −det σ · 1 and (Tr σ)σ = σ² + det σ · 1. Both are
  slips of the pen; the argument only uses that both sides are scalars and is unaffected.
- **N21 — scan 311, converse — unproved claim — correct.** The gap closes by Nakayama: if σ is nowhere scalar, 1 and σ
  are independent on every fibre, so O_S · 1 ⊕ O_S · σ is a direct summand of the Azumaya algebra, and (Tr σ)σ ∈ O_S · 1
  forces Tr σ = 0 also over non-reduced bases.
- **N22 — scan 311, (8)–(9) — unproved claim — keep.** The comparison of G* with the inverse image of G̃* needs local
  liftability of PG-sections to G (G → PG is a G_m-torsor), which the page does not discuss.
- **N23 — scan 312, (14)–(15) — unproved claim — keep.** The fibre description is stated, not proved; it is used on
  scan 313.
- **N24 — scan 313, uniqueness — unproved claim — correct.** The subscript reads α in the ink (T_α, a slip for T_ρ,
  see C9), and the intersection T_ρ ∩ B′₀ = {1} holds scheme-theoretically (C9).

### Scans 314–317

- **N25 — scan 314, corollary (e)–(f) — unpursued implication — keep.** The corrected heading reads "les conditions
  équiv. du lemme sont impliquées par les suivantes ; et l'impliquent si σ est régulière :"; the "aussi" of the first
  pass is not in the ink.
- **N26 — scan 315, opening — unproved claim — keep.** The local conjugacy of regular sections with equal trace and
  determinant (for GL₂, fppf or étale locally) is quoted from Steinberg, not proved.
- **N27 — scan 315, (23) — unproved claim — keep.** Exactness at T_σ is the definition of the centraliser; local
  surjectivity onto μ₂ is the argument (e) of scan 314–315 (εσ is regular with the same trace and determinant).
- **N28 — scan 316, after (24) — unpursued implication — correct.** As corrected (changes, row 34), the ink says
  "toute section de N′_σ est en fait une section de N_σ", the trivial inclusion; the argument (2b = 0 with 2 a
  non-zero-divisor gives b = 0, then det gives a² = 1) proves the converse N_σ(S) ⊂ N′_σ(S), which is what is meant. It
  is a statement about sections over S and does not survive base change to a base where 2 becomes a zero-divisor.
- **N29 — scan 316, characteristic 2 — own idea — keep.** Checked: with σ = I + J, τ = diag(a,1), τστ⁻¹ = I + aJ and
  [τ,σ] = I + (a+1)J, scalar only for a = 1; so τ ∈ N_σ but τ ∉ N′_σ for a ≠ 1.
- **N30 — scan 316, the two equations — own idea — keep.** C11 solves the system explicitly.
- **N31 — scan 317, (25)–(26) — unproved claim — correct.** Existence is immediate from the text's own data: given
  (α,β,μ,ε) in G*, take u ∈ B′₀ with ([u,ρ],[u,σ],det u) = ([α,ρ], ε[β,σ], μ) (B′₀ ≅ X_{ρ,σ}); then a = α⁻¹u ∈ T_ρ and
  b = β⁻¹u satisfies bσb⁻¹ = εσ, so b ∈ N′_σ with ε_σ(b) = ε. With the monomorphy proved on the page, (26) is an
  isomorphism.

### Scans 318–320

- **N32 — scan 318, (27)–(28) — own idea — keep.** (28) is sufficient only together with ũ′ = ũ; the necessity
  argument of the page indeed yields ũ = ũ′ first.
- **N33 — scan 318, (29)–(30) — unproved claim — correct.** (29) holds over any base: if x = c + dρ ∈ T_ρ equals λy with
  y ∈ B′₀ and λ a unit, the (2,1) entry gives dv = 0, so d = 0 and x is scalar, x̃ = 1. The rest of the note stands.
- **N34 — scan 319, (31)–(32) — unpursued implication — keep.** With u = (μ λ; 0 1), uN₀u⁻¹ = μN₀, so (32) does give
  δ(ũ) = μ = det u and is independent of the representative; this is the intrinsic meaning of μ asked for in N4.
- **N35 — scan 319, right NB — unproved claim — keep.** C13 gives the short proof.
- **N36 — scan 319, left NB — own idea — correct.** The count is consistent: B̃′₀, T̃_ρ, Ñ′_σ have relative dimensions
  2, 1, 1 and α̃ has 3, the remaining 1 being the operation of Ñ′_σ on β̃ inserted in the remark ("mod op. de Ñ′_σ sur
  β"). In the NB the middle factor is a T written over an A.
- **N37 — scan 319, η̃ = ζ̃ε̃₁(μ−1) — own idea — correct.** Verified slip: ζ = ηε₁(μ−1) by (41), and η = ζε₁(1−μ) is
  written on scan 304 and checked on (46); "ε̃₁(μ−1)" on scan 319 should be ε̃₁(1−μ).
- **N38 — scan 320, (33)–(37) — unpursued implication — keep.** The complement det(dρ + c) = 0 is not studied here.
- **N39 — scan 320, (37) — own idea — correct.** Verified: O_S[ρ] = O_S[X]/(X² − wX − v) is free with basis 1, ρ, and
  the norm of c + dρ is c² + cd Tr ρ + d² det ρ = c² + cdw − vd² = det(c + dρ). So (−δv)D is the norm of c + dρ and the
  condition of (58) is independent of the basis.

## Claude (second pass) — new notes

- **C1 — scan 302, insertion "N₀ (= ρN₁ρ⁻¹)" — own idea.** With N₁ = ρN₀ρ⁻¹, stated three lines above, one has
  N₀ = ρ⁻¹N₁ρ; with the normalised ρ, ρN₁ρ⁻¹ = (−w/v 1/v; −w²/v w/v), equal to N₀ only when w = 0. The insertion was
  enlarged and reads ρN₁ρ⁻¹; it is kept as written. Confidence high (direct multiplication).
- **C2 — scan 303, the γ display — own idea.** The recalled form "[α,ρ] = [β,ρ]ε₁(γ)" has ρ in the second bracket (its
  descender is visible); (32), (49) and (*) have [β,σ]. A slip of the pen, kept in the transcription.
- **C3 — scan 305, second factor of [u,σ] — own idea.** (1 −λ; 0 μ)(w′ 1; −v′ 0) has lower-left entry −μv′; the ink's
  −μw′ is a slip, and the next line is computed with −μv′, so no later formula is affected.
- **C4 — scan 306, (52) — own idea.** ρ + σ = N = (0 0; 0 v₁) forces v′ = −v, v′₁ = −v₁ and w + w′ = v₁, the last of
  which is written; the value of v′₁ seems to carry a prime in the ink (−v′, unresolved), which would be a slip, and (55)
  is computed with v′₁ = −v₁ (v′v′₁ = vv₁, v′₁² = v₁²).
- **C5 — scan 307, (55) — own idea.** Recomputing η = [β,σ] gives (−vv₁δ_β)S = −(v₁v)su + v₁²tr − v₁w′st. The enlarged
  ink has no minus before (v₁v)su, while the U formula has it: a sign slip in S only, kept in the transcription.
- **C6 — scan 307, last line — unpursued implication.** After (58) the text breaks off ("mais je m'aperçois que c'est une
  forme plate,", last word unresolved) and §V opens with the projective equation and the condition Tr σ = 0. The explicit
  completion condition returns on scan 320 ((33)–(37)), now with w = 1; what the author noticed is not stated.
- **C7 — scan 308, heading — own idea.** The heading is "V Étude (de l'équation) dans GP(1), et la condition Tr σ = 0",
  while the author's later table (Grothendieck Circle) titles §48.V "L'équation αρ(α⁻¹) = ε(βσβ⁻¹)ε₁(μ−1); le groupe
  G*_{ρ,σ}", naming equation (2) and the group G* that the section introduces on scans 311–317.
- **C8 — scan 312, (13) — own idea.** The relation is written "=" (changes, row 27), but by (12) and "‖ déf ε̃⁻¹(1)",
  G̃_{ρ,σ} is an open and closed subscheme of G̃*_{ρ,σ}, proper as soon as ε̃ takes the value −1, which (23) and (26)
  provide locally when 2 is invertible. "⊂" is meant.
- **C9 — scan 313, uniqueness — own idea.** The display reads T_α ∩ B′₀ = {1}; T_ρ is meant, as on scans 317–318. It
  holds over any base: c + dρ = (c d; dv c+dw) lies in B′₀ iff dv = 0 and c + dw = 1, i.e. (v being a unit) d = 0, c = 1.
- **C10 — scan 316, "donc si b est inv." — own idea.** Enlarged, the letter is the b of "2b", not a 2; the step
  2b = 0 ⇒ b = 0 needs 2 invertible, as (24) ("en car. résiduelle ≠ 2") and the next paragraph show. A slip, kept.
- **C11 — scan 316, the system in characteristic 2 — own idea.** In characteristic 2, −va² + b² = −v reads
  b² = v(a + 1)². Over a field containing s with s² = v every unit a gives b = s(a + 1), a one-parameter family of
  normalising τ outside N′_σ; if v is not a square only a = 1, b = 0 occur on the field's points, and over k[t]/(t²) every
  b = tc with a = 1 occurs. So N_σ ≠ N′_σ as schemes in characteristic 2 even where field points agree. Confidence high.
- **C12 — scan 317, "En effet" — own idea.** The second line reads βρ(β)⁻¹ (ρ with its descender, kept) where βσ(β)⁻¹ is
  computed; the rest of the line, u b⁻¹σ(b⁻¹)⁻¹σ(u)⁻¹ = εuσ(u)⁻¹, concerns σ.
- **C13 — scan 319, right NB — own idea (proof of an unproved claim).** τ̃ centralises σ̃ in PG iff τστ⁻¹ = λσ for a
  unit λ; taking determinants, λ² det σ = det σ, so λ ∈ μ₂ and τ ∈ N′_σ with ε_σ(τ) = λ. Hence the centraliser of σ̃ is
  Ñ′_σ, an extension of μ₂ by T̃_σ by (23).
- **C14 — scan 319, lower-left NB — own idea.** ζ = αρ(α)⁻¹ does not depend on the lift α of α̃: for a unit λ,
  ρ(λα) = λρ(α), so (λα)ρ(λα)⁻¹ = αρ(α)⁻¹. The reading "ζ ne dépend [pas] du choix du relèvement" is the true
  statement; if the heavy bar through "ne" (unresolved) is a strike, the note as corrected would be false.
- **C15 — scan 320, "(α,β,μ,ε) ∈ ΓG…_{ρ,σ}" — own idea.** The scheme meant is the G*_{ρ,σ} of (8) and (26); the
  α-like superscript of the ink (changes, row 41) is a slip or a hasty star.


# ===== LM_C3-321-339 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-321-339 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 321–339)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-321-339/LM_C3-321-339_notes.md, §5: 12 + 13 + 8 + 11
= 44 notes, numbered N1–N44 here in their order), each checked against LM_C3-321-339_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep,
correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's
conjectures, questions, expectations, programme), unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not
proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks: the normal form (5)/(45) ρ = (0 1; v w), σ = (0 −1; v' w'), v + v' = 0, w + w' = 1,
v invertible; the standard triple (57) ρ₀ = (0 1; −1 1), σ₀ = (0 −1; 1 0), ε₀ = (1 −1; 0 1); N = ρ + σ. Every identity
stated below was verified by hand on these matrices.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 321–326

- **N1 — scan 321, Remarque after (39) — unpursued implication — correct.** The page itself names what β alone loses:
  the "signe" ε, with μ = 1/U = ε/U₀ in (41)–(42). Scan 322 adds that U₀ invertible is necessary and depends only on
  β, and that then every ε ∈ Γμ₂ occurs, α being found locally up to α ↦ αb, b ∈ ΓT_ρ. What remains unpursued is a
  description of the β for which U₀ is invertible.
- **N2 — scan 322 — unproved claim — keep.** "ζ pourra bel et bien s'écrire loc.t sous la forme αρ(α)⁻¹" rests on the
  theory of (II) and on the lemma p. 455 (the solutions of det η = 1, Tr ησ = Tr σ form the ss-schéma A_σ); neither
  is proved again here.
- **N3 — scan 322 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The infinitesimal part of μ₂ in characteristic 2 is not a
  formality on these pages: it is exactly what makes (60) fail (C3).
- **N4 — scan 323, (1)–(5) — unproved claim — correct.** "Il existe un épinglage unique*" is qualified by the author's
  own NB*, now read "L'unicité de v,w (i.e. de ρ₀,σ₀) n'est pas vraie sans quelques restrictions sur S, il suffit que
  tte section τ de O_S telle que 2τ=0, τ²=0 soit nulle". Existence is not proved on the page, and uniqueness does fail
  without the restriction (C2, C3).
- **N5 — scan 323, first NB — unproved claim — correct.** Corrected reading: "Si ε₀, ε₁ sont les relèvements
  canoniques de ε̃₀, ε̃₁ dans G, réguliers, avec σ̃(ε₀)=ρ̃(ε₀)=ε₁, on peut relever ρ̃,σ̃ de façon unique en ρ,σ" with
  −σ⁻¹ρ = ε₀ and (ρ+σ)² = ρ+σ. Once ε₀ is fixed the uniqueness is immediate (lifts λρ, μσ need λ = μ by the first
  relation and λ = 1 by idempotency); but a unipotent lift λε₀ of ε̃₀ needs only λ² = 1 and 2λ = 2, so the
  "canonical" lift is unique only where there is no τ ≠ 0 with 2τ = τ² = 0 (checked by Claude).
- **N6 — scan 323, NB* — unproved claim — correct.** The reading is "n'est pas vraie sans quelques restrictions sur
  S" (not "demande …", with "n'est pas vraie" struck), and the conditions 2τ = 0, τ² = 0 are now secure. The page
  gives neither a counterexample nor a proof that the condition suffices; C2 gives the counterexample and C3 shows
  that the same condition is exactly what (60) needs.
- **N7 — scan 323, NB* — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The proposed test has been carried out: the coefficients
  (v, w) are not unique over F₄[τ]/(τ²) (C2), and the épinglage is not unique over F₂[τ]/(τ²) (C3).
- **N8 — scan 324, (9)–(10) — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N9 — scan 325, (13)–(15) — unproved claim — keep.** The equivalence of (12) with the épimorphisme N'_σ → μ₂ is
  stated without proof.
- **N10 — scan 325, (17)–(18) — unproved claim — correct.** The connective now reads "donc si les car. rés. sont ≠ 2
  i.e. 2 inv": the page passes from "2 régulier" (sections of N_σ over S lie in N'_σ) to the equality of group schemes
  (18) when 2 is invertible. The doubt about the reading is settled; the point of keeping the two hypotheses apart
  stands (the first gives b = 0 from 2b = 0 in gσg⁻¹ = aσ + b, the second makes this hold after every base change).
- **N11 — scan 326, paragraph before 3° — conjecture (programme) — keep.** The wording is "On va se tourner vers des
  conditions qui doivent|vont servir à fixer v (ou encore v') de façon explicite".
- **N12 — scan 326, (20) — unproved claim — keep.** Immediate from (11) (det σ = det ρ = −v) and (6)
  (ε₀ = (1 1/v; 0 1)).

### Scans 327–332

- **N13 — scan 327, Dém. — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N14 — scan 327, left margin — unproved claim (deferred) — keep.** The upper right margin, now read "cf cor. 2 plus
  bas", in addition refers the boxed ρ² − ρ + 1 = … to Corollaire 2 on scan 331 (C5).
- **N15 — scan 328, "sauf la dernière (27)", "Essayons de prouver" — conjecture (exploratory proof) — keep.**
- **N16 — scan 328, final matrix — own idea (first pass) — correct.** Done: ρ²σ = (−vw, w−(v+w²); −v(v+w²),
  −w(w²+2v)+v+w²), and ρ²σ = ε₀⁻¹ = (1 −1/v; 0 1) holds iff v = −w² and w³ = 1, which is (27bis) on scan 329. The
  ink's lower right entry "+v−w²" (kept as \unsure) cannot give the w³ that the author writes on the next page;
  "+v+w²" does.
- **N17 — scan 329, lemma (30)–(32) — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N18 — scan 329, lemma — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N19 — scan 330, struck "ρ+1 est inversible" — own idea (first pass) — keep.** det(ρ₀ + 1) = 3, so ρ₀ + 1 is not
  invertible at the fibre p = 3 of Spec Z although ρ₀ is nowhere scalar; this agrees with the marginal "faux".
- **N20 — scan 330, Corollaire 1 — unproved claim — keep.** Verified inside the normal form: det = Tr = 1 gives
  ρ²−ρ+1 = 0 and so ρ³ = −1; ρ³ = −1 with det ρ = 1 gives v = −1, w² = 1, w³ = 1, hence w = 1; ρ³ = −1 with
  Tr ρ = 1 gives v = −w² = −1. For arbitrary matrices it fails (ρ = −1).
- **N21 — scan 331, after (37) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N22 — scan 331, (38)–(40) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** For ρ₀, ρ₀² + ρ₀ + 1 = 2ρ₀ ≠ 0, so (40) as written
  does not hold under the page's normalisation. The "+" of (40) is in darker ink, and so is a "+" written over a
  pencil "−" in the boxed (56) on scan 334; see C5.
- **N23 — scan 332, (41)–(44) — unproved claim — correct.** "De façon canonique" needs the NB* restriction: over a
  base with τ ≠ 0, 2τ = τ² = 0, (ρ₀, σ₀) and (ρ₀, (1+τ)σ₀) both satisfy (44) above the same σ̃₀, ρ̃₀ (C3).
- **N24 — scan 332, (45)–(47) — unproved claim — correct.** The "unique triplet (φ, ρ₀, σ₀)" fails over such bases
  (C2 for ρ₀, σ₀; C3 for φ).
- **N25 — scan 332, normal form from kernels and images — own idea (first pass) — keep.**

### Scans 333–335

- **N26 — scan 333, Théorème d'épinglage modulaire — unpursued implication — keep.** The cancelled heading on scan 332
  already reads "Théorème d'épinglage modulaire".
- **N27 — scan 333, (49)–(51) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** Over Spec Z the sheaf of projective modular triples
  is not the sheaf of trivializations: by C3 the map from Isom(GP(1), PG) is not injective on F₂[τ]/(τ²)-points. Over
  bases without τ ≠ 0, 2τ = τ² = 0 the question remains as stated.
- **N28 — scan 334, (56) — unproved claim — keep.** The boxed sign is a dark "+" over a pencil "−" (C5).
- **N29 — scan 334, (57) — unproved claim — correct.** Verified (Claude) over Z: −σ₀⁻¹ρ₀ = (1 −1; 0 1) = ε₀,
  ρ₀ + σ₀ = diag(0,1) is idempotent, N₀ = (0 −1; 0 0), N₁ = ρ₀N₀ρ₀⁻¹ = (0 0; 1 0), σ₀² = −1 and
  Tr ρ₀ = det ρ₀ = 1. So (52)–(56) hold, and N₀, N₁ are non-homothetic in every fibre.
- **N30 — scan 335, "Toutes les flèches de (58) sont bijections" — unproved claim — correct.** False without the NB*
  restriction: over F₂[τ]/(τ²) the lower horizontal arrow and the right vertical arrow of (58) are not injective (C3).
- **N31 — scan 335, (59) — unpursued implication — correct.** (59) holds over any base (Claude): a g commuting with
  ρ₀ and σ₀ commutes with N = diag(0,1), so it is diagonal, and commuting with σ₀ makes it scalar.
- **N32 — scan 335, (60) — unproved claim — correct.** False in general; it holds exactly when S has no section
  τ ≠ 0 with 2τ = τ² = 0, the condition of the NB* on scan 323 (C3).
- **N33 — scan 335, dual numbers — own idea (first pass) — correct.** Done: (60) fails on F₂[τ]/(τ²) and holds on
  F₃[τ]/(τ²), indeed wherever 2 is a non-zero-divisor (C3).

### Scans 336–339

- **N34 — scan 336, Rmq and (62)–(63) (R336.1) — conjecture — keep.** "Il est plausible" and "cela vaudrait bien la
  peine de tirer cette question au clair" make it a question; the inserted "seule" makes the claim "la seule relation
  (R)". See C7.
- **N35 — scan 336, margin of (61) (R336.2) — unproved claim — correct.** Verified: for N'_σ, det(εσ) = det σ gives

- **N36 — scan 336 (R336.3) — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N37 — scan 337, Rmq (R337.1) — conjecture — keep.** The doubt "l'ens.|l'une des trois conditions" remains in the

- **N38 — scan 337–338, N ≠ 0 and N ≠ 1 (R337.2) — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N39 — scan 337 (R337.3) — own idea (first pass) — drop.** The page itself excludes N = 0 and N = 1 in every fibre
  (scans 337–338), which is the scalar case for the idempotent N; (64) has no solution with ρ, σ scalar over any
