- **N40 — scan 338, "(sauf erreur)" (R338.1) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N41 — scan 338, (69) (R338.2) — conjecture (question) — correct.** "Il faudrait voir si oui ou non elle résulte
  des autres relations" is answered yes on scan 339 within the parametrisation (70) ((73) gives Tr ρσ = 2 in (74)), and
  in general by C4.
- **N42 — scan 338 (R338.3) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** Done in C4: no parametrisation is needed.
- **N43 — scan 339, (70bis)–(74) (R339.1) — unpursued implication — keep.** The parametrisation ρ = ρ₀,
  σ = uσ₀u⁻¹ with u ∈ ΓSl(2) needs local conjugacy and a square root of det u, i.e. it is fppf-local; C4 avoids it.
- **N44 — scan 339 (R339.2) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** Done in C4.

## Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scan 321, (39) — own idea (editorial check).** (37) on scan 320 gives (−δv)D = −vd²+cd+c² = det(dρ+c), so
  1/D = −v(ad−bc)/(−vd²+cd+c²) and, with (38), B/D = (ac+bc−vbd)/(−vd²+cd+c²). The ink of (39) has d² for the last
  term of both denominators and a leading minus before ac in the second numerator; both are kept as written.
  Confidence: high for the computation, medium for the readings.
- **C2 — scan 323, NB* — own idea (counterexample).** Take R = F₄[τ]/(τ²), w ∈ F₄∖F₂ (so w² + w = 1) and v = 1. The
  normal forms for (v, w+τ) are carried by g = 1 + τ(1 1; 1 0) (det 1+τ) to (1+(w+1)τ)ρ and (1+wτ)σ, where ρ, σ are
  the normal forms for (v, w). So two épinglages give the same ρ̃, σ̃ with w ≠ w + τ: uniqueness of v, w fails on a
  base with τ ≠ 0, 2τ = τ² = 0, as the NB* says. Confidence: high (checked entry by entry).
- **C3 — scans 335 and 323, (60), (58), NB* — own idea (counterexample and criterion).** If τ ∈ Γ(S,O_S) satisfies
  2τ = τ² = 0, then g = 1 + τρ₀ = (1 τ; −τ 1+τ) commutes with ρ₀ and gσ₀g⁻¹ = (1+τ)σ₀, so g ∈ N'_ρ ∩ N'_σ and g is
  not scalar if τ ≠ 0. Conversely every g in N'_ρ ∩ N'_σ has ε = 1 (Tr ρ₀ = 1) and ε' = 1+τ with 2τ = τ² = 0
  (σ₀² = −1, and ρ₀ + ε'σ₀ = gNg⁻¹ is idempotent), and then g = a(1 + τρ₀); so (60) holds exactly when S has no such
  τ ≠ 0. On F₂[τ]/(τ²), int(g) is a non-trivial automorphism of GP(1) fixing (ρ̃₀, σ̃₀, ε̃₀), and (ρ₀, (1+τ)σ₀,
  (1+τ)ε₀) is a second modular triple over the same projective triple, so two arrows of (58) are not injective.
  Confidence: high.
- **C4 — scans 338–339, (64)–(69) — own idea (proof).** With N = ρ + σ, σ² = −1 and N² = N one has
  ρ² − ρ + 1 = (N−σ)² − (N−σ) + 1 = σ − Nσ − σN, so (64) amounts to σ = Nσ + σN. Multiplying on the left by N gives
  NσN = 0, hence Tr σN = Tr NσN = 0, i.e. Tr σρ = 2, which is (69), over any commutative ring. This answers "il
  faudrait voir si oui ou non elle résulte des autres relations" without the parametrisation (70)–(74). Confidence: high.
- **C5 — scans 327, 331, 334 — unpursued implication (editorial).** ρ² − ρ + 1 = 0 is written with "−" in (36), (38),
  (39), (64) and in the struck block of scan 330, while (40) has "ρ² + ρ + 1 = 0" with the "+" in darker ink and the
  boxed condition of (56) has a dark "+" over a pencil "−"; the corner margin of scan 327 refers "ρ² − ρ + 1 = …" to
  "cor. 2". Under the page's normalisation the "+" forms are false (ρ₀² + ρ₀ + 1 = 2ρ₀). Whether the dark signs belong
  to a later change of convention (ρ ↦ −ρ turns ρ³ = −1 into ρ³ = 1 and ρ² − ρ + 1 into ρ² + ρ + 1) is to be checked
  on the following pages. Confidence: medium.
- **C6 — scan 323 — own idea (editorial).** Two slips are kept as written: "inéquivalente à ε̃₀", where the sentence
  compares ε̃₀ with ε̃₁ (cf. (41) 2° and (51)), and "Algèbre d'Azumaya M de rang 1", where the glyph is his 1 but scan
  333 has "rg. 4", the rank of M₂. Confidence: medium.
- **C7 — scan 336, (62)–(63) — own idea.** On ℤ-points the subgroup generated by σ̃₀, ρ̃₀ and ε̃₀(ℤ) is
  PSL₂(ℤ) ≅ ℤ/2 * ℤ/3, of index 2 in PGL₂(ℤ), so "engendré" can only hold for the fppf sheaf. For instance diag(1, −1)
  becomes diag(i, −i), a product of elementary matrices in SL₂(ℤ[i]), after the fppf extension ℤ → ℤ[i]. The relations
  (62) and (63) do hold for the standard matrices: ρ₀⁻¹σ₀ε₀ = −1 = ρ₀³ = σ₀². The "seule relation" part remains
  untested. Confidence: medium.


# ===== LM_C3-340-358 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-340-358 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 340–358)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-340-358/LM_C3-340-358_notes.md, §5, notes 1–47),
each checked against LM_C3-340-358_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked
**Claude (second pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations), unproved
claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks. Scans 340–343: σ² = −1 and ρ² = ρ − 1 (the underbraces of scans 341–342), hence, for
σ, ρ regular, Tr σ = 0, det σ = 1, Tr ρ = det ρ = 1 by Hamilton–Cayley (the values used on these pages); ε₀ = −σ⁻¹ρ =
σρ = 1 + N₀, ε₁ = −ρσ⁻¹ = ρσ = 1 + N₁ (scan 342), N = σ + ρ. Scans 353–358: ε₀ = −σ⁻¹ρ, ε₁ = −ρσ⁻¹ (scan 355), and
the pinned forms ρ = (0 u; v w), σ = (0 u′; v′ w′) of (26).

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 340–343

- **N1 — scan 340 — conjecture — keep.** "Ce calcul suggère fortement que les relations (64) impliquent déjà (68)…
  c'est m̄ à peu de choses près une démonstration" is an expectation, not a proof. What remains, as stated, is that
  (64) (essentially (51)–(56) without (55)) implies (55), the independence of ε₀, ε₁ on every fibre, i.e. N₀, N₁
  non-homothetic on every fibre; the page argues by contradiction on a fibre where N₁ = λN₀.
- **N2 — scan 340 — drop.** The "calcul immédiat" and "(\*\*) N₀ = ρ⁻¹N, N₁ −" are cancelled by Grothendieck (zigzag
  and slanted strokes, framed); a cancelled step is not a claim of the text.
- **N3 — scan 340 — own idea (first pass) — correct.** λ² = λ³ = 1 gives λ = λ³(λ²)⁻¹ = 1 in any commutative ring.
  On the page the two relations come from N₁ = λN₀ on the fibre together with N₁ = σ̃(N₀), σ̃² = 1 (λ² = 1) and
  N₁ = ρ̃(N₀), ρ̃³ = 1 (λ³ = 1), cancelling N₀ ≠ 0 (N₀ regular). The λ³ step needs N₁ = ρ̃(N₀), which supports N₀ in
  the inserted "= ρ̃(N₁|N₀)" of (\*), where the ink shows N₁. Revised: derivation and dependency added.
- **N4 — scans 341–342 — conjecture — correct.** "Je ne serais pas étonné qu'on ait aussi des conditions
  équivalentes à (51) à (56) sous la forme (75)… ε₀ = −σ⁻¹ρ est unipotent régulier, en exigeant de plus, au besoin,
  que σ, ρ soient également réguliers". The ink of (75) has ρ³ and a blotted sign; the relations used on these pages
  (σ² = −1, ρ² = ρ − 1) make the intended polynomial ρ² − ρ + 1 (ρ³ = −1 follows from it, a possible source of the
  slip). The consequence sought, N = ρ + σ idempotent, is reduced on scan 343 to Tr σρ = 2 and left open.
- **N5 — scan 342 — unproved claim — correct.** "(ce qui équivaut, si N régulier, à (77) Tr N = 1, det N = 0 (?))":
  Hamilton–Cayley gives N² − N = (Tr N − 1)N − det N, and for N regular 1 and N are locally part of a basis (a⇔a′ of
  scan 344), so N² = N ⇔ Tr N = 1, det N = 0; the "(?)" is answered positively. The display is numbered (75) in the
  ink, repeating the (75) of scan 341.
- **N6 — scan 343 — conjecture (open question) — keep.** det(σ+ρ) = det σ + det ρ + det σ·Tr(σ⁻¹ρ) = 2 − Tr σρ
  (σ⁻¹ = −σ), so det N = 0 ⇔ Tr σρ = 2, numbered (69), with the margin "calcul déjà fait V. p. – ? (cf formule (69))".
  "Ce n'est pas entièrement élucidé — à revenir à la charge!": the implication (75) ⇒ (69) is left open, with the
  proposal to start from (75) instead of (68).

### Scans 344–349

- **N7 — scan 344 — unpursued implication — keep.** The proposition of "Sections régulières de G" puts fibrewise
  non-scalarity, the rank-two subalgebra A_ρ, cyclic vectors and companion forms in one list; b)–c″) are stated only
  for M = End(F), with differing locality qualifiers, so their use for a non-split Azumaya algebra goes through a
  local splitting.
- **N8 — scan 344 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** c) carries no locality qualifier in the ink ("Le O_S[T]-module
  F_ρ est isomorphe à O_S[T]/(T² − wT + v)"). For F = L ⊕ L, ρ = 0 ⊕ 1, F_ρ ≅ L ⊕ L over O_S[T]/(T(T−1)) ≅ O_S × O_S,
  which is free only if L is trivial, while a) holds: c) is meant locally. Confidence high.
- **N9 — scan 345 — unproved claim — keep.** "a ⇔ a′ trivial (algèbre linéaire des Modules M et des sous-modules
  O_S1 loc facteur direct)" and "b ⇔ b′ ⇔ b″ trivial": the passage from fibrewise non-scalarity to a locally free
  direct summand O_S + O_Sρ, and the lifting of a cyclic vector from a fibre (a ⇒ b), are only sketched.
- **N10 — scans 345, 347 — unproved claim — correct.** d) "et il en reste ainsi après chaque chgt de base S′ → S":
  the proof on scan 347 shows that u commuting with ρ equals u′ = a + bρ (u(e₀) = u′(e₀), then u(e₁) = ρu(e₀) =
  u′(e₁)), and "Comme b stable par chgt de base" gives the base-change statement, since the cyclic basis of b) pulls
  back. Revised: the argument on the page is complete once a ⇒ b is granted.
- **N11 — scan 346 — unproved claim — correct.** The ink reads "OPs α ∈ ΓSℓ(2)" (not ΓSG). Replacing a solution α
  of (7) by λα multiplies det α by λ², and by αt (t ∈ A_ρ*) by det t = c²v + wcd + d²; making det α = 1 needs a
  square root or a norm, so "On peut alors prendre α ds SG" holds fppf (étale if 2 is invertible) locally, not
  Zariski locally as the corollary's "loc. Zar".
- **N12 — scan 346 — unpursued implication — keep.** Corollary 3 describes the indeterminacy of α as right
  multiplication by A_ρ* (condition ⑧: c²v + wcd + d² invertible): the local solutions of (7) form a torsor under the
  unit group of the quadratic algebra A_ρ, a formulation the page does not pursue.
- **N13 — scan 347 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Corollary 1 is a local (Zariski) conjugacy criterion; the global
  obstruction for regular ρ, ρ′ with equal trace and determinant can be phrased with the isomorphism torsor of the
  cyclic modules over the common algebra O_S[T]/(T² − wT + v). Confidence medium.
- **N14 — scan 348 — unproved claim — correct.** The parenthesis reads "(Alors D est uniquement déterminé par ε i.e.
  par N, [et réciproquement struck] D détermine N mod homothétie)", and the inserted c′) "∃ loc. base|Zar e₀, e₁ tel
  que ε = (1 1; 0 1)". The equivalences with D, B, SB and the uniqueness of D, B, SB are stated without proof.
- **N15 — scan 348 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Over k[η]/(η²) with 2 ≠ 0 in k, N = ηI₂ has N² = 0 and
  det N = 0 but Tr N = 2η ≠ 0: square-zero does not give the "strictement nilpotent" condition det N = tr N = 0, which
  explains the adjective. Confidence high.
- **N16 — scan 349 — unpursued implication — keep.** If ρ is a scalar λ on a fibre, δ(ρ) = (2λ)² − 4λ² = 0 there, so
  δ invertible ⇒ ρ regular; regular nilpotents (δ = 0) show the converse fails. The page does not describe the regular
  locus beyond this sufficient condition.
- **N17 — scan 349 — unproved claim — keep.** "une corr. biunivoque entre les projecteurs élémentaires dans M, et les
  couples de Killing (B,T)" continues on scan 350 (T = A_P*, B₀ the stabiliser of D₀ = ker P, (13)); the inverse
  construction is not written.

### Scans 350–352

- **N18 — scan 350 — unproved claim — keep.** "Le schéma des ε unipotents réguliers, ou encore des N nilp. régul.
  est un fibré en droites épointées (i.e. un G_m-torseur) sur le schéma 𝒫 des Borels": stated without proof.
- **N19 — scan 350 — unproved claim — keep.** "lisse de dim relative 2" and "fibré-lisse de dim relative 1 sur le
  schéma des Borels": the counts agree (Borel scheme of relative dimension 1, G_m-torsor of dimension 1 over it; tori
  of a fixed Borel form a B_u-torsor), but no proof is given.
- **N20 — scan 350 — unproved claim — correct.** The identification of the fibre product with "le schéma des
  épinglages de G … i.e. des iso Gℓ(2)_S ⥲ G" is given without construction, and for G it needs a restriction on the
  isomorphisms: see C2.
- **N21 — scan 350 — unpursued implication — correct.** "Le proj. él. associé au couple de Killing opposé (T,B₁) est
  1 − N" (confirmed in the ink, a later darker hand). With P = (0 0; 0 1) the projector of the opposite pair, with
  image D₀ and kernel D₁, is 1 − P; "1 − N" is a slip of letter, the projector having first been called N on this
  page ("associant à [N struck] P", "le schéma des projecteurs élémentaires N").
- **N22 — scan 351 — unproved claim — correct.** "un torseur sur GP(1)_S ≃ Aut_{S-gr} Gℓ(2)_S ≃ Aut_{S-gr} Sℓ(2)_S ≃
  Aut_{S-gr}(GP(1)_S)": the first isomorphism fails for the full automorphism functor of Gℓ(2), which has a non-inner
  involution (C2); the other two hold.
- **N23 — scan 351 — unproved claim — correct.** (15) PN = 0, NP = N: with N = (0 1; 0 0) and P = (0 0; 0 1) (image D₁,
  kernel D₀ = N(F)) both hold, and (16) Pε = P, εP = P + N follow at once from ε = 1 + N. Conversely PN = 0 says
  N(F) ⊂ ker P and NP = N says N kills ker P, so for N regular nilpotent ker P = N(F) = ker N: the same Borel. Revised:
  verified.
- **N24 — scan 351 — unpursued implication — keep.** (17)–(19): Tr²ρ/det ρ is invariant under ρ ↦ λuρu⁻¹, and the
  next page asks when it is a complete invariant for local conjugacy in PG.
- **N25 — scan 352 — unpursued implication — correct.** The invariant is a morphism PG → 𝔼¹ (affine line; the ink has
  a double-stroke E). "Ces conditions sont suffisantes quand Δ(ρ̃) inv. i.e. Tr ρ inv." (det ρ is a unit); scan 353
  shows that the condition cannot be dropped.
- **N26 — scan 352 — unproved claim — keep.** "loc. fppf, existe λ ∈ ΓG_m telle que … λ² = det ρ′/det ρ": the cover
  O_S[X]/(X² − a), a = det ρ′/det ρ, is free of rank 2, hence faithfully flat, also in residue characteristic 2.
- **N27 — scan 352 — unpursued implication — keep.** τ′² = τ² with τ invertible gives τ′ = ετ, ε = τ′/τ ∈ Γμ₂; the
  proof uses μ₂, not the two constant signs, which matters when 2 is not invertible.
- **N28 — scan 352 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The proof breaks exactly at the division by Tr ρ; the
  conjugacy quotient over the locus where Tr ρ is not invertible could be studied with square-zero test bases such as
  that of scan 353. Confidence medium.

### Scans 353–354

- **N29 — scan 353 — unproved claim — correct.** "il y a des contre-exemples évidents": ρ = (0 1; −1 w), ρ′ =
  (0 1; −1 w′) over A = A₀[w, w′]/(w², ww′, w′²) have det 1 and Δ = 0. A projective conjugacy over a faithfully flat
  B would give λ² = 1 and w′ = λw in B; but I = (w, w′) has I² = 0, and IB ≅ I ⊗_A B is free over B/IB ≠ 0 with
  basis w, w′, so w′ ∉ (B/IB)w. Revised: proof supplied (A₀ a field, or any A₀ ≠ 0 with the same argument).
  Confidence high.
- **N30 — scan 353 — own idea (first pass) — drop.** Repeats N29 (the traces w, w′ are lost by squaring) without a
  further idea.
- **N31 — scan 353 — unproved claim — keep.** "proviennent canoniquement des ε₀, ε₁ unipotents réguliers de SG":
  Grothendieck takes this up himself on scan 358 ("vérifier avec soin une assertion faite par la bande un certain nb
  de fois") and reduces injectivity to λ² = 1, (44); see N46, N47.
- **N32 — scan 353 — unproved claim — correct.** Margin "pour un relèvement plus commode (?) de σ et ρ, soit
  ε₀ = −σ⁻¹ρ, ε₁ = −ρσ⁻¹": if ε₀ = cσ⁻¹ρ with c a unit, then ε₁ = ρ(ε₀) = cρσ⁻¹ carries the same scalar, and the
  one rescaling σ ↦ −c⁻¹σ gives both normalised forms at once. Revised: the simultaneous normalisation is proved.
- **N33 — scan 353 — unpursued implication — keep.** Condition 2° was first "partout" and became "en chaque fibre
  'non équivalents'"; it is exactly what makes Tr(σ+ρ) invertible (C3) and the off-diagonal entries u, v units in
  (26).
- **N34 — scan 354 — unproved claim — keep.** "ε̃₀(G_a), ε̃₁(G_a), ε̃₀ ∈ Γε̃₀(G_a) définissent un épinglage canonique
  de PG donc de G": the two root groups and the root-group section are named, but canonicity and descent are not
  argued.
- **N35 — scan 354 — unpursued implication — correct.** From (26), −ρσ⁻¹ = (−u/u′ 0; (vw′ − wv′)/(u′v′) −v/v′); with
  u′ = −u, v′ = −v its lower left entry is (w + w′)/u, and ε₁ = (1 0; 1 1) gives w + w′ = u, i.e. w′ = u − w, as in
  (36) "w + w′ = u" and (41) "w′ = 1 − w". The ink has w′ in the denominators of that matrix and, in (29), "w′ + w = u
  i.e. w′ = w − u" (the + retraced over another sign, the right side unchanged): slips, kept in the transcription.
- **N36 — scan 354 — unproved claim — correct.** (30): with u′ = −u, v′ = −v, w′ = u − w one gets ρ + σ = (0 0; 0 u) =
  uP, P the elementary projector of the pinning, so σ = uP − ρ is determined by ρ, its coefficient u and P, as the
  margin says. Revised: verified.
- **N37 — scan 354 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** After fixing the pinning the pair (ρ, σ) is described by the
  units u, v and w ((26)–(31)); a classification must still account for the residual scalars and for descent.
  Confidence medium.

### Scans 355–358

- **N38 — scan 355 — conjecture (aim) — keep.** "On aimerait donner des 'critères numériques' intrinsèques": the
  proposition that follows (a)–f)) carries this out on the same pages.
- **N39 — scan 355 — unproved claim — correct.** The margin "ceci exprime que ε₀ est régulier et indépendant de ε₁,
  cf loc. cit." (for Tr(σ+ρ) invertible, given (32 a, b)) is proved in C3.
- **N40 — scan 355 — unpursued implication — keep.** "P et u sont uniquement déterminés, u = Tr(σ+ρ), P = (1/u)(σ+ρ)":
  the formulas commute with base change, so the decomposition (34) is compatible with base change wherever Tr(σ+ρ)
  is invertible.
- **N41 — scan 356 — unproved claim — correct.** "cet isom est unique", "cet φ est encore unique": among isomorphisms
  induced by algebra isomorphisms M₂(O_S) ≅ M this holds (an inner automorphism fixing (1 0; 1 1) and normalising the
  upper unipotent group is trivial), but the involution g ↦ det(g)⁻¹g of Gℓ(2) fixes both unipotent matrices, so among
  all group isomorphisms φ is unique only up to it (C2). The ink of (36) also has w′ for the upper right entry of
  φ⁻¹(σ), where "u + u′ = 0" needs u′.
- **N42 — scan 357 — unproved claim — correct.** (40), "que j'ai oublié d'expliciter": if det Q = 0 and u = Tr Q is a
  unit, Hamilton–Cayley gives Q² = uQ, so P = u⁻¹Q has P² = P, det P = 0, Tr P = 1; by the remark of scan 349
  (condition b) already implies regularity) P is an elementary projector. Conversely Q = uP gives det Q = 0,
  Tr Q = u. Revised: the cited prop. p. 475′ (scans 348–349) suffices.
- **N43 — scan 357 — unproved claim — correct.** In the pinned frame of (31), 1 = I, N₀ = (u/v)E₁₂, N₁ = E₂₁,
  P = E₂₂ form a basis of M₂(O_S) (u/v a unit), and ρ = v(N₀+N₁) + wP, σ = −v(N₀+N₁) + (u−w)P hold. The coefficient
  matrix (v w; −v u−w) has determinant uv, a unit, which gives the stated replacements of N₀ or N₁ (or of P) by ρ or
  σ. Revised: verified.
- **N44 — scan 357 — unpursued implication — keep.** "Notre point de vue ici est que nous partons de ρ̃, σ̃ … —
  l'épinglage est en effet défini déjà en termes de ρ̃, σ̃": the normalisation Tr(ρ+σ) = 1, (40), gives a preferred
  lift, whose uniqueness is asked on the next page.
- **N45 — scan 358 — conjecture (question) — keep.** "Y. a-t-il unicité de ρ, σ en termes de ρ̃, σ̃, par les
  conditions ε₁ = −ρσ⁻¹ (i.e. ε₀ = −σρ⁻¹) et (40)?" The question is left for the injectivity check that follows; the
  parenthesis does not match the definition of ε₀ (C4).
- **N46 — scan 358 — unproved claim under examination — correct.** From (44) λ² = 1 and the traces (Tr ε′ = λ Tr ε,
  both equal to 2) one gets 2(λ − 1) = 0, hence (λ − 1)² = λ² − 2λ + 1 = 0: injectivity holds when 2 is invertible or
  the base is reduced, and in general λ − 1 is a square-zero element killed by 2. Revised: the next step completed.
- **N47 — scan 358 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Over k[t]/(t²), char k = 2, ε = I + E₁₂ and ε′ = (1+t)ε are
  both regular unipotent (det ε′ = (1+t)² = 1, Tr ε′ = 0 = Tr 1, ε′ − 1 = tI + (1+t)E₁₂ regular nilpotent) with the
  same image in PG and ε′ ≠ ε. It is the case λ − 1 = t of N46. Confidence high.

## Notes of the second pass

- **C1 — scan 340 — own idea (error in the text) — Claude (second pass).** The insertion "N = −σN₀ = ρ⁻¹N₀" is false
  under the relations of these pages: −σN₀ = −σ(σρ − 1) = ρ + σ = N holds, but with ρ⁻¹ = 1 − ρ one finds
  Tr(ρ⁻¹N₀) = Tr σρ − 2 − Tr σρ² + 1 = −1, while Tr N = 1, so N ≠ ρ⁻¹N₀ unless 2 = 0. The argument uses only
  N = −σN₀ (N commutes with σ, ρ when N₀ does), so "σ et ρ commutent" stands. Confidence high.
- **C2 — scans 350–351, 356 — own idea (claim needing a restriction) — Claude (second pass).** g ↦ det(g)⁻¹g is an
  automorphism of Gℓ(2) (an involution, equal to transpose-inverse up to an inner automorphism); it is not inner, since
  it inverts the centre, and it preserves T and B₀ and fixes (1 1; 0 1) and (1 0; 1 1). Hence Aut_{S-gr} Gℓ(2)_S ≅ GP(1)_S × Z/2, a
  pinning of G corresponds to two group isomorphisms Gℓ(2)_S ⥲ G, and "cet isom est unique" fails for arbitrary group
  isomorphisms. All three statements hold for isomorphisms induced by algebra isomorphisms M₂(O_S) ≅ M (G = M*), and
  for SG and PG as written. Confidence high.
- **C3 — scan 355 — own idea (proof of the margin claim) — Claude (second pass).** Put Q = σ + ρ; then
  N₀ = −σ⁻¹ρ − 1 = −σ⁻¹Q and N₁ = −Qσ⁻¹. Under (32 a, b) (det Q = 0, N₀² = 0) one has σ(ker Q) = Im Q on each
  fibre where Q ≠ 0, so ker N₁ = σ(ker Q) = Im Q while ker N₀ = ker Q. If Tr Q ≠ 0 on the fibre, Q is a non-zero multiple of a
  rank-one idempotent, Im Q ≠ ker Q and N₀, N₁ are regular and non-homothetic; if Tr Q = 0, either Q = 0 (N₀ not
  regular) or Im Q = ker Q (N₀, N₁ homothetic). So "ε₀ régulier et indépendant de ε₁ en chaque fibre" ⇔ Tr(σ+ρ)
  invertible, as the margin says. Confidence high.
- **C4 — scans 353–358 — own idea (slips in the formulas) — Claude (second pass).** Several displayed formulas
  disagree with the definitions they use; the transcription keeps them as written: (23) "= σ̃(ε̃₁)" for σ̃(ε̃₀) (cf.
  (24)); the w′ of (27), (29) and (36) noted in N35, N41; "ε₁ = −σ⁻¹ρ" on scan 357 for ε₀; and "(i.e. ε₀ = −σρ⁻¹)" on
  scan 358, which is ε₁⁻¹, not ε₀. None of them changes the conclusions drawn on these pages. Confidence high.


# ===== LM_C3-359-377 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-359-377 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 359–377)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-359-377/LM_C3-359-377_notes.md, §5: R359.1–R363.2, the
unnumbered notes on scans 364–368, notes 1–14 on scans 369–373, R374.1–R377.2), each checked against
LM_C3-359-377_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second pass)** are new.
Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, programme), unproved claim, unpursued implication,
own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks (C1): 2×2 matrices; ρ(u) = ρuρ⁻¹; ε₀ = −σ⁻¹ρ, ε₁ = −ρσ⁻¹ (56); for invertible 2×2 A,
A⁻¹ = (Tr A − A)/det A, hence Tr A⁻¹ = Tr A/det A; in Π₀,₃ the loops satisfy ℓ∞ℓ₁ℓ₀ = 1, matching (64).

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 359–363

- **R359.1 — scan 359 — unpursued implication — keep.** The criterion "the only sections satisfying (46) are zero" is
  stated over S; whether (43) is a monomorphism after every base change is the stronger question, and (43) lies before
  this package. The label (46) of 2ν = 0, ν² = 0 is now settled, so the cross-reference "qui satisfont (46)" is coherent.
- **R359.2 — scan 359 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Over F₂[η]/(η²), λ = 1 + η gives λ² = 1 + 2η + η² = 1 and
  2λ = 2, with λ ≠ 1; checked.
- **R359.3 — scan 359 — unpursued implication — correct.** The computation it asks for is easy and done in note C359a
  below: in the coordinate S = T − 1 the group law is S ↦ S⊗1 + 1⊗S + S⊗S, so the isomorphism with the "schéma des nbs
  duaux … relatif au module Z/2Z" holds as schemes, not as group schemes with the additive law.
- **R360.1 — scan 360 — unproved claim — keep.** "Il se trouve que μ'₂S opère librement par translations" on the scheme of
  unipotents "(ou non)" of G and (47) for the regular ones are stated without proof. Freeness on points follows from
  359 (λε = ε forces λ = 1); the quotient statement (47) needs an fppf argument not given here.
- **R360.2 — scan 360 — conjecture (question) — correct.** In the corrected reading the vertical arrows of (48) carry ≀,
  so the diagram first asserts bijections Borels ⥲ one-parameter subgroups; the question "ne seraient pas injectives" doubts
  exactly these marks. Scans 361–362 give the other explanation, so the ≀ marks record the starting expectation, not the
  conclusion.
- **R360.3 — scan 360 — unproved claim — keep.** B = Norm(B_u) is said to be checked "à coups d'alg. lin. dans M"; the
  projective formula B̃ = Norm(B̃_u) is left (scan 361: "pas très trivial"). (49) is used again as a label on scan 362.
- **R361.1 — scan 361 — conjecture (proposed explanation) — keep.** The quantifier "qu'une|qu'aucune" remains doubtful in
  the ink; the "ne … pas" that follows favours "qu'une".
- **R361.2 — scans 361–362 — unpursued implication — correct.** The sign before "valeur" is now read m̄: "bien qu'ils
  aient même valeur pour t = 1". The point stands: evaluation at 1 does not determine the parametrised subgroup in PG, and
  the derivative at 0 (scan 362) separates the two.
- **R361.3 — scan 361 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Checked: over R = F₂[η]/(η²), c(1 + tN) = 1 + t(1+η)N + tη in
  R[t] forces c = 1 + tη and then η(t² − t) = 0, false in R[t]; the two parametrisations differ in PG though they agree at
  t = 1.
- **R362.1 — scan 362 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** "Partout non nulle" must mean non-zero in every fibre, so that a
  coordinate of Ñ is locally a unit and νÑ = 0 gives ν = 0.
- **R362.2 — scan 362 — unpursued implication — keep.** The conclusion that one must give the parametrised subgroups
  ε̃₀, ε̃₁ in addition to ρ̃, σ̃ is the formulation of the Proposition on scan 363.
- **R363.1 — scan 363 — unproved claim — correct.** Uniqueness in "∃ uniques ε₀, ε₁" is the computation of scans 361–362
  (two lifts λε, ε give the same parametrised subgroup only if λ = 1); what the page leaves unproved is existence of a
  lift of the given parametrisation, locally on S.
- **R363.2 — scan 363 — unproved claim — keep.** The normalisation λ₀ = 1 "ce qui détermine ρ de façon unique en termes
  de σ, et inversement" is asserted; the item now opens "Soient(?) ρ, σ des relèvements de ρ̃, σ̃".

### Scans 364–368

- **Scan 364, items 4–5, (57)–(57′) — unproved claim — keep.** The trace criterion for fibrewise independence and the
  uniqueness under Tr(ρ + σ) = 1 (with lifts found "loc. fppf") are asserted, with a pointer to the proposition of
  p. 478′ (§48 VII).
- **Scan 364, Remarques 1, (58)–(59) — unpursued implication — correct.** (59) checked: ε′₀ = −ρ⁻¹σ = (−σ⁻¹ρ)⁻¹ = ε₀⁻¹
  and ε′₁ = −σρ⁻¹ = ε₁⁻¹. In the corrected reading "(ρ⁻¹, σ⁻¹) satisfait aux m̄s conditions", the symmetry then
  extends to inversion, which (60)–(63) treat.
- **Scan 365, (62) — unproved claim — correct.** The "calcul matriciel immédiat" checks: unipotence of ε₀ = −σ⁻¹ρ gives
  det ρ = det σ, and Tr ρ⁻¹ + Tr σ⁻¹ = (Tr ρ + Tr σ)/det ρ = (det ρ)⁻¹ under (57′). Only the middle term −1/v needs
  det ρ = −v from the canonical épinglage (§48 IV, p. 446 in the author's table), not on these pages; the modular case
  (det ρ = 1, entry v₁/v = −1) is consistent with it.
- **Scan 365, four épinglages, char. 2 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** In characteristic 2, (1 + N)⁻¹ = 1 − N = 1 + N
  for N² = 0, so ε₀⁻¹ = ε₀ and ε₁⁻¹ = ε₁ on those fibres: the four épinglages reduce to two there, those of L₀ and L₁.
- **Scan 366, "On a préféré normaliser" — unpursued implication — keep.** The choice of L₁ and of ε₁ rather than ε₁⁻¹ is
  tied to the form [α,ρ] = [β,σ]ε₁(ν) of the loop equation (§§44–45, pp. 356 and 384 in the author's table).
- **Scan 366, item 2, (64) — unproved claim — correct.** The group's letter is now read 𝔖 with a mark (row 21 of the
  changes). The preservation of the product relation is immediate and checked: cyclic permutation preserves a product
  equal to 1, and ε̃₀⁻¹σ̃⁻¹ρ̃ = 1 because σ̃ε̃₀ = ρ̃. The identification with (ρ,σ) ↦ (σ,ρ) holds only up to that
  cyclic permutation (note C367a).
- **Scan 367, opening question — conjecture (question) — keep.** It is answered on the same page by composition with
  τ′∞ and τ∞.
- **Scan 367, automorphisms and Z/2Z × Z/2Z — unproved claim — correct.** The relations check: τ′∞ and τ∞ are
  commuting involutions with τ∞τ′∞ = the exchange ℓ₀ ↔ ℓ₁ = σ∞, so τ∞ = σ∞τ′∞ and the group has order 4. But the group
  does not stabilise L₀ as written; see C367a.
- **Scan 367, ε̃₁ without inverse — own idea (first pass) — correct (resolved).** The triple must end with ε̃₁⁻¹: ρ̃σ̃⁻¹ε̃₁⁻¹
  = 1 while ρ̃σ̃⁻¹ε̃₁ = ε̃₁², and (61) gives ε″₀ = ε₁⁻¹. The ink has ε̃₁ without exponent, a slip of the author; the
  transcription keeps it.
- **Scan 368, recapitulation and (2) — unproved claim — keep.** The parametrisation by B′₀ × Tρ × Tσ is recalled from
  (III) (§48 III, p. 443); its bijectivity is not reproved here.
- **Scan 368, diagonal margin note — conjecture (question) — correct.** The reading is now settled: "Nous partons
  maintenant de ρ, σ, B′₀, ε₁ (d'où L₁) et nous posons la question à quelles conditions, on peut avoir une
  paramétrisation (2)". The answer starts on scan 369 with condition (4) and on scan 370 with surjectivity.
- **Scan 368, between (2) and (3) — unproved claim — correct.** The algebra checks: for a ∈ Tρ, ρ(ua⁻¹) = ρ(u)a⁻¹, so
  [ua⁻¹, ρ] = uρ(u)⁻¹, likewise for σ, and (1) becomes uρ(u)⁻¹ = uσ(u)⁻¹ε₁(ν(u)), i.e. (3). Only the scheme-theoretic
  points (ν a morphism of schemes B′₀ → G_a) remain.
- **Scan 368, ν(u) = det(u) − 1 — unpursued implication — keep.**

### Scans 369–373

1. **Scan 369, "par un argument connu" — unproved claim — keep.** σ(B′₀) ⊂ B′₁ ⇒ σ(B′₀) = B′₁ needs that both are
   "pré-Borels" of the same rank, fibrewise.
2. **Scan 369, (4) — unpursued implication — keep.** The criterion is now read in context: "un morphisme … → 𝒢ρ,σ =dfn
   schéma des solutions de (1)" (corrected from "relations").
3. **Scan 369, (5) and right margin — unproved claim — keep.**
4. **Scan 369, NB in the lower left margin — unpursued implication — keep.** Most words remain doubtful; no use of its
   content should be made beyond "détermine pratiquement B₀ … ambiguïté … deux Borels".
5. **Scan 370, surjectivity of (6) — conjecture (question) — keep.**
6. **Scan 370, necessity of (8) — conjecture (doubt) — keep.** The table dates (8) to §48 II (p. 434), the section titled
   by the equation ζ = ηε₁(μ − 1) that (7) restates.
7. **Scan 371, "La suite montrera si cette restriction est judicieuse" — conjecture (expectation) — keep.**
8. **Scan 371, stability of 𝒳σ under μ₂ — own idea (first pass) — correct (done).** With the universal point ε of
   μ₂ = Spec O_S[ε]/(ε² − 1), det(εη) = det η and Tr(εησ) = ε Tr(ησ), so stability is (ε − 1)Tr σ = 0 in O_S[ε]/(ε² − 1);
   since 1, ε is a basis, this is Tr σ = 0, over any base, residue characteristic 2 included. The author's "condition de
   stabilité" is thus exact.
9. **Scan 371, (10) — unproved claim — correct.** The phrase is « le résultat essentiel auquel nous tenions » (corrected
   from « crucial »); the content of the note stands.
10. **Scan 372, "trivial par définition" and "exige justement Tr σ = 0" — unproved claim — keep.** Necessity is
    immediate: b(σ) = −σ makes σ and −σ conjugate, so 2 Tr σ = 0, i.e. Tr σ = 0 when 2 is invertible, and for the
    universal point ε of μ₂ (note 8) Tr σ = ε Tr σ gives Tr σ = 0 over any base. Sufficiency, the local existence of b,
    is not shown on the page.
11. **Scan 372, transported group law — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
12. **Scan 373, (16)–(17) — unproved claim — keep.**
13. **Scan 373, margin "ε² = 1 résulte déjà de la première relation" — unproved claim — correct (checked).** Taking
    determinants in (19): det[α,ρ] = det[β,σ] = det ε₁(μ − 1) = 1 and det(ε·1) = ε² for 2×2 matrices, so ε² = 1.
14. **Scan 373, eliminating ε′ in (20) — own idea (first pass) — drop.** The author does it himself on scan 375: "on aura
    (det b)² = (det a)²", then the inverse image of the diagonal by δρ², δσ².

### Scans 374–377

- **R374.1 — scan 374, (22) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R374.2 — scan 374, after (24) — unpursued implication — correct.** The wording is "déduit de l'expression de ceux-ci
  en termes de (B′₀, T₀), Tρ, N′σ en remplaçant N′σ par Tσ" (corrected from "des descriptions"); see also C374.
- **R375.1 — scan 375, projection mono and iso — unproved claim — correct.** The inverse is explicit: u ∈ T₀ is recovered
  from det u = det b by T₀ ⥲ G_m, u ↦ det u. In the corrected reading the image is "défini par l'équation (21)" and the
  author adds "on aura (det b)² = (det a)²".
- **R375.2 — scan 375, (22) épi onto μ₂ × μ₂ — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R376.1 — scan 376, (25)–(26) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R376.2 — scan 376, tori except in char. 3 and 2 — unproved claim — correct (checked).** Tρ, the centraliser of the
  regular ρ, is the unit group of O_S[ρ] ≅ O_S[x]/(x² − x + 1), étale where the discriminant −3 is invertible, and det
  restricts to the norm, so STρ is the norm-one torus away from 3; likewise STσ with x² + 1 (discriminant −4) away from 2.
  (25) is then an extension of G_m by a 2-dimensional torus, a torus of dimension 3 away from 2 and 3.
- **R376.3 — scan 376, characteristic polynomials — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **R377.1 — scan 377, the action through δσ — unpursued implication — correct (checked).** For u = (μ λ; 0 1) ∈ B′₀
  and ε₀(λ′) in the upper unipotent L₀, uε₀(λ′)u⁻¹ = ε₀(μλ′) = ε₀(det(u)λ′), and det u = det b = δσ(b) by (21); the
  kernel of δσ on SG*∘(0) acts trivially on Λ₀.
- **R377.2 — scan 377, (28) — own idea (first pass) — keep.**

## Notes added in the second pass

- **C359a — scan 359 — own idea (check) — Claude (second pass).** With S = T − 1 the ring is Z[S]/(S², 2S) as written,
  and the multiplication T ↦ T⊗T of μ₂ gives S ↦ S⊗1 + 1⊗S + S⊗S, where S⊗S ≠ 0 (Z/2 ⊗ Z/2 = Z/2). So μ′₂ is not the
  additive group of Z ⊕ (Z/2)S: its fibre over F₂ is μ₂ (1 + S grouplike), not α₂, and it is trivial over Z[1/2].
- **C359b — scan 359 — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** The cancelled "est réduit" shows a first sufficient
  condition (S reduced: ν² = 0 ⇒ ν = 0), replaced by "2 section régulière de O_S" (2ν = 0 ⇒ ν = 0). Non-zero solutions of
  (46) need a section that is both 2-torsion and nilpotent, which is the criterion stated for (43).
- **C362 — scan 362 — unproved claim (check) — Claude (second pass).** The margin "σ⁻¹ρ = −λ₀ε₀ donc … = ρ(ε₀)": from
  ρ = −λ₀σε₀, int ρ = int σ ∘ int ε₀, so ρ(ε₀) = σ(ε₀). The mathematics gives σ(ε₀), not σ⁻¹(ε₀), in the doubtful
  reading; the transcription keeps the doubt.
- **C365 — scan 365 — unproved claim (check) — Claude (second pass).** (60)–(61): ε″₀ = −σρ⁻¹ = (−ρσ⁻¹)⁻¹ = ε₁⁻¹ and
  ε″₁ = −ρ⁻¹σ = ε₀⁻¹. The overwritten index in (61) must be 1; the ink is a blot, so the doubt mark stays.
- **C367a — scan 367 — own idea (check of a claim) — Claude (second pass).** As written, τ′∞ sends ℓ₀ to ℓ₁⁻¹, so the
  group {1, τ′∞, τ∞, σ∞} exchanges L₀ and L₁ and does not stabilise L₀ (the ink, checked enlarged, reads L₀); what it
  stabilises is the class of L∞ (τ′∞(ℓ∞) = ℓ∞⁻¹, τ∞(ℓ∞) = ℓ₀ℓ₁ = ℓ₀ℓ∞⁻¹ℓ₀⁻¹), as the index ∞ suggests. The two
  identifications hold up to the operations of scan 366: φ∘τ′∞ gives (ρ̃, ε̃₀⁻¹, σ̃⁻¹), a cyclic permutation of
  (σ̃⁻¹, ρ̃, ε̃₀⁻¹), and φ∘τ∞ gives (σ̃⁻¹ρ̃σ̃, σ̃⁻¹, ε̃₀⁻¹), the conjugate by σ̃⁻¹ of (ρ̃, σ̃⁻¹, ε̃₁⁻¹). "Stabilise L₀" and
  "conserver ou remplacer par son inverse le troisième facteur" are therefore true only after normalising the cyclic order.
- **C369 — scan 369 — unproved claim (check) — Claude (second pass).** "Deux sections de B′₁ qui ont m̄ déterminant … donc
  qui coïncident bien modulo (B′₁)u": B′₁ is conjugate to {(μ λ; 0 1)}, on which det = μ gives B′₁/(B′₁)u ≅ G_m, and
  conjugation preserves det. The step is complete without the doubtful "donc ablt".
- **C374 — scan 374 — unproved claim (possible slip) — Claude (second pass).** The ink reads "intersections de SG*ρ,σ,
  SG*♭ρ,σ(0) avec SGρσ", which would define SGρ,σ through itself. The intended intersection is with 𝒢ρ,σ, as in (15)
  (𝒢♭(0) = 𝒢ρ,σ ∩ 𝒢*♭(0)) and as "en remplaçant N′σ par Tσ" requires.
- **C376 — scans 375–376 — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** In the modular case, outside characteristics
  2 and 3, SG∘(0) is a 3-dimensional torus and SG*♭(0) is an extension of μ₂ × μ₂ by it (23); with (22) and (27),
  SG*ρ,σ is the semidirect product of Λ₀ ≅ G_a by that group, which acts through the character δσ. The exceptional fibres (STρ in
  characteristic 3, STσ in characteristic 2) are where non-reduced or unipotent parts can appear, and the page does not
  treat them.


# ===== LM_C3-378-396 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-378-396 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 378–396)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-378-396/LM_C3-378-396_notes.md, "Research notes",
1–51), each checked against LM_C3-378-396_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked
**Claude (second pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, programme),
unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks (from the ink): G = Gl(2)_S; in the arithmetic case ρ = (0 1; −1 1) and
σ = (0 −1; 1 0) (scan 379), so Tr ρ = 1, Tr σ = 0, det ρ = det σ = 1, ρ³ = −1, σ² = −1. G*_{ρ,σ} ⊂ B′₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ
acts on G by inner automorphisms through the factor B′₀ (scans 391–392); u̲(x) means u̲xu̲⁻¹. Elements of the
semidirect products are written i_B(b)i_ρ(y)i_σ(z)i_G(g), with i_B(b)i_G(g)i_B(b)⁻¹ = i_G(bgb⁻¹).

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 378–382

- **N1 — scan 378, Proposition and proof — unproved claim — correct.** The proof is complete modulo two facts it uses
  without proof: the centralisers of ρ and σ in G are T_ρ and T_σ with T_ρ ∩ T_σ = G_m (the centre), and
  G_m ∩ SG = μ₂. Then the first two formulas of (29) fix u̲ modulo G_m and det u̲ = μ fixes it modulo μ₂. The
  characteristic-2 restriction belongs to the following remark, not to the Proposition. Revised: the target of λ is μ₂
  in the statement but an Ɛ₂ (ε₂?) at the end of the proof (change 2).
- **N2 — scan 378, normalisation remark — unproved claim — correct.** The "sauf erreur" step works on the neutral
  component: if T_ρ, T_σ are tori, B′₀ × T_ρ × T_σ is connected, and a morphism from it to μ₂ (étale when 2 is
  invertible, hence "du moins en car. ≠ 2") with λ(1) = 1 is constant, hence trivial. The remaining freedom is a
  character of the component group, which the corrected sentence names: "la multiplication de u̲ par ε (correspondant
  à l'hom. can. (non trivial) de N′_σ/T_σ sur μ₂)" (change 3). That λ is a homomorphism on all of G*_{ρ,σ} still
  needs this component argument.
- **N3 — scan 378, diagonal margin — unpursued implication — keep.** On SG_{ρ,σ} instead of G*_{ρ,σ} the
  indeterminacy becomes multiplication by ε^iε′^j (i, j ∈ Z/2Z), now read without doubt (change 4): two independent
  sign characters instead of one. Comparing the component groups of the two schemes would explain the difference; not
  done on these pages.
- **N4 — scan 379, first paragraph — unproved claim — keep.** "nous pourrions faire leur construction aussi sans
  cette condition supplémentaire" (Tr σ = 0, i.e. working with G_{ρ,σ} instead of G*_{ρ,σ}) is asserted, not shown.
- **N5 — scan 379, normalisation Tr(ρ+σ) = 1 — unproved claim — correct.** The arithmetic pair of display (2) already
  satisfies both conditions: Tr σ = 0 and Tr(ρ+σ) = 1 + 0 = 1. The general "Nous pouvons au besoin supposer" (by which
  change of ρ, σ?) is not justified here; the conditions referred to are on p. 477′/478′ and in §48.V (pp. 455–457).
- **N6 — scan 379, display (2) — unpursued implication — correct.** Since ρ³ = −1 and σ² = −1, the images of ρ, σ in
  Gl(2,Ẑ)/{±1} have orders 3 and 2, matching the order-3 action ρ̇ on {0,1,∞} used on scan 381. The map (2) is only
  named "homomorphisme canonique"; its construction is not on these pages.
- **N7 — scan 380, opening — unproved claim — keep.** The promised compatibility of the new group law with "ledit
  morphisme" is a programme for the following pages (done for S₀M̃^!₀,₃ on scans 384–390).
- **N8 — scan 380, (3)–(4) — unproved claim — correct.** The corrected reading (change 7) is "déduite d'une extension
  de Ã₀,₃ = M̃₀,₃/Π̂₀,₃ ⊂ 𝒯̂₀,₃, et même d'une extension de 𝒯̂₀,₃ (via M̃₀,₃ → 𝒯̂₀,₃)": the extension is defined over
  𝒯̂₀,₃ and pulled back, and (3) indeed takes u ∈ 𝒯̂₀,₃. Still to prove: conjugators exist for every u (surjectivity)
  and the kernel is the product of the centralisers L_i ≅ Ẑ.
- **N9 — scan 380, (5) — unpursued implication — keep.** The product is stated only "en nous bornant (dégonflage!)"
  to u̇ = 1; for general u̇ the components would be permuted.
- **N10 — scan 381, (6)–(7) — unproved claim — correct.** For u = 1 (μ = 1), (4) reads ℓ_i = int(f_i⁻¹)(ℓ_i), so
  f_i centralises ℓ_i and the kernel is ∏ Z(ℓ_i). "f_i ∈ L_i" then uses that the centraliser of ℓ_i in Π̂₀,₃ is the
  procyclic L_i ≅ Ẑ, a known property of free profinite groups that is not proved here.
- **N11 — scan 381, (9)–(11) — unpursued implication — correct.** The identity under the brace follows by cubing (9):
  uρ³u⁻¹ = (gρ)³ = gρ(g)ρ²(g)ρ³, so gρ(g)ρ²(g) = 1 as soon as ρ³ = 1 as an automorphism of Π̂₀,₃ (ρ̇ of order 3);
  then f_i⁻¹ = ρ³(f_i⁻¹) closes the recurrence. In the ink the underline labelled 1 starts under ρ(g) (still
  unresolved), but the identity needs the first factor g.
- **N12 — scan 382, (11)–(12) — unproved claim — keep.** Closure of the quadruples satisfying (11) and the
  identification with the pairs (u, f₀) are asserted; (11) itself shows that f₀ determines f₁, f∞.
- **N13 — scan 382, (13)–(15) — unproved claim — keep.** The connectors of (14) are headless strokes (change 9);
  nothing mathematical changes.
- **N14 — scan 382, (15) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Comparing the three-factor extension modulo the diagonal Ẑ
  with the finer one remains a reasonable check.

### Scans 383–387

- **N15 — scan 383, (16)–(18) — unproved claim — keep.** The parametrisation by (α, β, f) is imported from §46.V
  (p. 407 ff, which the Circle table confirms as "Récapitulation en termes de M̃!₀,₃[0]").
- **N16 — scan 383, after (17) — unproved claim — correct.** The text says μ is determined by α "et m̄ par la seule
  connaissance de ξ = α̲ρ(α̲)⁻¹". This cannot come from u̲(ρ) = ξ̲ρ alone, since u̲ is fixed only modulo T_ρ, whose
  determinants are arbitrary; it must come from (17), where the factor ε₁(μ−1) = ℓ₁^ν carries μ. Not proved here;
  scan 384 repeats it ("μ = μ(ξ) est déterminé par ξ").
- **N17 — scan 383, (18) — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N18 — scan 384, (23)–(24) — unpursued implication — correct.** The stated sign-independence checks out: ρ and σ
  act by conjugation, so replacing α̲ by −α̲ (or β̲ by −β̲) leaves α̲ρ(α̲)⁻¹ (or β̲σ(β̲)⁻¹) unchanged, and (24) is
  well defined for lifts "déterminés au signe près". The relation is imposed in Sl(2,Ẑ), "pas dans Sl(2,Ẑ)′" (Gl/Sl
  as in the ink, change 13).
- **N19 — scan 384, (21)–(22) — own idea (first pass) — correct (settled).** The prose before (22) ("l'élément
  (u_{α,β,f}, f)") agrees with (21) and with scan 388, where the triple (1,1,f) corresponds to the pair
  (int(f⁻¹), f) = (u_{1,1,f}, f). So (22)'s (u_{α,β}, f) is a slip of the pen; the transcription keeps it.
- **N20 — scan 385, opening–(28) — unproved claim — keep.** The re-verification is completed on scan 386 for the α
  and β components; the μ, ε components follow from μ″ = μμ′ if ε₂ is a character, as "d'où ε₂″ = ε₂ε₂′" assumes.
- **N21 — scan 385, (27) — unproved claim — keep.** "(cf …)" is left blank in the ink.
- **N22 — scan 385, (25)–(28) — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N23 — scan 386, before (29) — unproved claim — correct.** Corrected reading (change 17): "dans les conditions
  générales de V (ou même de III, si on s'intéresse à G_{ρ,σ}, au lieu de G*_{ρ,σ})", i.e. the verification is
  claimed even under the weaker hypotheses of III. Since (30) uses only the group structure of the decomposition (29),
  the claim reduces to the existence of that decomposition over the base, which is not shown here.
- **N24 — scan 386, after (30) — unproved claim — correct (checked).** The omitted computation is literally parallel:
  u̲′(β̲)β̲′ = u̲′u̲b̲⁻¹u̲′⁻¹·u̲′b̲′⁻¹ = (u̲′u̲)(b̲′b̲)⁻¹. So "et de m̄ pour la deuxième relation 30" holds; only
  associativity is used.
- **N25 — scan 386, (29)–(30) — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N26 — scan 387, (31) and the bracket — unproved claim — keep.** The reading is now "par multiplication, [pour,
  struck] (α,β,μ,ε), de α et β chacun par une section de G_m" (change 20). The conditions α, β ∈ Γ{±1}→ΓG_m stay
  overwritten.
- **N27 — scan 387, Σ₀ — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N28 — scan 387, own idea (first pass) — keep.**

### Scans 388–392

- **N29 — scan 388, (31) — unproved claim — keep.** The number (31) is used twice (scans 387 and 388), as in the ink.
- **N30 — scan 388, (32) — own idea (first pass) — correct (settled; consequential).** With the product (5) on pairs,
  (u′,f′)(u,f) = (u′u, f′u′(f)), one gets (int(f′), f′⁻¹)(int(f), f⁻¹) = (int(f′f), (f′f)⁻¹). So f ↦ (int(f), f⁻¹) is
  a homomorphism Π̂₀,₃ → S₀M̃^!₀,₃, and by (21) (int(f), f⁻¹) is the pair of the triple (1,1,f⁻¹), as (32) says. The
  written pair (int(f), f) is not in the subgroup {(int(h⁻¹), h)} unless f² = 1, so the missing −1 is Grothendieck's
  slip; the prose's f ↦ (int(f⁻¹), f) is an anti-homomorphism, which is why the inverse appears.
- **N31 — scan 388, (33) — unpursued implication — correct (checked).** For (u,f) = (1, ℓ₀ⁿ), (12) holds (ℓ₀ commutes
  with ℓ₀ⁿ, μ = 1), and (21) gives u_{α,β,f} = int(ℓ₀⁻ⁿ)int(ℓ₀ⁿ) = 1 with α = β = ℓ₀ⁿ, as written. The subgroup is the
  kernel of S₀M̃^!₀,₃ → M̃^!₀,₃ in (13), hence "invariant"; centrality is not claimed. The ambient group is now read
  Aut_lac (change 22).
- **N32 — scan 389, (34)–(36) — unpursued implication — correct (checked).** (34) agrees with (5) and (21):
  f″ = f′·u′(f) with u′ = int(f′⁻¹)u_{α′,β′} gives f″ = u_{α′,β′}(f)f′, and the display at the top of the page gives
  the same f″⁻¹ = f′⁻¹u_{α′,β′}(f⁻¹). The warning "Attention au passage de f à f⁻¹ !" concerns (36), where
  U·f ↦ (U, f⁻¹) turns this law into the semidirect product (35).
- **N33 — scan 389, own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N34 — scan 390, (37)–(38) — unpursued implication — correct.** Σ₀ is no longer called "impuissant" (the word is
  struck, change 25). Σ̃ = Σ₀ × {±1} is invariant as stated: Σ₀ acts trivially on G (its elements have u̲ = 1,
  scan 387), and −1 ∈ Sl(2,Ẑ) is central and fixed by inner automorphisms. Σ̃ is moreover central if Σ₀ is central in
  SG*_{ρ,σ}(Ẑ).
- **N35 — scan 390, (39)–(40) — unproved claim — correct (checked).** In the semidirect product,
  i_G(λ⁻¹)i_B(λ′) = i_B(λ′)i_G(λ′⁻¹λ⁻¹λ′), hence i(λ)i(λ′) = i_B(λλ′)i_G((λλ′)⁻¹) = i(λλ′) for any λ, λ′ ∈ B′₀;
  so (40) is a homomorphism, injective by its B′₀-component. Distinguishedness is checked on scans 391–392 (N37).
- **N36 — scan 390, own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N37 — scan 391, (40)–(42) — unproved claim — correct (checked).** The four verifications on scan 392 are right:
  i_B(x)i(λ)i_B(x)⁻¹ = i(xλx⁻¹) needs only that L₀ is normal in B′₀; i(λ) centralises i_G(G) because i_B(λ) acts on G
  as int(λ); i_ρ, i_σ commute with both factors (N39). So i(L₀) is distinguished in G*_{ρ,σ}·G.
- **N38 — scan 391, own idea (first pass) — keep.** The cancelled block (U = (u̲, a̲, b̲, ε f) = U₀·f, with a marginal
  "où U₀", change 28) begins the direct conjugation that N41 below makes general.
- **N39 — scan 392 — unproved claim — correct (settled).** "Pour les deux premiers, c'est trivial": G*_{ρ,σ} acts on G
  only through B′₀ ("via le facteur B′₀", top of the page) and B′₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ is a direct product, so i_ρ(x),
  i_σ(x) commute with i_B(λ) and with i_G(G), hence with i(λ).
- **N40 — scan 392 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N41 — scan 392, own idea (first pass) — correct (settled).** The general fact: if H acts on G through a
  homomorphism φ: H → G by inner automorphisms, then k(h) = i_H(h)i_G(φ(h)⁻¹) is a homomorphism H → H⋉G whose image
  centralises i_G(G), and (h,g) ↦ k(h)i_G(g) is an isomorphism H × G ≅ H⋉G. Here i(λ) = k(λ) for λ ∈ L₀ ⊂ B′₀, and
  k(L₀) is distinguished exactly when L₀ is normal in H.

### Scans 393–396

- **N42 — scan 393, dimensions — unproved claim — correct (checked).** The counts are coherent if B′₀ (dim 2) is
  T₀·L₀, with T₀ ≅ G_m by det (scan 396: "N′_σ →det G_m ←det,≃ T₀ ↪ G") and L₀ ≅ G_a. Then S̃M = G*·G has 6 + 4 = 10;
  SG*_{ρ,σ}, cut out of G* by the two conditions (20) of p. 487′, has dim 4, so SM has 4 + 3 = 7; the quotients by
  i(L₀) have 9 and 6. Flatness ("groupe plat") is asserted, not proved.
- **N43 — scan 393, (44) — unproved claim — correct (consequential).** With the struck S (change 29), the first line
  of (44) describes M_{ρ,σ} = SM_{ρ,σ}/i(L₀) ≃ SG*(0)·SG (dim 3 + 3 = 6), and the second M̃_{ρ,σ} ≃ G*(0)·G with
  G*(0) ≅ T₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ (dim 5 + 4 = 9), matching the dimensions above. "Le passage au quotient ne fait pas de
  difficultés" is the unproved step: it amounts to G* = G*(0)·i_B(L₀) with G*(0) ∩ i_B(L₀) = 1, so that G*(0)·G maps
  isomorphically onto the quotient.
- **N44 — scan 394, (45) — unpursued implication — keep.** The decomposition under (45) has T_σ(Ẑ) where (44) and (1)
  of scan 396 have N′_σ; the text does not explain the change.
- **N45 — scan 394, remark and (46) — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N46 — scan 394, (47) — own idea (first pass) — correct (settled).** As written, (47) has M̃^!₀,₃ on both sides (no
  "(0)", confirmed on a crop) and ends with a separate mark read "!" or ":" (change 33). The intended decomposition is
  presumably that of (31) on scan 388, S₀M̃^!₀,₃ ≃ M̃^!₀,₃[0]·Π̂₀,₃. In M̃^!₀,₃ itself, M̃^!₀,₃ = M̃^!₀,₃(0)·int(Π̂₀,₃)
  holds (by (12) every u is int(f⁻¹) times a normaliser of L₀) but is not semidirect, since the intersection contains
  int(L₀).
- **N47 — scan 395, (48)–(49) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N48 — scan 395, own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N49 — scan 396, (1)–(3) — unpursued implication — keep.** The section title in the ink is "Étude du groupe
  M_{ρ,σ} et de M_{ρ,σ} → Gl(2)"; the author's later table calls §48.X "Structure de Mρ,σ".
- **N50 — scan 396, last paragraph — unproved claim — correct (checked).** Θ is a homomorphism:
  (i_B(x)i_G(f))(i_B(x′)i_G(f′)) = i_B(xx′)i_G(x′⁻¹fx′f′) ↦ xx′x′⁻¹fx′f′ = (xf)(x′f′), the factors i_ρ, i_σ being
  ignored by Θ; and Θ(i(λ)) = λλ⁻¹ = 1. So Θ descends to M̃_{ρ,σ} once the quotient exists (N43).
- **N51 — scan 396, (2) — own idea (first pass) — correct (settled).** Θ(i_G(f)) = f, so i_G is a section of Θ, and
  by N41 the kernel {i_B(x)i_ρ(y)i_σ(z)i_G(x⁻¹)} ≅ T₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ centralises i_G(G): M̃_{ρ,σ} ≅ (T₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ) × G,
  with Θ the projection onto G. The same untwisting applies to (T_ρ × N′_σ)·G of scan 396, whose action is inner
  through N′_σ →det G_m ≅ T₀ ⊂ G; it does not apply to M_{ρ,σ} = SG*·SG itself, since T₀ acts on SG by automorphisms
  that are not inner in SG.

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scan 379, margin — conjecture (programme) — Claude (second pass).** The note announces "Par la suite,
  l'« officialisation » … des schémas(?) en groupes S₀M_{ρ,σ}, cf p. 559 ff": a later, official definition of
  S₀M_{ρ,σ} as a group scheme, which the Circle table places in §49.II ("Les groupes Mρ,σ etc. « sans signes »",
  p. 553). On these pages S₀ appears only on the arithmetic side (S₀M̃^!₀,₃) and, struck or doubtful, in (39) and (45).
  Confidence: medium.
- **C2 — scan 381, (8) — own idea (editorial) — Claude (second pass).** (8) writes S₀M̃₀,₃ ⊂ M̃₀,₃ (no S on the right,
  confirmed on a crop), while the text builds S₀M̃^!₀,₃ as a subgroup of SM̃^!₀,₃ (scan 382) and (14) maps it to
  SM̃^!₀,₃. The inclusion as written is a slip for ⊂ SM̃₀,₃; the transcription keeps the ink. Confidence: medium.
- **C3 — scan 383, (17) — own idea — Claude (second pass).** The condition "si μ − 1 = 2ν" in the label is
  automatic: for μ ∈ Ẑ*, μ is a unit in Z₂ and 2 is invertible in Z_p for odd p, so μ − 1 ∈ 2Ẑ, and ν = (μ − 1)/2 is
  unique because Ẑ has no 2-torsion. So the label ε₁(μ−1) = ℓ₁^ν applies to every μ. Confidence: high.
- **C4 — scan 386, (29) — own idea (editorial) — Claude (second pass).** The membership conditions to the right of (29)
  read α̲, α̲′ ∈ T_ρ(Ẑ) and β̲, β̲′ ∈ N′_σ(Ẑ) (the Greek letters are clear on a 2.4× crop), but (30) uses the factors
  a̲, a̲′ ∈ T_ρ(Ẑ) and b̲, b̲′ ∈ N′_σ(Ẑ) of the decomposition G*_{ρ,σ} ⊂ B′₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ. The Greek letters are slips
  for a̲, b̲; the transcription keeps them. Confidence: high.
- **C5 — scan 390, (37)–(38) — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** Whether (37) lifts from
  SM_{ρ,σ}(Ẑ)/Σ̃ to SM_{ρ,σ}(Ẑ) is measured by pulling back 1 → Σ̃ → SM_{ρ,σ}(Ẑ) → SM_{ρ,σ}(Ẑ)/Σ̃ → 1 along (37): a
  class in H²(S₀M̃^!₀,₃, (Z/2Z)³) when Σ̃ is central (N34). The text passes to the quotient instead; the Circle table's
  §49.IV ("Où on se bat avec « les signes » … dans la relation des lacets", p. 568) returns to the signs.
  Confidence: medium.


# ===== LM_C3-397-415 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-397-415 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 397–415)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-397-415/LM_C3-397-415_notes.md, "Research notes —
English", 70 notes, numbered N1–N70 in their order), each checked against LM_C3-397-415_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep,
correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's
conjectures, questions, expectations), unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved
in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks (all from these pages): M_{ρ,σ} = τ_{ρ,σ}.SG with τ_{ρ,σ} = SG^*_{ρ,σ}(0) ⊂
T_0×T_ρ×N′_σ, group law componentwise; I = Ker Θ (rel. dim 2), J = Ker(M→PG) (rel. dim 3) (scan 398); modular case
ρ = (0 1; −1 1), σ = (0 1; −1 0), τ_∞ = diag(−1, 1) (scan 403); an element n ∈ N′_σ with ε(n) = −1 acts on
T_σ = units of O_S[σ] by a + bσ ↦ a − bσ, i.e. y ↦ ȳ, and ȳ = y⁻¹ when det y = 1.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 397–399

- **N1 — 397, after (4)–(7) — unproved claim — keep.** Θ is called epi because its image contains SG and its composite
  with det is the multiplier χ, itself epi. The reduction needs that a subgroup sheaf of G containing SG and mapping onto
  G/SG ≃ G_m is all of G, which is immediate fppf-locally; the page gives only the reduction.
- **N2 — 397, (8) — unpursued implication — keep.** M^+ := Ker χ is defined just before the hom of exact sequences (9).
  How M^+ sits over SG is the content of (9); see C1.
- **N3 — 398, after (9) — unproved claim — keep.** "nécessairement plat de dim relative 2" for I = Ker Θ needs Θ
  faithfully flat (a fibrewise surjective homomorphism of flat group schemes of finite presentation) and the dimension
  count 6 − 4 = 2; neither is argued.
- **N4 — 398, last paragraph — conjecture — keep.** "on prévoit qu'il est commutatif, (de dim relative 3) — et à
  composantes toriques si T°_ρ, T°_σ sont des tores": the insertion "à composantes" is confirmed. The expectation is
  conditional and is not proved here (for the neutral component it follows from (17) on scan 400).
- **N5 — 398, (10)–(11) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Compare the filtration 1 ⊂ I ⊂ J ⊂ M (M/I ≃ G,
  M/J ≃ PG ≃ SG/μ_2, J/I ≃ G_m) with the coordinate descriptions (14) and (17)–(18). Medium confidence, as a
  consistency check.
- **N6 — 399, (12)–(13) — unproved claim — correct.** (13) is an equality in the ink (vertical "="): M_{ρ,σ} =
  {(x,y,f) | det f = det y = ε′ det x, ε′² = 1} ⊂ T_ρ×N′_σ×G. Its derivation from "par construction du produit ½ direct"
  (M = SG^*(0).SG, SG^*(0) ⊂ T_0×T_ρ×N′_σ, T_0.SG = G) is not written out. Revised: the first pass read "≃".
- **N7 — 399, (14) — unproved claim — correct.** The ink superposes I and J on the first label (now `\unsure{I|J}`) and
  has J on the second. By the definitions of scan 398 the λ-set {(x,y,λ) | det y = ε det x = λ², ε² = 1}
  (rel. dim 2+2+1−2 = 3, f = λ scalar so det f = λ²) is J, and the set {(x,y) | det y = 1, (det x)² = 1} (rel. dim 2,
  f = 1) is I. So either the overwriting ran J→I against the mathematics or the two labels are swapped; the mathematics
  does not depend on which.
- **N8 — 399, last paragraph — unpursued implication — keep.** "Il me semble plus naturel" switches to dévissage through
  the neutral component; methodological, not a connectedness proof.

### Scans 400–401

- **N9 — 400, (15) — unproved claim — keep.** M/M° ≃ G^*(0)/G^*(0)° ≃ μ_2×μ_2 rests on "cf p. 489 (23)" (§48.VIII,
  which the Circle table starts at p. 485), outside this package.
- **N10 — 400, (16)–(18) — unproved claim — keep.** The passage from the determinant equations to the kernels J° and I°
  is asserted; the isomorphisms should be given as morphisms of group functors.
- **N11 — 400, (17) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The forgetful map (x,y,λ) ↦ (x,y) from J° = {det x = det y =
  λ²} to T_ρ×_{G_m}T_σ has kernel {(1,1,±1)} ≃ μ_2 and is surjective only fppf-locally (det x needs a square root): it
  is an isogeny of degree 2, not the "≃" of (17). Whether some other isomorphism exists is not settled on the page;
  Grothendieck wrote "≃ T_ρ×_{G_m}T_σ" on the λ-line, struck it, and rewrote it on the next line. High confidence in
  the diagnostic; revised from "possible μ_2 kernel".
- **N12 — 401, theorem (19)–(20) — unproved claim — keep.** The theorem recapitulates (15); it depends on the
  definition "p. 487′ (20)" in §48.VIII, outside this package.
- **N13 — 401, (21) — unproved claim — correct.** As written ("central (dans M_{ρ,σ} tout entier)") the claim is false
  for the factor ST_σ: an element with ε = −1 acts on ST_σ by y ↦ ȳ = y⁻¹. Counterexample in the modular case on
  integral points: σ = (0 1; −1 0) gives (1,1,σ) ∈ ST_ρ×ST_σ ⊂ M(Z), τ_∞ = (τ_∞,1,τ_∞) ∈ M(Z) (36), and
  τ_∞στ_∞⁻¹ = σ⁻¹ ≠ σ (cf. scan 407, "τ_∞ opère … par symétrie sur le dernier"). ST_ρ×ST_σ is central in M°, and
  ST_ρ×μ_2 in M; the later computation (49) of scan 411 agrees. Revised from "requires the action of the quotient".
- **N14 — 401, NB between the boxes — unproved claim — correct.** "J° est encore central dans M_{ρ,σ}" fails for the
  whole M for the same reason: (1,σ,1) ∈ J° (det 1 = det σ = 1²) is conjugated to (1,σ⁻¹,1) by τ_∞. J° is central in
  M°; in fact J° = Centre(M°) (C6). The NB's unfinished "J° ∩ SG ≃" is {(1,1,λ) | λ² = 1} ≃ μ_2, the centre of SG.
- **N15 — 401, cancelled passage below (24) — unpursued implication — keep, with reason.** The inclusion G → M° is
  annotated "inexistant": a homomorphic section f ↦ (x,y,f) would need x ∈ T_ρ, y ∈ T_σ with det x = det y = det f
  chosen multiplicatively in f, which the page does not have; the page itself withdraws the stronger display.
- **N16 — 401, (25) — unpursued implication — correct.** Given (24) M^{°+} ≃ ST_ρ×ST_σ×SG (which holds: χ = det f = 1
  forces det x = det y = 1 in (16)) and the inclusions (26), the three μ_2 lie in distinct direct factors, so (25) is
  injective, as its hook arrow says. Revised: the first pass doubted injectivity.

### Scans 402–404

- **N17 — 402, (27) — unproved claim — keep.** Σ = (Z/2Z)³ → ΓM^{°+} "déjà envisagés par ailleurs"; scan 411 places
  Σ "déjà dans (IX)", §48.IX (p. 490 in the Circle table). The final superscript of (27) stays unsure (+ or *).
- **N18 — 402, (30)–(31) — unproved claim — correct.** The splitting section is named on scan 403 (32): M_{ρ,σ}(0) ↪
  M_{ρ,σ}, mapped isomorphically onto τ^!_{ρ,σ}; scan 415 (17), (22) defines M(0) = M[0] ∩ (T_0×T_ρ×N′_σ) and proves
  M(0) → τ an iso by the same pattern. What remains to check is that M(0) is a subgroup and that it acts on SG by
  conjugation through its T_0-component.
- **N19 — 402, left margin — unpursued implication — correct.** New reading: the sign "!" "est tombé du ciel" (came
  from nowhere), "même dans la section suivant le X", and Γ_{ρ,σ} (not τ_{ρ,σ} "sans l'exposant") would be the right
  name. Scan 415's margin makes the same proposal (Γ_{ρ,σ}, after Γ_{g,ν}, Γ_{0,3}); the dark ink suggests both notes
  were added after XI was written.
- **N20 — 403, Rmq — conjecture rejected — correct.** The reason is now legible: "vu que G̃^+_{ρ,σ} ≃ Sl(2)_S est
  [struck word] relt connexe sur S". A group scheme with connected fibres has no non-trivial finite étale quotient, so
  no π with G̃^+/π ≃ G̃_3 exists among schemes; that is the stated obstruction. Revised: the first pass called the
  reason faint.
- **N21 — 403, (33)–(34) — unproved claim — keep.** The canonical section τ̇ = τ̇_∞ with representative τ_∞ =
  diag(−1, +1) in ΓM^!_{ρ,σ}(0): membership is checked on scan 405 ((36)), canonicity is asserted.
- **N22 — 403, last line → 404 — conjecture — keep.** "Il serait intéressant de voir si, en dehors du cas modulaire, on
  peut introduire des anti-involutions …"; the question continues on 404.
- **N23 — 404, (35) — conjecture — keep.** "je suis convaincu que oui": r_0, r_1, r_∞ are involutions of det −1 (r_1² =
  1 checked); the reference "p. 335′, §42, IV" is §42.IV "Relations avec SL(2,Z)" (p. 335). A general construction
  would need the base hypotheses that replace the modular coordinates.
- **N24 — 404, middle correction — unpursued implication — keep.** "je me mélange là les pinceaux": the −1 sections of
  the three μ_2 lie in M°, the reflections have multiplier −1 and lie over (−1,−1) ∈ M/M°. The struck qualifier of
  "les sections" reads "modulaires".
- **N25 — 404, struck centre sentence and inserted Z — unproved claim — keep.** Z_{ρ,σ} := (Centr M°_{ρ,σ}) replaces a
  struck claim about the centre of M; the centre of M° is computed in C6.
- **N26 — 404, struck calculation after (36) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** j_ρ, j_σ: G_m → T_ρ, T_σ composed with
  det give λ ↦ λ²; this is where the squares in (17) and (49) come from. Medium confidence as a check.

### Scans 405–406

- **N27 — 405, invariants of τ_∞ — unproved claim — keep.** "les calculs déjà faits donnent" α, ζ, β, η, μ, ε, ε′ from
  earlier pages; ζ = αρ(α)⁻¹ = −ℓ_1⁻¹ = −(1 0; −2 1) agrees with ℓ_1 = (1 0; 2 1). See C3 for the matrix entry B/D.
- **N28 — 405, NB → 406 — question — keep.** For u̲ = (μ λ; 0 1) the invariants are computed on 406: α = u̲, a̲ = 1,
  ε = ε′ = μ = (−1)^k, β = uτ_∞^k, b̲ = τ_∞^k.
- **N29 — 405, tuples (36) and NB — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The ring is settled on 406: the tuple of (37)
  lies in M_{ρ,σ}[0](Ẑ). The NB's "B′_0(Ẑ) ⊂ Gl(2,Z)" has no hat on the second Z (a slip for Ẑ, or for B′_0(Z)); the
  check of the coordinates over the stated ring stays useful.
- **N30 — 406, after (37) — unproved claim — keep.** "il n'est pas dans ΓG^*(0), sauf si λ = 0" explains why the group
  name SG^*(0)(Ẑ) of (37) is replaced by M[0](Ẑ).
- **N31 — 406, left margin NB — unproved claim — keep, verified.** r_1 = (−1 1; 0 1) = τ_∞·(1 −1; 0 1) with
  (1 −1; 0 1) ∈ L_0, so r_1 ≡ τ_∞ mod L_0 and both give (−1, 1, τ_∞) in τ_{ρ,σ} = M[0]/L_0.
- **N32 — 406, 3° — expectation — keep, verified.** r_1r_∞ = (1 1; 0 1) and r_∞r_0 = (0 −1; 1 0) generate SL(2,Z), and
  det r_∞ = −1, so r_0, r_1, r_∞ generate GL(2,Z), as "ça vaut mieux" requires.
- **N33 — 406, last paragraph → 407 — unpursued implication — keep.** τ′_∞ = τ_∞σ_∞ gives σ_∞ = (0 −1; 1 0) ∈ SG(Z);
  the common image (−1, 1, τ_∞) of the three reflections is stated on 407 (39).
- **N34 — 405–406, comparison of (36)–(38) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Comparing the three images in M and in
  M/SG is useful; N31 and N33 now settle it for the quotient.

### Scans 407–408

- **N35 — 407, (40) — unproved claim — keep.** The numeral in "groupe dihédral d'ordre 4" is a 4 at enlargement;
  |{1,τ_∞}.Z/4Z| = 8, so "4" is the index n of D_n or a slip.
- **N36 — 407, (41) — unproved claim — correct.** τ_∞ lies in the third factor of a direct product, so its action on
  the first two factors is trivial without computation; on T_σ(Z) = ⟨σ⟩: τ_∞στ_∞⁻¹ = σ⁻¹, so "par symétrie" is
  inversion and (Z/2Z.Z/4Z) = D_4. Revised: verification added.
- **N37 — 407, margin NB at (42) — unpursued implication — keep.** "automatique, car G_m(Z) = {±1}" holds for
  integral points only.
- **N38 — 408, (43)–(44) — unproved claim — correct.** The ink has Z/4Z in (43) and twice in (44), while the first line
  of 408 and (42) have Z/6Z and (45) overwrites 4 by 6: (43)–(44) keep an uncorrected slip for Z/6Z×D_4. The
  semi-direct product uses the section M(0)(Z) ≃ τ(Z), acting on Sl(2,Z) through D_4 → {1,τ_∞} by conjugation.
- **N39 — 408, (45) — unpursued implication — keep.** The sign map is trivial on Z/6Z×D_4^+ and (−1,−1) on τ_∞, so its
  image on integral points is the diagonal {(1,1),(−1,−1)}: "pas surjectif" (409).

### Scans 409–410

- **N40 — 409, "pas surjectif" — unproved claim — keep.** The failure is on integral points; the scheme map
  M → μ_2×μ_2 is epi (15).
- **N41 — 409, first paragraph and margin "faux" — unproved claim — keep.** The margin's objection (g.f central
  without g acting trivially) is exactly what N13/C6 exhibit.
- **N42 — 409, (46) — unproved claim — correct.** The argument needs B′_0 to act faithfully on SG; the ink says "opère
  trivialt" (kept as written), which contradicts its use. The kernel of conjugation G → Aut(SG) is the centre G_m of
  G, and B′_0 ∩ G_m = {1}, which gives the faithfulness and implies (46).
- **N43 — 409, "Reste" and lower margin — unpursued implication — keep.** Reduction to the invariants of μ_2×μ_2 on τ°;
  the margin limits it to the intersection of the centre with τ°.
- **N44 — 409, own idea (first pass) — keep.** Centrality of (n,a) in N⋊A: a acts on N as conjugation by n⁻¹ and n is
  fixed by A. High confidence as a strategy.
- **N45 — 410, (48) — unproved claim — keep.** The action of μ_2×μ_2 on τ° factors through the first projection
  ε(b̲).
- **N46 — 410, invariants — unproved claim — keep, with caveat.** The invariants of σ ↦ −σ on T_σ are the scalars
  G_m only where 2 is invertible.
- **N47 — 410, last paragraph — unproved claim — correct.** The corrected sentence is "la trace du centre de τ_{ρ,σ}
  sur le sous-groupe τ′_{ρ,σ} … est aussi la trace, sur ce sous-groupe, de T_0×T_ρ×G_m": Centre(τ) ∩ τ′ =
  (T_0×T_ρ×G_m) ∩ τ′. With faithfulness on τ′ (τ′ → T_σ epi) Centre(τ) ⊂ τ′, hence Centre(τ) =
  (T_0×T_ρ×G_m) ∩ τ, the second line of (49). Revised: the first pass read "portion" (deleted).
- **N48 — 410, own idea (first pass) — keep.** Test the fixed-point equations over rings with 2 = 0 (see N46).

### Scans 411–412

- **N49 — 411, (49)–(50) — unproved claim — keep.** (50) is written as an equality ST′_ρ = {a̲ | (det a̲]² = 1}; the
  restriction u = 1 for trivial action on SG relies on (47)/(46), see N42.
- **N50 — 411, (51) and "faux le centre est plus grand" — unproved claim — keep.** (52) is a computation for the

- **N51 — 411, (52) NB — unpursued implication — keep.** "Ce groupe est en fait contenu dans M°(Z)".
- **N52 — 411, after (52) — unproved claim — keep, verified.** ρ³ = −1, so −ρ has order 3; det ρ = 1, so ST′_ρ(Z) =
  T_ρ(Z) ≃ Z/6Z and the group is Z/6Z × μ_2(Z) × μ_2(Z) as in (52); Σ is referred to "(IX)".
- **N53 — 411, last line — expectation — keep.** "Plus intéressant serait sans doute le centre de M°", continued on 412.
- **N54 — 411, own idea (first pass) — keep.** Solving centrality against SG and the complement together is what C6
  does.
- **N55 — 412, margin "faux ! Centre est de dim 3" — unproved claim — keep.** Confirmed by C6.
- **N56 — 412, (54) and "deux fois plus gros" — unpursued implication — keep.** Cancelled comparison on integral
  points; the label is written "(14)" for (54).
- **N57 — 412, struck Rmq — unproved claim (cancelled) — keep.** "La composante neutre du centre de M° est bien sûr
  ST_ρ×ST_σ" is withdrawn by the dimension-3 margin (C6).
- **N58 — 412, own idea (first pass) — keep.** Superseded in substance by C6, which gives the centre.

### Scans 413–415

- **N59 — 413, (1)–(3) — unproved claim — keep.** Existence, uniqueness and base change of the canonical lifts are not
  argued; (3) fixes the sign of σ (C4).
- **N60 — 413, (5)–(8) — unproved claim — keep, verified.** From (3) and (7): ε_1 = −ρσ⁻¹ = (1 0; 1 1) (6), ε_0 =
  −σ⁻¹ρ = (1 1/v; 0 1) (8), det ρ = det σ = −v, Tr ρ = 1, Tr σ = 0. The épinglage is fixed by (6); its global

- **N62 — 413, (7) — unpursued implication — keep.** The single parameter v ∈ ΓG_m.
- **N63 — 414, λ versus λ/v — own idea (first pass) — keep.** L_0 = {(1 λ; 0 1)} as a set and ε_0(λ) = (1 λ/v; 0 1)

  v = −1 they are −3 and −4, the units Z[ζ_6]^* ≃ Z/6Z and Z[i]^* ≃ Z/4Z of (40).

- **N67 — 415, (17)–(19) — unproved claim — keep.** M[0] ≃ M(0).L_0 (½-direct) is asserted.
