## New notes (Claude, second pass)

- **C1 — 398, (9) — own idea (Claude, second pass).** The right vertical arrow G_m → G_m (its label "det" struck) must be
  the identity, since det∘Θ = χ (397). The snake lemma then gives M^+ → SG epi with kernel I_{ρ,σ} (I ⊂ M^+ because
  χ = det∘Θ is trivial on Ker Θ). Confidence high.
- **C2 — 401, (24) — own idea (Claude, second pass).** M^{°+} = Ker(χ|M°) = {(x,y,f) | det x = det y = det f = 1} =
  ST_ρ×ST_σ×SG inside T_ρ×T_σ×G by (16): (24) holds, with relative dimension 1+1+3 = 5 = 6 − 1. Confidence high.
- **C3 — 405, matrix u — own idea (Claude, second pass).** The ink writes the upper-right entry as "B/D = μD"; with
  1/D = μ it is μB. Here B = 0, so u = diag(−1, 1) = τ_∞ either way; the slip does not affect (36). Confidence high
  for the algebra, the ink reading kept.
- **C4 — 413, (3) and (7) — unpursued implication (Claude, second pass).** In the modular case v = −1, (7) gives
  σ = (0 −1; 1 0), which is −1 times the σ of scan 403 and equals σ_∞ of scan 406. The sign is forced by (3): with
  σ = (0 1; −1 0), −σ⁻¹ρ = (−1 1; 0 −1) is not unipotent. So the canonical lift of σ̃ in XI is not the σ of the
  "cas modulaire" of 403; formulas carried over from 403–412 should be checked for this sign. Confidence high.
- **C5 — 401, (21) and NB — unproved claim (Claude, second pass).** The statements "ST_ρ×ST_σ … central (dans M tout
  entier)" and "J° est encore central dans M" are false as stated for the whole group; the counterexample is in N13
  and N14 (τ_∞ inverts σ ∈ ST_σ(Z)). Both are true for M°. The later pages (409–412) compute the centre with T_σ-part
  G_m and so contradict (21) without saying so. Confidence high.
- **C6 — 412, centre of M° — own idea (Claude, second pass).** Centre(M°) = J°: Θ: M° → G is epi, so a central element
  maps into the centre G_m of G, whence Centre(M°) ⊂ Ker(M° → PG) = J°; conversely J° ⊂ T_ρ×T_σ×G_m is central in
  M° ⊂ T_ρ×T_σ×G. So the centre is {(x,y,λ) | det x = det y = λ²}, of relative dimension 3, as the margin "faux !
  Centre est de dim 3" says against the struck (53), and its neutral component is not ST_ρ×ST_σ (dimension 2).
  Confidence high.
- **C7 — 409, (46)–(47) — unpursued implication (Claude, second pass).** With N42 (faithful action of B′_0), the
  subgroup of τ acting trivially on SG is τ ∩ (1×T_ρ×N′_σ) = {(1,a̲,b̲) | det b̲ = 1, (det a̲)² = 1}, which is (47) as
  written; the left-margin "faux" concerns the next step (centre of a semi-direct product), not (47). Confidence
  medium.
- **C8 — 403, reference — unpursued implication (Claude, second pass).** "(§46, V)": the Circle table's §46.V,
  "Récapitulation en termes de M̃^!_{0,3}[0]; les sous-groupes M̃^!_{0,3}(q), q ∈ {0,1,∞}" (p. 407), is the source of
  M^{!~}_{0,3}(0) ⊂ M̃_{0,3} with which M_{ρ,σ}(0) is compared; reading the two together would show whether the
  analogy extends to the three subgroups q ∈ {0,1,∞} (here: r_0, r_1, r_∞). Confidence medium.


# ===== LM_C3-416-434 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-416-434 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 416–434)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-416-434/LM_C3-416-434_notes.md, "Research notes",
1–52), each checked against LM_C3-416-434_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked
**Claude (second pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, plans),
unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved at the cited place. Totals: 31 kept,
20 corrected, 1 dropped, 6 new.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 416–419

- **N1 — scan 416, (24) — unproved claim — correct.** The margin, now read securely as "car on aura T₀ —det, ∼→ G_m",
  is the author's reason for "donne un mono": the T₀-coordinate of a point of M(0) is recovered from its determinant.
  Still unproved here is that this determinant is a function of (a, b) on M(0) (presumably χ(a, b) = det b, (25)).
- **N2 — scan 416, (27)–(28) — unproved claim — keep.** Exactness and the surjectivity of χ are asserted, not proved;
  the meaning of "épi" (sheaves, not points) matters, as C6 shows for a similar map.
- **N3 — scan 416, (30)–(31) — unpursued implication — keep.** The reading g(ε₀(λ)) = ε₀(μλ), μ = χ(g), is now secure.
  The compatibility is asserted after (38) and restated at the top of scan 418.
- **N4 — scan 417, (37)–(38) — unproved claim — correct.** The ink says that i "prolonge les inclusions" of the common
  subgroup M(0), i.e. i extends both M(0) ⊂ M[0] and M(0) ⊂ M. What needs proof is that i(λ) = j(λ) on L₀ is compatible
  with the two actions of M(0), which the parenthesis after (38) asserts. The NB's "[0)" is in the ink and designates the
  subgroup M(0) as against the quotient T, so the delimiter question raised by the first pass is settled.
- **N5 — scan 418, Remarque — unproved claim (was: conjecture) — correct.** The relation is ⊂ in the ink: the Remarque
  states M[0] ⊂ Norm_M(L₀), true "par construction", and what is "pas vrai du tout" is the reverse inclusion, which would
  make L₀ its own normalizer in SG. The struck "Proposition" and "on a ⊂, pour ⊃" record the downgrade from a planned
  equality. The first pass's "the displayed normalizer equality remains" was a misreading.
- **N6 — scan 418, last paragraph — conjecture — keep.** The profinite analogue (the subgroup generated by ε₀ in
  G̃_{0,3}^{+̂}, and by l₀ in Π̂_{0,3}, self-normalizing) is asserted "moyennant vérification ! d'ailleurs que je n'ai
  tjrs pas fait". The notation G̃_{0,3}^{+̂} and the word "fait" are now secure.
- **N7 — scan 418, normalizer — own idea — correct.** No matrix model is needed: by (30)–(31) M(0) acts on L₀ through
  χ, and a scalar multiple of an element with χ ≠ 1 lies in SG (fppf-locally), normalizes L₀ without being in it (L₀ is
  commutative). So the reverse inclusion fails, as the author says; see C1.
- **N8 — scan 419, (39)–(41) — unproved claim — keep.** The component group μ₂ × μ₂ and the identification (40) need the
  base and connectedness hypotheses the note names.
- **N9 — scan 419, (45) — unproved claim — keep.** The centrality of ST_ρ × ST_σ in M° but not in M is computed through
  the component action, explicit in (68).

### Scans 420–424

- **N10 — scan 420, margin of (47) — unproved claim — correct.** The strictness is settled within the package: by (75)
  Centr(M°) = R°, which maps onto G_m by (70), while 1 × ST_ρ × ST_σ × μ₂ maps into μ₂ ⊂ G_m. So the inclusion (47) is
  strict, as the margin warns; "cf plus bas" points to (75). See C2.
- **N11 — scan 420, (48)–(52) — unproved claim — keep.** Centrality in all of M has to be checked against the components
  outside M°, as the note says.
- **N12 — scan 420, (53) — unpursued implication — correct.** "cf VI" is §48.VI, "Caractérisation du triplet (·, σ, ρ)
  modulaire" (p. 462 in the author's table), outside this package. The equivalences listed in the parenthesis are proved
  there, if anywhere, not here.
- **N13 — scan 421, after (55) — unproved claim — correct.** The ink reads "l'hom (53)", but the homomorphism meant is
  (54), ℤ/6ℤ × ℤ/4ℤ → Centr(M°); the transcription keeps (53). Injectivity when 2 is invertible is immediate: i_ρ and
  i_σ land in different factors of T₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ, and ρ, σ have orders 6 and 4. In characteristic 2, ρ³ = −1 = 1 and
  σ² = 1, so the kernel is non-trivial.
- **N14 — scan 421, (56) and addition — unproved claim — keep.** The integral points and the action through ε₂ and τ_∞
  are asserted without computation.
- **N15 — scan 421, (56) — own idea — keep.** The test by explicit integer matrices is sound. The group letter of the
  addition (𝔻/ℬ/𝒯) is still unread; C6 computes part of the integral points.
- **N16 — scan 422, (57)–(58) — unproved claim — keep.** That the restrictions define a homomorphism Θ needs their
  compatibility with the semi-direct product structure.
- **N17 — scan 422, (59)–(62) — unproved claim — keep.** Accurate.
- **N18 — scan 422, struck identifications under (61) — own idea — correct.** (75) settles it: Centr(M°) = R° contains
  Ker Θ° = SR° with quotient G_m (70), so the struck "Ker Θ° ≃ Rad(M°) ≃ Centr°(M°)" would be false once R° is connected,
  which (88) asserts ("comp. neutre de R"). The deletion is borne out; see C3.
- **N19 — scan 423, (64)–(66) — unproved claim — keep.** The splitting of (64) by Θ^♮ is asserted in one line.
- **N20 — scan 423, last paragraph — unpursued implication — correct.** The ink, re-checked, says "où SR_ρ,σ est central
  dans M_ρ,σ", against "distingué mais pas central dans M tout entier" at the top of the page. The inference "donc G y
  opère trivialement" needs only centrality in M° (G ≃ M°/SR°), so M° is meant. The base-change question stands.
- **N21 — scan 423, before (65) — own idea — correct.** Re-checked on enlargement: "étant trivial sur R_ρ,σ" has
  neither S nor circle. Θ is trivial on SR° = Ker Θ°, not on R°, whose image is G_m, so this is a slip for SR°_ρ,σ, kept
  as written.
- **N22 — scan 424, (68) — unproved claim — keep.** The action σ ↦ εσ and its compatibility with σ² − ν = 0 are
  asserted.
- **N23 — scan 424, (69)–(70) and last formula — unpursued implication — keep.** Accurate; the square-root condition
  becomes the cartesian square (74).
- **N24 — scan 424, diagonal margin — conjecture (plan) — correct.** The margin reads "Il y aurait intérêt à
  commen[cer] tt de suite avec l'introduction de R_ρ,σ et la réalisation (102) plus bas de M_ρ,σ, en termes de sommes
  amalg. des prod… cf 6°". It is a plan to reorder the exposition. It cites (102), which lies beyond scan 434, so it was
  added later.
- **N25 — scan 424, last formula — own idea — keep.** Keeping λ with λ² = det u explicit is useful for C6.

### Scans 425–429

- **N26 — scan 425, (73)–(74) — unproved claim — keep.** The adjective after "revêtement" is still unread; nothing
  should rest on "algébrique".
- **N27 — scan 425, (74) — own idea — keep.** Comparing bases with and without 2 invertible is the right test (cf. C6).
- **N28 — scan 426, proof of (75) — own idea — correct.** The section on scan 426 reads (j_{T₀}(μ²), a, b)·(λ j_{T₀}(μ⁻²))
  (λ secure, subscript blotted), against μ j_G(μ⁻²) in (71). With (71)'s factor the argument is complete as written;
  see C5.
- **N29 — scan 427, (77)–(79) — unpursued implication — keep.** Accurate.
- **N30 — scan 428, middle paragraph — unpursued implication — keep.** The reconstruction is "en termes des schémas en
  groupes (± toriques) en l'air T_ρ, T_σ" and the characters δ_ρ, δ_σ, without the embeddings in G.
- **N31 — scan 428, last paragraph — conjecture — keep.** "le scindage ``de travers''" is now secure: the splitting
  given by M°(0) is called skew, and is to play "un rôle essentiel".
- **N32 — scan 429, Remarque — conjecture — keep.** The deferred construction stands. The superscript of the new R is a
  heavy wave, more likely ~ than ∞, matching the struck "il faudrait peut-être noter R~".
- **N33 — scan 429, diagonal margin — unproved claim — correct.** The sequence is numbered (81) in the ink, over an
  illegible struck number, and repeats the number of the different (81) of scan 430; it is (87) of scan 431. Surjectivity
  onto PG follows from Θ̃ restricted to G̃⁺ ≃ SG being SG → PG.
- **N34 — scan 429, components of the scalar preimage — own idea — keep.** Still open in these pages.
- **N35 — scan 430, (81)–(82) — unproved claim — correct.** The identities T_ρ(ℤ) = ST_ρ(ℤ), T_σ(ℤ) = ST_σ(ℤ) hold
  because the norm forms of ℤ[ζ₆] and ℤ[i] are positive definite, and (82) follows from (74) because μ² = 1 for
  μ ∈ G_m(ℤ) = {±1}. Verified under the assumption stated in C6.
- **N36 — scan 430, (84)–(85) — unpursued implication — drop.** It repeats N37 and says nothing specific; C6 gives the
  integral-point computation it calls for.
- **N37 — scan 430, (84)–(85) — own idea — keep.** Testing on rings other than ℤ is the way to see that (85) is special
  to ℤ (cf. C6).

### Scans 431–434

- **N38 — scan 431, (88) — unproved claim — keep.** That R ∩ M° is the neutral component of R needs a connectedness
  argument.
- **N39 — scan 431, (89)–(90) — unproved claim — keep.** Accurate.
- **N40 — scan 431, (92)–(93) — unpursued implication — correct.** (93) is immediate from (91)–(92): for g ∈ R,
  Θ(g) = κ(g)·1 and det(κ·1) = κ² in Gl(2). Only the epimorphism R → G_m in (90) needs care over a general base.
- **N41 — scan 432, margin NB — unproved claim — correct.** There is no "(?)": the mark was the label (94) of the main
  display, crossed by the slanting note. Grothendieck says a verification is needed, reduces it to SR → μ₂ × μ₂ being an
  epimorphism, and calls it trivial. On ℤ-points this map is trivial (C6), so the claim holds only for sheaves.
- **N42 — scan 432, (94)–(95) — unproved claim — keep.** "cart et cocart" is asserted for each square.
- **N43 — scan 432, (96) — unpursued implication — keep.** Accurate.
- **N44 — scan 432, (95)–(96) — own idea — correct.** The lower middle vertex of (95) reads SR_ρ,σ on enlargement (no
  circle), where R°_ρ,σ is required; the fibre-product check must use R°.
- **N45 — scan 433, first sentence — unpursued implication — keep.** Accurate.
- **N46 — scan 433, cube — unproved claim — correct.** The cube is numbered (95) in the ink, repeating the prism's
  number, not (96). The claims (faces cartesian and cocartesian, kernels μ₂ or 1) are otherwise as the note says.
- **N47 — scan 433, (97) — unproved claim — keep.** Accurate; the proof is on scan 434.
- **N48 — scan 433, dashed squares of the cube — own idea — correct.** It refers to the cube numbered (95); otherwise
  sound.
- **N49 — scan 434, opening displays — unpursued implication — correct.** The circle on the first R is inked over and
  the underbrace reads Ker Θ̃. The first display therefore concerns R and the second R°, which is what row 1 of (97)
  needs. Both kernels are μ₂ because Θ̃|SG = Θ̃°|SG is SG → PG.
- **N50 — scan 434, (98) — own idea — keep.** The second isomorphism compares (62) with (28); the first adds the
  five lemma over μ₂ × μ₂.
- **N51 — scan 434, last paragraph — unpursued implication — keep.** The second splitting is only announced.
- **N52 — scan 434, last paragraph — own idea — keep.** Using SR ≅ T⁺ with SR ∩ SG = {1} for the second splitting is a
  reasonable expectation. The page does not yet define the splitting.

## Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scan 418, Remarque — unpursued implication.** Since M = M(0)·SG and M(0) normalizes L₀,
  Norm_M(L₀) = M(0)·Norm_SG(L₀), so the defect of the Remarque's inclusion is exactly Norm_SG(L₀)/L₀. By (30)–(31) an
  element of T₀ acts on L₀ by λ ↦ χ·λ; its product with a scalar c, c² = det⁻¹, lies in SG (fppf-locally), normalizes L₀
  and is not in L₀ when χ ≠ 1. Confidence medium-high: it assumes L₀ commutative, as the parametrization ε₀(λ)
  suggests.
- **C2 — scans 420 and 425, (47) and (75) — unpursued implication.** The subgroup 1 × ST_ρ × ST_σ × μ₂ of (47) has image
  μ₂ under the scalar map R° → G_m (ST_ρ × ST_σ = Ker Θ°, and the centre μ₂ of SG goes to ±1). That map is an
  epimorphism by (70), and (75) gives Centr(M°) = R°. So (47) is a strict inclusion, as its margin says. Confidence high.
- **C3 — scans 420, 422 and 431, R and the radical — own idea.** The struck "Rad(M°)" under (61), the margin "à propos des
  radicalités" (scan 420) and the heading "Groupes mixtes du radical (R, SR, R°, SR°…)" (scan 431) suggest that the
  letter R names radicals. (75) identifies R° with the centre of M°, i.e. the radical once R° is connected (88).
  Confidence medium: the naming is an inference.
- **C4 — scan 425, (72) — own idea.** The matrix as written, (μ 1; 0 1), has determinant μ, so it is not in SG despite
  "∈ ΓSG", and it is not multiplicative in μ. The author's struck S in "j_G : G_m → G" points the same way. The NB of
  (71) (det j_G(μ⁻²) = μ⁻²) needs a homomorphism G_m → T₀ ⊂ G with determinant the identity, e.g. diag(μ, 1); which torus
  T₀ is meant must be read from its definition earlier in §48. Confidence high on the arithmetic, low on the intended
  matrix.
- **C5 — scan 426, proof of (75) — unproved claim checked.** Take the section in the form (71),
  (j_{T₀}(μ²), a, b)·μ j_G(μ⁻²). The first factor acts on SG through T₀ → PG as int j_G(μ²) (31). The second has
  determinant μ²·μ⁻² = 1, so it lies in SG, and acts as int j_G(μ⁻²) because the scalar μ is central. Hence the product
  acts trivially, and the proof is complete once μ exists, i.e. fppf-locally on the base. Confidence high.
- **C6 — scans 430, 432 and 434, integral points in the modular case — own idea.** Take T_ρ, T_σ to be the centralizer
  tori with det = norm, as (56) and (81) presuppose. The norm forms of ℤ[ρ] = ℤ[ζ₆] and ℤ[σ] = ℤ[i] are positive definite,
  so every integral point of T_ρ, T_σ has determinant 1. The non-identity coset of N′_σ(ℤ) = 𝔻₄ consists of matrices
  (a b; b −a) of determinant −(a² + b²) = −1. Hence S′T_ρ(ℤ) = ST_ρ(ℤ), SN′_σ(ℤ) = ST_σ(ℤ), SR(ℤ) = SR°(ℤ) (by (59) and
  (98)), and SR → μ₂ × μ₂ (scan 432, NB) is trivial on ℤ-points; it is an epimorphism of fppf sheaves, the conics
  a² + b² = −1 and x² − xy + y² = −1 being flat and surjective over ℤ. Confidence high on the arithmetic, medium on the
  identification of the tori.


# ===== LM_C3-435-453 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-435-453 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 435–453)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-435-453/LM_C3-435-453_notes.md, §5, notes 1–46). Each
is checked against LM_C3-435-453_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked
**Claude (second pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations), unproved
claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Notation used in the checks (all read off these pages):
- Θ: M → G and Θ̃ = can∘Θ: M → PG.
- R = Ker Θ̃, SR = Ker Θ, κ: R → G_m ≃ Centr(G) ((112)), and χ = det∘Θ.
- M = (R × SG)/μ₂, with μ₂ embedded by ε ↦ (ε, ε⁻¹). This is (102), called "la définition de M_ρσ" at (148) on 452.
- Elements of T_σ are written cσ + d, with σ² = ν and det = d² − νc² (445).
- On N′_σ the determinant is written δ_σ up to scan 446 and δ′_σ from 448 on; (137) sets δ_σ = δ′_σ|T_σ.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 435–439

- **N1 — 435, (99)–(101) — unproved claim — correct.** That R commutes with SG holds by construction: (148) defines M
  as (R × SG)/μ₂ ("c'est ici la définition de M_ρσ", 452), and R ∩ SG = μ₂ is the kernel of that quotient. The author
  first wrote and then struck "mais R_ρσ ne commute plus". The stated reason, "par définition de R comme Ker Θ̃", only
  puts the commutator [r, s] in SR = Ker Θ, because Θ(r) is central. Revised: the definition (148) supplies the
  commutation.
- **N2 — 436, (104)–(105) — unproved claim — correct.** The square is numbered (105), not (105′) (change 7). The chain
  checks out as follows. uf = λ with det f = 1 gives det u = λ² (the author's "d'où λ² = det u"). Then u and
  ε′ = det b/det a are eliminated, which leaves {(a, b, λ) | det b = λ², (det a)² = (det b)²}. That set is the
  displayed iterated fibre product. For the cartesian square, see C1.
- **N3 — 437, after (106) — unpursued implication — correct.** The inserted claim can be checked directly. The inverse
  image of 𝒯⁺ in R is SR·μ₂ = SR × μ₂: μ₂ = Ker(R → 𝒯) meets SR = Ker Θ trivially, because Θ maps that central μ₂
  isomorphically into the centre of G. So the face (*) is not cartesian. The lower square of (106) is the cartesian
  square (105); the cubes (95)–(96) themselves are on earlier pages.
- **N4 — 438, (111)–(114) — unproved claim — correct.** The descent is immediate. The map (r, s) ↦ κ(r)s on R × SG kills
  (ε, ε⁻¹), because κ restricted to μ₂ is the inclusion (the "‖" column of (106)). Exactness of (114) follows from (102)
  together with R/μ₂ ≃ 𝒯 ((151)). Revised: verified.
- **N5 — 439 — conjecture/expectation — keep.** "permettront peut-être de les retrouver" is an expectation. The data
  named in it remain doubtful readings, both the word "entiers" and the argument (w|ω), so the structure meant cannot be
  stated precisely from this page.

### Scans 440–444

- **N6 — 440, end — unproved claim — keep.** The common quotient μ₂ × μ₂ of the vertical inclusions is given on 451 by
  (ε₃, ε₂) ((145)–(146)). That identifies the four quotients the author calls isomorphic. The cartesian and
  cocartesian properties of the lateral faces are still unverified.
- **N7 — 436–438 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Testing on rings with nilpotents is relevant: the isogeny R → 𝒯 and
  λ ↦ λ² have kernel μ₂, which is not étale over 2. C1 gives the fppf argument for (105).
- **N8 — 438 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Characters of M that are trivial on SG factor through 𝒯 = M/SG. (107)
  builds 𝒯 together with χ and (ε₃, ε₂), and (143) calls (χ, ε₃, ε₂): M → G_m × μ₂ × μ₂ an epimorphism.
- **N9 — 441, after (117) — unproved claim — correct.** The face is "la face composée antérieure gauche" (change 14): the
  composite of the left faces of the two adjoining cubes (SR → M⁺ → 𝒯⁺ over the ° row). The product formula follows
  from (123): χ(r, s) = κ(r)², so M⁺ = (κ⁻¹(μ₂) × SG)/μ₂ = (SR × μ₂ × SG)/μ₂ ≃ SR × SG. This is (149).
- **N10 — 441, (118) — unpursued implication — correct.** The ink cites "(cf. (107), (103))"; the 7 is probably a slip
  for (102) (change 15). The μ₂-covers M̃ = R × SG and M̃° = R° × SG are the middle terms of (102) and (103). Where μ₂ is
  not étale, "revêtement" has to be read as a μ₂-torsor (fppf).
- **N11 — 442, (118)–(120) — unproved claim — correct.** The corrected text reads "dont le composé avec χ : 𝒯° → G_m
  est donné en effet par λ ↦ λ² (104)" (changes 16, 17). So χ∘i_𝒯 = (λ ↦ λ²), not χ = i_𝒯. R° is the fibre product of
  𝒯° and G_m over χ and λ ↦ λ² (the ° form of (105), C1). The pair (i_𝒯, id) therefore gives a unique i_R with
  can∘i_R = i_𝒯 and κ∘i_R = id, and (120) and (122) record exactly these two conditions.
- **N12 — 442, opening — unproved claim — keep.** That the four squares of the faces sup. and inf. become cartesian and
  cocartesian after passing to the μ₂-covers is not verified on these pages.
- **N13 — 443, (122)–(123) — unproved claim — correct.** The centrality needed for a direct product can be read off
  (124). i_R(λ) = (1, 1, λ) maps to (λ, λ), a pair of scalars, which are central in T_ρ × N′_σ. The kernel of
  R → T_ρ × N′_σ is the central μ₂ of (125), so every commutator [i_R(λ), r] lies in μ₂ and is trivial at λ = 1. That
  makes the product direct over reduced bases; the general case needs the explicit description (104).
- **N14 — 443, (121) — unpursued implication — keep.** Only one row and an unattached vertical stroke are drawn. Nothing
  may be supplied.
- **N15 — 443, (123) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The inverse r ↦ (r·i_R(κ(r))⁻¹, κ(r)) is right, since Ker κ = SR
  (on R, Θ = incl∘κ by (112)).
- **N16 — 444, (124)–(126) — unpursued implication — keep.** The kernel is computed on points. (126) presents 𝒯 as a
  quotient by the diagonal μ₂; reading it as a quotient of fppf sheaves needs no extra argument.
- **N17 — 444, right margin — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The computation: det(λa) = λ² det a and (det a)² = 1,
  so det(λa)² = λ⁴ = det(λb)², since det b = 1. The equality holds. The label "λ²" under det(λa) is right only up to the
  sign det a = ±1, which the square removes.
- **N18 — 444, (127) — unpursued implication — keep.** Identifying the triple map with (48) needs the earlier page.

### Scans 445–447

- **N19 — 445, (128) — conjecture/question — keep.** The "?" under Cent(SN′_σ) marks the open step. The cancelled
  element-wise computation and the inserted (130) are two successive attempts at it (C3).
- **N20 — 445, inserted (130) — unproved claim — correct.** The step holds over any base (C3). The conjugation action of
  N′_σ/T_σ ≃ μ₂ on T_σ is the μ₂-action d + cσ ↦ d + tcσ. Its scheme-theoretic fixed points are c = 0, i.e. G_m. Hence
  Cent(SN′_σ) = ST_σ ∩ G_m = μ₂ without "si 2 inv.", which the author struck.
- **N21 — 445, cancelled computation — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The comparison it proposed explains the
  revision. The struck argument used the point −1 (acting by x ↦ x⁻¹), so it computed ₂(ST_σ). That equals μ₂ only if
  2 is invertible; for sections it suffices that 2 be a regular section of 𝒪_S (the cancelled hypothesis, change 25).
  The μ₂-fixed-point argument removes the hypothesis.
- **N22 — 445, Cent(M) = Cent(R) — unpursued implication — keep.** Verified on the page's own terms. Cent(M) ⊂ R because
  PG has trivial centre. An element of R commutes with SG, so it is central in M = R·SG iff it is central in R. Then
  R ≃ S′T_ρ × SN′_σ × G_m gives (128).
- **N23 — 446, (130)–(132) — unproved claim — correct.** Assume T_ρ and T_σ are the unit tori of ℤ[ζ₆] and ℤ[i] in the
  modular case; this is not restated here. Then T_ρ(ℤ) ≃ ℤ/6ℤ and T_σ(ℤ) ≃ ℤ/4ℤ, and all these units have norm 1.
  Elements of N′_σ(ℤ) outside T_σ(ℤ) have determinant −1. So S′T_ρ(ℤ) = T_ρ(ℤ) and SN′_σ(ℤ) = T_σ(ℤ), as the labels
  of (130) say.
- **N24 — 446, (133) — unpursued implication — keep.** On ℤ-points the quotients μ₂ × μ₂ and μ₂ of the horizontal
  arrows disappear (N26). The dimensions 3 and 2 are those of the generic fibres.
- **N25 — 446, (131)–(132) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** The orders are 24 and 48. The inclusion is
  Cent(M)(ℤ) = (Cent(M) ∩ M°)(ℤ) ⊂ R°(ℤ), of index 2, as 447 states ("qui en est un sous-groupe d'indice 2").
- **N26 — 447, opening — unproved claim — correct.** Verified given N23. R°(ℤ) → R(ℤ) is bijective because
  S′T_ρ(ℤ) = ST_ρ(ℤ) and SN′_σ(ℤ) = ST_σ(ℤ). The same holds for ST_ρ(ℤ) × μ₂(ℤ) × G_m(ℤ) → S′T_ρ(ℤ) × μ₂(ℤ) × G_m(ℤ).
  This is a statement about ℤ-points only, as written.
- **N27 — 447, (136) — conjecture/expectation — keep.** "on aura un homomorphisme canonique M̃₀,₃ → M_ρσ(Ẑ)/Σ"
  announces a construction that is not given here.
- **N28 — 447, (135)–(136) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Σ = {±1}³ is exactly the 2-torsion of
  Cent(M)(ℤ) ≃ ℤ/6ℤ × {±1}² ("d'ordre 8, d'indice 3"). Whether it is the right ambiguity group remains open on this page.

### Scans 448–451

- **N29 — 448, after (134) — unproved claim — keep.** "se reconstruit … à partir des données (135)" is announced. The
  checks follow on 449–451 ((137)–(147)).
- **N30 — 448, (134)–(135) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** (137) on 449 settles the notation: SN′_σ = Ker δ′_σ and
  δ_σ = δ′_σ|T_σ. The unprimed δ_σ on N′_σ in (104), (108)–(110), (135) and (152′) is either earlier notation or a slip;
  (144) writes δ′_σΘ_σ.
- **N31 — 449, first paragraph — unproved claim — keep.** M is described as the quotient of the "somme" of the four
  factors by the diagonal μ₂. The struck "amalgamée" shows that the amalgamated sum was replaced by this quotient.
- **N32 — 449, second paragraph — unproved claim — correct.** The inserted wording is "et que leur produit de leurs
  valeurs sur les images d'un ε ∈ Γμ₂ est triv." (change 27). This is the condition for the four composites to descend
  through the diagonal μ₂. (138)–(141) record it as "compatibilités triviales / satisfaites".
- **N33 — 449, (137)–(138) — own idea (first pass) — keep, extended.** Three visible slips are recorded, without
  emendation:
  - "μ₂ ⊂ G_m —i_ρ→ T_σ" in (137); (135) has i_ρ: G_m → T_ρ.
  - "Θ_Gj_μ: G_m → G_m incl. can." in (138); (136) has j: G_m ↪ G.
  - "Θ_ρj_ρ: S′T_σ ↪ T_ρ" in (139) on 450, for S′T_ρ.
- **N34 — 450, (139)–(140) — unproved claim — keep.** "compatibilités triviales" is asserted, not checked.
- **N35 — 450, (143) — unproved claim — keep.** The map is an epimorphism of fppf sheaves at best: on G_m (via j_μ),
  χ is λ ↦ λ², which is not surjective on points.
- **N36 — 450, (142)–(143) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The arrow M → PG in (142) is a plain map Θ̃ (change 29),
  not a two-headed one.
- **N37 — 451, (144) — unproved claim — correct.** This is consistent with (110): there ε₃ = δ_ρ(a)/δ_σ(b) and
  χ = δ_σ(b), so δ_ρ(a) = ε₃χ, which is the second line of (144). The first line is χ = δ′_σ(b) in the primed notation.
- **N38 — 451, end and (147) — unproved claim — correct.** The word is "dévissages" (change 31), not "clivages". (147)
  lists the four product decompositions; the cartesian and cocartesian claims for (94)–(96), (116), (117) remain
  unverified here.
- **N39 — 451, margin — unproved claim — keep.** "dont le sens est évident sur le diagramme (134)" points to the recap
  diagram.
- **N40 — 451, (145)–(146) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** In R°_ρσ = 'M_ρσ ∩ R_ρσ, the circle of the first M is
  displaced or missing, as the first pass noted.

### Scans 452–453

- **N41 — 452, (148)–(150) — unpursued implication — keep.** (148) is declared the definition of M (see N1). The
  topology in which the exact sequences are meant is not stated.
- **N42 — 453, upper diagonal note — unproved claim — keep.** "Il faudrait expliciter que les égalités (152)′
  caractérisent M_ρσ comme sous-groupe de G × T_ρ × N′_σ — il faudrait faire la vérification" is the author's own
  request. The last clause stays doubtful.
- **N43 — 453, (152′) — unpursued implication — correct.** The second line now reads \unsure{ε′|ε} = ε_σ(b) (change 32).
  With the notation of (104) and (109) (ε′ the sign in δ_σ(b) = ε′δ_ρ(a)), the first-pass reading fits. If the ink's
  ε′ is right, the signs have been renamed.
- **N44 — 453, foot — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N45 — 453, NB — unproved claim — keep.** "multiplicatif" stays doubt-marked.
- **N46 — 453, last note — unpursued implication — keep.** "On pourrait introduire ξ, η, g, h … mais peu nous chaut ici."

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — 436, (105) — unproved claim (checked).** The square "R → 𝒯 over G_m —δ²→ G_m, diagramme cartésien" is
  cartesian as fppf sheaves.
  - Injective: r ↦ (r̄, κ(r)) has kernel Ker(R → 𝒯) ∩ Ker κ = μ₂ ∩ Ker κ = 1, since κ|μ₂ is the inclusion ((106)).
  - Surjective: given (t, λ) with χ(t) = λ², lift t locally to r. Then κ(r)² = λ², so κ(r)λ⁻¹ = ε ∈ μ₂, and rε⁻¹ is
    the required point.
- **C2 — 437, (106) and 442 — own idea.** The author's correction "celle-ci n'est pas cartésienne" (N3) and the later
  "unique hom (118)" (N11) rest on one fact: the cartesian square is the lower square (105), not the upper face (*).
  The second pass's renumbering (105′ → 105) makes the references on 437 and 442 point to that square.
- **C3 — 445, centre of SN′_σ — unproved claim (checked; assumption stated).** Assume ε_σ(n) is defined by
  nσn⁻¹ = ε_σ(n)σ, as T_σ = Ker ε_σ and N′_σ/T_σ ≃ μ₂ suggest. Then n acts on T_σ by d + cσ ↦ d + ε_σ(n)cσ: a μ₂-action,
  i.e. a ℤ/2-grading. Its fixed-point subscheme is c = 0 over every base, because c(t − 1) = 0 in R[t]/(t² − 1) forces
  c = 0. So T_σ^{μ₂} = G_m (the inserted (130)) and Cent(SN′_σ) = ST_σ ∩ G_m = μ₂ hold without "si 2 inv.", provided
  ε_σ: SN′_σ → μ₂ is an epimorphism, as the faithfulness statement asserts. The struck route through the element −1
  gives only ₂(ST_σ), which is larger in characteristic 2. For example, with σ² = −1 (the modular σ), over 𝔽₂ the
  identities g² = d² + c² = det g make every point of ST_σ 2-torsion.
- **C4 — 440–441, (116)/(117) — own idea.** The same arrow R° ↪ M° carries PG in (116) and SG in (117) (change 13). Since
  R° = Ker Θ̃° and Θ̃° is onto (SG → PG is), M°/R° ≃ PG, so "SG" in (117) is probably a slip. The arrow just beside it,
  SR° ↪ M⁺°, rightly carries SG (M⁺°/SR° ≃ SG).
- **C5 — 441–446, numbering — editorial note.**
  - The number (118) is used twice: on 441 for the μ₂-covers M̃, and on 442 for i_R. The "(118)" later on 442 means
    i_R.
  - On 445 the numbers run (128), (129), (130), (131|129); on 446 they restart at (128). So (128)–(131) each occur
    twice, and a reference to one of them is ambiguous unless its page is given.
- **C6 — 448–449, (134)–(138) — unpursued implication.** The recap reconstructs M from the data (135) as a quotient of
  SG × S′T_ρ × SN′_σ × G_m by μ₂ embedded diagonally (four μ₂'s identified). This is equivalent to (102) because
  R ≃ S′T_ρ × SN′_σ × G_m by (123)/(147). The compatibility "produit … triv." of N32 is the descent condition for that
  quotient.


# ===== LM_C3-454-472 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-454-472: research notes checked against the corrected transcription (scans 454–472)

**Source:** the first pass's research notes in `answers/unzipped/LM_C3-454-472/LM_C3-454-472_notes.md` (§ "Research
notes", notes 1–52). Each note was checked against `LM_C3-454-472_checked.tex`.

**Verdicts:**
- keep: the note stands as written.
- correct: the note is revised; the reason is given.
- drop: the note is withdrawn.

**Kinds:**
- conjecture: Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations and programme.
- unproved claim: a claim without proof in the cited passage.
- unpursued implication.
- own idea.

Notes marked **Claude (second pass)** are new.

The pages are §48 XI, upper numbers 530–538. The letter defined by ξ = αρ(α)⁻¹ is read ξ throughout (changes row 16).

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 454–457

- **N1 — scan 454 — unproved claim — correct.** The decomposition U = f⁻¹u̲, with a unique pair
  (f,u) ∈ Γ(SG×T₀), follows at once from (156). Since δ_G|T₀ : T₀ → G_m is an isomorphism, u̲ must be
  (δ_G|T₀)⁻¹(det U), and then f = u̲U⁻¹ has determinant 1; this pair works over any base. It is therefore not a
  gap. The real hypothesis is (156), repeated as (162 c).
- **N2 — scan 455 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** "on a admis, bien sûr" that the δ_ρ, δ_σ of (135) are induced
  by δ_G through (155). The Proposition of scan 458 makes this an explicit hypothesis, the inserted
  (163 a): δ_G|T_ρ = δ_ρ, δ_G|N′_σ = δ_σ.
- **N3 — scan 456 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** "n'est guère concevable que si" T₀ normalises L₀ and σ⁻¹ρ has the
  form (159). The Proposition adopts these as hypotheses (163 c, d) rather than deriving them, so their necessity is
  asserted only heuristically.
- **N4 — scan 456 — unpursued implication — correct.** The reference reads $\overline{\mathrm{VII}}$ (V and two
  strokes), not VIII. The Circle table names §48 VII "Récapitulation sur les épinglages" (p. 476). The comparison
  behind "encore de la marge" with the "conditions draconiennes" of VII is announced, not carried out.
- **N5 — scan 456 — conjecture — keep.** "il doit y avoir des façons plus convaincantes et exigeantes de l'amener":
  the formulas (160) are the proposed route, and scan 457 derives (157) from them ("OK !").
- **N6 — scan 456 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** C2 below gives an explicit Gl(2) model in which (156), (159 b) and
  (163 c) hold.
- **N7 — scan 457 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The expectation "ça doit être un calcul formel" is confirmed on the
  page. The sign bookkeeping is in C1: the second pass reads the ε of "σ(u) = ·η⁻¹u" as struck, as the result line
  η⁻¹ξ requires.
- **N8 — scan 457 — unpursued implication — keep.** (161) is (160) after conjugating by f. With U̲ = f⁻¹u̲, α₀ = f⁻¹α and
  β₀ = f⁻¹β, int(f) turns the relations for U̲, α₀, β₀ into the same relations for u̲, α, β, so f drops out.

### Scans 458–461

- **N9 — scan 458 — unproved claim — correct.**
  - "un unique syst. (153)" is the decomposition of N1, which comes from (162 c), followed by the explicit
    formulas (165).
  - What the Proposition adds is scan 459's "on a alors les formules (160), (161), (161 bis), et la formule du lacet
    (157)".
  - These were derived on scans 456–457 from (158 a) and (159 b), i.e. from (163 b, c). The derivation of (160) from
    them is the short computation on scan 457, which is complete.
- **N10 — scan 458 (163 d) — unpursued implication — keep.** The "∃ loc.ᵗ section λ₀ ∈ ΓG_m" is local. How two
  local choices of λ₀, ε₀u₀ compare on overlaps is not discussed. It matters if (163 d) is to be read as a condition
  on the sheaf rather than on points.
- **N11 — scan 458 (163 d) — own idea (first pass) — correct, sharpened.**
  - The ink reads σ̃⁻¹ρ̃ ∈ Γε̃₀L̃₀, after a struck T̃₀.
  - The i.e. clause says λ₀σ̃⁻¹ρ̃ = ε₀u₀ with u₀ ∈ ΓT₀, i.e. σ̃⁻¹ρ̃ ∈ Γε̃₀T̃₀, as in (159 a).
  - Since ε₀ ∈ L₀ (scan 462: L₀ ≅ G_a through ε₀), Γε̃₀L̃₀ is just ΓL̃₀, which does not match the i.e. clause.
  - L̃₀ is probably a slip for T̃₀. The transcription keeps the ink.
- **N12 — scan 459 — conjecture — keep.** The question separates the reconstruction of (152) from (α,β,f) from the
  admissibility conditions on (α,β,f). Scan 460 answers the first part, and scans 470–472 come back to the second.
- **N13 — scan 459 — unproved claim — keep, corrected reading.** The margin reads "en fait, on aura m̄
  T₀∩T_ρ = {1} dans les cas envisagés dans II, III et \unsure{suivants|suite}…". The stronger triviality is asserted
  for the cases of §48 II and III (Circle table pp. 434 and 443) and is not proved here.
- **N14 — scan 459 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N15 — scan 460 — unpursued implication — keep.** The claim follows from (165)–(166): replacing φ₀ by f⁻¹.φ₀ changes
  only f (f₁ = f₀f), so admissibility conditions can involve α, β alone. f is a free parameter in SG.
- **N16 — scan 460 — conjecture — keep, corrected reading.** The text reads "par la validité de la formule des lacets
  (157)", not "du lacet". It is a wish ("On voudrait"), and the margin note (N17) immediately says that the condition
  is not sufficient.
- **N17 — scan 460, diagonal margin — unproved claim — keep, corrected reading.** "on trouvera \unsure{donc|dans} un
  u̲ ∈ B′₀". The extra condition B = 0, with B = ac+bd+bc for u̲ = (a b; c d), is given "cas modulaire" with a
  reference to "prop. p. 412′"; it cannot be checked from these pages. The last five lines remain too uncertain to
  interpret.
- **N18 — scan 461 — conjecture — keep.** "plus restrictives que nécessaires … dans un contexte plus général que
  celui des formes de Gl(2), GP(1), Sl(2)": the hypotheses are working hypotheses for these forms.
- **N19 — scan 461 — unproved claim — keep.** "μ est déjà défini en termes de α seul" refers back to earlier pages. In
  the lacet relation (31), αρ(α)⁻¹ = εβσ(β)⁻¹ε₁(μ−1), μ and ε occur as parameters.
- **N20 — scan 461 — unpursued implication — keep, with the reason.** With ε = 1, β is determined modulo β ↦ βt with
  t ∈ T_σ. Indeed βtσ(βt)⁻¹ = βσ(β)⁻¹, since σ(t) = t, σ being a central section of T_σ (162 a). So what α
  determines is the coset βT_σ.

### Scans 462–467

- **N21 — scan 462 — unproved claim — keep.** (166) holds "par construction" of SM_{ρ,σ} as a semidirect product,
  with M_{ρ,σ} acting on L₀ through χ. The letter of the map (ℰ_{L₀} or ε_{L₀}) remains uncertain.
- **N22 — scan 462 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** This is immediate from (166) with μ = χ(U) = 1.
- **N23 — scan 463 — unproved claim — keep.** "des ss-groupes … pour le troisième, ce sera une section partielle
  seulement, au dessus du sous-groupe 𝒯′_{ρ,σ} d'indice 2 … noyau de ε₃". Only the first case (0) is worked out on
  these pages (scans 464–465).
- **N24 — scan 463, margin — authorial reservation — keep, corrected reading.** The note reads "finalement ça servira
  pas à grand chose…", with no "ne". It judges the general bracketed discussion of sections and cancels no statement.
- **N25 — scan 463, (1)–(4) — unproved claim — correct.**
  - The prose reads "Les hom … qui satisfont à (3) correspondent aux scindages …".
  - The step to arbitrary homomorphisms ψ⁰ : R_{ρ,σ} → SG in (4)–(5) uses only (3 b), "remonte (1)".
  - (3 a) then becomes (6): ψ⁰(x) = x on μ₂ = R_{ρ,σ} ∩ SG. That is the descent condition through the kernel μ₂.
- **N26 — scan 463 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The descent equation is (6).
- **N27 — scan 464 — unproved claim — correct.** "Reste à vérifier … est épi" is carried out on scan 465. The inverse
  image of M_{ρ,σ}^{(0)} in M̃_{ρ,σ} = R_{ρ,σ} × SG is the graph (12) of the homomorphism (13). So the subgroup is a
  section over all of 𝒯_{ρ,σ}, and the claim is proved there.
- **N28 — scan 464, (7)–(8) — unpursued implication — keep.** M^{(0)} = Θ⁻¹(T₀) depends on the choice of the torus
  T₀ ⊂ G with δ_G|T₀ an isomorphism. How the other subgroups M^{(1/2)}, M^{(−J̄)}, announced on scans 463–464, compare
  with it is not treated here.
- **N29 — scan 465 — unproved claim — correct.** That ψ⁰₀(r) = κ(r)⁻¹i_{T₀}(κ(r)²) is a homomorphism is immediate:
  κ(r) is a scalar, hence central, and i_{T₀} is a homomorphism into the commutative T₀. Its values lie in SG because
  a scalar κ has determinant κ² in rank 2 ("déterminant κ(r)²" on the same page) and det i_{T₀}(λ) = λ.
- **N30 — scan 465, (11)–(13) — unpursued implication — keep.** On μ₂, κ(r)² = 1 and ψ⁰₀(r) = κ(r)⁻¹ = κ(r) = r,
  which is condition (6).
- **N31 — scan 466 — unproved claim — correct.** The remark allows "un hom quelconque, noté i′_{T₀}, de G_m dans SG"
  in (13). With values in SG, κ(r)⁻¹i′(κ(r)²) has determinant κ(r)⁻², not 1. So (13) defines a section into SG only
  if det∘i′ = id_{G_m}, i.e. if i′ : G_m → G is a section of the determinant, as i_{T₀} is. "SG" is probably a slip
  for G, or the condition is tacit; the ink reads SG and is kept.
- **N32 — scan 466, (14)–(16) — unpursued implication — keep.** M^{[0]} = Θ⁻¹(B′₀) = M^{(0)}.L₀ makes sense "dès que
  l'on a un T₀ comme dessus et un sous-groupe L₀ de SG, invarié par T₀". The minimal hypotheses are exactly these
  two.
- **N33 — scan 467, cancelled block — keep.** The struck passage, "L₀ … opère trivialᵗ sur SG", is a rejected step.
- **N34 — scan 467 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** C2 supplies such a model.
- **N35 — scan 467 — unpursued implication — keep, corrected reading.** "Revenons" is struck. The sentence reads "je
  commence à nouveau à m'y perdre, je vais me raccrocher — pour une compréhension de la signification ‘géom.’ de
  SM_{ρ,σ}, M^{[0]}_{ρ,σ}, aux notations de la p. 529".

### Scans 468–472

- **N36 — scan 468 — unproved claim — correct.** The image description is proved on scan 469:
  - the reduction "OPS" to the case where λ² = μ;
  - a choice of r with κ(r) = λ;
  - R_{ρ,σ} ≃ S′T_ρ × SN′_σ.

  The Proposition is therefore proved, given local square roots (see N39).
- **N37 — scan 468, NB — unpursued implication — keep.** The formulas ε = ε_σ(b̲), ε′ = δ_ρ(a̲)/δ_σ(b̲),
  μ = δ_σ(b̲) = δ_G(U̲) recover the last three coordinates of (19). That is why the Corollary (21)–(22) drops them.
- **N38 — scan 468 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N39 — scan 469 — unproved claim — correct, new reading.**
  - The text reads "OPS qu'on a un λ tel que λ² = μ =dfn det U̲": on peut supposer.
  - This reduction to the case where det U̲ is a square is legitimate for proving an epimorphism of sheaves. Square
    roots of a unit exist fppf-locally, and étale-locally where 2 is invertible.
  - r with κ(r) = λ then exists because R_{ρ,σ} contains G_m (scan 463, (1)).
- **N40 — scan 469, Remarque — unpursued implication — keep, corrected wording.** "On ne peut remplacer G par PG": the
  kernel of M_{ρ,σ} → PG × T_ρ × N′_σ (× G_m × μ₂ × μ₂) is the subgroup μ₂ of 𝒢⁺_{ρ,σ} = SG, which maps to 1 in PG.
- **N41 — scan 469 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** In characteristic 2, μ₂ is infinitesimal. The "OPS" step of N39
  then needs the fppf topology.
- **N42 — scan 470 — unproved claim — correct.** The uniqueness "Cette condition assure l'unicité de f, u̲" follows
  from SG ∩ T₀ = {1} (scan 464), exactly as in N1. It is proved.
- **N43 — scan 470 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N44 — scan 470 — own idea (first pass) — keep, made explicit.**
  - For given x, the admissible u̲ ∈ B′₀ (those with det u̲ = det U̲) form the coset i_{T₀}(det U̲)L₀.
  - Because T₀ normalises L₀, both left and right multiplication by L₀ act on this coset simply transitively.
  - f = u̲U̲⁻¹ changes accordingly, by f ↦ u̲ℓU̲⁻¹ or f ↦ ℓf.
- **N45 — scan 471 — unproved claim — keep.** "il f. et s. que l'on ait la relation (31)": the converse
  (sufficiency) is not proved on this page.
- **N46 — scan 471 — unproved claim — correct, new reading.**
  - The observation begins "(NB", not "Obs".
  - It continues with the insertion "Alors le couple (x,u̲) est unique. Pour f = 1, (i.e. u̲ = U̲),", followed by the
    live "On peut dire aussi qu'on a une correspondance 1-1 entre l'ens. · sections du sous-groupe M_{ρ,σ}[0] … et
    l'ens. des couples α, β satisfaisant …".
  - The uniqueness and the correspondence are asserted without proof.
- **N47 — scan 471 — unpursued implication — keep.** The parenthesis "(pas M_{ρ,σ}(0)!)" stresses that the solutions
  (α,β) correspond to sections of the larger M^{[0]}.
- **N48 — scan 471 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** C2 gives the model.
- **N49 — scan 472 — conjecture — keep.** "il me faudra revenir en termes plus géométriques": an announced
  reinterpretation of the "groupe étrange" on the triples (α,β,f).
- **N50 — scan 472 — unproved claim — keep.** SM_{ρ,σ} "sera isomorphe à schéma (en groupes) des solutions communes
  de (27)(31)". Only the scheme-level presentations (32) and (33) are given.
- **N51 — scan 472, (32)–(33) — unpursued implication — correct, verified.** The condition in (33) follows from (29)
  and (32): f = u̲U̲⁻¹ ∈ SG ⟺ det u̲ = det U̲ = δ_G(Θ_G(x)), and this equals χ(x) by (19)–(20) (μ = χ(x)). So (33) is
  the fibre product of χ : M_{ρ,σ} → G_m and det : B′₀ → G_m.
- **N52 — scan 472 — own idea (first pass) — keep, made explicit.** As schemes the fibre product (33) is identified
  with M_{ρ,σ} × L₀ by (x, ℓ) ↦ (x, i_{T₀}(χ(x))ℓ). Indeed u̲ = tℓ ∈ T₀.L₀ with det u̲ = χ(x) forces
  t = i_{T₀}(χ(x)). Whether this is a group isomorphism with the semidirect product of scans 462 and 467 depends on
  the group law on the triples, which is not given here.

## Notes added by Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scan 457 — own idea (computation checked) — Claude (second pass).**
  - From (161), u̲(σ) = ε int(β)(σ), one gets [u̲,σ] = εβσ(β)⁻¹ = η, hence σ(u̲) = η⁻¹u̲.
  - With ρ(u̲) = ξ⁻¹u̲ this gives σ([u̲,ε₀]) = σ(u̲)ρ(u̲)⁻¹ = η⁻¹ξ, as on the page.
  - An ε in "σ(u) = εη⁻¹u" would give εη⁻¹ξ and contradict (157) by the factor ε. The ink has a blotted ε there,
    read as struck.
  - This is what the margin of scan 456 warns about: "attention au signe ε".
- **C2 — scans 456, 458, 466 — own idea (verified) — Claude (second pass).** A model for all the hypotheses:
  - G = Gl(2), T₀ = {diag(t,1)}, L₀ = {(1 λ; 0 1)} and ε₀(λ) = (1 λ; 0 1).
  - Then δ_G|T₀ is an isomorphism, G = T₀.SG is semidirect and SG ∩ T₀ = {1}.
  - diag(t,1)ε₀(λ)diag(t,1)⁻¹ = ε₀(tλ) with t = det, which is (159 b) and (163 c).
  - B′₀ = T₀.L₀ is the group of matrices (a b; 0 1), of dimension 2.
  - L₀ has dimension 1, so dim M^{[0]} = dim M^{(0)} + 1, as on scan 466 ("de dim 4, au lieu de 3").
- **C3 — scan 457, (161 bis) — unpursued implication — Claude (second pass).** [u̲,ε₀] = ε₀(μ−1) is
  u̲ε₀u̲⁻¹ε₀⁻¹ = ε₀(μ)ε₀(1)⁻¹ in the additive parametrisation, using (159 b). This is the only place where the action
  through the determinant enters the derivation of (157). The label is (161 bis), live (changes row 15), and scan 459
  cites it.
- **C4 — scans 457, 471 — unpursued implication (notation) — Claude (second pass).**
  - Scan 457 defines η = εβσ(β)⁻¹.
  - (28) on scan 471 writes η′ = βσ(β)⁻¹, with a prime, and β₀ = f⁻¹εβ.
  - (31), αρ(α)⁻¹ = εβσ(β)⁻¹ε₁(μ−1), is therefore ξ = εη′ε₁(μ−1) = ηε₁(μ−1), i.e. (157).
  - The prime is in the ink and marks the change of normalisation; it should not be harmonised away.
- **C5 — scan 463 — unpursued implication (structure) — Claude (second pass).** The struck "XI" before "9°" matches
  §48 XI of the Circle table ("Récapitulation sur M_{ρ,σ}", from p. 508). Subsection 9° (from p. 534) belongs to XI,
  and the author's later table does not list it separately.
- **C6 — scan 469, NB — claim without proof — Claude (second pass).** The margin defines Θ_{M̃} as "l'hom can.
  M̃_{ρ,σ} =dfn R_{ρ,σ} × SG → G". Grothendieck says he previously wrote it Θ_G. The proof uses Θ_{M̃}(r,U₀) =
  κ(r)U₀ (scan 465) as the definition, and it is a homomorphism only because κ(r) is central (see N29).


# ===== LM_C3-473-491 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-473-491 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 473–491)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C3-473-491/LM_C3-473-491_notes.md, notes 1–60), each
checked against LM_C3-473-491_checked.tex. Verdicts: keep, correct (text revised), drop. Notes marked **Claude (second
pass)** are new. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations, programme), unproved claim,
unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved in the cited passage.

Conventions used in the checks (from (8), scan 479): ρ = R = (0 1; −1 1), σ = J = (0 1; −1 0), acting on G = Gl(2) by
conjugation, so ρ(α) = RαR⁻¹, σ(b) = JbJ⁻¹; ρ³ = σ² = −1. Cross-references: p. 526' = scan 448, p. 535' = scan 466,
p. 536' = scan 468 (proposition (19)–(20), corollary (21)–(22)), p. 538' = scan 471 ((27)–(31), existence theorem).

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 473–478 (§48.XI, 9°–10°)

- **N1 — scan 473, (34)–(36) — unproved claim — correct.** With (34) as definition, SM = M ×_{G_m} B′₀ is a fibre
  product of group schemes over homomorphisms, so the group law is automatic and the two projections give (35), (36)
  with kernels L₀ = Ker δ_{B′₀} and M⁺ = Ker χ. What remains to check is surjectivity: for (35) it is clear (det on
  B′₀ is split by T₀, scan 474); for (36) it needs χ: M → G_m to be fppf-surjective. Revised: the group law is not an
  issue, surjectivity of χ is.
- **N2 — scan 473, (37) — unpursued implication — keep.** (37) is the graph of Θ_G on M[0]; it meets L₀ trivially and
  maps isomorphically onto M[0] under (35); its image in B′₀ under (36) is Θ_G(M[0]). The cross-reference beside it is
  now read "(cf (14|19) p. 536')" (live, not struck).
- **N3 — scan 474, (38) — unproved claim — correct.** Checked: i_{T₀}(μ) = diag(μ,1) conjugates (1 λ; 0 1) to
  (1 μλ; 0 1), so through the embedding x ↦ (x, i_{T₀}(χ(x))) (scan 479, (7)) M acts on L₀ ≅ G_a by λ ↦ χ(x)λ, as the
  text says. Revised: verified.
- **N4 — scan 474, theorem (39) — unproved claim — correct.** The isomorphism is the existence-and-uniqueness statement
  of p. 538' (scan 471: (α,β,f) satisfying (27), (31) come from a unique (x,u) by (30)). The group law on SΣ* is the
  transported one, not the componentwise one: (x,u)(x′,u′) gives α″ = uu′a′⁻¹a⁻¹ ≠ αα′ in general (scan 472 speaks of a
  "groupe étrange … via relation de cocycles"). The ink cites "(17) et (31)"; see C2.
- **N5 — scan 475, (40)–(41) — unproved claim — correct.** Immediate: (1,ℓ)(x,u) = (x,ℓu) and a, b, U depend on x
  only, so α, β, f become ℓα, ℓβ, ℓf. Since the L₀-action on SM is free with quotient M, the quotient SΣ*/L₀ exists and
  is M through (22). Revised: verified.
- **N6 — scan 475, proposition (42)–(43) — unproved claim — correct.** The corollary is on scan 468: x ↦ (U,a,b) is a
  monomorphism with image δ_G(U) = δ_σ(b), [δ_ρ(a)/δ_σ(b)]² = 1. Adding det u = det U from the fibre product gives
  (43) at once, so "conséquence immédiate" holds. Revised: the missing corollary is located.
- **N7 — scan 475, (43) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The condition det a/μ ∈ Γμ₂ is functorial and μ₂ is not
  étale at 2, so checks over rings where 2 is not invertible are warranted; C6 gives an instance where the 2-adic
  behaviour differs from the fibres over fields.
- **N8 — scan 476, (44) — unproved claim — correct.** Immediate from (22) of p. 536': for (a,b) ∈ 𝒯, (a,b,b) satisfies
  det b = det b and (det a/det b)² = 1, and (a,b) ↦ (a,b,b) is a homomorphism because M ⊂ T_ρ × N′_σ × G is a subgroup
  with the componentwise law and N′_σ ⊂ G. Revised: verified.
- **N9 — scan 476, (45)–(47) — unpursued implication — correct.** The asymmetry is located: (a,b,a) lies in M only if
  det a = det b, i.e. on 𝒯′ = Ker(ε₃) = T_ρ ×_{G_m} N′_σ, whereas (a,b,b) lies in M for all of 𝒯. (In (45) the ink
  writes "dans M", not "∈ M".)
- **N10 — scan 476, (46) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** On 𝒯′ the two sections agree exactly where a = b in G,
  i.e. on the diagonal copy of T_ρ ∩ N′_σ. Over a field this is the scalar G_m (cρ + d normalising ±σ forces c = 0), but
  over ℤ/4 the element 2ρ + 1 = (1 2; −2 3) lies in T_ρ and conjugates σ to −σ, so the intersection is larger at 2.
  Computation checked; confidence high for these facts, the subgroup-scheme description not worked out.
- **N11 — scan 477, Remq — unproved claim — correct.** The general situation is that of p. 526' ff (now read
  "avec 7°", doubtful): embeddings T_ρ, N′_σ ↪ G with δ_ρ, δ_σ induced by δ_G. Under these hypotheses N8 and N9 go
  through verbatim, the second only over 𝒯′. Revised: hypotheses identified.
- **N12 — scan 477, (50)–(52) — unpursued implication — correct.** ρ_G ∈ SG has ρ_G³ = −1, central, so int(ρ_G)³ = id,
  matching ρ̇³ = id; ρ̇ is the homography of the matrix ρ of (8), q ↦ (0·q + 1)/(−q + 1). So transporting M(0) and
  M(½) by int(ρ_G) is consistent on the two 3-cycles. Revised: settled.
- **N13 — scan 477, last paragraph — conjecture — keep.** "Je voudrait : définir M(−j)" (the ink has "voudrait"); the
  choice within the Sl(2,ℤ)^∧-conjugacy class is left open (see N15).
- **N14 — scan 477, (52) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** Verified: 0 ↦ 1 ↦ ∞ ↦ 0 and ½ ↦ 2 ↦ −1 ↦ ½; the fixed
  points of ρ̇ are the roots of q² − q + 1, i.e. −j and −j̄, which is why M(−j̄) = M(ρ) in (49) and M(−j) is a separate
  problem. Confidence high.
- **N15 — scan 478, (53) — conjecture — correct.** Scan 478 holds only the end of the sentence and (53), with σ_i
  clearly read; the rest of the page is blank and the next page starts §48.XII. The restricted choice among
  int(σ_i)(M(0)), i ∈ {0,1,∞}, is therefore left without criterion.

### Scans 479–482 (§48.XII, the group schemes)

- **N16 — scan 479, (1)–(4) — unproved claim — keep.** With the corrected readings: (1) M ⊂ T_ρ × N′_σ × G,
  (2) SM ⊂ B′₀ × T_ρ × N′_σ × G, (3) coordinates (a, b, U), (4) (u, a, b, U).
- **N17 — scan 479, (5)–(7) — unproved claim — correct.** The coordinate orders are now resolved from the ink (see C3):
  (3), (4) are revised to (a,b,U) and (u,a,b,U), but (5) "λ ↦ (1,1,1,λ)" and (7) "x = (U,a,b) ↦ (U,a,b,i_{T₀}(μ))" are
  in the older order, with U first and u last. In the revised order (5) is λ ↦ (λ,1,1,1); the semidirect product is N3.
- **N18 — scan 479, (9)–(11) — unproved claim — correct.** The kernel of ε_σ is the centraliser of σ, the matrices
  (u t; −t u) = T_σ, and ε ↦ diag(1,ε) is a homomorphism μ₂ → N′_σ with diag(1,ε)σdiag(1,ε)⁻¹ = εσ over any ring. So
  (11) is split and surjective on points over every ring, not only fppf-locally. Verified.
- **N19 — scan 479, (9) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** t² + u² ≡ (t + u)² mod 2 and c² + cd + d² ≡ (c − d)² mod 3
  verified.
- **N20 — scan 480, (12)–(16) — unproved claim — correct.** The image of (13) is the locus (14) by the proposition of
  p. 536'. The last form of the sign condition in (14), "b ᵗb = ε", is wrong on Gl(2) (C1); the conditions themselves
  ("ε = ε_σ(b) i.e. b(σ) = εσ") are right.
- **N21 — scan 480, (17) — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The ink reads G_a (closed a, unlike the G_m of (13) on the
  same page). With G_a the equations of (17) do not force μ to be invertible, while (22) sends (u,a,b,U) to systems with
  μ = det u ∈ G_m and (31) of p. 538' has μ ∈ ΓG_m; either the relation forces invertibility or G_m is meant.
- **N22 — scan 481, (19) — unproved claim — correct.** If two systems share (α,β,f), then εε₁(μ − 1) = ε′ε₁(μ′ − 1),
  so ε₁(μ − μ′) is the scalar ε′/ε; a lower unipotent matrix is scalar only if it is 1, so μ = μ′ and ε = ε′ over any
  ring. Hence (19) is a monomorphism of functors. Verified.
- **N23 — scan 481, "librement" — unproved claim — correct.** ℓα = ℓ′α forces ℓ = ℓ′ (α invertible), so the action map
  L₀ × SΣ* → SΣ* × SΣ* is a monomorphism: the action is free scheme-theoretically. The quotient (21) exists through
  (22), being SM/L₀ = M.
- **N24 — scan 481, (22)–(23) — unproved claim — correct.** The inverse of (22) is the existence-and-uniqueness theorem
  of p. 538' (scan 471); compatibility of the actions is N5. The group structure is the transported one (N4).
- **N25 — scan 481, (24)–(25) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N26 — scan 482, (26)–(27) — unpursued implication — correct.** Computed: Σ sits in M as the scalar triples via (30)
  and SG = {(1,1,U)}, so SG ∩ Σ = {(1,1,±1)} ≅ μ₂, central. Hence SG(Ẑ)/SG(Ẑ)∩Σ = Sl(2,Ẑ)/±1, consistent with reading
  the prime of Sl(2,Ẑ)′ as "mod ±1" (as in "le quotient Sl(2,Ẑ)′ de Sl(2,ℤ)^∧′", scan 487).
- **N27 — scan 482, (28)–(30) — unproved claim — correct.** Scalar triples (ε₁,ε₂,ε₃) with ε_i² = 1 satisfy (3) with
  ε′ = 1 and are central, so μ₂³ → M is a central monomorphism and Σ = μ₂³(ℤ) ≅ (ℤ/2ℤ)³. Verified.
- **N28 — scan 482, (31)–(32) — unpursued implication — correct.** M → 𝒯, (a,b,U) ↦ (a,b), has kernel SG; by (26) the
  image of G^{+∧}_{0,3} lies in SG(Ẑ)Σ/Σ, which dies in 𝒯(Ẑ)/Σ₀ with Σ₀ the image {±1}×{±1} of Σ. So (25) factors
  through M̃₀,₃/G^{+∧}_{0,3} = Γ̃₀,₃, giving (31).

### Scans 483–486 (the four homomorphisms)

- **N29 — scan 483, (33)–(36) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N30 — scan 483, (37)–(40) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N31 — scan 483, last line — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N32 — scan 484, upper half — unproved claim — keep.**

  (fibre product over G_m), so D = μ⁻¹ ∈ Ẑ*; by (42) this says c² + cd + d² ∈ Ẑ* for these α. Not stated on the page.

- **N35 — scan 484, (43) — own idea (first pass) — correct.** No separate closure check is needed: M̃₀,₃(0) is the
  inverse image of T₀(Ẑ) under the homomorphism (33) restricted to M̃₀,₃[0], hence a subgroup once (33) is one.
- **N36 — scan 485, (44)–(45) — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N37 — scan 485, before (46) — unproved claim — keep.**

- **N39 — scan 486, 3°) — unproved claim — keep.** ("ici encore", corrected reading.)
