## Scan 602

- **FP 602-1 — conjecture (question) — keep.** (88) "(?)" is resolved on the same page by (89).
- **FP 602-2 — unpursued implication — keep.** The square criterion is turned into the description (90)–(92).
- **FP 602-3 — own idea — correct (computed).** By (89)–(90) the square map 𝓔 → μ is q(e₃, e₂) = e₃ + e₃e₂; its polar
  form e₃f₂ + e₂f₃ is non-degenerate, so 𝓔 is non-abelian, and q has Arf invariant 0 (it takes the value 1 once).
- **Claude (second pass) — 602 and 595 — own idea; confidence high for the algebra.** The cocycle read off (63),
  c(x′, x) = e₃(x)(e₂(x′) + e₃(x′)), gives c(x, x) = q(x), the squares of 602, and c(x′, x) + c(x, x′) = e₃f₂ + e₂f₃,
  the commutators. So the sign computation of 594–595 and the order computation of 602–603 agree.

## Scan 603

- **FP 603-1 — unproved claim — correct.** Immediate: the two elements over (−1, 1) differ by the central −1 = x², so
  they are x and x³ = x⁻¹; 𝓔″ = {ε₂ = 1} has order 4 and contains both, so it is the cyclic subgroup of order 4.
- **FP 603-2 — unpursued implication — keep.** A split central extension would be (μ × μ) × μ, of exponent 2.
- **FP 603-3 — conjecture (question) — correct.** The sign "qu'il faut déterminer" is forced (Claude note below). The
  ink writes "sur 𝓔′" and "Aut(𝓔′)" where the sense is 𝓔″.
- **FP 603-4 — own idea — correct.** 𝓔 is the dihedral group of order 8.
- **Claude (second pass) — 603, the operation of 𝓔/𝓔″ on 𝓔″ — unpursued implication; confidence high.** A group of
  order 8 and exponent 4 (601) with exactly two elements of order 4 (Corollaire 1) is dihedral (ℤ/4 × ℤ/2 has four, the
  quaternion group six), and there every element outside the cyclic subgroup of order 4 inverts it. So the sign sought
  on 603–605 must be ε = −1 unless Corollaire 1 fails; the matrix calculation (94)–(100) is a check of it.

## Scan 604

- **FP 604-1 — conjecture (question) — keep.** (94) is the congruence that would fix ε.
- **FP 604-2 — conjecture (stated difficulty) — keep.** Elements from Gl(2,ℤ) all have ε₂ε₃ = 1, while the test element
  needs ε₂ = 1, ε₃ = −1.
- **FP 604-3 — unproved claim — keep.** Working in the quotient Gl(2,ℤ̂) needs the sign and the lifting of the chosen
  matrix to survive there; not checked on the page.
- **FP 604-4 — own idea — correct.** Still useful as a check, but by the Claude note under 603 the expected answer is
  ε = −1.
- **Claude (second pass) — 604–605 — own idea; confidence medium.** On the elements used, ε₃ and ε₂ behave like the
  signs of det modulo 3 and modulo 4: det = ±1 on Gl(2,ℤ) gives ε₂ε₃ = 1 as stated, and u = diag(μ, 1) with μ ≡ −1 (3),
  μ ≡ 1 (4) is chosen to have ε₃ = −1, ε₂ = 1. This is an inference from the choices, not a statement of the page.

## Scan 605

- **FP 605-1 — unproved claim (deferred) — keep.** "un peu bref… cf plus bas…" flags the parametrisation (96)–(98).
- **FP 605-2 — unproved claim — correct.** Partly checkable: with ℓ₁ = (1 0; 2 1) as settled, (−ℓ∞)(−ℓ₁)(−ℓ₀) = 1
  exactly, and the presented group is dicyclic of order 12. The identification Sl(2,ℤ̂)/Π̂₀ ≃ Sl(2,ℤ)/Π₀ still needs Π̂₀
  to be the closure of Π₀ with the same finite index.
- **FP 605-3 — unproved claim (deferred) — keep.** T₀ acts through ε₂(ζ|ξ), with reference to pp. 593′, 594, (34), (35),
  (35 bis), outside this package.
- **FP 605-4 — unpursued implication — keep.** The extract stops at σa; the congruence σa ≡ u (Π̂₀) is not yet solved.
- **FP 605-5 — own idea — correct (checked).** det(cρ + d) = c² + cd + d² and det τ′ = −1, so (98) μ = −(c² + cd + d²)
  is det u = det(σa), Π̂₀ lying in Sl(2,ℤ̂); (97) and (100) agree with the matrix products.


# ===== LM_C3-606-624 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-606-624: research notes checked against the corrected transcription (scans 606–624)

**Source:** the first pass's research notes in `answers/unzipped/LM_C3-606-624/LM_C3-606-624_notes.md` (§5,
notes 1–39). Each note was checked against `LM_C3-606-624_checked.tex`.

**Verdicts:**
- keep: the note stands as written.
- correct: the note is revised; the reason is given.
- drop: the note is withdrawn.

**Kinds:**
- conjecture: Grothendieck's conjectures, questions and expectations.
- unproved claim: a claim without proof in the cited passage.
- unpursued implication.
- own idea.

Notes marked **Claude (second pass)** are new.

The pages are §49 V of the Circle table, upper numbers 607–615. Readings used below (see the changes file):
- ξ = c²+cd+d².
- ε₀ is the generator of L₀^𝒢 (not ξ₀).
- 𝒢₃ = 𝒢^{+∧}/Π (not 𝔖₃).
- (120) has α ∈ Π̂_{0τ}.
- (122) reads u ≡ ε₃[σ]a (Π̂).
- (7) reads N_σ^* ∩ 𝒢^{+∧} = L_σ.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 606–611

- **N1 — scan 606 — unproved claim — correct.** The margin "On aura néc.ᵗ μ ≡ −1 (3)" has a one-line proof:
  c²+cd+d² ≡ (c−d)² (mod 3), and a square that is a unit mod 3 is 1. So μ ≡ −1 (3), and there is no gap.
- **N2 — scan 606 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** For c even and d odd, c²+cd+d² ≡ cd+1 (mod 4), so (105) forces
  c ≡ 2 (mod 4). The live NB after (103) (changes row 7) is the same kind of check, made at the level of σa ≡ u (4).
- **N3 — scans 606–607 — unpursued implication — correct.** The computation settles the independence.
  - With c even and d odd, (cρ⁻¹+d)² ≡ 1 (mod 4), so aτ'(a)⁻¹ ≡ ξ⁻¹ ≡ ε₂(ξ) (mod 4). This depends on ξ = −μ only.
  - Under (105) the sign is −1. This is why the struck "le signe ε est égal à +1" was withdrawn.
  - The step from mod 4 to mod Π̂₀ is justified in C2.
- **N4 — scan 607 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
  - ᵗu = (Tr u) − u is the adjugate of a 2×2 matrix. So ᵗa₀/det a₀ = a₀⁻¹, and ᵗu = u⁻¹ when det u = 1, as the margin says.
  - The margin is live text: "On pose pour u ∈ M₂(Ẑ)". "pose" is not struck (changes row 10).
- **N5 — scan 608 — unpursued implication — keep.** For abelian extensions the argument is sound: Ext¹_Z(−, μ) is
  additive, μ×μ = (1×μ) ⊕ diagonal, and the extension is trivial over both summands. It proves only that ℰ is not abelian,
  which is what the Corollaire states.
- **N6 — scans 608–609 — unproved claim — correct.** "les seuls élémts d'ordre 4 étant dans ℰ''" follows from the paragraph
  just before it. ℰ − ℰ'' lies over 1×μ and the diagonal, the extension is trivial over both, so these elements have order 2.
  The claim is proved on the page.
- **N7 — scan 609 — own idea (first pass) — correct (answered by C3).** The class of ℰ is the quadratic form
  x₁² + x₁x₂ = ε₃ ∪ (ε₃ε₂) on μ×μ, coordinates (ε₃, ε₂). It contains a square term, and as a whole it is one cup product of
  two distinct characters.
- **N8 — scan 610 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N9 — scan 610 — conjecture / question — keep.**
- **N10 — scan 610 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** If C3 holds, the extension induced on Γ_Q is split (c = 0). Its
  restriction to the subgroup Γ'_Q = Γ_Q ∩ Γ'^∼_Q is then split as well, against "et même l'extension qu'elle induit au
  sous-groupe Γ'_Q". The presumption about S₀^ΠΓ'' as a whole is not decided by this.
- **N11 — scan 610 — conjecture / question — keep.**
- **N12 — scan 611 — unproved claim — correct.** The ink has "ξ', ξ'' ∈ H²(Q, μ₂)" (changes row 22). Classes of
  quadratic fields lie in H¹(Q, μ₂) = Q*/Q*², and their cup product lies in H²(Q, μ₂) ⊂ Br(Q). So the H² is a slip for
  H¹, and it is kept in the text. Read this way, c = (−3) ∪ (3).
- **N13 — scan 611 — own idea (first pass) — keep, confirmed.** (−3, 3) = 0 in Br(Q): 3 = −(−3), and (a, −a) = 0 because
  −a = N(√a). So "c ainsi explicité est ≠ 0" is false for c = ξ'ξ''. C3 shows that ξ'ξ'' is indeed the inflation of the class
  of ℰ, so the slip is not in the choice of the two fields.

### Scans 612–618

- **N14 — scan 612 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The short symbols that the note left open are now read:
  - (120): α ∈ Π̂_{0τ}.
  - (121): ε₂(σ).
  - (122): u ≡ ε₃[σ]a (Π̂).

  C4 derives both congruences of (122) from (120). The "théorème récapitulatif" asked for in the margin is in effect
  9°), scans 621–624.
- **N15 — scan 613 — unpursued implication — keep.** The sentence now reads "Z = Z_{ρ,σ} ⊄ M̃_{0,3}". The index-2 relation
  is L₀^Π = ℓ₀^Ẑ = ε₀^{2Ẑ} ⊂ L₀^𝒢 = ε₀^Ẑ (C5).
- **N16 — scan 614 — unproved claim — correct.** The value b = −1 is proved by what the page has.
  - Existence: −ℓ₀ is one of the generators of Π₀ (scan 605), so −1 ≡ ℓ₀ (Π₀), and −1 ∈ N_σ^*.
  - Uniqueness: N_σ^* ∩ Π₀ = {1}, scan 622 (7).
- **N17 — scan 615 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N18 — scan 615 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N19 — scan 615 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N20 — scan 616 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The two lines "Im(… → N_ρ^*)" of (134) are slips for N_σ^*. The
  defined group, the set {β⁻¹u | u(σ) = ε₂(u) int(β)(σ)} and the last line all concern σ. The ink is kept.
- **N21 — scan 617 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N22 — scan 617 — unpursued implication — keep.** M^! = Ker(M → 𝒢₃) is normal in M, and M₀^! is a direct factor of
  M^! ≃ M₀^! × μ, so it is normal in M^!. Non-normality in M is asserted and deferred ("cf plus bas").
- **N23 — scans 617–618 — unproved claim — keep.** The ink puts v = α⁻¹u in N_ρ^{!*} (changes row 39), the group of (141).
  The Proposition on 618 is the conclusion of this argument.
- **N24 — scan 618 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N25 — scan 618 — own idea (first pass), low — keep.**

### Scans 619–624

- **N26 — scans 619–620 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The nontriviality of M(0)^∼ ("dont on s'est convaincu plus ou
  moins") is not proved here. If C3 holds, the extension splits over Γ_Q ⊂ M(0). Any nontriviality must then come from
  M(0) beyond Γ_Q.
- **N27 — scan 619 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N28 — scan 620 — unproved claim — keep.** The case split uses α ∈ Π̂_τ = {1,τ}.Π̂. The inserted condition reads
  ε₃ \unsure{=|≠} −1, and only "= −1" fits the case split. A struck "α = α₀" stands before "α = α₀τ" (changes row 43).
- **N29 — scans 619–620 — own idea (first pass), medium — keep.**
- **N30 — scan 621 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **N31 — scan 621 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **N32 — scan 622 — unproved claim (recapitulated) — keep.**
- **N33 — scan 622 — own idea (first pass) — correct.**
  - The labels of (5) now read ε'_γ = ε^ρ_γ and ε''_γ = ε_γ^{ρ|σ}.
  - In the second label the raised letter is partly hidden by the arrow. σ is expected, and (5 bis) identifies the two maps
    with ε₃ and ε₂.
  - The suggested audit stands.
- **N34 — scan 622 — unproved claim (recapitulated) — correct.**
  - The second line of (7) is an equality: N_σ^* ∩ 𝒢^{+∧} = L_σ. The only ≃ is in "(≃ Z/4Z)" (changes row 48).
  - There is no isomorphism symbol to retain, so that remark of the first pass is withdrawn.
  - The four intersections are restated without proof, as the note says.
- **N35 — scan 623 — unproved claim (recapitulated) — keep.** The iso (10) was first stated as (138) on scan 617.
- **N36 — scans 622–623 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N37 — scan 624 — unproved claim (recapitulated) — keep.** The first Cent subscript stays doubtful (M(0)|M).
- **N38 — scans 623–624 — own idea (first pass) — keep.**
- **N39 — scan 624 — unpursued implication — keep.**

## Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scan 606 — unpursued implication.** The live NB explains why (102)–(103) are mod 2. By scan 605, (100),
  σa = (−d −c; −c c+d). If σa ≡ u = diag(μ,1) (mod 4), then −d ≡ μ and d ≡ 1, so μ ≡ −1 (4), against (95). The moduli 2
  written over 4 and the struck "(102) est trop fort … (102 bis)" are the trace of this correction.
- **C2 — scans 607–608 — own idea; confidence high.** "a fortiori on a la congruence mod Π̂₀" is valid.
  - In Γ(2) = Π, squares and commutators lie in Γ(4), and both subgroups have index 8. So Γ(4) is the Frattini subgroup of
    Γ(2).
  - Every index-2 subgroup of Γ(2) therefore contains Γ(4). This includes Π₀, of index 2 because Π = Π₀ × {±1} (scan 605),
    and it holds for the closures.
  - So a congruence mod 4 to the scalar ε₂(ξ) gives the congruence mod Π̂₀.
- **C3 — scans 609–611 — own idea; confidence medium-high.** The class of ℰ in H²(μ×μ, μ) is the squaring form
  q(g) = (lift of g)².
  - q = 1 on the image (−1, 1) of ℰ'' (u² = −1). q = 0 on 1×μ and on the diagonal: the restrictions are trivial (scan 608),
    and τ_ℰ² = 1.
  - In the coordinates (ε₃, ε₂) this gives q = ε₃² + ε₃ε₂ = ε₃ ∪ (ε₃ε₂). Since q takes the value 1 once, ℰ ≃ D₄ as in (111).
  - Inflated along Γ_Q → (Z/12)^* ≃ μ×μ, ε₃ goes to (−3) and ε₃ε₂ to 3 in Q*/Q*². This is exactly Grothendieck's ξ'ξ''
    for Q(√−3) and Q(√3), and it is 0 in Br(Q) (N13).
  - So if the extension on Γ_Q is the pull-back of ℰ, it splits, and "c ≠ 0" is false. The pull-back reading is suggested
    by τ_ℰ = τ_{S₀Γ} mod S₀^ΠΓ⁰, i.e. ℰ = S₀^ΠΓ^!/S₀^ΠΓ⁰.
- **C4 — scan 612 — own idea; confidence high.** (122) follows from (120).
  - With τ = diag(−1,1) and τ' = (0 1; 1 0) as on scan 605, ττ' = σ. From (120), u = α ε₃[τ'] a.
  - If ε₃(u) = 1: α ∈ Π̂₀ and u ≡ a (Π̂).
  - If ε₃(u) = −1: α = α₀τ and u = α₀ττ'a = α₀σa ≡ σa (Π̂).
  - This is u ≡ ε₃[σ]a (Π̂), with ε[x] = x^{(1−ε)/2}. It needs α ∈ {1,τ}.Π̂₀, the subscript reading Π̂_{0τ}, not the
    coset Π̂₀τ.
  - The other congruence, u ≡ b (Π̂₀), comes from b = β⁻¹u.
  - The ink's ε₂(σ) in (121) is most likely a slip for ε₂(u), compare (119) and (134).
- **C5 — scans 613–614 — unpursued implication.**
  - Take ε₀ = (1 −1; 0 1) and ℓ₀ = ε₀² = (1 −2; 0 1), as in the first passes of 568–586 and 587–605.
  - Then (127) reads L₀^Π = ℓ₀^Ẑ = ε₀^{2Ẑ} ⊂ L₀^𝒢 = ε₀^Ẑ, of index 2, and (129) gives L₀^Π/L₀^{Π₀} ≃ μ.
  - The first pass's ξ₀ named no element of the manuscript.
- **C6 — scans 616, 617, 623 — unpursued implication.**
  - 𝒢₃ = 𝒢^{+∧}/Π^∧ ≃ SL(2, Z/2) ≃ 𝔖₃. Since 𝔖₃ has trivial centre and only inner automorphisms, the map
    𝒢^{+∧}/Π^∧ → Aut(𝒢^{+∧}/Π^∧) in the NB of 616 is an isomorphism.
  - M^! is the kernel of the action of M on 𝒢₃, as (8) on scan 623 restates.
  - The letter is 𝒢 on all three pages (changes rows 35, 38).
- **C7 — scan 609 — unproved claim (reading).** In "x.u serait d'ordre 4|8" only 4 makes the argument valid: x.u would be
  an element of order 4 outside ℰ'', against scan 608. The ink's figure is ambiguous, so the reading stays open.
- **C8 — scan 622 — unpursued implication.** If the raised letter of the second label of (5) is ρ, as the ink suggests,
  both labels read ε^ρ_γ. That would be a slip, since (5 bis) sends the lower map to ε₂, the character attached to σ.


# ===== LM_C3-625-643 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-625-643: research notes checked against the corrected transcription (scans 625–643)

**Source:** the first pass's research notes in `answers/unzipped/LM_C3-625-643/LM_C3-625-643_notes.md` (§ 5: notes 1–15 for scans 625–630, R631.1–R636.2 and R632.4 for scans 631–636, R637.1–R643.3 for scans 637–643; 47 notes). Each was checked against `LM_C3-625-643_checked.tex`. These pages belong to § 49 V, "Les groupes M_{ρ,σ} généraux (contexte profini)", upper numbers 616–624.

**Verdicts:** keep (stands as written), correct (revised; reason given), drop (withdrawn). **Kinds:** conjecture (Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations), unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea. Notes marked **Claude (second pass)** are new.

## Scans 625–630

**1. Scan 625 — unproved claim — correct.** "μ_τ′ ne normalise pas Z^!_{ρ,0}" is not left unproved in the package: scan 641 proves it ((43): τ′(x) ∈ Z^!_{ρ0} ⟺ ε_2(x) = 1, then the Corollaire). The proof needs some x ∈ M′(−j̄) ≅ Γ̃′_Q = Ker ε_3 with ε_2(x) = −1, which holds because ε_2 ≠ ε_3.

**2. Scan 625 — unpursued implication — keep.** (22) makes N_ρ^? a quasi-section of multiplicity 2 of N_ρ^* → γ_{ρ,σ}; the next pages track which quotient or restriction removes the factor μ.

**3. Scan 626 — unproved claim — correct.** The corrected text reads: the extension (22) "est l'extension notée γ̃_{ρσ} dans la section précédente, elle est sûrement non scindée" (the first pass dropped the σ). So the claim is that (22) is the extension γ̃_{ρ,σ} of the previous section and that it does not split; scan 620 had only "on s'est convaincu plus ou moins qu'elle n'était pas triviale". No proof here; scans 628–634 analyse the obstruction.

**4. Scan 626 — conjecture — correct.** With τ′ now read without doubt, the bet "il y a fort à parier que celui-ci est justement ε_2" concerns the μ-component of the action of τ′ on Z_ρ^! ≃ Γ̃′_Q × μ. Scan 641, (40)–(41) (τ′(x) ≡ ε_2(x)τ(x) mod Π_0), confirms it in the form needed there, given the recalled relation (40).

**5. Scan 626 — unpursued implication — keep.** The proposal to redo the study of the extension from the viewpoint of the associated α, β refers to definitions outside these pages.

**6. Scan 627 — conjecture — correct.** The group is not struck (changes, row 8): the new partial section "serait au dessus de Γ‴~_Q", the kernel of ε_2ε_3, i.e. the field Q(√3). Scan 635 constructs it: M‴(−j̄) = μ_τ′·M^0(−j̄) with (35) M‴(−j̄) ⥲ Γ̃‴_Q, modulo the normalization checked on scan 641. The proposed names M(√3), M(q) are not used there.

**7. Scan 627 — unproved claim — keep.** Sections of M_0^! → Γ̃_Q are said to correspond to rational points of U_{0,3}, the adverb in quotation marks still unresolved (invariablement|inévitablement|inavouablement). The quotation marks flag an assumed, not proved, correspondence of the section-conjecture type for U_{0,3} over Q.

**8. Scan 627 — unpursued implication — correct.** The last group is written N_ρ^∼ in the ink (the N_ρ^? of (21)); otherwise the note stands: the section of b) matches the point 1/2, that of c) the point −j̄, and N_ρ^∼ is not contained in M_0^!.

**9. Scan 628 — conjecture — correct.** The doubt reads "(Chose que semble absurde — on doit pouvoir sur un corps donner n'importe quel invariant…)", not "Alors que". Scan 629 then separates the two questions: the chosen point of M′_{1,1}(Q) does not lift, which "ne signifie pas que son image dans E^1 ne se remonte pas".

**10. Scans 628–629 — own idea — keep.** Comparing the obstruction for one point of M′_{1,1} with all points over its coarse image is the organisation that scans 629–634 follow.

**11. Scan 629 — unproved claim — keep.** (27)–(30) identify M_{1,1}, M′_{1,1} with (U_{0,3}, 𝒢̃_3), (U_{0,3}, 𝒢_3) and their coarse space with E^1 (the affine line; both signs in (27) are ≃). The equivalence of the data (29) and (30) is asserted, not proved.

**12. Scan 629 — unpursued implication — correct.** The margin now reads, with the nouns still doubtful: "T est le torseur sous SL(2,Z/2Z) des rigidifications des points d'ordre 2 et T̃ celui des « pri-rigidifications des points d'ordre ≤4 précisées » (le premier est défini pour une courbe ellipt. mod symétrie, et le 2ième seulement pour une vraie courbe elliptique)". T is thus the torsor of level-2 structures, matching 𝒢_3 = SL(2,Z/2Z); T̃ needs a level-4 datum.

**13. Scan 630 — unproved claim — keep.** The obstruction is identified with a class in H^2(Q, μ_2) ≃ ₂Br(Q) without being computed; see note C2.

**14. Scan 630 — unproved claim — correct.** The margin now reads "corrigé|erroné, cf rectification plus bas"; the rectification follows on the same page (k|Γ′_Q = 1, so k(Γ_Q) has order 2) and on scan 631 (it is {1, σ̇_∞}). The faulty step: connectedness of {−j̄, −j} over Q only shows that the image contains an element of sign −1, not that it is all of 𝒢_3.

**15. Scan 630 — own idea — keep.** Computing the actual image first and pulling the extension back along it is what scan 631 does.

## Scans 631–636

**R631.1. Scan 631 — conjecture — correct.** The expectation "ça ne peut guère être que Z/4Z !" is confirmed in the margin: σ = σ_∞ has order 4 in 𝒢̃_3, "donc E″ ≃ Z/4Z canoniquement". The corrected line also names the extension E″ before the display.

**R631.2. Scan 631 — unproved claim — correct.** The lower margin reads "(Γ_Q)_ab ≃ Ẑ^* ⊃ Z_3^*, or (Z_3^*)_4 ≃ Z/2Z (donc déjà sur Z_3^* ça ne se remonte pas…)". This is a complete argument: a lift Γ_Q → E″ factors through Ẑ^*, the character of Q(j) is nontrivial on the factor Z_3^*, and Z_3^* ≃ Z/2 × Z_3 has no quotient Z/4Z.

**R631.3. Scan 631 — own idea — keep.** Writing the pull-back of E″ along the Galois character and asking whether it splits separates the group data from the margin calculation.

**R632.1. Scan 632 — unproved claim — correct.** The claim that the obstruction splits over the subgroup Γ‴_Q of Q(√3) is in tension with the argument of R631.2 transported to the real places: Q(√3) is totally real, complex conjugation maps to the generator of μ, and E″ ≃ Z/4Z has no element of order 2 above it. See R632.4 and C2.

**R632.2. Scan 632 — unpursued implication — keep.** The non-lifting of one chosen point and the expected lifting of its image 0 ∈ E^1 concern different torsors over the same reduced fibre.

**R632.3. Scan 632 — unproved claim — correct.** Keep, with a reason for the reduction P_0 = Q_red: the map U_{0,3} → E^1 (λ ↦ j) is ramified of order 3 over j = 0, so the fibre Q is non-reduced; its reduction is the pair {−j̄, −j}, ≃ Spec Q(−j̄), on which 𝒢_3 acts through sg.

**R633.1. Scan 633 — unproved claim — keep.** The kernel of 𝒢̃_3 → μ has order 6, contains the central μ and is generated by ρ; checking the order of ρ and its image in Z/3Z completes the NB.

**R633.2. Scan 633 — unproved claim — keep.** The equivalence of the lifting category with the category of 𝒢_3- resp. 𝒢̃_3-torsors over P_0, and the classes "mod noyaux 𝒢_3^+, 𝒢̃_3^+", are asserted without construction.

**R633.3. Scan 633 — unpursued implication — keep.** The failure for P = T leaves other torsors over P_0 to examine; scan 634 begins that classification.

**R634.1. Scan 634 — unproved claim — correct.** The list is exhaustive: the subgroups of 𝒢_3 ≃ S_3 not contained in the kernel of sg are the three conjugate subgroups of order 2 and S_3 itself. Non-lifting is invariant under conjugation, so the reduction to epimorphisms is complete; but see C1 for what it leaves.

**R634.2. Scan 634 — conjecture — correct.** The question whether an epimorphism Γ_Q → 𝒢_3 lifts to 𝒢̃_3, left as "un exercice plaisant et délectable", has a negative answer within the model of (27)–(28): the obstruction depends only on the composite with sg (note C1).

**R634.3. Scan 634 — unpursued implication — keep.** Lifts of the homomorphism of Γ_Q are to be related to splittings of M_{0,3} and of M, with the equivalence relations kept.

**R635.1. Scan 635 — unproved claim — correct.** The deferred verification ("que je vais faire plus bas") is done on scan 641: (42) gives M_0″^! normalised by τ (unprimed in the ink) and the Corollaire states that M^0(−j̄) = Z_ρ ∩ M_0″^! is normalised by τ′.

**R635.2. Scan 635 — unproved claim — correct.** The right-hand side of (32) now reads Z_ρ ∩ M_0″^! (two primes, as on scans 640–641); the ‴ alternative is gone. The equality of the two kernels in (33) is immediate: ε_2 and ε_3 factor through (Z/4Z)^* and (Z/3Z)^*, and (Z/12Z)^* = (Z/4Z)^* × (Z/3Z)^* ≃ μ × μ.

**R635.3. Scan 635 — unpursued implication — keep.** (35) asserts that the projection is an isomorphism on M‴(−j̄), which is more than the existence of the subgroup.

**R636.1. Scan 636 — own idea — keep.** In the struck diagram (37) the two routes from M^0(−j̄) to N_ρ^* have indices 2·6·2 = 24 and 2·4·3 = 24, matching "d'indice 24" on scans 637 and 642.

**R636.2. Scan 636 — unproved claim — keep.** The struck (39) ends at "N_ρ^*/"; scans 642–643 supply the intended statement ((44)–(46)).

**R632.4. Scans 631–632 — own idea — keep.** The real-place test applies as stated: E″ is the constant group Z/4Z and the character is that of Q(j), nontrivial on complex conjugation (scan 631). C2 gives the Brauer-class form.

## Scans 637–643

**R637.1. Scan 637 — conjecture — keep.** The stated programme (invariance of the intermediate subgroups and structure of the quotients) is carried out on scans 638–643.

**R638.1. Scan 638 — unproved claim — correct.** "M′(−j̄).μ_τ′ n'est pas un sous-groupe, car μ_τ′ ne normalise pas M′(−j̄)" is proved on scan 641 ((43) and the Corollaire); see note 1.

**R638.2. Scan 638 — conjecture — keep.** "Sont ils invariants dans Z_ρ ?" is answered yes on scan 640, then refined for N_ρ^* on scan 641.

**R639.1. Scan 639 — unproved claim — keep.** Z_ρ = L_ρ.Z_ρ^! is called "± trivial"; it follows from (19) of scan 624 (Z_ρ ≃ M′(−j)×L_ρ, Z_ρ^! ≃ M′(−j)×μ).

**R639.2. Scan 639 — unproved claim — keep.** (*) is recalled ("or on sait que"); the lemma rests on it.

**R639.3. Scan 639 — own idea — keep.** The exponent glyph in (*) and c) still differs from the ε_2(u) closing c) (it has a top curl), and c) needs an additive exponent (0 or 1) for "i.e. sg(x)=1 ou ε_2(u)=1" to hold; whether that is a separate symbol ξ_2 or a loose ε_2 is open.

**R640.1. Scan 640 — unpursued implication — keep.** Normality in Z_ρ does not transfer to N_ρ^*; scan 641 shows the exception.

**R641.1. Scan 641 — own idea — keep.** (42)–(43) isolate the obstruction to normalisation by τ′ as the value of ε_2.

**R641.2. Scan 641 — unpursued implication — keep.** M′(−j̄) is the only one of the four subgroups (39) not normal in N_ρ; the action table on the quotient of order 24 is not given.

**R642.1. Scan 642 — unproved claim — correct.** The normaliser of L_ρ ≃ Z/6Z in GL(2,Z) is the automorphism group of the hexagonal lattice, dihedral of order 12: a standard fact that answers the note. In the corrected text the denominator of (44) is written M^0(j̄), a slip for M^0(−j̄).

**R642.2. Scans 642–643 — conjecture — keep.** "Il serait tentant" leads to the queried isomorphism N_ρ^*/M^0(−j̄) ≃? 𝒢^∧/Π_0, compatible with the two embeddings of 𝔻_ρ, made a Proposition on scan 643.

**R643.1. Scan 643 — unproved claim — keep.** The proof of the Proposition stops at "L'image de|des"; kernel and image remain to be checked.

**R643.2. Scan 643 — own idea — correct.** Add the order check: |GL(2,Z/4Z)| = 96 = 4·24, consistent with "épi de degré 4" onto a group of order 24 = [N_ρ^* : M^0(−j̄)] (scan 642) in which 𝔻_ρ (order 12) has index 2.

**R643.3. Scan 643 — own idea — keep.** The heavy superscript on the first 𝒢 in (45) is still unresolved (* or ∧).

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

**C1. Scans 628–634 — own idea.** By (28), the margin of scan 631 and the NB of scan 633, 𝒢̃_3 has order 12, a central μ, a normal Z/6Z (kernel of sg) and an element σ of order 4 over a transposition: it is the dicyclic group Z/3 ⋊ Z/4, the pull-back of Z/4Z → μ along sg: 𝒢_3 → μ. The obstruction to lifting any φ: Γ_Q → 𝒢_3 to 𝒢̃_3 is therefore the pull-back of the class of Z/4Z along sg∘φ, which is the character of P_0 ≃ Spec Q(j), so it is the same nonzero class for T and for every epimorphism (already at the real place). In the model (27)–(28), with 𝒢̃_3 acting through 𝒢_3, j = 0 would have no Q-rational lift to M_{1,1}, contradicting y² = x³ + 1 and confirming the doubt of scan 628; the model must need a twist over Q (the Legendre substitutions such as λ ↦ 1/λ act on curves only after extracting √λ). Confidence: high for the group theory, medium for the reading of the model.

**C2. Scans 630–632 — own idea.** For the constant extension E″ ≃ Z/4Z and the character χ of Q(j) = Q(√−3), the obstruction is the cup square χ ∪ χ, the quaternion class (−3,−3) = (−3,−1) in ₂Br(Q), ramified exactly at 3 and ∞. This matches the margin argument at 3 (scan 631) and the identification H^2(Q,μ_2) ≃ ₂Br(Q) (scan 630). Over Q(√3) the class still ramifies at both real places, so it does not split there, contrary to the statement on scan 632 unless a different extension is meant. Confidence: high.

## Second pass: own notes

1. **Scans 646–647 — claim without proof; own note.** By the cancelled Proposition's filtration, |𝒢/Π₀| = |μ|·|𝒢₃|·|μ| = 2·6·2 = 24, which is the "même ordre 24" used to make the injective (46) an isomorphism. The order of N_ρ*/M°(−j̄) is not computed on these pages.
2. **Scan 646 — implication; own note.** With M′ = Ker ε₃, M″ = Ker ε₂ and M° = M′ ∩ M″ (scan 643: M°(0) = {ε₂ = ε₃ = 1}), the last display M′(−j̄) ∩ M″ = M°(−j̄) is immediate. The substantive step is N_ρ* ∩ M₀! = Z!_{ρ0} = M′(−j̄), taken from earlier pages.
3. **Scan 652 — conjecture (tentative); own note.** Both clauses carry "peut être": in the adelic group-scheme case ρ, σ, τ may generate 𝒢⁺ only up to a finite group, i.e. an open subgroup. No hypothesis is given that would decide it, and the constructions that follow assume generation.
4. **Scan 653 — slip; own note.** "il est clair que la condition c′) définit un sous-groupe": there is no c′); the condition just rewritten is b′) (5*). An edition for readers should flag it, not alter the transcription.
5. **Scan 654 — slip; own note.** In the addition to (6) the ink has α_𝒢(α′) = ε′₃; (7) uses ε_𝒢(α′), so α_𝒢 is a slip for ε_𝒢.
6. **Scan 656 — claim without proof (cancelled); own note.** In the proof on scans 653–655, a) is shown closed under composition using b) for u′ only through u′(σ)˙ = ε′₂σ̇ mod 𝒢^♮. So the cancelled remark holds with a) and b) exchanged: for a) to be multiplicative it suffices that u(σ) ≡ ±σ (𝒢^♮). The ink has b) … a).
7. **Scan 656 — question; own note.** A route for "pourquoi (ε₀,1,1,1) ∉ Z_{ρ,σ}" in case b): with ε₃ = 1, condition a) asks for α ∈ Π with ε₀ρε₀⁻¹ = αρα⁻¹, i.e. ε₀ ∈ Π·Centr_{𝒢⁺}(ρ). Since ε₀ ∉ Π, the claim reduces to ε₀ ∉ Π·Centr(ρ), which still needs the centralizer of ρ. Confidence medium; this is a proposed check, not the manuscript's argument.
8. **Scan 657 — claim without proof; own note.** "l'opération de M(0) ⊂ M! sur 𝒢⁺/Π₀ se fait par le caractère ε₂*" repeats the claim of scan 644 (cf. p. 594). The star may be a call mark for the cancelled upper-left note ("Il en est ainsi si dans la définition (4) … on exige dans d) que u induise l'op. triviale sur 𝒢⁺/𝒢^♮"), which would make the claim depend on the version of (4) d).
9. **Scan 660 — notation; own note.** (23) puts ε_{3γ} on the upper arrow and ε_{2γ} on the lower one, (33) the reverse; ε_{3G} = δ_G ε_{3γ} is written in the opposite order to ε_{2G} = ε_{2γ}δ_G. Whoever uses these formulas should read both as "ε_γ after δ_G".
10. **Scan 661 — reading question; own note.** In (39) b) the sign is written like ε_τ[τ]σ; by analogy with a) ε₃[τ]ρ and with (5*) σ^{ε₂}, ε₂[τ] is meant, but the ink alone does not settle it.
11. **Scan 662 — implication; own note.** The remark's last phrase, "on m̄ a besoin des μ, ε, ε′ pr dessus le marché", says that G^♮_{ρ,σ} needs the data μ, ε, ε′ in addition to the automorphism of 𝒢⁺. Sequence (44) shows what the automorphism description alone loses: Z₀^♮/L₀^♮.


# ===== LM_C3-663-681 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-663-681: research notes, checked against the corrected text (scans 663–681)

Pages: manuscript pp. 634–643, §49 VI of the Circle table ("Les groupes M_{ρ,σ} généraux: récapitulation et
raffinement de la construction précédente"). Each first-pass note (from `LM_C3-663-681_notes.md`, "Research notes")
is marked keep, correct or drop, with the reason. Notes added in this pass are marked "Claude, second pass". Readings
quoted are those of `LM_C3-663-681_checked.tex`; the zigzag superscript is written ♮.

## Scan 663

- **663 · unproved claim · first pass — keep.** The image of (45) is said to consist of the systems (u, ε₃, ε₂)
  satisfying (46), and its kernel is (47); neither inclusion is proved here, and (38) lies outside the package. The
  corrected (47) reads f⁻¹u̲ ∈ G^♮_{ρ,σ}: the ∈ is written over the set-builder bar, it is not ∉.
- **663 · unproved claim · first pass — keep, corrected.** (46 d) "u stabilise 𝒢^♮ (automatique si ε₃ = ε₂, vu a) b))":
  the reference is still doubtful (a) or c)); the letter is a closed blob, nearer a). In (46 a) a "+" seems to have
  been written first and overwritten by ♮, so the condition is conjugacy by 𝒢^♮, not by 𝒢^+.
- **663 · unproved claim · first pass — keep, corrected.** (50)–(51): SÑ₀^♮ ≅ SN₀^♮ "si Ẑ → L₀^𝒢, n ↦ ε₀ⁿ est
  injectif", then the "hom canonique injectif" (51). The generator is ε₀, not ξ₀ (the letter of ε₃; cf. ε₀ of
  scan 613), and (49) reads {(u, μ) | u(ε₀) = ε₀^μ}.
- **663 · own idea (Claude, second pass) · confidence high.** The equality in (51), f⁻¹·(int(f), μ, 1, 1) = u·f⁻¹,
  holds with u = u̲ = (int(f), μ, 1, 1): u̲ acts on 𝒢^+ by int(f), so u̲ f⁻¹ u̲⁻¹ = f f⁻¹ f⁻¹ = f⁻¹, i.e. f⁻¹ and u̲
  commute. The ink writes a plain u on both sides of the brace; it denotes the tuple.

  L₀^♮ ⊂ SÑ₀^♮" gives the exact sequence (52); the inverse-image statement is the point to prove.

# ===== LM_C3-682-696 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C3-682-696: research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (§5 of `LM_C3-682-696_notes.md`, in its order) is marked KEEP, CORRECTED or DROPPED, with the reason. The notes marked "Checker's note" are new. Scan numbers are the file numbers. Quotations follow `LM_C3-682-696_checked.tex`.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 682, unproved claim. CORRECTED.** "Je dis que M♮ est un sous-groupe de M", with a pointer to the calculations of p. 629 ff. The claim is not left open: on scans 685–686 M♮ is identified with the image of the group G♮_{ρ0,σ0} in M ((114), (115)), "qui montre en m̄ temps dans la foulée que M♮ est bien un sous-groupe de M". Condition (107) d) reads "u stabilise M♮" in the ink (see Checker's note A).

2. **Scan 682, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP, as optional.** A direct check that (107) is closed under products and inverses would still test condition d), which is circular as written. The manuscript instead proves the subgroup property through the image of G♮ (scans 685–686), so this check is not needed for the argument.

3. **Scan 683, unproved claim. KEEP.** "(108) … inclusion qui est une égalité si 𝒢₀♮ = 𝒢₀⁺, et qui identifie Z_{ρ,σ} à M![0] ≃ M(0)·L₀^π si 𝒢₀♮ = π := Π₀" depends on definitions made before this package (p. 629 and earlier). Neither direction is proved on these pages.

4. **Scan 683, unproved claim. CORRECTED.** α, β "sont déterminés par u = u₀ℓ de façon unique (m̄ si on suppose seulement u ∈ M![0] sans plus, et qu'on exige α, β ∈ Π_{0τ̄})". The cancelled words before the final condition read "α ∈ 𝒢₀♮", not "avec …", so the revision widens the group in which α and β are sought, from 𝒢♮ to Π_{0τ̄}. Uniqueness amounts to the triviality of the centralizers of ρ and σ in that group, which is not shown here.

5. **Scan 683, margin judgement. KEEP.** "cette explicitation ne semble servir de rien". The package bears it out: the description (109) is not used again, and the argument of scans 684–686 runs through the systems (110)–(111) and the digression 9°.

6. **Scan 684, unproved claim. CORRECTED.** The hom of systems is "de (110) dans (111)" (the label is best read as 110 written over 109). It is the identity on 𝒢₀⁺, (108) on Z_{ρ0,σ0}, and the canonical inclusion L₀♮ ⊂ L₀^𝒢. The induced hom (110′) → (111′) and the map of exact sequences (112) rest on the construction of 9°, which is carried out on scans 691–693 at the level of objects. As written, (111′) ends "M[0]′ ↪ 𝒢₀⁺" (see Checker's note B).

7. **Scan 685, unproved claim. CORRECTED.** The injectivity of (113) is in fact complete as stated. With L₀♮ := Z_{ρ0,σ0} ∩ 𝒢₀⁺, L₀♮ ⊂ L₀^𝒢 and L₀^𝒢 ↪ 𝒢₀⁺, one has Z ∩ L₀^𝒢 = L₀♮, and the identity on 𝒢₀⁺ in (112) then gives G_{ρ0,σ0} → M injective. Only the parenthesis "c'est un iso. si 𝒢₀♮ ⊃ Π₀ …" needs an argument: surjectivity, i.e. Z_{ρ0,σ0}·L₀^𝒢 = M[0].

8. **Scan 685, unproved claim. KEEP.** "On voit alors immédiatement que l'image de G♮_{ρ0,σ0} dans M n'est autre que M♮ (107)". Both inclusions are left to the reader, and this identification is what carries the subgroup claim of note 1.

9. **Scan 686, question ("à vérifier"). KEEP.** The margin (116) describes γ_{ρ0,σ0} = Γ₀♮ as the set of u ∈ Γ̃_Q whose lift u₀ ∈ M(0) satisfies conditions a) b) d) of (107). The author marks it "à vérifier", and it is not verified in this package.

10. **Scan 686, unproved claim. CORRECTED.** No separate product-and-inverse check is needed. By (115) M♮ is the image of the group G♮_{ρ0,σ0} under the injective hom G_{ρ0,σ0} → M of scan 685, so it is a subgroup once the image is identified (note 8).

11. **Scan 686, unpursued implication. CORRECTED.** The size of the kernel is given on the next scan: (120) SZ_{ρ0} × SZ_{σ0} ≃ L_ρ × L_σ ≃ Z/6Z × Z/4Z, a group of order 24.

12. **Scan 686, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Comparing the two fibre-product descriptions (117) and (118) by an explicit hom on triples remains a sensible test, especially because the bases change from γ_{ρ0,σ0} to Γ₀♮ in the revisions.

13. **Scan 687, expectation. KEEP.** "Nous allons améliorer encor la situation, en découpant dans M♮_{ρ0,σ0} un groupe encor plus petit, qui soit une ext. de M♮ par μ." Scans 688–690 carry this out with M♮?_{ρ0,σ0} = M♮_{ρ0,σ0} ∩ M? (124), kernel μ_ρ (126). Surjectivity is reduced on scan 690 and left unverified there (note 27).

14. **Scan 687, unproved claim. KEEP.** The "dernier produit fibré" N_{ρ0} ×_{Γ0} N_{σ0} ×_{Γ0} M is said to be the earlier M_{ρ,σ} (p. 626 ff.) for 𝒢₀^{♮′} = 𝒢₀⁺ "tt entière". This needs the earlier pages.

15. **Scan 687, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Following a candidate subgroup through the two cyclic factors of (120) is a reasonable test. Scan 689 shows that the kernel of the refined group is μ_ρ.

16. **Scan 688, unproved claim. KEEP.** "Z!_{σ0} = Z_{σ0} ∩ M₀^! (= N_{σ0} ∩ M₀^!)" asserts that on M₀^! the normalizer of L_{σ0} already centralizes it. No reason is given here.

17. **Scan 688, unproved claim. KEEP.** "N?_{ρ0} = Z!_{ρ0}·μ_{τ′} ⊂ N_{ρ0}" (the μ subscript is doubtful). That this is a subgroup needs μ_{τ′} to normalize Z!_{ρ0}, which is not discussed.

18. **Scan 688, unproved claim. KEEP.** The margin reads "indépendant(amment) du choix d'un 𝒢♮", attached to the definition of M? by (122)–(123). This is consistent: none of the data of (122)–(123) involves 𝒢♮.

19. **Scan 688, unpursued implication. CORRECTED.** The inclusion before (124) is written "M? ⊂ N_{ρ0} ×_{γ0} N_{σ0} ×_{Γ0} M", with γ₀ as the first base in the ink, whereas (122) and (124) use Γ₀ (see Checker's note C). The comparison of kernels and images through the square (125), now with hooked (injective) vertical maps, is carried out on scan 689 for the kernel and reduced on scan 690 for the image.

20. **Scan 688, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Testing whether (125) is Cartesian is useful. By (124) the top-left group is the intersection, inside the common fibre product, of the other two groups; the open question is the image (scan 690).

21. **Scan 689, unproved claim. KEEP.** "On a vu (p. 626′, (55)) que M? → M fait de M? une extension de M de noyau μ_ρ". This relies on a page outside the package.

22. **Scan 689, unproved claim. KEEP.** "il est clair que l'on a μ_ρ ⊂ M♮_{ρ0,σ0} cf (119)". The inclusion is asserted with a reference only.

23. **Scan 689, question. KEEP.** "il reste à voir si l'hom (∞) … est un épimorphisme, et sinon, caractériser son image." Scan 690 reduces it, and the margin of scan 690 explicitly leaves it unverified.

24. **Scan 689, unpursued implication. CORRECTED.** It is pursued on the next scan. The image of (∞) is the inverse image in M♮, under M♮ → γ₀, of the image of (∞∞): N♮?_{ρ0} ×_{Γ₀♮} N♮!_{σ0 0} → Γ₀♮.

25. **Scan 689, model's own idea (first pass). DROPPED.** The proposed route (image as an inverse image of the images of the two restricted factors) is exactly what the manuscript does on scan 690, so it is not an idea beyond the text.

26. **Scan 690, unproved claim. KEEP.** "Il reste donc à montrer que cet hom est surjectif, ou encore que les hom N♮?_{ρ0} → Γ₀♮, N♮!_{σ0 0} → Γ₀♮ sont surjectifs (le premier sera alors une ext. de Γ₀♮ par μ_ρ, le deuxième un iso)." Both surjectivities and both predicted kernels remain to be proved.

27. **Scan 690, deferred verification. KEEP.** The margin reads: "Ici j'ai évité les vérifications, pensant y revenir dans les pages suivantes, il s'avère que ce n'est pas urgent — il importait plutôt de développer les cas particuliers 'tour(s) de Fermat' pour préciser les idées des formulations générales des M_{ρ,σ}…". The proof that has just begun (β₀ ∈ 𝒢₀♮ with β⁻¹u₀ ∈ N(σ₀)) stops there.

28. **Scan 691, unproved claim. CORRECTED.** The reconstruction is supplied on scans 692–693: S₀G = N.𝒢, the hom i: SN → S₀G, u ↦ uφ(u)⁻¹ (checked to be multiplicative), normality of i(SN), G := S₀G/i(SN), and (7)–(10). What remains unproved is the categorical statement of scan 693 (note 34), not the construction.

29. **Scan 691, model's own idea (first pass). CORRECTED.** In the ink, N → G in (1) is a hook arrow (N a subgroup, as the prose says), and the label φ_N and "d'où SN = φ⁻¹(𝒢)" are added. The author does treat an arbitrary φ_N afterwards: (9) Ker φ_SN ≅ Ker φ_N, (10) "φ_N est un plongt ⇔ φ_SN est un plongement", and (11) uses arbitrary φ_i. The only remaining point is the hook left in (1).

30. **Scan 692, unpursued implication. KEEP.** The NB derives from (3) a) that x = φ(u) commutes with u. Multiplicativity of i uses (3) a), and the conjugation formula uses (3) b) ("et l'hyp. (b)", inserted).

31. **Scan 692, unproved claim at the page boundary. CORRECTED.** It is settled at the top of scan 693: uφ(u)⁻¹ induces the trivial operation on 𝒢, "ce qui est en effet trivial par (3) a)". So i(SN) commutes with 𝒢 and, being normalized by N, is normal in S₀G.

32. **Scan 692, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP.** A universal property for G = (𝒢 ⋊ N)/i(SN) (maps from 𝒢 and N agreeing on SN through φ and compatible with Θ) would be a clean way to state the construction. It is not in the manuscript.

33. **Scan 692, model's own idea (first pass). CORRECTED, now a firm finding.** The ink reads "aφ(u⁻¹)a⁻¹ = φ(au⁻¹a⁻¹) = φ(au⁻¹a⁻¹)⁻¹". The first equality is (3) b). The second should read φ(aua⁻¹)⁻¹, so the doubled inverse is a slip. It does not affect the conclusion a·i(u)·a⁻¹ = i(aua⁻¹).

34. **Scan 693, unproved claim. KEEP.** "On trouve ainsi des équiv. de catégories, inverses l'une de l'autre, entre les cat. des situations (1), et celle des situations (2)". Morphisms are not specified, and functoriality and the natural isomorphisms are not given.

35. **Scan 694, expectation. KEEP.** "Il est tentant de reconstituer G … comme une sorte de 'somme amalgamée'". On scan 695 this is replaced by "Mais il est plus vraisemblable de dire …" (corrected reading), i.e. by the transitive system of isomorphisms between the G_i.

36. **Scan 695, unproved claim. KEEP.** That (11) is reconstituted "lui-m̄" from a transitive system of isomorphisms between the G_i inducing the identity on 𝒢 needs the comparison with the given G. That comparison is made on scan 696 through (19)–(20).

37. **Scan 696, unproved claim. KEEP, verified in Checker's note D.** "équivaut alors à la donnée d'un sous-groupe G de 𝔤, satisfaisant (20) a) b)". The equivalence holds for I ≠ ∅.

38. **Scan 696, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP, superseded.** The graph argument for two groups is subsumed by Checker's note D, which does the general case.

39. **Scans 694–696, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Nonemptiness of I is needed. For I = ∅ one has 𝔤 = γ and 𝒢^I = 1, so (20) forces G = γ, and the original G (any extension of γ by 𝒢) is not recovered. The manuscript does not exclude I = ∅.

40. **Scan 696, unpursued implication. KEEP.** The extra structure is the "restriction" of the extension 𝔤 of γ by 𝒢^I to an extension by the diagonal δ(𝒢). Which families of extensions admit such a restriction, and how many, is not pursued.

41. **Scan 696, model's own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Small finite examples (two non-isomorphic extensions of γ by 𝒢 with the same outer action) separate the existence of 𝔤 from the existence of G satisfying (20).

42. **Scan 694, question. KEEP.** "peut on la reconstituer à partir de (13)". The answer developed on scans 695–696 adds the transitive system of isomorphisms as extra data; (13) alone does not determine (11).

## Checker's notes (new)

A. **Scan 682, claim without proof / probable slip (checker's note).** In (107) d) the ink reads "u stabilise M♮", which is circular inside the definition of M♮. The parallel condition (109) d) on scan 683 reads "u ε stabilise 𝒢₀♮", and the margin (116) uses only a) b) d). The intended condition is probably that u stabilises 𝒢♮ (𝒢₀♮). The transcription keeps the ink.

B. **Scan 684, implication not pursued / probable slip (checker's note).** (111′) is written "(M, 𝒢₀⁺ ⊂ M, M[0]′ ↪ 𝒢₀⁺)". The analogue (110′) has "Z_{ρ0,σ0} ↪ G_{ρ0,σ0}", and the construction of 9° ((7), scan 693) maps N into the reconstructed G. So the third entry should be M[0]^! ↪ M, and the comparison (112) uses M[0] → M in exactly this way.

C. **Scan 688, implication not pursued (checker's note).** Before (124), the ink reads "M? ⊂ N_{ρ0} ×_{γ0} N_{σ0} ×_{Γ0} M". A fibre product over γ₀ = Γ₀♮ needs N_{ρ0} → γ₀, which holds only after passing to the ♮-parts (N♮_{ρ0}, as in (127)–(128)). Either this is a slip for Γ₀ (as in (122) and (124)), or Grothendieck means that the ρ-component of M? already lies over γ₀; the latter would need proof.

D. **Scan 696, verification of a claim (checker's note).** Let I ≠ ∅ and G ⊂ 𝔤 = ∏_γ G_i satisfy (20) a) and b). Then each projection p_i: G → G_i is injective: an element of its kernel maps to 1 in γ, so it lies in G ∩ 𝒢^I = δ(𝒢), and a diagonal element with one trivial coordinate is 1. It is also surjective: lift the image in γ of h ∈ G_i to g ∈ G by b), then multiply by δ(p_i(g)⁻¹h) ∈ G. Hence θ_ij = p_j∘p_i⁻¹ is a transitive system that induces the identity on 𝒢 and is compatible with the identifications γ_i ≃ γ. Conversely, such a system gives G = {(θ_{i0 i}(g))_i}, which satisfies a) and b), so "équivaut" is correct for I ≠ ∅.

E. **Scan 693, implication not pursued (checker's note).** By (10), the situations (1) in which N really is a subgroup of G (the hook in (1), "sous-groupe N" in the prose) correspond exactly to the systems (2) with φ_SN injective. (9) measures the defect in general, so the equivalence of categories stated with arbitrary φ_N restricts to an equivalence between these two subcategories.

F. **Scan 690, claim without proof (checker's note).** The proof of surjectivity just started uses condition b) of (107): u₀(σ) is 𝒢♮-conjugate to ε₂[τ](σ), which gives β₀ ∈ 𝒢₀♮ ⊂ M♮ with β⁻¹u₀ ∈ N(σ₀). The text writes β⁻¹ for β₀⁻¹. The parallel step for ρ, and the passage from N(σ₀) to the restricted group N♮!_{σ0 0}, are not reached before the author breaks off (margin).

G. **Scan 691, probable slip (checker's note).** (3) a) reads "l'action de SN sur 𝒢 induite par celle de G" with a G-like letter in the ink. In the system (2) there is no G yet, and the action meant is that of N through Θ, restricted to SN. The transcription keeps the ink as `\unsure{\mathcal G|G}`.
